# Entscheidungen (Ruling-Log)

Kleine Festlegungen mit Datum und Grund (ADR 0001). Tragende Entscheidungen stehen als ADR unter docs/adr/, Rulings eines Kapitels in seinem Abschnitt "Rulings" bzw. "Entscheidungen".

The sections from M1-01 to the integration of `int/m1-wave14` stand unchanged in
[DECISIONS-EARLY.md](DECISIONS-EARLY.md) (INT-W40/3, INT-W50/5, INT-W52/3).

## M1-42 Groups from OIDC claims

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-42/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave14` at `fd504d9`, branch `feat/M1-42-identity-oidc-groups` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in the German chapters and `docs/operations/oidc-entra.md`. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-45/1). |
| M1-42/2 | 2026-10-14 | Preconditions verified: `group.member.sync` is in `actions.yaml` (resource `user`, right `system`, audit, rights change, no rate-limit class), in 05 rule 1.6 and in 02 4.6 for `authz`; M1-41 carries the external identity in `auth_login_flows` and maps in `completeOidcLogin`; M1-29 provides the rights hook with `permission_version`, `access.changed`, `VerifyInvariants` and the deny entry in its own transaction. | Brief "Preconditions". |
| M1-42/3 | 2026-10-14 | The port types use `ids.ID` instead of `uuid.UUID`, and `ReconcileClaimMemberships` takes the provider id as first parameter and `MembershipWithGroup`/`Group` as inputs; `ClaimDiff` carries `ExpiresAt`. `SyncClaimGroups` is declared in the interface `authz.ClaimSync`, which `Admin` embeds, so identity depends on that one method. | Every id of the code base is `ids.ID`; without the provider the pure function cannot keep claim groups of other providers untouched, which its table test asks. |
| M1-42/4 | 2026-10-14 | The unit of work of `completeOidcLogin` runs first without a rights change. When the sync finds a change it returns `authz.ErrClaimSyncNeedsRightsChange` without writing; identity rolls the unit back and runs it once more declared as a rights change of `group.member.sync` (resource kind `user`, the id is filled in by authz). A login with a present claim but nothing to change therefore never raises `permission_version`; a login that changes pays a second attempt. | 03 section 6.3 requires `permission_version` to be locked before any domain row of a rights change, and 08 section 3.4 item 3 forbids raising it without a change; the person is only known after the flow row is locked. `AllowLateRightsChange` is reserved for the Idempotency-Key stage. |
| M1-42/5 | 2026-10-14 | The sync leaves a per-unit state that the rights check reads: `access.changed` and `group.changed` name the person as actor (the unit's request is anonymous), and the deny entry of an I1 violation names the person as actor and resource and adds `provider_id`, `group_ids` (every group of the change) and `agent_ids` to the violations; the error carries the hint "Sign-in blocked by a group mapping. An owner must switch the affected agent to organization first." (`HintI1ClaimSync`). | Brief "Transaction" and "I1 violation": actor = the person; the generic deny entry of M1-29 names the declared resource, which has no id before the person is known. |
| M1-42/6 | 2026-10-14 | Extending a membership clears `expiry_reminded_at` (brief) and also `expiry_reported_at`; 03 now says the report is written once per expiry time. | Every rights change folds due expiries first (M1-30), so an expired claim membership is reported in the same unit and then extended; with the marker kept, its next expiry would never be reported and the client would keep a stale view. |
| M1-42/7 | 2026-10-14 | An I1 violation ends the flow as `failed` (own unit after the rollback) and answers the `InvariantError`; it does not count a failed attempt of the enrollment code and writes no deny entry of `auth.login.complete` (the deny entry of `group.member.sync` is the record). | Brief "I1 violation": the enrollment code stays unused, and one deny entry names what happened. |
| M1-42/8 | 2026-10-14 | `groups_state` is written only for a provider with `groups_claim`; `groups` only with `present` (sorted, without duplicates, empty omitted); `ParseExternalIdentity` refuses other combinations. A JSON `null` claim is a claim of another form (login fails); `_claim_names` that is not an object is ignored. | Fail closed for a claim the provider sends malformed; absent keeps the old memberships until they run out. |
| M1-42/9 | 2026-10-14 | Claim values never reach logs or audit details; `group.changed` carries the `Group` of the contract, whose `external_ref` is the owner's mapping, so `TestGroupClaimSync_ValuesNeverLogged` checks logs and audit for every value and events only for a value without a group. | The `GroupChangedPayload` schema of M1-48 embeds `Group` with `external_ref`, which `group.read` shows anyway; changing the contract is out of scope. |
| M1-42/10 | 2026-10-14 | Removing an expired claim membership whose value left the claim is a change (sets `removed_at`, `member_removed`, rights version raised). | The row is not removed yet (07 section 2.5 keeps expiry and removal apart); leaving it would let a later claim extend it after a removal the claim already asked for. |
| M1-42/11 | 2026-10-14 | Tests: the brief's integration tests run through the login in `identity/adapters/httpapi` (one world per group, as M1-41/13); `TestGroupClaimSync_OtherProviderUntouched`, the concurrency of the store path, `TestGroupClaimSync_ExpiryWithoutJob` and the AZ-P7 case of `group.member.sync` run in the authz PostgreSQL world, which has the Authorizer with session fakes. The red runs: the new domain and parsing tests failed to build before the code; the app and login tests were written with the use case and were shown red by mutation (hook state ignored, callback not reading the claim). | The AZ-P7 table of M1-29 lives in authz; the expiry check needs a session that outlives `auth.group_claim_max_age`, which the identity world's `auth.session_max_age` does not. |

## M1-49 Who has access, preview and view as

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-49/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave14` at `fd504d9`, branch `feat/M1-49-authz-explain` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-44/1). |
| M1-49/2 | 2026-10-14 | The preview text takes the primary subtag of the caller's `users.locale`: `de` or `en`, anything else `de`; `text_locale` names it. Dates are the instant `expires_at` in the caller's `users.time_zone` (invalid or empty: UTC), `de` as `02.01.2006`, `en` as `2006-01-02`. The templates live in `authz/domain/previewtext` (`catalog_de.go`, `catalog_en.go`); the golden files hold the example of 07 section 9.3. | Brief "Plain-text rendering"; 01 section 9 (language follows `users.locale`, product default German). |
| M1-49/3 | 2026-10-14 | A delegation limit of 07 section 2.6 (`delegation_exceeds_own_level`, `delegation_exceeds_own_capabilities`, `subject_kind_not_allowed`, `owner_only`) gives `200` with `allowed = false` and `denial_reason`, decided with `CheckCreate` on the caller's rights read like `authorize`. Such a preview runs no dry run: `effects`, `invariant_violations` and `hints` are empty, the text holds the summary, abilities, capabilities and the denial line; the target must still exist (`404` otherwise). | 07 section 9.3: the preview shows why a grant would fail; a refused grant has no effects, and an agent grant with `trigger` cannot even be stored (the check of `grants`). |
| M1-49/4 | 2026-10-14 | The preview runs the apply step of `createGrant` (`createGrant` after `authorizeGrant`, both shared with `CreateGrant`) in a unit of work with the same rights change (`grantRightsChange`), calls `RightsHook.Evaluate` (the invariants and the views after the change, the code of `BeforeCommit` without appending or publishing) and the hints inside it, and returns the package sentinel `errPreviewRollback`, so the unit rolls back before the sync and audit hooks. `kernel/txn` gets no dry-run mode. A preview inside an ambient unit of work is an internal error, because its rollback would fail the outer unit. | R-RE-10; brief "Dry run in the real transaction". `previewGrant` is exempt from the Idempotency-Key stage, so no ambient unit exists on the HTTP path. |
| M1-49/5 | 2026-10-14 | "View as" in M1 is a snapshot: `actor.Actor.ViewAs` is `*actor.ViewAs{UserID, StartedAt}`, set only by `authz` inside `viewAsUser`; `kernel/authorize.Principal` gains `ViewAsStart`. From `StartedAt + authz.view_as_max_duration` on, `authorize` and `Scope` answer `step_up_required` with reason `step_up_required` (no new reason code); a view without a start counts as ended. The snapshot lists the spaces and channels of `Scope` for `message.read` and `space.read` with that context, without dm channels and archived structure, each with the intersected rights of `authorize`; a space seen only through a channel is `read` without capabilities. Audit entry `access.view_as` on the person with `target_user_id` and `expires_at`. | 07 section 9.2 and 4.2 steps 4 and 7; brief "access.view_as". |
| M1-49/6 | 2026-10-14 | The capabilities line of the preview always names upload files and confirm approval cards (yes or no) and appends export and manage only when the grant has them; `terminal` never appears (host grants answer `404` before M2-39). | Brief catalogue; the two always-listed capabilities are the ones of channel grants a person meets every day. |
| M1-49/7 | 2026-10-14 | The three new methods of `authz.Admin` take the caller like every other method of the port: `ExplainAccess(ctx, caller, target)`, `PreviewGrant(ctx, caller, cmd)` with the existing `CreateGrantCmd`, `ViewAs(ctx, caller, userID)`. `AdminDeps` gains `Engine` (`*Authorizer`) and `Hook` (`*RightsHook`), taken from `Authorizer` and `Rights` when they are these types, so other modules' test worlds stay unchanged. | The port passes the caller explicitly since M1-29; the use cases need the snapshot code and the rights check of authz itself. |
| M1-49/8 | 2026-10-14 | Source ports: `PrincipalSource` gains `Profiles` (public view, status, `locale` and `time_zone` of people) and `OwnerIDs` (owners rows not revoked, any status); `StructureSource` gains `SpaceDetails` and `ChannelDetails` (names, default operator, the fields of `Space` and `Channel`); `AgentPolicy` gains `BillingModesSupported`, which revises M1-51/11 because `organization_ready` now reads it. The agent status comes from `PrincipalSource`, the effective tool class is `domain.EffectiveToolClass`. Identity and agents implement the additions with the queries they had; chat has no adapter in the base, so the fake and the empty structure of `internal/wire` implement `StructureSource`. | Brief scope "source ports": capabilities, names of the base kept. |
| M1-49/9 | 2026-10-14 | No new SQL in `authz/adapters/postgres`: the candidates of an explanation come from `ActiveGrantsOnTargets` (grants not revoked on the instance and the target's path, valid or not) and `ActiveMembershipsOfGroups`, the trigger set of any agent from the I1 world, now the shared `triggerWorld` that `VerifyInvariants` uses too. | The brief asks to generalise the I1 triggerer query, not to copy it; the existing queries already return every row needed. |
| M1-49/10 | 2026-10-14 | `access.explain` without `owner` or `manage` is `403 forbidden` with reason `owner_only`, the reason of the first alternative of the catalogue requirement `owner oder manage`, not `capability_missing` as the brief's design note says. | The same requirement row serves `grant.revoke`, whose `owner_only` tests M1-48 holds; changing the order would change their reason. |
| M1-49/11 | 2026-10-14 | Explanation of a space: grants on the instance and the space count, grants on its channels do not, so a person who sees the space only through a channel is not listed. The default operator of the space has level `write` and an `operator` source (R-AZ-13); `guard_blocked` names the default operator for a space and the effective operator for a channel. Agents whose horizon counts for `via_agent` are the agent subjects of grants on the path and the operators of the target's channels. Grants not in effect are no source; an entry with level `none` stays only with `via_agent`. | Brief "Candidates" and "Sources". |
| M1-49/12 | 2026-10-14 | `TestExplainSources` checks G-5 (Lotse, `write`, space Support) as `inherited` on Support/Allgemein and as `direct` on the space; the brief's line says "direct G-5" for the channel. | G-5 is a grant on the space (07 section 11.1), so on a channel of the space it is inherited by the brief's own source rules. |
| M1-49/13 | 2026-10-14 | `effects` of a preview: one per principal and target of `DiffVisible` (added, removed, changed); a channel carries the levels before and after and the capabilities it gains, a space appears as `none` to `read` or `read` to `none`. | The difference of `access.changed` (04 section 8.2) knows spaces only as visible or not. |
| M1-49/14 | 2026-10-14 | Hints: `trigger_blocked_tool_class` per active operator agent of the target with effective class `host` when a person or group gains `trigger` and SR2 blocks it (a person that is an active owner is not blocked); `read_horizon` per operator A, channel K in H(A) and person of the subject who may trigger A but cannot read K after the change (a group: one hint per pair with the count); `group_reach` with the active members whose membership is in effect; `expiry_missing` for a person without expiry. The trigger agents of the abilities line exclude agents with an I1 violation and those SR2 blocks. | Brief "hints" and "Plain-text rendering". |
| M1-49/15 | 2026-10-14 | Files outside the brief's scope that the ticket changed: `kernel/authorize/authorizer.go` (`ViewAsStart`), `identity/app/principalsource.go`, `agents/adapters/authzsource/source.go`, `internal/wire` (the key `authz.view_as_max_duration`, the empty structure), `api/routes/real_test.go`, `cmd/bothy/config_test.go`, the generated configuration schema and `docs/operations/configuration.md` (the key is `aktiv`). | Enabling an operation and registering a key need these places (as M1-48/12). |
| M1-49/16 | 2026-10-14 | Review round: a `read_horizon` hint names only channels the caller reads (rights of `authorize`); a manager who cannot read the channel K gets no hint for it, the owner gets all. The template renderer substitutes placeholders in one pass, so a name holding `{where}` stays as written. Not changed (Minors): `via_agent` on a space target does not consider agents whose only grant is on a channel of the space (M1-49/11); the default operator's `write` on a space is not part of the differential test, whose worlds have no default operator; `read_horizon` is reported for every person of the subject, also one that had `trigger` before. | Hints must not reveal what the caller may not see (05 rule 2, R-AZ-01); the rest are hint details without wrong access. |
| M1-49/17 | 2026-10-14 | Red team round 1 (`NEIN`, one Major), fixed with `TestPreviewEffectsOnlyOfTheProposedGrant`: the dry run folds in every due expiry of the instance (M1-30), so `effects` listed principals and channels that have nothing to do with the grant, also to a manager who cannot read them. `effects` now hold only the principals the grant reaches (its subject, or the active members of its subject group), only targets at or below the grant's target and only targets the caller reads (a space through itself or one of its channels). Minors not changed: a view-as call for an id that is no person answers `404` without an audit row; a manager sees the name and reach of a group subject; I1 violations cover the whole instance like the `409` of `createGrant`; a host target is `400` from the shape check before `authorize`. | 05 rule 2; the preview shows the effect of this grant. |

## M1-52 Spaces and sidebar

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-52/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave14` at `fd504d9`, branch `feat/M1-52-chat-spaces` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The migrations take the next free numbers of the base, `00023` and `00024`. Text this ticket adds is English. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-51/1). |
| M1-52/2 | 2026-10-14 | Fractional index scheme (`chat/domain/fracindex`): the widely used base-62 scheme with integer part; digits `0-9A-Za-z` in ASCII order, head letters `a` to `z` for 1 to 26 integer digits and `A` to `Z` for 26 to 1 (negative range), no fraction ending in `0`, `A` with 26 zeros invalid. `KeyBetween("", "")` is `a0`, an open upper end increments the integer part, an open lower end decrements it, otherwise the shared prefix plus the rounded-up digit midpoint. Lists order by `sort_key COLLATE "C"`, then `id`. | The brief's scheme and vectors; byte order equals key order, appending keeps keys short (10000 appends stay at four characters). |
| M1-52/3 | 2026-10-14 | Lock for create and move: first the transaction advisory lock `0x626f746879737063` (a fixed value of its own), then every space that is not archived in ascending id (`FOR UPDATE`), then the neighbours are read in a new statement and the key computed. Archive and update lock only their row. | With no space that is not archived there is no row to lock, and two first creates computed the same key `a0` (`TestConcurrentFirstCreatesKeepDistinctKeys` fails without the advisory lock). Read committed sees the rows a waiting writer committed in the next statement, so later keys stay distinct (`TestConcurrentMovesKeepDistinctKeys`). |
| M1-52/4 | 2026-10-14 | `sidebar.updated` carries no `resource.version`; the payload `SidebarPayload` carries the version of the personal row. The scope is the space. A repeated identical PUT answers the same state and still counts the row version up (last write wins). | 04 section 2.2 rule 3 names `sidebar.updated` among the types without `resource.version`, and the catalogue says the same; the brief's "resource version = state version" contradicts it. |
| M1-52/5 | 2026-10-14 | `Structure.ListSidebarStates(ctx, a actor.Actor)` takes the caller instead of a user id. | Visibility runs through `authorize`, which needs the credentials of the caller (owner role only in a session, SR5). |
| M1-52/6 | 2026-10-14 | authz: `Effective` caps the level at `read` on an archived space path as on an archived channel path (one function, `archivedCap`); `resolveSpace` reads `SpaceArchived`. The rights views carry the level on each space itself (`AccessView.SpaceLevels`), and `DiffVisible` reports a space whose own level changes in `changed` with `level` and `previous_level`, for every rights change, not only for archiving. The new port `authz.RightsQuery` (`EffectiveRights`, implemented by the Authorizer on the same snapshot and resolution as `authorize`) gives the level and capabilities of `SpaceView`; a space visible only through a channel reports `read` without capabilities. | 07 section 4.5 point 3 asks for a `changed` entry at `read` for a space without channels, which the views of M1-29 could not express; spaces in the view only appeared or disappeared before. |
| M1-52/7 | 2026-10-14 | Order of the checks: `updateSpace` authorizes, locks, refuses an archived space with `409`, then checks If-Match and the body; `moveSpace` authorizes `space.reorder` on the instance, refuses both fields (`/before_id`), locks the order, answers an unknown space `404` and an archived one `409`, then checks the target (`/after_id` or `/before_id`: itself, unknown or archived); `putMySidebarSpace` refuses agents and apps with `403 subject_kind_not_allowed` before any lookup, then authorizes `sidebar.update` (`404` for an invisible space); an archived but readable space keeps its sidebar row writable. | The brief's codes; the kind check reveals nothing about a space. |
| M1-52/8 | 2026-10-14 | `grants_target_space_id_fkey` is added `NOT VALID` in `00023` and validated in `00024`. `bothy_app` may read, insert and update `spaces` (never delete: archiving is final) and read, insert, update and delete `user_space_states` (personal data). Test fixtures of authz and identity that put a space grant into the database now insert the spaces row first. | upgrade.md rule M5; 03 `spaces` retention class `content`. |
| M1-52/9 | 2026-10-14 | `docs/api/openapi.yaml`: the examples of the space operations use icon keys of the design system (`headset`, `flask`) instead of `icon.headset` and `icon.flask`, and `moveSpace` declares `409`. | An example must pass the rule it documents; moving an archived space is `409` (07 section 4.5 point 2). |
| M1-52/10 | 2026-10-14 | Field codes: `/name` `required`, `too_long` or the textcheck rule, `taken` with `409 conflict`; `/color`, `/icon`, `/sort_key`, `/after_space_id`, `/after_id`, `/before_id` `invalid`; `/use_global_order` `not_allowed`; `/reason` `required` or the textcheck rule. Audit details: none for `space.create` and `space.reorder` beyond the resource, `changed_fields` for `space.update`, `reason` for `space.archive`, `no_change: true` for an update without change and for archiving an archived space; `sidebar.update` is not audited (05). | Ids and codes only (SEC-AUD-5). |
| M1-52/11 | 2026-10-14 | Files outside the brief's scope that the ticket had to change: the space fixtures of the authz and identity tests (foreign key), `api/routes/real_test.go` (the enabled table and the stub server), the configuration tests, `tools/configdoc/planned.yaml` (the rule `space.` is stale once the key is registered) and the generated configuration reference, and `wire/empty_sources.go`, whose structure placeholder keeps only the channel and thread part. | Enabling operations and registering a key need these (M1-48/12). |
| M1-52/12 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held for `fracindex`, the domain checks, the authz changes and the migration (each red run failed to build or failed before the code); the HTTP tests over PostgreSQL were written after the use cases and adapters. Two mutations show they bite: without the space archive cap `TestSpaceArchiveIsRightsChange` finds no `access.changed`, and without the advisory lock `TestConcurrentFirstCreatesKeepDistinctKeys` finds the duplicate key `a0`. | Honest record of the order of work. |
| M1-52/13 | 2026-10-14 | `fracindex.Valid` refuses only the bare smallest integer (`A` with 26 zeros), not that integer with a fraction, as the reference scheme does; `KeyBetween("", k)` below such a key appends a fraction. Unit tests over in-memory fakes cover the use cases (`app`), the repository runs against PostgreSQL in its own package, and the structure source has its own test, so the server coverage stays above `quality.coverage.go_min`. | The first full suite measured 78.7 % because coverage counts per package and only `adapters/httpapi` had tests; the vector test of the smallest integer showed the stricter check. |

## M1-53 Channels and forum tags

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-53/0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave15` at `e0113d6`, branch `feat/M1-53-chat-channels` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The migrations take the next free numbers of the base, `00026` and `00027`. Text this ticket adds is English. The port methods take the caller as `a actor.Actor` like the space methods of M1-52 (M1-52/5), not from the context. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language; the brief asks to follow the conventions of the space part. |
| M1-53/1 | 2026-10-14 | Validation of channels (D1): names and tag names are trimmed and checked with `kernel/textcheck` like space names; a control or bidi character gives the field code of the textcheck rule (`control_character`, `bidi_control`, `invalid_utf8`) instead of `invalid_characters`, the codes M1-52 already uses; the topic is checked, not trimmed. `forum_default_sort` on a text channel is `/forum_default_sort` `not_allowed_for_kind`, `after_channel_id` and `after_tag_id` that are no live sibling are `not_a_sibling`, a body without a member is a field error with the empty pointer (`required`), `not_a_forum` carries the empty pointer (06 FieldError: empty for a rule of the whole request) instead of `/`. Create and move lock the space row (the order of the channels of a space), every other channel change locks the channel row, tag changes lock their channel row; the lock order space then channel is the same everywhere. `moveChannel` first checks `channel.read` in filter mode, so an invisible channel is `404` like an unknown one before `channel.reorder` on the space can answer `403`. | One validator and one set of codes for all display names (SEC-T07); a channel of a visible space must not reveal its existence through the space-level action (AZ-P4). |
| M1-53/2 | 2026-10-14 | Archived channels (D2): the chat source reports `Archived` for an archived channel and for every channel of an archived space; `kernel/authorize` maps the decision reason `channel_archived` to the problem code `channel_read_only` (also for an archived space, which shares the reason). Manage changes of an archived channel or a channel of an archived space and of their tags are `409 conflict`; archiving a channel of an archived space is still allowed. `archiveChannel` runs as the rights change `channel.archive` with the channel as target, publishes `ChannelArchived` and records the reason. | 07 sections 4.1 and 4.5 and 06 name `channel_read_only` with `reason: channel_archived`; before this ticket the reason fell back to `forbidden`. |
| M1-53/3 | 2026-10-14 | Forum tags (D3): every tag create, update, move and delete bumps `channels.version` once and appends `channel.updated` with the channel; an update without change and deleting a deleted tag write no event and record `no_change`. A tag of another channel, an unknown one and (for update and move) a deleted one are `404`. Audit details are `{op, tag_id}`; the tag name is not recorded, and `channel.create` records `{space_id, channel_id, kind}` and `channel.update` `changed_fields` without names. | 03 `forum_tags` and 05 section 6; audit details carry only codes and ids (SEC-AUD-5, M1-52/10), so the names the brief lists cannot be recorded. |
| M1-53/4 | 2026-10-14 | `listSpaceChannels` drops a row that passed the SQL filter but not `authorize` without counting it; the alarm `filter_mismatch` of `bothy.authz.errors` is not wired for any list yet. | Same as `listSpaces` of M1-52; wiring the counter for all lists is cross-cutting work outside this ticket. |
| M1-53/5 | 2026-10-14 | Channel defaults: `history_backfill_limit` is `channel.history_backfill_limit_default`; `app.New` refuses a default outside 0 to `channel.history_backfill_limit_max`. The column has no database default. | 03 names the key as default and the upper bound as an application check. |
| M1-53/6 | 2026-10-14 | This ticket creates `threads` (and the insert of the main thread in `queries/threads_main.sql` of the chat repository) although the plan row of M1-55 names the table, because R-DM-11 needs the main thread at channel creation. The structure source keeps `Thread` empty until M1-55. | chat owns `threads`; M1-55 builds the thread use cases on the same table. |
| M1-53/7 | 2026-10-14 | Channels created after a snapshot (D8): no change to the cache of M1-28; `TestNewChannelVisibleWithoutNewPermissionVersion` holds it against the real chat source. The archived cap and the reason `channel_archived` already existed (M1-29/8, M1-52/6). | The test passed without a change in authz. |
| M1-53/8 | 2026-10-14 | Test fixtures: the foreign key of `grants.target_channel_id` needs a channels row behind every channel grant. `authz/adapters/fake.Structure` gets `OnPutChannel`, and every PostgreSQL test world over the fake (authz, agents, identity) mirrors each fake channel into `spaces` and `channels` there; the authz repository tests create real channel rows. The chat HTTP world uses the real structure source of `wire`. | The same class as the space fixtures of M1-52/8; one hook covers every fake channel instead of patching each grant fixture. |
| M1-53/9 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held for the domain rules, the problem mapping and the migration (red runs: build failure and the wrong problem code); the HTTP tests over PostgreSQL were written after the use cases. | Honest record of the order of work. |

## M1-54 Operators with invariant I1

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-54/0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave16` at `1e260ad`, branch `feat/M1-54-chat-operators` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English. The port methods take the caller as `a actor.Actor` like every structure method (M1-53/0); the commands carry target, assignment, reason and If-Match. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language. |
| M1-54/1 | 2026-10-14 | Order of checks (D2): load, `authorize` (unknown and invisible `404`, non-owner with read `403 forbidden` with `reason: owner_only`, stale step-up `401`), If-Match (`428`, `412` with `current`), the body of D1, then `409 conflict` for an archived target (an archived space freezes its channels). A request without If-Match is answered `428` by the request chain before any handler, as for every operation with a required If-Match; this leaks nothing, because unknown and foreign targets get the same answer. Like `archiveSpace` the use case runs two units: a first one decides and records the same assignment as a call without effect (`200`, no version, no event, no `permission_version`, allow entry with `no_change`); only a real change runs the second unit, declared as the rights change with the target as resource, which locks `permission_version` first (03 6.3), then the target row and, for a space, its inheriting channels in ascending id, and checks If-Match and the archive state again under the lock. A personal API token of an owner acts with the grants of the person without the owner role (SR5): without a grant that reads the target it is `404`, with one `403`. | 06 "Ohne Wirkung" and 03 6.3; a no-op must not raise the rights version; SR5 decides the token case, D2 names its `403` for a token that sees the target. |
| M1-54/2 | 2026-10-14 | Served targets (D5): chat implements `ServedTargets` in `adapters/authzsource` with the query of 10 5.3 (`queries/operators.sql`): channels that are not archived in spaces that are not archived whose effective operator is the agent, channels with a thread of kind `thread` whose fixed operator is the agent (open or closed), and every space that is not archived whose default operator is the agent as an entry with a zero channel (D5 point 4). The I1 query of authz already accepted such space entries (M1-29), so `internal/authz` is unchanged. A channel of an archived space never counts, as in 10 5.3. | Without point 4 a grant of `trigger` on an empty space with a personal default operator would pass, and a later `createChannel` (no I1 action) would break I1; `TestI1_SpaceDefaultOperatorServesSpace` holds it. |
| M1-54/3 | 2026-10-14 | `OperatorSet` accepts no `resolve_i1` before M2-21, although 05 rule 1.6 names it for both operator actions: the request decoder refuses the unknown member with `400 validation_failed`. | The guided switch to organization is the M2 feature of M2-21 (D8 of the brief). |
| M1-54/4 | 2026-10-14 | Affected principals (D3): the second unit names the old and the new effective operator of the channel (for a space: the old and the new default) through `authz.RightsChanges.AddAffected` after the row locks and before the write, so the set is exact under the lock of `permission_version`; the rights hook verifies I1 for every personal agent (M1-29) and writes `access.changed`. `chat/app.Deps` takes the rights hook, the principal directory of identity and the agent policy source (an agent_id is valid when the principal is an active agent and has an agents row); `wire.ChatWrites` gains `Rights`, and `wire.NewChat` builds the directory and the policy source over the pool. | 02 8.1 and 10 5.1; chat may use the ports of identity and authz (02 4.4). |

Thread creation stays with M1-55: `TestBR_02_ClosedThreadWithFixedPersonalOperator` inserts its closed thread as a SQL fixture, because the chat repository has no thread insert besides the main thread yet. | D6 of the brief; the brief's "through the chat repository" needs a use case that M1-55 builds. |
| M1-54/6 | 2026-10-14 | Test world (`test/integration`): the world of 07 sections 10 and 11.1 through the API with the real identity, authz, agents, chat, audit and sync modules and the production sources; only the login is the test authenticator. The grant G-6 of the app Tracker is left out (apps come with M2). The `via_agent` case of the explanation uses the organization agent Texter (07 10.2, hint H1), not a personal agent as the brief says: a personal agent is triggered only by its holder, an owner who reads every channel, so it can never produce `via_agent`. | The brief asks for the world of 07 with real sources; the via_agent semantics of M1-49 (07 section 6) decide the agent. |

## M2-39 Host records and the target host

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-39-1 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket is built on `feat/M2-39-terminal-hosts` from `int/m1-wave14` (`fd504d9`) in its own worktree and is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it (task of the workflow over the brief's local merge). The base copy of chapter 24, the terminal part of chapter 03 (`hosts.reported_session_users`, `host_session_users`, the composite key of `terminal_sessions`, R-DM-39 to R-DM-41, `host.os_user_max_chars`) and the host schemas of `openapi.yaml` lag `plan/spezifikation`; this branch carries the plan text of these parts, merged three-way so the base edits of M1-03, M1-15, M1-50 and M2-38 stay, and regenerates the REST models and the SDK clients. | Ruling T12, the heart of the ticket, exists only in the plan text; carrying it verbatim is no deviation from the core contract, and the plan port then brings identical content. |
| D-M2-39-2 | 2026-10-14 | Every transition into a state other than `active` ends the open sessions of the host in the same transaction; where the table names no close reason (into `pairing` or `confirming`) the reason is `host_rekeyed`. A request for the state a host already has is no transition and answers without effect. `pairing` and `confirming` cannot be retired (24 3.1 draws no such edge; spec gap in the report). | When leaving `disabled` there are normally no open sessions; ending them anyway costs one indexed query and closes any row a crash left behind. |
| D-M2-39-3 | 2026-10-14 | `TerminalOn` is true for an effective owner on every host and for a grant `terminal` in effect; the exception of I6 for owners on a `personal` host (07 2.12 point 3) is decided by the guard SR11 of M2-40 with `i6_holder_only`, after the requirement. | Before M2-40 no host can be `personal` (only `confirmHost` and `setHostLlmCredentials` set it), so the atom does not repeat the guard. |
| D-M2-39-4 | 2026-10-14 | `host.read` is `All(HumanSession, Any(Owner, Assignee, Terminal))`, `host.manage` `All(HumanSession, Owner)`. A requirement whose first part is `HumanSession` is a gate: `authorize` and `Scope` evaluate it right after the liveness and pending checks, before snapshot, resolution and visibility, and answer `forbidden` with `subject_kind_not_allowed`; 07 4.2 says so in one paragraph. | 05 4.14 refuses agents, apps and personal tokens with `subject_kind_not_allowed` (TM-T17, AZ-M59), which visibility first would turn into `404`; the answer depends only on the principal, so it reveals nothing (AZ-P4). |
| D-M2-39-5 | 2026-10-14 | `access.changed` lists the hosts with `TerminalOn` per principal: over every host for an effective owner and over the host grants otherwise; a host is `added` with `capabilities: ["terminal"]` and no level, `removed` with kind and id only, never `changed`. State and holder do not enter the set, so `setHostState` and a holder change write no `access.changed`; `owner.add`, `owner.remove` and `createHost` report hosts like every other target. A rights change on a host target affects every active owner, the current holder and the subjects of the grants on the host (groups expanded). | M2-46 can end sessions from `removed` entries alone; the set follows 07 2.12 point 3, which names neither state nor holder. |

Retired hosts stay readable records (retention `security` after `retired_at`), and the contract has no filter parameter. |

| D-M2-41-13 | 2026-10-15 | New keys `terminal.report_label_max_chars` (default `100`) and `terminal.report_max_entries` (default `64`) of chapter 24; the start check `fingerprint_keys` requires `terminal.fingerprint_confirm_chars` between 1 and the length of the compact fingerprint and `terminal.fingerprint_max_attempts` at least 1. | Limits only through named keys; M2-43 reuses the report keys for `report`. |
| D-M2-41-14 | 2026-10-15 | The job `terminal.sweep` (and its clause on expired codes) is not built here; an expired code is rejected at redemption, and its hash stays while the host is `pairing`. | No ticket row names the job; follow-up. |
| D-M2-41-15 | 2026-10-15 | Names that differ from the brief: `PairingCodes.Issue` instead of `New`; the port methods take `actor.Actor` and commands carry `terminal.Precondition` (D-M2-39-16); `RepairEffects(state, disabledReason)`; field errors are `errs.FieldError`; `Cache-Control: no-store` comes from the chain for every API answer, not from the handler; `issueHostPairingCode` declares `Idempotency-Key` in the contract and marks its answer as a one-time secret like `createHost` instead of `x-idempotency-exempt`; `TestPairingRoutes_AZP1` is part of `TestAZP1_HostRoutes`; `StorePairing` writes `reported_session_users` with the encoder of the report of M2-40 in the pairing statement instead of calling `ApplySessionUserReport`, which would write a second version and event; the instance id comes from `instance.State.Load`; the test world makes Alex an owner by a fixture row; 08 section 12 already named the kind `hpc`. | The merged code of M1, M2-39 and M2-40 wins over the brief (Boundaries). |
| D-M2-41-16 | 2026-10-15 | The spec chapters on this base are German, so the changes to 02, 06 and 24 are written in German like those of M2-40; the planning rulings D-W-65, D-W-101 and D-W-102 of main change no contract and are not ported. ## M1-55 Threads

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-55/0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave16` at `1e260ad`, branch `feat/M1-55-chat-threads` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00028`. Text this ticket adds is English. The methods of `chat.Threads` take the caller as `a actor.Actor` like `chat.Structure` (M1-52/5, M1-53/0); the brief's signatures leave it out. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language; one convention for every chat port. |
| M1-55/1 | 2026-10-14 | `listChannelThreads` (D6): a `tag_id` that is no tag of the channel, unknown or deleted, gives an empty page, not an error. The list authorizes `thread.read` on the channel as container (a thread resource without an id and with the channel as path hint), then each row in filter mode, in batches of the page size plus one. The cursor is the keyset `(sort time, id)` bound to caller, channel, state, tag and sort; another caller, filter or sort gives `400 cursor_invalid`. The codec is `httpserver.EncodeCursor` behind the port `app.Cursors`, as in agents. | The brief's ruling D6: an error for an unknown tag would let a caller probe the tags of a forum. |
| M1-55/2 | 2026-10-14 | `thread.placeholder_titles` defaults to `["Neuer Chat", "New chat", "Chat", "Ohne Titel", "Untitled", "Test"]` without the empty entry of 09; chapter 09 says so. The empty title is a placeholder through the rule itself, and a configured empty entry still matches only the empty title. | A list element of the configuration is never empty (`platform/config`); 09 section 12 point 2 already makes an empty title `auto`. |
| M1-55/3 | 2026-10-14 | `setThreadTitle`: a person sets the source `manual`; an agent or app keeps the source and gets `409 title_manual` on a `manual` title, not only the operator agent. Who may call at all stays with `authorize` (write and creator, or manage, or the operator agent). | 09 section 12 point 1 and brief requirement 12: a manual title is never overwritten automatically; agents and apps are the automatic writers. |
| M1-55/4 | 2026-10-14 | D8 finding in authz: `Path.ThreadOperator` fell back to the channel's effective operator for a thread of kind `thread` without a fixed operator, so a later channel operator counted as the operator of such a thread (`thread.title.update`, `agent.invoke`). It now returns the fixed operator, zero for none; main threads and channels keep the channel's operator. The level rule is unchanged: the channel operator keeps `write` on every thread of the channel through the channel. One fixture of `TestAgentInvoke_MentionRule` gets the fixed operator its thread would have (R-DM-12). | 09 section 3.1 and R-DM-12: the operator of a thread is `threads.operator_agent_id`, fixed at creation; "Ohne Bediener antwortet kein Agent". |
| M1-55/5 | 2026-10-14 | Thread actions are not audited (05 column Audit "nein"); a call without effect appends no event. `setThreadTags` also sends `ETag`, which `openapi.yaml` declares. `AppendToThread` sets `last_activity_at` from the server clock instead of `now()`, so the tests control the order. Close, reopen, title and tags lock the thread row after `authorize`; `AppendToThread` locks first and authorizes `thread.reopen` only for an implicit reopen. | 05 section 4.5; the contract of `setThreadTags`; the same value inside one transaction. |
| M1-55/6 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held for the domain rules, the migration and the authz fix (red runs in the report); the HTTP and port tests over PostgreSQL were written after the use cases and checked against mutations of the tag deletion and of the operator fallback. | Honest record of the order of work. |

## M1-39 Local accounts with password and TOTP

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-39/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e5`, branch `feat/M1-39-identity-local-accounts` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. If M1-40 enters first, both entries of `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` and both `PrincipalSource` methods stay. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-37/1). |
| M1-39/2 | 2026-10-14 | TOTP is enrolled in two steps as the spec states (08 section 2.5, `LocalCredentialSet` with `password`, `totp_enroll`, `totp_code`): `totp_enroll` seals a new 20-byte secret, sets `totp_enabled_at` and `totp_last_step` to NULL and returns the otpauth URI once; a valid `totp_code` sets `totp_enabled_at` (if NULL) and the step. The members of one request apply in the order `password`, `totp_enroll`, `totp_code`, and any rejected member rolls the whole request back; a re-enrollment of an established path makes it pending again until confirmed. The URI is marked with `idempotency.MarkOneTimeSecret`. | Brief "SetLocalCredential"; the spec counts the path as established only with a confirmed secret, so a new, unconfirmed secret cannot keep it established. |
| M1-39/3 | 2026-10-14 | `authz` learns "credentials exist" from `PrincipalSource.HasEstablishedLocalCredential(ctx, userID)` (a row of an active identity of the person with `totp_enabled_at IS NOT NULL`). The map `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` lives in the new file `authz/app/stepup.go`: step 8 itself is in the pure `authz/domain/decide.go`, which may not call a port, so `gather` asks the source for the person of the resource (its id for a `user` resource, else its owner) and sets `Input.NoCredentialYet`; `decidePrincipalRules` then skips only the freshness check of a `bedingt` action, never the check "human with a session". `passkey.register` stays "ja" until M1-40 adds its entry. | 07 section 4.2 step 8 and B7 (the rule stays in `authz`); the brief fixes the map name and signature for a clean merge with M1-40. |
| M1-39/4 | 2026-10-14 | The otpauth issuer is the host of `server.public_url`; label `issuer:login name`, every part URL-escaped. | Brief "TOTP": the display name lives in the instance settings (M1-46), which this ticket must not depend on. |
| M1-39/5 | 2026-10-14 | AEAD scheme of the TOTP secret: AES-256-GCM, key = the 32 bytes behind `auth.totp_key_ref` as hex (the form `bothy secrets init` writes for `totp_key`, generator `key`), 12-byte random nonce, AAD `"totp/v1" || 0x00 || identity_id`, stored `0x01 || nonce || ciphertext and tag`, the sealing ref in `totp_key_ref`. The start resolves the current key once in the identity step (`wire.TOTPBox`); a missing key or another length stops it. `Open` of a row with another ref resolves that ref through the secret provider (bounded by 5 seconds, its port has no context). | Brief "Sealing", 19 secrets table: a new key applies to new rows while old rows stay readable; the identity in the AAD keeps a copied ciphertext from opening for another person. |
| M1-39/6 | 2026-10-14 | Common passwords: SecLists `10k-most-common.txt` at `3a5f62f` (MIT), stored only as the first 16 hex characters of `SHA-256(lowercase(NFC(password)))`, sorted and unique (adapter README). The NFC normalisation lives in `adapters/passwordpolicy` (vendor group `xtext` now also allows `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm`); `domain.CheckPassword` gets the NFC form, and app gets the extra outbound port `PasswordNormalizer` beside the three ports the brief fixes. | The domain and app layers may not import vendors (02 "Schichten im Modul"); no word list in the repository (white-label scan, size). |
| M1-39/7 | 2026-10-14 | Failure window: the column `local_credentials.fail_window_started_at` (03) starts with the first failure; a failure at or after its end starts a new window; reaching `auth.login_fail_limit` sets `locked_until`, clears count and window and writes a deny entry on the person with `{lock: true, identity_id}` in the same unit of work as the counter. A wrong TOTP code with the right password, a person that is not active and a path without confirmed TOTP count as failures of an existing row; a locked row counts nothing. Every rejection is one aggregated denial `{cause, identity_id}` (no identity for an unknown name), never the login name, the password or the code. | 08 section 13 (failures per account: password and TOTP), brief step 6; the login name could be a mistyped password. |
| M1-39/8 | 2026-10-14 | Exactly one Argon2id computation per rejected request: `Verify` for an existing row, also while locked, else `VerifyDummy` against a hash computed at start with the current parameters. Only a successful login with changed parameters computes a second hash (the rehash) after the verification. `TestIDT21_UnknownNameTimingEqualsWrongPassword` takes the samples in pairs back to back and compares the median of the pair differences, which cancels the drift of a loaded machine; without the race detector it measures the real Argon2id, under it (as in `make check`) Argon2id is instrumented to hundreds of milliseconds with a spread far above `authz.not_found_timing_tolerance`, so the test swaps in a hasher of constant cost and measures the database work, where an unknown name could still differ. `TestDummyUsesConfiguredParameters`, `TestStaleParametersCostLikeUnknownNames` and the case `TestLockedAccountStillHashes` hold the one computation of equal cost. The integration cases run as subtests of `TestLocalAccounts` on one world, as M1-44/13, and the timing test runs in parallel with the package. | 08 section 13, ID-T21; a rehash only happens after the password was proven, so it reveals nothing about unknown names. |
| M1-39/9 | 2026-10-14 | The Argon2id floors are the minimums of the configuration keys (`auth.argon2id_memory_kib` 19456, `auth.argon2id_iterations` 2, `auth.argon2id_parallelism` 1), so the start and `bothy config check` stop with a problem that names the key (ID-T24); the hasher refuses them too. Upper bounds keep a configuration from exhausting the server: memory 4194304 KiB, 1000 passes, parallelism 255 (the limit of the library), `auth.password_min_length` 1024. | Brief "Argon2id"; the configuration loader already reports every key problem before the start. |
| M1-39/10 | 2026-10-14 | `wire.(*Identity).BindLocal(sealer, hasher)` builds the local accounts after `BindSessions` in the start step `sessions`; `nil` builds the Argon2id hasher of the configuration, tests pass a counting one. `bothy setup` does not build them. The allow entry of `local_credential.set` carries `{identity_id, password_changed, totp_enroll_started, totp_confirmed}`. | The login completion of `BindSessions` is a collaborator; the host commands never log in with a password. |
| M1-39/11 | 2026-10-14 | The login verifies the password on a read of identity, person and row without locks and only then opens the unit of work, which reads them again with the row `FOR UPDATE` and the provider and identity share-locked, and decides there (lock, counter, TOTP step, login). A row whose hash changed in between is rejected without counting (cause `credential_changed`); an unknown name runs the same lookups in its unit of work. This deviates from step 3 of the brief, which locks the row before the hash. | With the lock held during Argon2id, parallel attempts on one known name queued behind each other while those on an unknown name did not, so the time revealed accounts and an attacker could hold the row of a person; the counter stays exact because it is decided under the row lock (code review M1-39). |
| M1-39/12 | 2026-10-14 | Every verification of `passwordhash` lasts at least the running average cost (weight 1/4) of the most expensive parameter set it has seen; the average of the configured parameters is learnt at construction. | Code review M1-39 (Major): after a change of `auth.argon2id_*` the rows keep their old parameters until their next login rehashes them (08 section 2.5), so without padding a wrong password on such a row took another time than an unknown name, which verifies with the configured parameters. |
