## M1-61 Read state, drafts and personal settings

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-61/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave21` at `8b981ed`, branch `feat/M1-61-sync-personal-data` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00035`. The brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the base holds every rule the ticket needs (03 `read_states`, `drafts`, `user_settings`, 04 section 14, 05 personal actions and events, R-AZ-21, 01 section 11, 13 section 3.3, the OpenAPI operations and schemas are the same apart from translation), so no contract commit was brought in. The test of the setting registry finds the chapters by their number (`docs/spec/01-*.md`, `13-*.md`) and their section numbers, so it reads the German chapters of the base and the English ones of `main`. `make check` runs with `SONAR_SKIP=1` as the launcher sets it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step; chapter names differ between the base and `main`. |
| M1-61/1 | 2026-10-15 | A setting that was deleted starts again at `version = 1` on its next PUT; the `settings.updated` of the delete carries the last value with the last version plus one and `deleted: true`. | Brief note 16: clients drop the local entry on `deleted: true`, so the new entry with version 1 applies; keeping a tombstone row only for the version would store a default the server must not store (04 section 14, row Settings). |
| M1-61/2 | 2026-10-15 | `draft.deleted` carries no `resource.version`, although the brief's event table names `version: last + 1`. | The catalogue (`docs/spec/actions.yaml`, ADR 0049) records `resource_version: false` for `draft.deleted`, and the frame conformance (G9) checks every delivery against the catalogue; the core contract is not edited by this ticket. Clients apply it in `seq` order (04 section 9 point 3). The empty final state of a PUT still answers version last plus one in its body. |
| M1-61/3 | 2026-10-15 | The six personal actions of this ticket (`read_state.update`, `read_state.mark_unread`, `draft.read`, `draft.write`, `settings.read`, `settings.update`) are refused to agents and apps by `authorize` itself: the requirement table of authz has override rows `All(Human, Self)` and `All(Human, Self, Level(read))` with the unchanged 05 texts. A person with a personal token passes. An agent or app without read on the thread or channel still gets `404` (visibility comes first). | Brief note 22: permission logic only in authz; the requirement step of M1-28 let an agent naming itself pass `self`. The table already holds overrides by action id (message.create, thread.tags.set). |
| M1-61/4 | 2026-10-15 | For `clear_draft`, `OnMessagePosted` deletes the author's channel draft of `ChannelID` when `CreatedThread` is set and the author's draft of `ThreadID` otherwise; it does not read the channel kind. Only messages of people clear drafts. | Only a forum has channel drafts (`putChannelDraft` refuses other kinds and a channel never changes its kind), so a new thread of a text channel finds none, exactly as brief note 12 requires; agents and apps have no drafts. |
| M1-61/5 | 2026-10-15 | `ReadState.mention_count` counts above `last_read_seq` with `messaging.Mentions.CountUnread`, without `threads.unread_floor_seq`. | M1-60/2: imports write no mentions, so the floor hides none; the list, the answers and the events then count the same way. |
| M1-61/6 | 2026-10-15 | An over-long draft is `413 payload_too_large`, not `400 too_long`. U+0000 in a draft is `400` on `/content_md` with `invalid`; a channel draft in a channel that is not a forum is `400 validation_failed` without a field; field codes are `out_of_range`, `too_many_items`, `duplicate`, `not_uuid_v7`, `not_in_thread`, `not_allowed` (reply on a channel draft) and `invalid`; a setting value whose event would not stay below `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` is `400` on `/value` with `too_large`. | Brief note 8 asks for M1-56's answer to an over-long message (`domain.CheckContent`); PostgreSQL text cannot hold U+0000 (M1-56/5); a setting has no reduced payload, and the appender would refuse the event with `500`. |
| M1-61/7 | 2026-10-15 | The reply target of a thread draft is checked through the new port `messaging.ReplyTargets` (`InThread`, implemented by the messaging service with the rule of `createMessage`); the chat port gains `Threads.InfoForShare` and `ThreadInfo.LastMessageSeq` and `UnreadFloorSeq`; `Structure.ListSidebarStates` of M1-52 is used as it is. The kind and space of a channel draft come from `Structure.GetChannel` after `draft.write` allowed the call. | Brief note 8 asks for the messaging port, and the base port had no reply check; additive changes only. |
| M1-61/8 | 2026-10-15 | `internal/wire` builds the subscribers of sync (`syncapp.Subscribers`) with the module objects of the txn registry and binds the appender in the start step of authz (and in `bothy setup`) next to the rights hooks; an unbound subscriber fails a message with `clear_draft`. The golden file of the registrations gains `subscribe messaging.MessagePosted -> sync`. The test world of messaging takes the option `WithPersonal`, which registers the subscriber of sync before the test subscriber and adds the twelve handlers; its people authenticate through the test authenticator of httpservertest as for M1-56 and M1-60, not with DPoP proofs. | The registry is built before the appender exists (02 "In-Tx-Ereignisse": one list); the world of the base is the shared HTTP world of chat and messaging. |

## M5-01 Device key for Android

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M5-01-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave23` at `d2d32fa`, branch `feat/M5-01-device-key-android` in its own worktree; this build neither merges locally nor pushes, the PR lane lands it. Every rule the brief cites (R-CL-04, R-CL-05, R-ID-11, D-L10) is on the base, so nothing is ported from `main`. The base names the chapters `docs/spec/15-client-architektur.md` and `08-identitaet.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Names that differ from the brief: the Kotlin packages are `devicekey.flutter` (plugin, generated `DeviceKeyApi.g.kt`) and `devicekey.core` (core module), without the product name (02 6); the plugin has its own Gradle wrapper (Gradle 9.3.1 with a pinned distribution checksum) and AGP 9.1.0, Kotlin 2.4.0 and compile SDK 36 of the pinned Flutter's plugin template; the instrumented tests run with `connectedDebugAndroidTest` and the JVM tests with `testDebugUnitTest`. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's landing step; merged and tool layouts win over the brief's names (D-W-100, D-W-120). |
| D-M5-01-1 | 2026-10-15 | Key parameters (brief note 2): alias `<prefix>.<sha256(scope)>.<purpose>` (lowercase hex, prefix `device_key`, tests `test.device_key.<uuid>`), `KeyPairGenerator` `EC` in `AndroidKeyStore` with `secp256r1`, purpose `SIGN`, digest `SHA256`. `dpop` without user authentication. `step_up` with `setUserAuthenticationRequired(true)`, from API 30 `setUserAuthenticationParameters(0, AUTH_BIOMETRIC_STRONG or AUTH_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL)`, before it `setUserAuthenticationValidityDurationSeconds(-1)`, and `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true)`. From API 28 StrongBox is tried first and the key is generated again without it on `StrongBoxUnavailableException`; below API 28 an `SDK_INT` check skips it. A missing step-up key is created only when `BiometricManager.canAuthenticate` accepts the authenticators of the API level, otherwise `not_available`. | 08 6.1, 15 9; the alias keeps server addresses out of the Keystore, as on Apple (D-M4-10-3). |
| D-M5-01-2 | 2026-10-15 | The attestation is the key's certificate chain from `setAttestationChallenge`: the DER certificates, leaf first, concatenated (the channel member is bytes and the DER framing separates them). Android binds the challenge when it creates the key, so `attestation` creates a missing key with the challenge and returns its chain, and returns null for an existing key and where the device does not attest (a chain of one certificate); the app asks for the attestation before `ensure` when it registers. | M4-10 defined the member as bytes without an encoding; returning a chain with an old challenge would look like a fresh attestation. |
| D-M5-01-3 | 2026-10-15 | Device credential rule: from API 30 the step-up accepts strong biometrics or the device credential (the prompt then has no negative button); on API 26 to 29 strong biometrics only (the prompt shows the negative button from Dart), and a device without enrolled biometrics on those levels gets `not_available` for `step_up`. Written into 15 9 and the Android row of 08 6.1. | A per-operation key cannot be unlocked with the device credential before API 30 (brief note 3); the app explains that step-up needs biometrics there. |
| D-M5-01-4 | 2026-10-15 | Signatures: `SHA256withECDSA`, DER converted to `R || S` with strict DER parsing (minimal integers, at most 33 bytes, left padding to 32). `hardwareBound`: from API 31 `KeyInfo.securityLevel` StrongBox or trusted environment, before it `isInsideSecureHardware`. Errors leave with a fixed text and never with a cause: `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException` is `key_invalidated`; `UserNotAuthenticatedException` and, from API 33, `android.security.KeyStoreException` with `ERROR_USER_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` (what the Keystore throws at `sign` for a step-up signature without the prompt) are `not_available`; below API 33 that case is `internal`, because the class is hidden there. Prompt errors: cancel, negative button and system cancel are `user_cancelled`; no hardware, nothing enrolled, no device credential and lockout are `not_available`; others `internal`. A step-up signature without prompt texts is `internal`. | Brief note 4; the emulator at API 36 showed the `KeyStoreException` path, not `UserNotAuthenticatedException`. |
| D-M5-01-5 | 2026-10-15 | `apps_platform.py` knows the system `android`, which is no host: its commands run on any host when `adb devices` lists exactly one device in state `device`, print `SKIP ... android: no device` without one (or without `adb`) and fail with two or more; `--system android` runs only them. The CI job `apps-android` (public phase, `ubuntu-24.04`, kvm enabled, Temurin 17, `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner` pinned by SHA, API 26 and 36, `x86_64`, `google_apis`) runs `apps_platform.py --system android` and feeds `ci-ok` like `apps-macos`. `make test` runs `./gradlew -p <module> testDebugUnitTest` for every Gradle module with a tracked `src/test/` under `packages/*/android/`; the jobs `gates` and `sonar` install Temurin 17 and the Android SDK (`android-actions/setup-android`) when such a wrapper is tracked. The run script sets a temporary PIN on an emulator without one (and clears it at the end), because the step-up key needs a secure lock screen; it never touches a physical device's lock screen. | Brief note 5 and its requirement that the JVM tests run in `make test`; an emulator can run on Linux and macOS, so a host check alone cannot select it. |
| D-M5-01-6 | 2026-10-15 | The Kotlin core is the Gradle library module `android/devicekeycore` with its own build file and tests. The plugin library compiles the module's main sources into itself (`sourceSets`), and its standalone `settings.gradle.kts` includes the module for the tests. An app (the push service of M5-05, the share activity of M5-06) uses `DeviceKeyCore.forContext` through its dependency on the plugin project `:device_key` and does not include `devicekeycore` as a second project, which would duplicate the classes. Both build files list the same dependencies (`androidx.biometric` 1.1.0); the plugin adds `kotlinx-coroutines-android` for Pigeon's `suspend` handler. | Flutter includes a plugin as one Gradle project and ignores its settings file, so a project dependency on the module would break every app build that does not include it by hand. |
| D-M5-01-7 | 2026-10-15 | M4-10 had no prompt texts on the channel, so the Pigeon source gains `PromptTextsMessage` (`title`, optional `subtitle`, `negativeButton`) as an optional last parameter of `sign`; the Swift host ignores it. The Dart API stays source compatible: `DeviceKeyClient.sign` takes an optional named `prompt` of the new type `StepUpPrompt`. Pigeon writes `analyzer: exclude: [android/**]` into the member's `analysis_options.yaml` when it generates Kotlin; the folder holds no Dart, so it stays. | Brief note 1; the texts come from `packages/l10n` (rule 8). |

## M4-16 Sync engine: run documents

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-16-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave24` at `2a72cca`, branch `feat/M4-16-sync-run-documents` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (R-SY-06 is in the German chapter 04 of the base), so nothing is ported. The base has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section of the ticket. Text this ticket adds is English. The brief's `15-client-architecture.md`, `04-sync-protocol.md` and `09-agents.md` are `15-client-architektur.md`, `04-sync-protokoll.md` and `09-agenten.md` on the base. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-16-1 | 2026-10-15 | The store depends on no transport and no `api_client` type: whole documents come through the port `RunDocumentFetcher.fetchRun(RunId)` and frames through `FrameTicker.requestFrame(callback)`; `run.updated` payloads come in as decoded maps. The ticker is a request per dirty period instead of a stream of ticks, so an idle store asks for no frame. | Goal and Design note 1 of the brief; M4-17 wires the ports to the dispatcher of M4-14, `getRun` and the scheduler of the app. |
| D-M4-16-2 | 2026-10-15 | An invalid patch (an `op` other than `add`, `replace`, `remove`, a missing target or parent, a missing `value`, a malformed pointer, the root pointer, a path through a non-container, `ops` that is not a list of objects, a frame with `ops` but without `base_version`) is a gap: the store refetches the whole document and never applies a part. `applyRunPatch` copies the root and the touched branches only and leaves its input unchanged. | Design note 2 of the brief; a partial state is a state the server never had (04 11.3 item 4). |
| D-M4-16-3 | 2026-10-15 | Coalescing per frame: every change marks its run dirty and requests one frame; at the frame each listener of a dirty run gets the newest document (null when the run is not focused, ended or has no document) unless it has seen exactly that state. A new listener gets the current state at the next frame, not synchronously. | Design note 1 and 15 7.4: the interface draws at most once per frame. |
| D-M4-16-4 | 2026-10-15 | During a refetch of a run its frames with ops wait in a queue; when the fetch returns, the fetched document replaces the local one only when its version is newer, and the queued frames run through the rule again in arrival order (older ones drop, one that still does not fit refetches again). A `snapshot` applies at once also during a fetch. A failed fetch keeps the last good document and drops the queue, so an always failing fetch cannot loop; the next frame or `focus` retries. Each fetch takes a new generation number; a result counts only while its run still waits for that number, so `unfocus`, `onRunEnded` or a newer focus make it stale. The frame's `version` is written into the document after a patch or a snapshot. | Design note 1 of the brief. |
| D-M4-16-5 | 2026-10-15 | `RunDocument` reads `stall_warned_at` from the raw map and gives null without it; the API schema `Run` of this base does not name the field (requirement 10 of the brief; the report recommends the optional field). A step without `ordinal` sorts after those with one in document order, a step whose parent is missing is a top-level step, and steps on a parent cycle appear at the top level, so every step appears exactly once. | Requirement 10 and Design note 3 of the brief; the server assigns `ordinal`, a step is never lost from the view. |

## M4-11 Device key for Windows

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-11-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave23` at `d2d32fa`, branch `feat/M4-11-device-key-windows` in its own worktree; this build neither merges locally nor pushes, the PR lane lands it. Every rule the brief cites (R-CL-04, R-CL-05, R-CL-12, R-ID-11, D-L10) is on the base, so nothing is ported from `main`. The base names the chapters `docs/spec/15-client-architektur.md` and `08-identitaet.md`; text added there is English (as D-M4-10-0). Names that differ from the brief: the core has, besides `key_provider.h`, `cng_key_provider.cpp`, `device_key_store.cpp` and `.h`, the headers `cng_key_provider.h` and `marker_store.h` with `credential_marker_store.cpp` (the marker of note 4 behind its own interface), and the tests have `fake_key_provider.h`, `test_support.h`, `main.cpp` and `device_key_store_test.cpp`; no `der.cpp`, because `NCryptSignHash` returns `R || S` for ECDSA. `codegen-check` already hashes every file under the member, so the Makefile only gains a comment that the C++ outputs are covered. `packages/device_key/analysis_options.yaml` gains `analyzer: exclude: [windows/**]`, which the pinned Flutter's `flutter pub get` writes itself once a plugin has a `windows/` folder ("Upgrading analysis_options.yaml to exclude build and platform directories"); the folder holds no Dart code. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's landing step; merged layouts win over the brief's names (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-11-1 | 2026-10-15 | Provider choice per key at creation, never migrated: a new `dpop` key goes to `MS_PLATFORM_CRYPTO_PROVIDER` when it opens and `NCryptIsAlgSupported(BCRYPT_ECDSA_P256_ALGORITHM)` succeeds, otherwise to `MS_KEY_STORAGE_PROVIDER` with `hardwareBound = false`. A marker (generic credential in the user's Credential Manager, `CRED_PERSIST_LOCAL_MACHINE`, target name = key name, blob `platform` or `software`) records the provider; a key is opened only there. Without a marker (a stop between `NCryptFinalizeKey` and writing it) an existing key is looked up in the platform provider, then the software provider, used where it is and the marker written. A marker without an openable key (`NTE_BAD_KEYSET`, `NTE_NO_KEY`, `NTE_NOT_FOUND`) is `key_invalidated` and nothing is recreated; a marker whose provider no longer opens is `not_available`. Keys are per user (no `NCRYPT_MACHINE_KEY_FLAG`), ECDSA P-256, export policy `0` set before `NCryptFinalizeKey`. | A key moved between providers would change the JWK and need a new device (08 5.4); the marker is how the plugin tells "not created yet" from "lost" (brief note 4). Which status a real TPM clear produces is not measured. |
| D-M4-11-2 | 2026-10-15 | No step-up key on Windows: `Ensure(step_up)` fails with `not_available`, and the plugin never offers a key whose confirmation only the plugin enforces. The DPoP key signs with `NCRYPT_SILENT_FLAG`. Sources, retrieved 2026-10-15: strong key protection (`NCRYPT_UI_FORCE_HIGH_PROTECTION_FLAG`) prompts for a password whose "exact behavior is specified by the KSP" (learn.microsoft.com, `X509PrivateKeyProtection`); the Microsoft KSPs accept that password from the process through `NCRYPT_PIN_PROPERTY` and "cache this value so that the user is only prompted once per process" (Key Storage Property Identifiers); a Microsoft answer on per-use reauthentication for Platform Crypto Provider keys names only a PIN or a custom KSP (Microsoft Q&A 1076208); Windows Hello keys (`KeyCredentialManager`) are RSA and sign with SHA-256 and PKCS #1 (Windows Hello for apps). Not checked on a Windows host: no Windows machine was reachable. Recommendation (08 6.1): a Windows Hello `RS256` step-up key needs `RS256` or `PS256` in 03 `device_keys.alg` and in the JOSE profile of ADR 0067, a core contract change; until then the step-up of a Windows client uses the other methods of 08 6. | 08 6.1: the confirmation must come from the person, not from code in the process; a password the process can set or that is cached per process does not meet that, and `ES256` is the only algorithm of the contract (R-CL-04). |
| D-M4-11-3 | 2026-10-15 | The CTest suite uses a minimal harness `windows/core/tests/check.h` (`TEST`, `CHECK`, `REQUIRE`, `SKIP`) without a third-party framework and without exceptions: one executable runs the test named by its argument, CTest registers one test per name, exit code 77 is a skip (`SKIP_RETURN_CODE`). The core library is built with `_HAS_EXCEPTIONS=0` like Flutter's `apply_standard_settings` for the plugin, and every operation returns a `Status` instead of throwing. | No new dependency for a small suite; the same exception setting on both sides of the plugin boundary. |
| D-M4-11-4 | 2026-10-15 | `tpm_path_when_available` skips with `platform crypto provider not available on this host` when `MS_PLATFORM_CRYPTO_PROVIDER` does not open or lacks P-256, and the platform half of `keys_are_not_exportable` prints the same reason; the fallback runs always, through `FakeKeyProvider` (TPM missing, or a TPM without P-256 simulated on a software provider handle) and against the real software provider. Every test key and marker starts with `device-key-test-`; `main.cpp` deletes leftovers in both providers and the Credential Manager before and after each test. | Brief note 5; CI runners have no TPM, so the TPM path counts as proven only on a host with one. |

## M4-12 Local database schema v1

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-12-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave24` at `2a72cca`, branch `feat/M4-12-local-store` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. Brief and spec were read from `main`; the brief relies on no core rule or D-W ruling that the base lacks, so nothing is ported first. The base names the chapters in German (`15-client-architektur.md`, `14-suche.md`, `03-datenmodell.md`, `04-sync-protokoll.md`); the text this ticket adds is English. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114): the removed-target kind is `RemovedTargetKind` with `TargetRef` (`AccessTargetKind` is a type of `api_client`, which `local_store` may not import, 15 3.1); `SyncStateDao` also has `markSnapshot` and `resetCursor`; the file entity is `FileMeta` (D-M4-08-7). | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win over the brief. |
| D-M4-12-1 | 2026-10-15 | Cipher `chacha20` (ChaCha20-Poly1305, the SQLite3MultipleCiphers default); the key is set as the first statement in the raw form `PRAGMA key = "x'<64 hex>'"`. The SQLite3MultipleCiphers documentation ("Raw key data (without key derivation)", configuration of SQL pragmas) states that a 64-digit hex key in this form is used directly as 32 bytes for the sqleet ChaCha20 scheme, without a key derivation; `PRAGMA hexkey` would derive. The opener then checks `SELECT count(*) FROM sqlite_master` (wrong key: `LocalStoreKeyError` without the key), `PRAGMA cipher` and FTS5; the hex text lives only in the setup call. | Design note 2; a random 256-bit key needs no derivation, and the check fails closed on a library without encryption. |
| D-M4-12-2 | 2026-10-15 | `hooks: user_defines: sqlite3: source: sqlite3mc` stands in the root `pubspec.yaml`. Verified with the pinned SDK (Flutter 3.47.7): with the define at the root, `PRAGMA cipher` reports `chacha20` and `sqlite3mc_version()` reports SQLite3 Multiple Ciphers 2.6.1 (`test/encryption_test.dart`). | Build hooks read user defines only from the workspace root. |
| D-M4-12-3 | 2026-10-15 | The version rule is one SQL statement per mirror upsert: `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... WHERE excluded.version > <table>.version`, returning whether a row changed; rows without `resource.version` are written with `put` after `decideApply`. Server times are stored as text with milliseconds and UTC (`store_date_time_values_as_text`), local times (`last_accessed_at`, outbox times) as epoch milliseconds. | Design note 3; one statement cannot race with a second writer, and the text form round-trips the entities unchanged. |
| D-M4-12-4 | 2026-10-15 | Removal deletes everything below the targets in one transaction and keeps outbox entries (they fail with `404` and keep their text, 04 8.3 item 4); file metadata goes only when no remaining message attaches the file. The threads of a removed channel come from every table that carries the channel (`threads`, `messages`, `message_attachments`, `runs`, `read_states`), and the files scoped to it (`files.scope_channel_id`) go with their cache rows when no message attaches them, because a thread row or an attachment link may be gone while content stays (code review and red team of M4-12). The cleanup deletes each thread in its own transaction. | 04 8.3; a long cleanup must not block the sync. |
| D-M4-12-5 | 2026-10-15 | `search_index` is FTS5 with the columns `kind`, `target_id`, `thread_id`, `channel_id`, `space_id` (all `UNINDEXED`) and `body`, tokenizer `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`, no stemmer; queries take each word as a quoted prefix term (FTS5 operators are plain text), ordered by `bm25`, with a `snippet` marked by the control characters U+0002 and U+0003. An absent space is stored as an empty text. | Design note 5; 14 6 accepts other word stems than the server, and quoting keeps user input from breaking the query syntax. |
| D-M4-12-6 | 2026-10-15 | `MigrationRules` is the helper of every later step (`discardCache` drops and recreates mirror tables and clears the cursor and the epoch; `keepRows` migrates preserved tables with `alterTable`); version 1 has no step, so `onUpgrade` refuses any other version until version 2 introduces `stepByStep`. `test/drift/survival_test.dart` checks the stored schema of the current version, migrates every pair of stored versions (`from <= to`) with fixture rows in `outbox`, `drafts` and `share_inbox`, fails when a stored version has no fixture rows, and runs the encrypted round trip of version 1. | Design note 4 and 15 6.2; with one version drift generates no migration test, so the survival test is the test M4-36 extends. |
| D-M4-12-7 | 2026-10-15 | `make codegen-check` runs `dart run build_runner build` and `dart run drift_dev make-migrations` in every Dart member whose `build.yaml` configures `drift_dev`, with `tools/ci/codegen_check.py` (the snapshot comparison of the Pigeon step) instead of a temporary copy; `.pre-commit-config.yaml` excludes `drift_schemas/*.json` from the end-of-file fixer. | drift writes the schema file without a final newline and `make-migrations` refuses a stored schema that differs by one byte; the snapshot comparison ignores the ignored build outputs and needs no copy of the workspace. |
| D-M4-12-8 | 2026-10-15 | `host_key_pins` is not in schema version 1; M4-36 adds it with its own schema version, adds it to `MigrationRules.preservedTables` and extends the survival test. | Fixed requirement 10 (specification first). |
| D-M4-12-9 | 2026-10-15 | License: SQLite3MultipleCiphers 2.6.1 is MIT (its repository's `LICENSE`), SQLite is public domain; the `sqlite3` hook bundles a prebuilt library checked by sha256, not sources, so the check is the upstream license file and the version the library reports; `make licenses` covers the Dart package `sqlite3` (MIT). | Design note 8; bundled native code is outside the lock files the gate reads. |

## M4-09 Login: DPoP, refresh and step-up

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-09-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave24` at `2a72cca`, branch `feat/M4-09-auth-dpop` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. No contract change of `main` is ported: the `openapi.yaml` of this base has every operation and schema the flows use, and the rulings the brief cites (D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-115) and the rules R-CL-04, R-CL-05, R-CL-07, R-SY-01 and R-SY-14 are present. The brief names the English chapter files of `main`; on this base they are `08-identitaet.md`, `15-client-architektur.md` and `04-sync-protokoll.md`, and text this ticket adds there is English. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-09-1 | 2026-10-15 | JOSE is built by hand on `crypto` and base64url (`lib/src/jose.dart`): compact JWS, JSON without whitespace, DPoP header members in the order `typ`, `alg`, `jwk`, ES256 signatures as `R \|\| S` with 32 bytes each from the signer port (RFC 7518 3.4), the RFC 7638 thumbprint over `crv`, `kty`, `x`, `y`. `htu` drops query and fragment and the default port and lowercases scheme and host; the gateway form maps `wss` to `https` and `ws` to `http`. | Brief Design note 2: a JOSE library would add algorithms and header forms the server's profile of ADR 0067 rejects. |
| D-M4-09-2 | 2026-10-15 | `NonceStore` keeps the newest nonce of the server, taken from every `DPoP-Nonce` header (success and error) and from `hello.dpop_nonce`. `DpopInterceptor` repeats a request once on `401 use_dpop_nonce` with a fresh proof; a second `use_dpop_nonce` goes to the caller. | Brief Design note 3, RFC 9449 8. |
| D-M4-09-3 | 2026-10-15 | Persist before publish: a rotated refresh token is written to the `SecretStore` and the write awaited before the new access token is published; refreshes of one family run as a single flight (`RefreshCoordinator`), and a request that failed with an older token repeats with the current one instead of refreshing again. `401 session_revoked` signs out with `sessionRevoked`, unless the request or the refresh was sent in an earlier session (before a logout and a new login), whose late answer must not end its successor, clear the store or emit an event, and is never resent with the successor's token; a refresh rejected with `unauthenticated`, `token_expired` or `invalid_dpop_proof` signs out with `refreshRejected`; a network or other error keeps the state and emits `RefreshFailed`. | Brief Design note 4: a crash between rotation and storage falls into the server's `auth.refresh_reuse_grace` (08 7.4) instead of a lost token; two refreshes of one family would look like theft. |
| D-M4-09-4 | 2026-10-15 | `LoginService` returns `LoginOutcome` (`LoginSignedIn`, `LoginPending`, `LoginNeedsSecondFactor`, `LoginFailed` with the problem code) and never throws for a server answer; `beginPairing` returns a `PairingTicket`. `loginWithPassword` sends password and TOTP in one request because `PasswordLoginRequest` carries `totp_code`; without a code it returns `LoginNeedsSecondFactor` and sends nothing. The API names the device keys `public_jwk` and `step_up_key.public_jwk` (the brief's `dpop_jwk` and `step_up_jwk`). Polling of `completePairing` and `getDevice` waits `client.outbox_retry_base` doubled per attempt up to `client.outbox_retry_cap` from `AppConfig`; a network error, `429`, a `5xx` and a refresh that fails for such a reason keep polling, a `202` of `completePairing` means not yet approved. | Brief Design note 5 and fixed requirement 9 (no new key). |
| D-M4-09-5 | 2026-10-15 | `StepUpInterceptor` runs the step-up once on `401 step_up_required` (any `reason`) and repeats the original request with the same `Idempotency-Key` and body, a new proof and the current token; a second `step_up_required` after a confirmation goes to the caller, a cancelled prompt or OS confirmation ends with `StepUpCancelled`. The step-up JWS has only `{"alg":"ES256"}` in its header and `challenge`, `sid` and `iat` in its payload. The prompt text comes from `RequestOptions.extra[stepUpPurposeExtra]`. Every auth repeat (nonce, refresh, step-up) sends a `FormData` body as a fresh clone, because Dio finalizes a `FormData` once; a stream body cannot be read twice, so its first error goes back to the caller. | Brief Design note 6, 08 6.2, 6.3 and 14.3: the same key lets the server deduplicate, and no loop can ask the person twice. |
| D-M4-09-6 | 2026-10-15 | `pointycastle` 4.0.0 (dev dependency of `packages/auth`, only the software P-256 signer of `test/support/`) gets a license override `MIT` in `osv-scanner.toml`, like the other pub packages deps.dev has no license for; its LICENSE file is the MIT text of the Legion of the Bouncy Castle. `quality/licenses/allowed.txt` stays unchanged. | `make licenses` reported it as UNKNOWN; the brief names the dependency, and the override records the checked license instead of widening the allow list. |

## M6-11 bothy_viz: primitives, models, fixtures and harness

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-11-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave24` at `2a72cca`, branch `feat/M6-11-viz-package` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. The base already holds every schema, operation and ruling the brief uses, so no contract change enters first. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the spec chapters are `25-visualisierung.md`, `15-client-architektur.md` and `03-datenmodell.md` on this base; the brief's `harness_test.dart` lives in `packages/bothy_viz/harness/test/`, because `bothy_viz` cannot import the harness that depends on it; `StatsQuerySource` throws the `ApiProblem` of M4-02 instead of returning it, as every call of the generated client does. Text this ticket adds is English, also inside the German chapters. | Instructions of the launcher; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100). |
| D-M6-11-1 | 2026-10-15 | View models are immutable classes built only from the generated types and app configuration values; they never call the network. Lanes group runs by space and channel in frame order; `runs_truncated` becomes the "More runs than shown" marker; a run card shows agent, state, step chip, badge, "Approval waiting" and the cost only in mode `organization` with `cost_micros` and `currency` present (`cost_is_estimate` gives "Estimate"); mode `personal` shows costs only in cost tiles with "information only"; a missing member, a missing cost response and a missing SLI are `null` ("not shown"), never 0. Executive tiles sum only the additive counters of the query responses (`messages`, `threads_created`, `runs`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `invocation_denials`); a level (percentile, rate, distinct count such as `active_people`) shows the latest bucket of a single group and no tile with several groups; a metric name of an earlier query keeps its first tile. A series of a grouped query carries the display name of its group (`VizNames.any`), and the area mark names it in the legend and the semantics label, so two groups of one metric never differ by color alone. The traffic light only maps `StatsSloState` to role, icon and text; an unknown state reads "not enough data". | Brief, design note 2; 25 4 rule 5 and 6.1. |
| D-M6-11-2 | 2026-10-15 | The stale rule is part of the port: `LiveFrameSourceBase` waits `viz.stale_after` on an injected `VizClock` after `start` and after every frame and emits one `stale` until the next frame; `start(spaceId)` closes the previous stream; error events carry the wire code and its kind (`forbidden`, `not_found`, `feature_disabled`, `rate_limited`, other). The apps of M6-16 extend the base, so every implementation behaves the same. | Brief, design note 3. |
| D-M6-11-3 | 2026-10-15 | Every mark is one semantics container with a label built from its model and excludes its parts, so a screen reader reads it once; the run card is a button that reports its thread ID and never navigates. The traffic light announces politely (`SemanticsService.sendAnnouncement`) only when the state of the same SLO changes between two builds, never on an unchanged frame; it uses no `liveRegion`, which would announce every label change. Every run bar of the bar mark starts with the icon of its run state, so the state is never color alone. | Brief, design note 4; 25 7.4. |
| D-M6-11-4 | 2026-10-15 | Fixtures are `<schema>.<state>.json`, or `<schema>.<query>.<state>.json` when one schema serves several queries (`StatsSeries.usage.normal.json`); the table "Fixtures" of `fixtures/README.md` lists each with its schema and readers. The check `viz-fixtures` of `make api-checks` validates them with the `jsonschema` package already pinned through `check-jsonschema` (no new dependency), and fails on an unknown schema, an invalid file, an unlisted file and a listed schema without a file. The fixtures are also assets of the package, so the harness reads the same files on desktop and web. | Brief, design note 5; 25 7.2. |
| D-M6-11-5 | 2026-10-15 | `tools/observability/sli_bounds.py` writes `slibounds.gen.go` (sorted map metric to SLO, objective, threshold, period) and `slo_catalog.g.dart` (sorted list without thresholds), both `DO NOT EDIT`; modes `--write` and `--check`. Label rules (exactly one `bothy_sli_metric` per SLO, a value of the `sli.*` list of `stats_minutes.metric`, one SLO per metric) are enforced by the generator and by `sli-labels`; `sli-bounds` compares the files with a fresh run. Labels per SLO: `bothy-api/requests-availability` `sli.api_availability`, `bothy-sync/delivery-latency` `sli.delivery_latency`, `bothy-sync/reconnect` `sli.reconnect`, `bothy-push/provider-acceptance` `sli.push_acceptance`, `bothy-agents/run-start` `sli.run_start`, `bothy-agents/turn-duration` `sli.turn_duration`. | Brief, design note 6; R-VZ-03. |
| D-M6-11-6 | 2026-10-15 | The harness is the workspace member `bothy_viz_harness` under `packages/bothy_viz/harness` with the members of `bothy_viz` plus `bothy_viz` itself (`rules.yaml`, kind `app`, rule 6). Its platform folders are not checked in, like `apps/client` and `apps/console`: `flutter create --platforms=macos,windows,web .` makes them locally (README). Its time-lapse moves the fake clock by one frame interval per step; the tests pass reduced motion so the spinning run icon lets the tree settle. The repository tooling learns the nested member in the same change: the node `bothy_viz_harness` with its edges in the diagram of 15 3.1, the lcov path in `sonar-project.properties`, and the member discovery of the arch-check and CI toolchain tests (one level plus `packages/*/harness`, counts derived from the workspace list). | Brief, design note 7; no generated native code in a package without native rule. |
| D-M6-11-7 | 2026-10-15 | Golden matrix: every mark in light and dark, the three densities, text scale 1 and 2, de and en (24 cells per file), CI goldens on every host and platform goldens on macOS, both for the full matrix. Every file stays under `repo.max_file_kb`; the area mark shows the daily usage series, because the minute series of the timeline made the anti-aliased file exceed the limit. | Brief, design note 8. |

## M4-07 Core components with goldens

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-07-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave24` at `2a72cca`, branch `feat/M4-07-core-components` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also inside the German chapter `docs/design/components.md`. Names that differ on this base and are followed: the chapters are `01-produkt.md` and `15-client-architektur.md`; the trigger state widget is `DsTriggerStateIcon`, because `DsTriggerState` is the enum of the view models. No core rule or ruling from main was needed (R-PR-04, D-W-100 and D-W-114 are on the base). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-07-1 | 2026-10-15 | Components take small immutable view models defined in `design_system` (`view_models.dart`: `DsMessageData`, `DsRunData`, `DsStepData`, `DsThreadRowData`, `DsChannelRowData`, `DsSpaceRowData`, `DsComponentData`, `DsComponentHeader`, `DsSuggestion`, `DsComposerCommand` and the enums `DsMessageOrigin`, `DsMessageKind`, `DsTriggerState`, `DsRunState`, `DsStepKind`, `DsStepStatus`, `DsBillingMode`, `DsComponentType`, `DsComponentState`, `DsButtonStyle`, `DsApprover`, `DsChannelKind`, `DsFieldType`, `DsCategory`). Enum values carry the codes of 03 and an `unknown` member (`DsCategory` falls back to `granite`); `fromCode` never throws. Callbacks are plain functions; no component performs I/O or imports a provider. | Brief note 1: testable with literal data, usable by the console and the golden harness; a newer server may send a new code (06 13). |
| D-M4-07-2 | 2026-10-15 | `DsMarkdown` parses with package `markdown` (GitHub flavored without the inline HTML syntaxes) and builds its own widgets for the subset of the brief. HTML renders as literal text; an image renders as the text "Image: <alt> (<domain>)" and never loads; links are active only for `https`, `http`, `mailto` and the internal paths `/t/` and `/c/`, show their domain (or the internal target) on hover and focus and reach `onOpenLink` only on activation; every other scheme renders as plain text; headings are at most `type.heading`; code blocks scroll horizontally and copy exactly the code. `markdown` 7.3.1 gets a checked `PackageOverrides` entry (BSD-3-Clause, from its LICENSE file) in `osv-scanner.toml`, because deps.dev knows no license for it, as D-M4-04-5 does for alchemist. Block quotes and lists nest as structure up to `DsMarkdown.maxNesting` (8) levels; a deeper element renders as its plain text, because the server accepts any nesting and thousands of levels froze the interface (red team of M4-07). | Brief note 2: a widget package would decide link and image handling and could load resources (SEC-OUT-1, SEC-OUT-2). |
| D-M4-07-3 | 2026-10-15 | `DsMessageComponents` draws a framed header with `security.step-up` built only from `DsComponentHeader` (agent, run, required capability, approval note), then a divider labeled "Text from <agent>", then the agent text as Markdown, then the components. `DsMessage` therefore does not draw the content of a `kind = card` message that has components. After the first activation the widget ignores every further one until the caller reports a new state of the card (another `state`, `pendingCustomId`, `errorText` or message); an equal view model rebuilt by the parent keeps the lock, and `pendingCustomId` from the caller locks the same way. For screen readers the group is labeled by the server header and the header is one node with the plain names; the agent's card title is read after the divider, and the message label of a card names only author and time. `onActivate` gets null for a button, the list of chosen values for a select and the field map for a form. Only Enter or Space on the focused button activates (the default `ActivateIntent`); no other key or shortcut does. | Brief note 5, SEC-OUT-4 and SEC-T14 at component level; 01 6.5 (a double click creates nothing twice); R-PR-04. |
| D-M4-07-4 | 2026-10-15 | `LiveAnnouncer` ignores the first observation, announces a change of the state text or a new question ID, never a patch of steps or numbers, and merges announcements closer than `minInterval` (`a11y.live_announce_min_interval`) into the latest candidate, which `flush` sends once the interval has passed; a state candidate equal to the last sent text is dropped, a new question never is (all questions of an agent read the same). The live box arms the flush timer again when it fires early. The live box sends through `SemanticsService.announce` with polite assertiveness, in all three forms: the collapsed and the mobile line also take `announceMinInterval`, so the end of a run and questions on a phone are announced. | Brief note 4, 01 8 and components.md 2.4. |
| D-M4-07-5 | 2026-10-15 | The golden matrix is one sheet per component (live box and components on messages split into an active and a final sheet), and one file per sheet, density and theme, each holding text scales 1 and 2 and the locales `de` and `en`: 9 sheets, 54 files for CI and 54 for macOS, the largest file below 700 KB, together about 22 MB (CI about 2.5 MB). `dsGoldenMatrix` passes the `l10n` delegates with the locale, because the alchemist app has none. `guidelines_test.dart` runs the four Flutter guidelines over the same sheets in light and dark with the `touch` density. | Brief note 8 allows a file per component, theme, density, text scale and locale; one file per component and density reached 2.5 MB and exceeds `repo.max_file_kb`, and no dimension may be dropped. |
| D-M4-07-7 | 2026-10-15 | The message label stays "<Name>, <Time>, <Text>" with the text joined by spaces between blocks, list items and table cells, and the content keeps its own semantics below it, so links (own nodes with a tap action) and the copy button stay reachable; a screen reader may read the text twice. A continuation message shows the trigger state at the end of its row. Trigger and run reasons cover the acceptance reasons of 09 3.5 and `user_cancelled` without a name. | Code review of M4-07: links and the copy button were unreachable; 01 6.3 wants the state at every triggering message. |
| D-M4-07-8 | 2026-10-15 | The composer leaves Enter, Tab, the arrows and Escape to the input method while a composition is active. The generated token files stay byte for byte as `tokens.py build` writes them; `dart format` runs only on hand-written folders. | Red team of M4-07: Enter during an IME composition sent half-typed text; a formatted copy made `make tokens` red. |
| D-M4-07-6 | 2026-10-15 | Flutter 3.47.7 has no tree role in `SemanticsRole`, so space rows are expandable nodes with the expanded or collapsed state and channel rows carry their label; the arrow is `action.expand` (caret down) when expanded and `action.collapse` (caret right) when collapsed, as `icon.tokens.json` maps them. Two opacity values without a token are named constants with a reason: the icon tile of a space (0.16) and the lowest point of the run dot pulse (0.4). | components.md 7 asks for a tree; the semantics that Flutter offers are the expanded state. The token set has no tint or pulse opacity role. |

## M6-12 bothy_viz: six views

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-12-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave27` at `3d4f0f3`, branch `feat/M6-12-viz-views` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. The schemas, operations and rulings the brief uses (T7, T8, D-W-100, D-W-120) are on the base; the statistics schemas differ from `origin/main` only in the chapter names of their descriptions, so no contract change enters first. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the spec chapter is `25-visualisierung.md` on this base; the golden test lives in `test/goldens/views_golden_test.dart` next to the mark goldens (the golden path resolver of M6-11); the switch "As table" is a toggle button with toggled semantics, because the Material switch animates in the reduced motion context; the performance test runs through `harness/tool/perf_macos.sh`, listed in the harness `platform-tests.json`, because the platform folders of the harness are not checked in (D-M6-11-6). New fixture states: `StatsBackendReport.rate_limits.json` (every `rate_limit` state, I2 and I3 denials, a mismatch) and `StatsTimeline.day_boundary.json`; `StatsTimeline.normal.json` gains buckets of the delivery metrics for the history of 6.3. | Instructions of the launcher; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100). |
| D-M6-12-1 | 2026-10-16 | Every view is `XxxView({live, queries, options, onOpenThread})`. `VizHostOptions` carries what only the host knows: form factor, scope (`VizScope.instance()` or `VizScope.space(id)`), the period of the queries, `allowTvMode`, `showCostsInTv` (default `viz.tv_show_costs`), `onOpenGrafana` (only with `telemetry.dashboard_url`), display names, the time zone of the person, the clock and the app configuration. `vizViewRegistry` lists per `VizViewKind` the title key, the icon, whether the view is instance only and whether it appears on mobile; `vizViewsFor` never offers TV mode on mobile or without `allowTvMode`. An error of a source (`forbidden`, `not_found`, `feature_disabled`, `rate_limited`) shows its `problemText` and stops the live source and every further query until the host builds the view again. Without a live source a live view reads `getStatsLive` once. | Brief, design note 1; 15 principle 1 (no permission logic in the client). |
| D-M6-12-2 | 2026-10-16 | In a space scope delivery and sync live shows "Only for the owner" and calls neither source; the other views pass the space to their sources and show what the server delivers. | Brief, design note 1; 25 4 rule 1. |
| D-M6-12-3 | 2026-10-16 | Data per view as 25 6: the live topology and delivery read the frames (delivery adds the history of its six metrics from `getStatsTimeline`), the backend view reads `getStatsBackends` once and takes presence, reported mode and rate limit from the `agents` of every frame, the executive page reads the five queries once, the timeline reads `getStatsTimeline` once. `forbidden` from `getStatsSlo` shows "Only for the owner" in the traffic light tile; `forbidden` from `getCostStats` leaves the cost tiles out (no `cost.read.aggregate`), never zero. The `offline` state shows the last frame with the stale marker. Delivery shows the count of the SLI window with its good share and the deliveries per minute as history, because the frame does not carry the length of `stats.live_sli_window` (build note in 25 6.3); catch-ups show their count and summed duration of the last minute. | Brief, design note 2; 25 4 rules 2 to 5. |
| D-M6-12-4 | 2026-10-16 | Each view has a toggle "As table" that swaps the chart for `VizTable`s with the same texts and column headers from `l10n`; a header tap sorts by its column (numbers by value), a second tap reverses. The render table gives the table, row and cell roles; header cells add the column header role and a "Sort by" label, every cell the label "column: value". A column that would only show "none" (cost without any cost) is left out. | Brief, design note 3; 25 7.4. |
| D-M6-12-5 | 2026-10-16 | TV mode is the route-less overlay `TvMode(views, options, live, queries, onExit)`: dark theme, density `comfortable`, the text scale times the step from `type.body-sm` to `type.body`, no controls (the views read `VizTvScope`), rotation every `viz.tv_rotation_interval` on the clock of the options with an `AnimatedSwitcher` of `motion.duration-move-slow` (zero in the reduced context), costs only with `showCostsInTv`, TV mode takes the keyboard focus after its first frame (autofocus would not take it from a focused host control behind an overlay), and the first key press or pointer down calls `onExit` once; the timeline keeps its playhead at the end there, so a screen shows the whole period. A view that leaves the rotation (or a tab) does not stop the live source when a newer view already started it: the closed stream tells the old view that the source is no longer its own. Constructing it without `allowTvMode` or on mobile fails an assertion; `buildVizView` refuses `tvMode`. | Brief, design note 4; 25 6.6; components.md 11. |
| D-M6-12-6 | 2026-10-16 | The performance test is `harness/integration_test/topology_perf_test.dart`: the `load` frame and a variant with every third run in another state, alternated every `stats.live_interval` of the fixture clock for 200 frames; it fails when the p95 of `FrameTiming.buildDuration` is above `viz.perf.frame_build_p95`. It runs with `flutter drive --profile -d macos` through `tool/perf_macos.sh` (which makes `macos/` for the run and removes everything the run added, because a native folder under `packages/` fails rule 4 of `make arch`) from `make apps-platform` (the discovery of `platform-tests.json` now includes `packages/*/harness`) and from `make perf`, whose new Dart step runs every member's `tool/perf_macos.sh` on macOS (`PERF_HOST`) and prints `SKIP perf-dart` elsewhere. The live topology keeps the widget of every unchanged card and lane between frames and paints each lane in its own layer, so a frame rebuilds only changed cards. | Brief, design note 6; 25 7.3; PLAN P-07. |
| D-M6-12-7 | 2026-10-16 | Goldens: every view with every fixture state that applies (the `load` frame belongs to the performance test), the delivery view in a space scope, the executive page of a space manager and TV mode, each in the full matrix as CI goldens; the timeline is split into one file per theme because its minute areas pass `repo.max_file_kb` in one file. Platform goldens on macOS keep one representative per view (the `normal` state and TV mode), as the size rule of D-M6-11-7 allows. | Brief, Tests; M6-11 design note 8. |
| D-M6-12-8 | 2026-10-16 | The timeline playhead moves in whole minutes, one minute every `60 / speed` seconds of the clock, and starts at the end of the period so the whole day is visible first; play at the end starts again at the beginning. In the full motion context the playhead glides with `motion.duration-move`, in the reduced context it jumps. Controls are buttons with text, because the icon set has no play or pause glyph. The rates leave out the `cost` buckets: they count bookings, and a count is no amount with currency and label (25 4 rule 2). | Brief, design note 5; 25 6.5 (minute precision); components.md 11. |

## M1-63 Catch-up over REST and snapshot

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-63/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave25` at `719cfaa`, branch `feat/M1-63-sync-catch-up` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. The brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the base already holds what the ticket needs (the query parameter `epoch` on `listEvents`, the required `features` on `Snapshot`, `sync -> identity` in 02 and `architecture/modules.yaml`, R-SY-03, R-SY-04, R-SY-16, R-SY-18), so no contract commit was brought in. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-63/1 | 2026-10-15 | `head_seq` is the committed value of `event_seq`, read without a lock through the new `instance.Versions.EventSeq`; `min_retained_seq` is `min(seq)`, or `head_seq + 1` for an empty log. | After retention the log can be empty while the counter is not, so `max(seq)` would report `cursor_ahead` wrongly; `instance.Counters` refuses calls outside the phase hooks (M1-15). A commit is visible before its counter lock is released (04 section 3.2). |
| M1-63/2 | 2026-10-15 | The resync check order, first match wins: an `epoch` different from `sync_epoch` gives `epoch_changed`, then `cursor > head_seq` gives `cursor_ahead`, then `cursor < min_retained_seq - 1` gives `retention`, then `head_seq - cursor > sync.catchup_max_events` gives `too_far_behind`. Without `epoch` there is no epoch check. | A new time line can overtake old cursors, so the epoch decides first (R-SY-18); `cursor_ahead` stays the second net for a client without a stored epoch (R-SY-04); a lost range is reported as `retention` before the size of the backlog. |
| M1-63/3 | 2026-10-15 | `active_runs` is empty (`adapters/noruns`, bound by `internal/wire`) until M2-05 binds the runs port, and every agent of `agents` has `state: unknown` without `reported_billing_mode` until heartbeats exist (M2-20). A caller the agent listing refuses as a whole (`403`, an app without the agents scope) gets an empty `agents`. The presence part is ended by D-M2-20-4 (M2-20): the snapshot shows the presence of the tracker. | The table `runs` and the heartbeats do not exist in M1; `AgentRuntimeState` documents `unknown`. A snapshot must not fail for an app because it may not read agents. |
| M1-63/4 | 2026-10-15 | The unread counters are computed through ports, never by a join across module tables: `chat.Threads.Counters` (new, threads of the listed channels with `last_message_seq > unread_floor_seq`), the person's `read_states` of those channels (new query `ListReadStatesInChannels`) and `messaging.Mentions.CountUnread` with the position `GREATEST(last_read_seq, unread_floor_seq)` per thread; unread is `last_message_seq` minus that position, never below 0. A thread is listed when unread or its mentions are above 0 and `thread.read` allows it in filter mode. Threads without a message above their floor are not listed, and their mentions do not count. The counters cover the channels the snapshot lists (visible, not archived). | 02 "Rules" point 1 and 07 section 7.4 rule 6; every message of such a thread is at or below the floor, so nothing there is unread for anybody; every `channel_id` of `unread_threads` then names an entry of `channels`. |
| M1-63/5 | 2026-10-15 | `trace_context` is in a catch-up envelope only when the caller is an agent; no delivery-only member is set in M1. | 04 "Envelope": the traceparent goes only to agent adapters; `invocation_token` and `redelivered` come with M2. |
| M1-63/6 | 2026-10-15 | The port is named `Snapshot` (02) and its DTO `SnapshotView`; the DTO and its parts (`SnapshotSpace`, `SnapshotChannel`, `UnreadThread`, `RunSummary`, `AgentPresence`, `DraftRef`, `EvaluatedFlag`, `SnapshotPrincipal`) are types of the sync port that mirror the OpenAPI schemas. | Go cannot give an interface and a struct the same name in one package; the port package may import only kernel packages (M1-16/4), so it cannot carry the views of chat. |
| M1-63/7 | 2026-10-15 | `is_owner` comes from `identity.Directory.User` (an active owners row) for people and is false for agents and apps; `permission_version` comes from `Authorizer.Scope`. | `authorize.Scope` has no owner flag: `InstanceWide` is also true for a grant on the instance, which is no owner role. |
| M1-63/8 | 2026-10-15 | `CatchUp.Check` authorizes `events.catch_up` on the instance; `CatchUp.Page` does not and checks each event with its receive action. The visible sets of the page query come from `Authorizer.Scope` with `channel.read`. | The gateway reuses `Check` (M1-64, M1-65), so REST and WebSocket apply the same rule, including `events:subscribe` for apps; `channel.read` is the list action whose scope is the set of readable channels and spaces. |
| M1-63/9 | 2026-10-15 | `authorize.ResourceFromEvent(kind, id, space, channel, thread)` (new in `kernel/authorize`, tests in `internal/authz`) names a thread, channel or space by its id without hints and gives any other kind only its most specific scope (thread, else channel, else space). | The scope of a stored event is a copy from the time of the event; a thread moved since then would contradict a channel hint, which authz treats as an internal error. With the most specific hint authz reads the current path, so the rights at delivery decide (04 section 8.1). |
| M1-63/10 | 2026-10-15 | `channels` lists the channels of `ListSpaceChannels` per visible space; a space that turns invisible or unknown between the two reads is skipped. DM channels without a space are not listed, because chat has no listing for them before direct messages (V3, `dm.enabled`). | The brief asks for the listings of `listSpaces` and `listSpaceChannels`; M1 creates no DM channel. |
| M1-63/11 | 2026-10-15 | Test code: `test/messagingworld.NewSync` builds the world of `NewPersonal` with catch-up and snapshot as `internal/wire` builds them (`wire.NewSyncReads`), with the identity and agents modules, the instance row with its `sync_epoch` (pgtest databases have none) and a configurable partition size; the retention case ages the first partition with `UPDATE event_log SET occurred_at` and runs the partition maintenance of M1-16 with the world's clock. AZ-P5 for the snapshot builds three random worlds through the public ports with a logged seed. | The world of 07 section 11.1 in `internal/authz` is a fake world without HTTP; the shared HTTP world of chat and messaging has every module the snapshot reads. |
| M1-63/12 | 2026-10-15 | Review finding (Blocker): `pin.added` and `pin.removed` (a message, received with `thread.read`), `command.changed` (a command, `command.read` on the channel) and `grant.changed` (a grant, `grant.read` on space, channel, host or principal) made every catch-up page that met them fail with `500`. Fixed for the whole class in one place: `authorize.ResourceFromEvent` sets `Resource.Event` with the full scope, and authz (`eventTarget`, for `Authorize` and for `webhook_out.deliver`) decides such an event on the first object the receive action takes: the target of a grant (the subject principal of an instance grant), then the thread, channel and space of the scope; an event with no fitting object, or whose receive action is still deferred (`file.read` until M3), is filtered as `not_found`, never an error. The webhook delivery of apps builds its descriptor with `ResourceFromEvent` too. `TestResourceFromEventCatalogue` decides every durable event type of the catalogue without an error. | A stored event that no rule can decide must not stop every reader at its `seq`; the fix in authz covers REST catch-up, the gateway (M1-65) and the webhooks, which had the same class. |
| M1-63/13 | 2026-10-15 | Red team round 1 (NEIN, CI red): the test package `api/routes` lacked the two new operations in its stub server and route table, and golangci-lint and semgrep reported five findings in the new lines. Fixed without a behaviour change, apart from one: `Page` takes the receive action through `authorize.ParseAction`, and an event whose receive action is outside the catalogue of the build is filtered instead of failing the page. | The semgrep rule allows catalogue ids only through constants or Parse functions; failing closed matches the webhook delivery. |

## M1-62 Moving threads

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-62/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave25` at `719cfaa`, branch `feat/M1-62-chat-thread-move` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. The brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the base holds every rule the ticket needs (03 `threads`, `thread_tags`, "I1 check", "Lock order", 05 `thread.move` and the events `thread.moved` and `thread.moved_out`, 04 "Basic rule", 07 4.2, the OpenAPI operation and schemas are the same apart from translation), so no contract commit was brought in. `make check` runs with `SONAR_SKIP=1` as the launcher sets it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-62/1 | 2026-10-15 | `thread.moved_out` is appended before `thread.moved`, both in the transaction of the move with consecutive `seq`. | A client that receives both removes the thread from the source first and then shows it in the target, so it ends with the thread in the target (brief note 1g). |
| M1-62/2 | 2026-10-15 | A thread without operator moves only into a channel without effective operator; a thread with operator only into a channel whose effective operator is that agent (`IS NOT DISTINCT FROM`). | 03 `threads`: the target's effective operator must equal `operator_agent_id`, and `NULL` equals only `NULL`; otherwise the move would give an operated thread to a channel run by nobody or another agent, against R-DM-12 and I1. |
| M1-62/3 | 2026-10-15 | The source as target is `400 validation_failed` on `/target_channel_id` with code `same_channel`; an archived target (or a target in an archived space) is `409 conflict`. The domain checks run after `authorize` in the order main thread, same channel, archived target, dm channel, operator; the reason check (`CheckReason`) follows them. | The same channel is a malformed request, not a state conflict; an archived channel accepts no management change (07 4.5). The order is brief note 1b. |
| M1-62/4 | 2026-10-15 | The domain rule is `CanMove(th Thread, source, target MovePlace, targetOperator ids.ID) error` with the zero ID for "no operator", not the signature `CanMove(ThreadState, ChannelState, ChannelState, *uuid)` of the brief. | `domain.ThreadState` already names the thread state (`open`, `closed`, M1-55); the existing types `Thread` and the zero `ids.ID` express the same facts without a second thread type or a pointer. |
| M1-62/5 | 2026-10-15 | After the row lock the use case compares the locked thread's channel with the one it authorized; a concurrent move that committed in between makes it authorize `thread.move` again on the new place before any check or write. | 07 4.2 and brief note 4: no decision may use the old location. The read before the lock is needed so an unknown and an invisible thread give the same `404` before any lock. |
| M1-62/6 | 2026-10-15 | sync keeps one subscriber object: `syncapp.NewSubscribers(drafts, moves)` takes the new port `ThreadMoveRows` next to `DraftRows`; messaging gains `messagingapp.Subscribers` with `ThreadMoveRows`, and `wire.Modules.MessagingSubscribers` is required. The registrations are `chat.ThreadMoved -> messaging` and then `-> sync`. | Brief scope; the subscriber of `MessagePosted` keeps its draft-only port (M1-61 note 5). |
| M1-62/7 | 2026-10-15 | Test code only: the cross-module world of `test/integration` registers the three subscriptions of internal/wire, binds the subscriber of sync, serves the personal handlers of sync and inserts a `devices` row for every session, because read states and drafts name the writing device (foreign key). | The move tests need read states and drafts through the real HTTP chain in the world with Support, Allgemein, Eskalation and Marketing of 07 sections 10 and 11.1. |
| M1-62/8 | 2026-10-15 | authz treats a zero `TargetChannelID` of `thread.move` as an unknown, invisible target (`not_found`), no longer as an internal error. | Red team: the nil UUID passes the request validator as `target_channel_id`; the internal error answered `500` for an existing thread and `404` for an unknown one, an existence oracle for invisible threads against R-AZ-01 and brief note 1a. |

## M7-11 API breaking change check

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-11-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave24` at `2a72cca`, branch `feat/M7-11-api-breaking-check` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. The base names the chapters in German and has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in this ticket section and the added text is English, also inside the German chapter 06 and `docs/quality/`. No contract change of `origin/main` is needed: the brief relies on 06 13 and 15, which the base has in substance. | Instructions of the launcher override the brief's landing step; merged names win (D-W-100 as the brief states it). |
| D-M7-11-1 | 2026-10-15 | Tool (Design note 1): G6 is the own comparison `tools/api-checks/breaking.py` over the parsed YAML (PyYAML, already locked), not oasdiff; nothing new is pinned. Evidence: oasdiff 1.33.0 (`darwin_all` release asset) parsed `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and reported "No changes detected" against itself, but on mutated copies it reported no breaking change for a removed property of `TypingStartedPayload` (an event schema no path references) and for a changed `x-authz-action` of `GET /instance/discovery`, and it reported a removed query parameter `cursor` only as a warning (`request-parameter-removed`); it did report a removed `201` as an error. A wrapper would have needed its own comparison for the events, the action and the pagination kind anyway. | 06 13 item 7 leaves the tool open; Design note 1 asks for the own comparison when oasdiff misses a row; the rows are structural and few. |
| D-M7-11-2 | 2026-10-15 | Base (requirement 12): the newest stable tag `server/vX.Y.Z` (SemVer precedence) among the tags merged into `HEAD`, read with `git show <tag>:docs/api/openapi.yaml` (and `asyncapi.yaml` for directions when present); any pre-release tag, not only `-beta.N`, is the base only when no stable tag is merged, the newest by SemVer precedence; no tag gives the notice `no published version` and exit 0; a tag without `docs/api/openapi.yaml` is an error. Only merged tags count, like `last_stable_tag` of `tools/ci/release_prep.py`, so a maintenance branch compares with its own line; CI checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`, so the tags are there. The gate runs when `server` is selected; a change under `docs/api/` selects every gate component. | Brief requirement 12 and Design note 3; the API document ships with the server (`RELEASE_EXTRA_PATHS`). |
| D-M7-11-3 | 2026-10-15 | Major version exception (requirement 14): the gate accepts the findings, printed as `accepted`, only when the major of `info.version` of the working tree is higher than that of the base, also from `0.x` to `1.0.0`; a minor step under `0.x` does not accept a break, and an `info.version` that is not SemVer is an error. Rules of the comparison that the 06 table leaves open: a schema's direction comes from paths, `webhooks` (reversed) and `asyncapi.yaml`, and a schema without one counts in both directions; a response property that is no longer required counts as row 1, a changed `default` as row 3 and, for a parameter named `sort`, also as row 6; a changed `operationId` is a rename (row 1); the pagination kind comes from the query parameters `cursor`, `before_seq`, `after_seq`, `around_seq` and an integer `after` (a time filter `after` is none); the schema of a response header is compared like a field; a removed response status is row 5, a new status passes; a changed status of a problem code or `x-status` of a response component is row 5, a removed problem code row 1. `asyncapi.yaml` changes of channels, operations and messages are not covered (brief Out of scope). | 06 13 items 4 and 5; requirement 14 says nothing else lowers the gate; an unknown direction must not hide a new required request field. |

## Integration int/m1-wave29

## Rulings from main: D-W-24 (gateway part)

This row of the ruling log on `main` is copied verbatim, because M4-13 builds on the close code
`4018`. Only its gateway part is ported: the row `4018` of 04 6.4 and its mapping row, the entry of
`x-close-codes` in `asyncapi.yaml`, the field names of `hello` in 04 6.3 item 2, the transition of
15 7.2 and the `client_error` codes of `docs/observability/signals.md`. The terminal names and the
rate-limit wording it also mentions are not needed by M4-13 and stay as they are on this base. The
links of the row are rewritten to the chapter names of this base (`spec/04-sync-protokoll.md`); its
section 13 of chapter 24 exists only on `main`, so it is named as a path.

| ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-W-24 | Gateway: a separate close code `4018 forbidden` for a valid login without permission (for example `scope_missing`), `4004` now only for invalid credentials; in 04, the fields of `hello` are named `heartbeat_interval_ms` and `resume_window_ms` as in the schema; `heartbeat` and `reauth` consume nothing from `rl.gateway_connect`; the RL class for "no class" is named `none` in the catalogue as in the API. Terminal: `4101` is named `terminal_token_stale`; 24 section 13 explains all codes `4100` to `4114`; a host service without login within `terminal.host_auth_timeout` gets `4113`. | Finding L-28: when a scope was missing, `4004` led to a pointless token renewal; names and explanations differed from each other. | [04 6.3 and 6.4](spec/04-sync-protokoll.md), 24 section 13 (`spec/24-terminal.md` on main), [asyncapi.yaml](api/asyncapi.yaml), [Brief M1-64](briefs/M1-64-gateway-connection.md), [PLAN M2-43](PLAN.md) |

## M4-13 Sync engine: connection and session

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-13-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave27` at `3d4f0f3`, branch `feat/M4-13-sync-connection` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. Brief and spec were read from `main`; the close code `4018` the brief relies on is on `main` (D-W-24) but not on the base, so its gateway part comes first as its own commit (section "Rulings from main: D-W-24"). The base names the chapters `04-sync-protokoll.md`, `15-client-architektur.md` and `08-identitaet.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114): the session class is `GatewaySession`, the backoff `ReconnectBackoff`, the close table lives in its own file `close_codes.dart` (`closeCodeTable`, `reactTo`, `CloseReaction`, `Reconnect`); `AppLifecycle` also has `current`, because `1000` reconnects only while the app is in the foreground and a mobile start in the background must not connect; `GatewayConnection` takes the `AuthSession` of `auth` and the `SyncStateDao` of `local_store` directly. The server gateway of M1-64 and M1-65 is not on this base, so no server behaviour could be compared with 04 6.4. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief. |
| D-M4-13-1 | 2026-10-16 | `ConnectionState(phase, reason)`; the reasons are `none`, `networkUnavailable`, `devicePending`, `deviceReplaced`, `forbidden`, `rateLimited`, `revoked`, `protocolUnsupported` and `closedByServer(code)`. Transitions beyond the diagram of 15 7.2: `4016` goes to `offline`/`devicePending` and reconnects only after `LoginService.pollPendingDevice` reports `active` (a rejected device ends in `signedOut`); `4017` goes to `offline`/`deviceReplaced` and reconnects on the foreground or `connectNow`; `4018` goes to `offline`/`forbidden` and reconnects only on `connectNow`; `4008` goes to `backoff`/`rateLimited` and waits `retry_after=<seconds>` of the close reason; `4015` goes to `offline` and is logged as unexpected; a server `1000` backs off in the foreground and waits `offline` for the foreground otherwise; a drop without a close code backs off, or goes `offline`/`networkUnavailable` while the device has no network. Without a listener `ready` and `resumed` lead to `live`, with one to `catchingUp`, and the listener sets `catchingUp`, `resyncing` or `live` through `SessionControl`. | Brief Design note 3; 15 7.2 shows the added transitions. |
| D-M4-13-2 | 2026-10-16 | The close reactions are one map from code to `CloseReaction` (phase, reason, reconnect mode, `identify`, `renewToken`, `signOut`, `unexpected`), and `reactTo` resolves only what depends on the close reason or the app state (`4008`, `1000`, a code outside the table). `4004` and `4011` renew the token through `RefreshCoordinator.refreshNow` and reconnect at once; a second renewal (`4004` or `4011`) before the next `ready` or `resumed` waits the backoff, so a server that keeps rejecting is not flooded; a refresh that ends the session signs out, a transient failure backs off. `4013` waits uniform in `[0, sync.reconnect_backoff_base]` without raising the counter. `4012` calls `AuthSession.sessionRevoked`, whose `SignedOut(sessionRevoked)` carries `deleteLocalData`. | Brief Design note 4: the test iterates the table. |
| D-M4-13-3 | 2026-10-16 | A network change and the return to the foreground start an attempt at once from `backoff` (and from `offline` with `networkUnavailable`; the foreground also from `suspended`, `deviceReplaced` and a server `1000` in the background) without raising the attempt counter; only a failed attempt draws the next backoff. A network loss during a backoff wait cancels it and goes `offline`/`networkUnavailable`. On mobile no path opens a socket in the background: an attempt that would start there (a backoff timer, a token renewal, an approved pending device) goes to `suspended` and waits for the foreground; a return to the foreground while the own background close is still running connects at once; a server close that arrived before the own close keeps its reaction (code review of M4-13). | 04 6.5, 15 7.2 `Suspended`. |
| D-M4-13-4 | 2026-10-16 | Unknown ops and unknown members of known ops are ignored; client ops coming from the server are ignored like unknown ops. A server frame that is not a JSON object with a string `op`, or whose `d` does not fit the schema of its op, counts as a decode error (`decodeErrors`), is logged with its cause only and skipped; the socket stays. `dispatch` keeps the raw envelope map for `decodeEnvelope` of M4-14. Outgoing `d` drops `null` members recursively. | 04 6.1 (additive changes), 04 6.4 (closing with a protocol code is the server's role), 04 2.2. |
| D-M4-13-5 | 2026-10-16 | Focus overflow: at most `sync.max_focused_threads` IDs are sent; the most recently shown threads win, where a thread counts as shown when it first appears in a `setVisible` call and IDs new in one call count in the iteration order of the set; `setVisible` returns the unfocused rest. Changes within one microtask send one frame; after `ready` the current set is sent when not empty, after `resumed` only a change made while disconnected; an `error` with `validation_failed` for `focus` restores the previous set. | Brief Design note 6; 04 11.3 item 2 and 6.3 item 7 (the server restores the focus on `resume`). |
| D-M4-13-6 | 2026-10-16 | The typing throttle is per connection as on the server: `typing.start` at most once per `sync.typing_min_interval`, `typing.stop` only after a start and it resets the throttle; a start for another thread inside the interval first stops the previous thread. A new socket resets the throttle; frames that cannot be sent (no session) are dropped. | 04 12 item 1: `typing.stop` resets the throttle of the connection. |
| D-M4-13-7 | 2026-10-16 | `disconnected_at` is stored when a socket that had reached `ready` or `resumed` ends, not after a failed attempt, so failed attempts do not move the start of the resume window. `resume` needs a stored gateway session, a stored cursor (`GatewayResume.cursor` is required) and a disconnect less than `resume_window_ms` of the new `hello` ago; otherwise `identify`, with cursor and epoch only when a cursor is stored. The heartbeat sends the stored cursor read at `ready` or `resumed` (0 without one) until the listener sets `lastProcessedSeq`. | Brief Design notes 2 and 5. |
| D-M4-13-8 | 2026-10-16 | Frames are decoded as they arrive; `heartbeat_ack` and `reauth_required` act at once, every other frame waits in the ordered queue behind the listener steps. The bookkeeping of a disconnect (store `disconnected_at`, `onDisconnected`) cannot keep the close reaction from running. | Red team of M4-13: an acknowledgement queued behind a long snapshot or resync step of the listener missed its interval and closed a healthy connection (04 6.3 items 5 and 6). |
