## M1-64 Gateway connection and session

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-64/0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave28` at `c83c68a`, branch `feat/M1-64-gateway-connection` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. The brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`. The gateway part of the contract change L-28 of the planning line was missing on the base and comes in as its own commit: close code `4018 forbidden` (04 6.4, asyncapi `x-close-codes`, 15, signals), the `_ms` names of `hello` in 04, and `heartbeat` and `reauth` not consuming `rl.gateway_connect` (04, 05, asyncapi, actions.yaml). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step; the brief maps "valid credentials, other `forbidden`" to `4018`, which the base did not define. |
| M1-64/1 | 2026-10-16 | Heartbeat deadline: the server closes with `4009` when no heartbeat arrived for `sync.heartbeat_interval` times (`sync.heartbeat_miss_limit` + 1) since `ready`, `resumed` or the last heartbeat, counted from the instant `ready` or `resumed` is sent. | The client delays its first heartbeat by a random share of the interval (04 6.3 step 5); the extra interval absorbs that delay, and counting from the frame instead of the start of `identify` keeps the snapshot build out of the client's budget. |
| M1-64/2 | 2026-10-16 | Reauth for people: `reauth_required {expires_at, dpop_nonce}` goes out once, `sync.reauth_lead_time` before the expiry of the current access token. `reauth` is authenticated like `identify`; a rejected token or proof answers `error {op: reauth, code}` and keeps the connection; a token of another principal, device or auth session, or a credential that is not a person's access token, closes with `4004`; then `gateway.connect` is authorized. Without a valid `reauth` the connection closes with `4011` at the expiry (exp, without `auth.clock_leeway`). Agents and apps get no `reauth_required` and are closed with `4011` at the `expires_at` of their API token. | 04 6.3 step 6; a failed refresh of the client must not cost the connection while the old token is valid, a token of another session must not take over a live connection. |
| M1-64/3 | 2026-10-16 | Resume: an unknown session, a session detached for `sync.resume_window` or longer, and a session of another principal or device all close with the same `4006`. Credentials are checked, rate limited and authorized as for `identify`. When the session's old connection is still open it is closed with `4017` first. A session stays resumable after the ends `0` (client gone), `1000`, `1001`, `4000`, `4001`, `4002`, `4009`, `4011`, `4013` and `4014`; after `4004`, `4012`, `4016`, `4017`, `4018` and the other ends it is removed. | Identical answers leak nothing about foreign sessions; `4006` for a still open old connection would make that client reconnect and push the new one out; the final ends tell the client not to resume. |
| M1-64/4 | 2026-10-16 | The sweeper of the session store runs every `sync.resume_window`; a detached session is removed at the latest two windows after its connection ended. | `resume` checks the window itself, so the sweep only frees memory and needs no key of its own. |
| M1-64/5 | 2026-10-16 | Every body of a client frame of the channel `gateway` is closed with `additionalProperties: false`, the stats ops of M6 included (12 schemas with `GatewayClientInfo`); the generator check walks the bodies of all client ops of `asyncapi.yaml`, not only of the enabled ones. The Python SDK models are regenerated accordingly. | W7 holds for every client frame; a check over the whole channel keeps later tickets from enabling an op whose body is still open. |
| M1-64/6 | 2026-10-16 | Frames pass the JSON guard of SEC-IN-2 with `http.max_json_depth`, then the envelope `{op, d}` with no other member, then the schema of the op (`httpserver.SchemaValidator`) and the strict decoder. | One nesting limit for every JSON the server reads; the brief names no gateway-specific key. |
| M1-64/7 | 2026-10-16 | The route `GET /api/v1/gateway` is bound in `internal/wire` (`GatewayOperation`, `GatewayRouteBinding`), not in the generated route table, because it is no operation of `openapi.yaml`; the start checks its binding with the REST bindings (AZ-P1). The marker `httpserver.Operation.AuthorizedInBand` makes `Chain.Wrap` skip every per-route stage for it; only the outer steps 1 to 4 run. | 02 "Chain in the api node" steps 1 to 3 for the upgrade; authentication and `authorize` happen on the connection (R-SY-01), so the proof stage would refuse the `101`. |
| M1-64/8 | 2026-10-16 | A missed `gateway.write_timeout` ends the connection with `4014` as its recorded close code, but coder/websocket tears the connection down when the context of a write ends, so the client sees the end without the close frame. A full outbound queue closes with a regular `4014` close frame. | The library offers no write without teardown; a peer that does not read cannot receive the close frame either. |
| M1-64/9 | 2026-10-16 | After a binary frame or a frame above `sync.ws_max_frame_bytes` the reader stops reading; the close handshake of the library reads until the peer's close frame. | Reading on would parse the rest of the refused frame as the next frame and turn the `4002` into a protocol error `1002`. |
| M1-64/10 | 2026-10-16 | `heartbeat` revalidates the credential through `actor.Authenticator.Revalidate` (identity reads token or session, device and key again, one query) and then authorizes `gateway.connect`; an unauthenticated answer of either closes with `4012`, another error of the revalidation answers `error` with its problem code and keeps the connection. The immediate end of open connections on `session.revoked` and `access.changed` comes with M1-65. | Red team round 1: the actor of `identify` carries the credential state of that time, which `authorize` trusts, so a revocation without a new rights version went unnoticed; brief note 14 requires `4012` at the heartbeat. |
| M1-64/11 | 2026-10-16 | An error while building `ready` or `resumed` maps like the table of note 10: `device_pending` `4016`, any other `forbidden` `4018`, `session_revoked` `4012`, another unauthenticated `4004`, the rest `4000`. Spec gap: an app with `events:subscribe` passes `gateway.connect`, but `sync.snapshot.read` has no `app_scope` in the catalogue (05, `actions.yaml`, also on `origin/main`), so the snapshot of `ready` is refused and the app is closed with `4018`; brief note 22 ("apps get `ready` like people") needs a contract change that this ticket may not make. | `4000` would make the app reconnect with backoff for ever; `4018` tells it not to. The core contract is not edited by this ticket. |
| M1-64/12 | 2026-10-16 | The WebSocket handler puts the transport of the upgrade request (TCP peer, header fields, action `gateway.connect`) into the context of the connection, as the authenticate stage does for REST. | The session binding of trusted header logins (M1-43) reads it on every authentication; without it every `identify` of such a session revoked the session (red team round 1). |

## M1-71 Contract tests G7 and G8

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-71/0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave28` at `c83c68a`, branch `feat/M1-71-contract-tests` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push, the integration lane lands it. Brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the base holds the rules the ticket needs in its German chapters (06 "Vertragstests und Gate" G7 and G8, 19 SEC-IN-1, SEC-IN-2 and SEC-T06, 23 R-QA-11, PLAN W7), so no contract commit was brought in. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-71/1 | 2026-10-16 | `TestG8_ProductionBuildExcludesValidator` checks that the untagged `cmd/bothy` does not depend on `contractcheck` and that the build with `contract_validate` does; `github.com/pb33f/libopenapi-validator` itself stays in the production build. | The request validator of the chain (SEC-IN-1, M1-18) already uses the same library in production; only the response check is test-only. |
| M1-71/2 | 2026-10-16 | A response that violates the contract in the test build writes its report to standard error and ends the process with exit code 3. | A hard exit is the only mechanism that fails every integration test regardless of how it asserts, without touching each test; it exists only in the test build. |
| M1-71/3 | 2026-10-16 | G8 runs on the request validator of the chain: `Chain.Public` hands its parsed contract and mounted operations to `contractcheck.Attach`, which checks only when that contract is byte for byte `docs/api/openapi.yaml` of the repository (SHA-256; the file is found by walking up, a missing file ends the process with exit code 3) and passes chains over test fixture contracts unchecked. It takes the operation from the route of the server (`net/http` patterns) and validates with the path item of that route, not with the path search of the library. A request that matches no mounted operation is not checked. | A second parse of the contract per test binary cost more than a minute under `-race` and broke the readiness waits of the serve tests; `api/rest.Spec` equals `docs/api/openapi.yaml` (codegen-check). The library prefers a literal path of another method (`GET /grants/preview` against `getGrant`) and reported a missing operation for a request the server routes correctly. |
| M1-71/4 | 2026-10-16 | The project check `correlation_id` requires a lower-case UUID (schema `CorrelationId`), not a UUIDv7 as the brief writes, and equality with `correlation_id` of a problem body. | 06 "Basic rules", row Correlation: the value is the trace ID written as a UUID, not a UUIDv7; the server could never pass a v7 check. |
| M1-71/5 | 2026-10-16 | The credential broker answers `kind: owner` with a new device of the first owner through a recovery code of the setup service (`bothy setup issue-enrollment-code`) and `kind: user` with a new device of Alex of the matrix world (`createEnrollmentCode` by a fresh owner session), not with a new person. | Brief design note 5 makes Alex the plain user of the second pass; a new person would see nothing of the world. A recovery code does not depend on any session, so a generated call that revokes the owner's sessions does not end the run. |
| M1-71/6 | 2026-10-16 | `harness.Start` reserves a free port of `127.0.0.1` and binds the public listener to it, retrying a start whose port was taken in between; the internal listener stays on port 0. `Client.Gateway` is not built (the gateway of M1-64 is not in this base), and `Options.Telemetry` is refused until M1-69 adds its sinks. | `server.public_url` must name the port before the listener exists, because every DPoP `htu` is built from it. |
| M1-71/7 | 2026-10-16 | G7 runs the seven built-in checks the brief names (`not_a_server_error`, `status_code_conformance`, `content_type_conformance`, `response_headers_conformance`, `response_schema_conformance`, `negative_data_rejection`, `ignored_auth`) plus the four project checks; the seed is `hypothesis.seed(CONTRACT_SEED)` with `derandomize` off and no example database. Positive cases get a fresh UUIDv7 as `Idempotency-Key`. | The listed checks define the scope; with `derandomize` on Hypothesis would ignore the seed. The schema `Uuid` allows any version, the server requires v7, so a generated key would turn every positive case into a `400`. |
| M1-71/8 | 2026-10-16 | The reproduction command calls `PYTHONPATH=tools/contract uv run --project tools python -m dpop`, not `python -m contract.dpop`; `tools/contract/live_contract.py` is left out of the tools coverage. `make contract-test` has the named time limit `quality.contract.timeout`. | The modules under `tools/` are top-level modules for pytest and mypy; the live module runs only against a server. Fixed requirement 8: a new gate value is a named value of chapter 23. |
| M1-71/10 | 2026-10-16 | `wire.WarmRequestValidator` (exported) builds the request validator of the enabled operations once per process; `harness.Main` calls it before the tests, and `harness.Start` waits up to three minutes for readiness. | The build takes tens of seconds under `-race` and host load; the serve tests of `internal/wire` warm it the same way (M1-31/18). |
| M1-71/11 | 2026-10-16 | The stateful phase is `test_stateful`, which runs the state machine with only the generate phase and, like the Schemathesis engine, treats a Hypothesis `Flaky` (a replay that draws differently because the server kept the state of earlier scenarios) as the end of the phase unless a check failed before it, which it then reports. The CI job `gates` gets `timeout-minutes: 240`. | The first full run at 100 examples failed only with `FlakyStrategyDefinition` while selecting a rule; G7 took about 40 minutes there, beside a `make test` of more than 30, so the former 60 minutes of the job cannot hold `make gates` with `contract-test`. |
| M1-71/9 | 2026-10-16 | `egress.Deps.DenyAll` refuses every request before resolution; `wire.Deps.EgressDenyAll` passes it to the OIDC client, the webhook sender and the audit checkpoint sink. The response validator test of `httpservertest` moved to a route of its test contract. | A resolver that fails would still let an IP literal through; a test with a deliberately wrong answer on a route of `docs/api/openapi.yaml` ends the binary under G8. |

## M4-14 Sync engine: catch-up and reconciliation

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-14-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave30` at `c0ad78b`, branch `feat/M4-14-sync-catch-up` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. Brief and spec were read from `main`; the brief relies on no core rule or contract change that the base lacks (R-SY-14 and R-CL-03 are in the German chapters 04 and 15 of the base, D-W-115 was ported earlier, D-W-100 and D-W-114 are planning rulings), so nothing is ported. The base names the chapters `04-sync-protokoll.md` and `15-client-architektur.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114): the sign-out notice is `SignedOutNotice` (`SignedOut` is an event of `auth`), the REST reads are the port `Fetcher` with `ApiFetcher` over the generated API, `Dispatcher.apply` takes a `DecodedEnvelope` (the raw envelope with the `DecodedEvent` of `api_client`, null when malformed) plus the stored cursor and an `AfterCommit`, `Fetcher.myReadStates` takes a channel because the generated `listMyReadStates` requires `channel_id`, the write path of drafts calls `CatchUpListener.applyDraftPutResult`, and a failed gap fill is retried by `CatchUpListener.onFocus`. The wire mappers (`wire.dart`) and the identity port (`LocalIdentity`, `AuthIdentity`) are files beyond the brief's list. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief. |
| D-M4-14-1 | 2026-10-16 | A gateway page is the run of `dispatch` frames up to the next `cursor` frame or `catchup.complete`; it is applied in one `commitPage` with the `seq` of that frame (never below the stored cursor) and the epoch of the session. In the live phase every durable `dispatch` is its own transaction with `cursor = seq`; ephemeral events apply at once without a transaction. A disconnect drops an unfinished page; its events come again from the stored cursor. | Design note 1; 04 7.1 (the server sends `cursor` at the end of every page) and 04 9 item 2. |
| D-M4-14-2 | 2026-10-16 | `ready` without a stored cursor empties the mirror and applies the whole snapshot with `cursor = head_seq` and the epoch in one transaction and goes `live` at once, because an `identify` without a cursor gets no catch-up and no `catchup.complete` (04 6.3 item 4; red team of M4-14); with a cursor only the parts that are not events apply (active run summaries by version, the counters, the agent presence, the feature flags into a stored instance row). After a whole snapshot the drafts are loaded with `listMyDrafts` when a draft reference is newer than or missing from the local drafts, the read states with `listMyReadStates` for the channels with unread threads, and the instance with `getInstance` (its flags replaced by the snapshot's). | Design note 1; without a cursor the mirror belongs to no known time line; the snapshot names drafts only by version and carries counters without a read state version. |
| D-M4-14-3 | 2026-10-16 | The outbox, the run store and the outbox start are hooks (`onEcho`, `onRunUpdated`, `onLive`), constructor parameters with no-op defaults, not dependencies. `onEcho` runs after the commit for a `message.created` with `client_msg_id` whose author is the person (or when the person is not known yet, after a `resume` in a new process); a truncated `message.created` echoes after its reload. | Design note 2; M4-17 wires them; outbox IDs are UUIDv7 of this device, so an echo of another author never matches an entry. |
| D-M4-14-4 | 2026-10-16 | `access.changed` in the middle of a page applies in its `seq` position (removal inside the page transaction, file bytes and `TargetsRemoved` after the commit) and the rest of the page applies after it. Only `4012` and `session.revoked` of this session stop a page: the page rolls back, the database is wiped (`LocalDatabase.wipe`), `SignedOutNotice(deleteLocalData: true)` is emitted and nothing is applied after that. `4012` reaches the listener as the connection state `signedOut`/`revoked` (`watchConnection`). | Design note 3; 04 8.1 and 8.3 item 2: the gateway withholds only events of removed targets, so dropping the rest would lose events that are never sent again. |
| D-M4-14-5 | 2026-10-16 | `session.revoked` wipes only when its `session_id` is this device's session (`LocalIdentity.sessionId`, the `sid` of `auth`) or the own session is unknown; a revocation of another session of the person (a logout on the phone) changes nothing here. | `session.revoked` has the audience `self` and reaches every device of the person (05 6); the revoked device itself is also closed with `4012` (04 8.5). |
| D-M4-14-6 | 2026-10-16 | Gaps: a `message.created` that continues a loaded range, or arrives in a thread with no loaded range, or lies below every loaded range joins the loaded ranges; otherwise it is stored without joining them, the gap from the end of the highest range below it up to it is recorded, and `Fetcher.range` loads it after the commit (`listThreadMessages` with `after_seq`, page by page); the fetched messages and the whole range up to the new message join the loaded ranges in one transaction. `MessagesDao.watchContiguous` therefore shows only the contiguous range ending at the newest loaded message. A failed fill keeps the gap recorded; `onFocus` retries it. | Design note 4; 04 4: a real gap is missing data, and a thread never opened here shows from its newest message without loading its history. |
| D-M4-14-7 | 2026-10-16 | Personal data: the echo of this device (`updated_by_device_id`) is ignored for drafts, settings and the sidebar; the read state is merged also for the own echo, because `mergeReadState` is monotonic and versioned and the counters come only from the server. A `draft.updated` or `draft.deleted` for a draft the person is typing on this device (`DraftEditing`) is not applied; the next local write wins. | Design note 5 and 04 14. |
| D-M4-14-8 | 2026-10-16 | A page whose transaction fails (a payload that breaks its mapping, a database error) leaves rows and cursor as they were; the listener then applies nothing until the next `ready` or `resumed`, which catches up from the stored cursor. An envelope that does not fit the schema of its type is skipped like an unknown type and the cursor passes it. | A later page must not move the cursor past events that were never applied; `SessionControl` has no close, and a reconnect repeats the page. |
| D-M4-14-9 | 2026-10-16 | When `getSnapshot` fails during a resync, the listener keeps mirror and cursor and retries the snapshot after the reconnect backoff (`sync.reconnect_backoff_base`, `sync.reconnect_backoff_cap`, full jitter) on the same connection; frames that arrive meanwhile wait in memory and apply after the snapshot with the duplicate rule. A disconnect ends the retries; the next session catches up from the stored cursor and gets `resync_required` again. `onLive` runs once per session, also when a `catchup.complete` follows a resync. | Code review of M4-14: dropping the mirror and halting left the app empty on an open connection until the next reconnect; after a restore every client asks for the snapshot at once. |
| D-M4-14-10 | 2026-10-16 | `packages/local_store` gets three methods beyond the brief's list, each with its test: `PersonalDao.deleteSettingIfNewer` and `deleteNotificationPreferenceIfNewer` (for `deleted: true`, 04 14) and `MessagesDao.reactionsOf` (reading the `me` flag of a reaction inside the page transaction, where a stream would wait for the transaction's end). | Fixed requirement 17 and Design note 5 need the deletions; no existing method removes those rows. |
| D-M4-14-11 | 2026-10-16 | `decideApply` decides per event between the version rule and the `seq` rule and drops a versioned type that arrives without `resource.version`; the comparison with the stored version is the conditional upsert of the DAOs (D-M4-12-3), one statement per row. Event payloads, the snapshot and REST answers go through one set of wire mappers from JSON to the domain types; the generated models of REST answers are serialized back to JSON first. The page size for the isolate decision is an estimate over the decoded frames (string lengths plus a few bytes per value). | One rule in one place; the gateway frames are already JSON-decoded by the connection, so their raw size is not at hand. |
| D-M4-14-12 | 2026-10-16 | REST reads that run after a commit (a truncated object, a gap fill, the newest page of a focused thread, the structure of `added` or `changed` targets, drafts and read states after a snapshot) store their result only when, inside the transaction of the write, the target is still held: content needs its channel row and neither the channel, its space nor the thread may have been removed in this process (`SyncContext.removedTargets`); a view of `changed` needs its row. An `access.changed` with `added` (for its spaces and channels and for every thread its structure load lists), a `thread.moved` and a snapshot that lists a target take it out of the removed set (follow-up review of M4-14: a moved-out thread stayed blocked after its new channel became visible). | Code review of M4-14, reproduced: a `getMessage` in flight during `access.changed` with `removed` stored the message and its search row again (04 8.3 item 3, T-DV-01). |

## M6-01 Console session following the BFF pattern

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-01-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave31` at `f63a8aa`, branch `feat/M6-01-console-session` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it (the brief's landing steps follow the task). The core contract change R-DM-49 and R-AZ-32 of main (`57b1553`) enters first as its own commit, with the generated catalogue code, the requirement table and the ERD. | The base predates that commit; the brief needs exactly that change and no other. |
| D-M6-01-1 | 2026-10-16 | Console form of the login operations: member `client` (`app` default, `console`) on `completeOidcLogin`, `completePasskeyLogin`, `loginWithPassword`, `loginWithTrustedHeader` and `beginOidcLogin`; the console form sends no `device` and no DPoP proof (the parameter `DpopProofLogin` is optional, the handler requires `device` for the app form); the login completion registers a web device `{platform: web, state: active, name: "Web console"}` without keys per login and starts a console session, no token and no refresh family; answer `200` (response `ConsoleLogin`). A pending person does not log in to the console. An OIDC console flow needs `/console/login/callback` on the origin of `server.public_url` (skipping the provider's `app_redirect_uris`) and completes only in the console form, an app flow only in the app form. Enrollment codes and pairing refuse the form (`400`). The allow entry of `auth.login.complete` carries `client_component = console`. | One login implementation per method (brief note 1); the redirect binding keeps tokens of an app login out of the browser. |
| D-M6-01-2 | 2026-10-16 | Cookie `__Host-console_session`, 32 bytes of `crypto/rand` as base64url without padding, stored as HMAC-SHA-256 with the token pepper; attributes `Path=/; Max-Age=<seconds to expires_at>; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`, no `Domain`. A new hash at login and in the transaction of every successful step-up of a console session; `logout` and every `401` of an unknown, revoked, expired or idle cookie answer with the clearing cookie. `Cache-Control: no-store` comes from SEC-HDR-2, which the chain sets on every API answer, so the contract does not repeat it per response. Plain HTTP never carries the cookie in production, because the transport protection refuses plaintext from untrusted peers (SEC-TLS-1) and `__Host-` requires `Secure`. | Brief note 2; session fixation. |
| D-M6-01-3 | 2026-10-16 | Idle rule: an accepted request writes `last_seen_at` (session and device) only when it is older than `auth.console_session_idle_timeout / 100`; a session is idle when `now - last_seen_at >= idle_timeout + idle_timeout/100`. The request that finds an idle or expired session and the job `identity.auth_cleanup` revoke it with `expired` (transition guarded by `revoked_at IS NULL`) and write one allow entry of the system action `session.revoke` with `revoke_reason = expired` and `cause` `idle` or `absolute`. The key minimum is `1m`, so the write bound is never zero. | Brief note 3; bounded writes without losing activity just before the deadline. |
| D-M6-01-4 | 2026-10-16 | The cookie is the second credential kind of `kernel/actor.Authenticator` (`Credentials.ConsoleCookie`, a redacting type; `Present` counts it, so failed cookies count in `rl.public_auth`). Cookie and `Authorization` together are `400 validation_failed`. The console actor has `ClientComponent = console`, `DeviceKeyActive = true` (no key binding exists) and the stored view; a `trusted_header` console session is bound to its header login on every request like an app session. | Brief note 4; one credential per request. |
| D-M6-01-5 | 2026-10-16 | CSRF header `X-Console-Request: 1` (parameter `ConsoleRequest`, referenced on the state-changing operations of this ticket; later console tickets reference it on theirs) with an `Origin` equal to the origin of `server.public_url`; ConsoleGuard runs as the last step of `StageAuthenticate`, only for requests the cookie authenticated, so the stage list and the golden order of 02 stay as they are. The gateway refuses an upgrade with the cookie and no `Origin`. No CORS header is added. | Brief note 5; a cross-site form or fetch cannot set the header. |
| D-M6-01-6 | 2026-10-16 | Step-up operations `stepUpWithPassword`, `beginStepUpOidcReauth`, `completeStepUpOidcReauth` (body of the completion: the existing schema `IdentityLinkComplete`). The OIDC re-login is a link-mode flow of the person whose stored redirect is `/console/step-up/callback?challenge_id=<id>`: the callback hands the challenge back with the `login_code`, `completeIdentityLink` refuses such a flow, and the completion needs the person's identity (same issuer and subject) and an `auth_time` not before the second of the challenge's creation (`auth_time` has whole seconds). Wrong confirmations fail the challenge and count. Both methods answer `403` outside a console session. | Brief note 6; the core contract has no column for the binding, and the redirect is the flow's own data. |
| D-M6-01-7 | 2026-10-16 | "View as person" on the console session: `viewAsUser` from a console session stores target and start through the authz port `ConsoleViews` in the unit of work of its allow entry (attributes `target_user_id`, `started_at`, `expires_at`); the authenticator sets `actor.ViewAs` from it; at or after `authz.view_as_max_duration` the next request clears it in its own unit of work with the entry `access.view_as.end` (`ended_reason = expired`, actor the owner) and answers `401 step_up_required`; `endViewAs` writes `ended_reason = ended`, also idempotently without a view. `domain.ViewAsExempt` makes `authorize` decide `access.view_as.end`, `auth.logout`, `auth.step_up` on the own session and `self.read` on oneself without the view. | Brief note 7, R-DM-49, R-AZ-32. |
| D-M6-01-8 | 2026-10-16 | Pre-step-up gate `consolesession.PassesGate`, called by the authenticator after the session checks: without `step_up_at` only `self.read`, `auth.step_up`, `auth.logout`, `instance.discovery.read` and the public login actions pass; everything else is `401 step_up_required` with reason `console_step_up_required`, which never counts as a failed credential. | Brief note 8; the login actions let a tab with an old valid cookie log in again. |
| D-M6-01-9 | 2026-10-16 | `docs/api/openapi.yaml` stays below `repo.max_file_kb` (1 MiB) with the console additions: two shared responses (`ConsoleLogin`, `StepUpDone`), the view inline in `ConsoleSession`, no descriptions or examples on the new operations and only `401`, `403` and `default` on them. The margin left is a few hundred bytes. The route generator splits the strict response objects into `objects.gen.go` and `objects2.gen.go` (cut at the operation group nearest the middle). | The base file had 8 KB below the limit and `objects.gen.go` 200 bytes; the gate must not be loosened. The next contract change needs a split of `openapi.yaml`, an owner decision (report). |
| D-M6-01-10 | 2026-10-16 | Tests follow the merged harness: the integration tests run in the session world of `identity/adapters/httpapi` (PostgreSQL through `pgtest`, the full HTTP chain, a fake clock, the fake IdP and `virtualauthn`) instead of a process of `internal/wire`, because only that world has a fake clock; the WebSocket case runs against the gateway handler on a real listener; the write during a view is `updateMyProfile` instead of `createMessage` and DMs stay with M1-49, because messaging is not in that world. | Brief "Tests"; D-W-100 differences in names and places, same behaviour. |
| D-M6-01-11 | 2026-10-16 | The generated clients follow the contract: the Dart and Python clients type the four login operations with the `200 ConsoleSession` of the console form, because openapi-generator takes the lowest success code. `packages/auth` therefore sends the app form of `completeOidcLogin`, `loginWithPassword`, `loginWithTrustedHeader` and `completePasskeyLogin` through the same Dio, interceptors and serializers itself and reads the `201 TokenResponse` (`_appLogin`); the console (M6-02) uses the generated methods. | Without it the apps would decode their token response as a console session and every app login would fail; the generator pipeline and the contract stay unchanged. |

Migration `00045` of M6-01 is the next free number. | Both squashes equal their ticket ranges file by file; no behaviour of either ticket changes. |
| INT-W33/2 | 2026-10-16 | The request validator checks a header parameter that arrives with only an empty value against the enum of its schema: when the enum does not list `""`, the answer is 400 `validation_failed` with `#header/<name>` `invalid_enum`, the same as for any other value outside the enum. | M6-01 added the optional header `X-Console-Request` (enum `"1"`) to seven operations, and G7 of M1-71 sends `X-Console-Request:` with an empty value under a bearer token: the validator library treats an empty header as absent and the console guard skips requests with `Authorization`, so the request got 204 instead of a 4xx (reproduced with the seed of the failing run). A value such as `"2"` was already refused; the empty value now is too. Test: `TestValidate_EmptyEnumHeaderRejected`. |

Wording only. |

Under a host load above 30 the readiness waits of `internal/wire` and the 10 minute package limit of a few packages fail inside `make check` as in INT-W32/2. G7 of the base can also fail for `addGroupMember` when Schemathesis sends a malformed `Host` header that `net/http` answers with a plain text 400 before any handler; this reproduces on `int/m1-wave32` with the same seed and is left to the owner of M1-71. | None of them comes from the integration. |

## M8-10 Login and API client of the import tool

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M8-10-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave32` at `3e9983c`, branch `feat/M8-10-import-login-client` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the earlier tickets. The spec parts of `main` the brief builds on (08 6.4, the `cli` paragraph of 22 8, the rows `login` and `logout` of 22 10.1, rule 5 of 22 10.2 and the row of 02 3.3) come in their own commit first, in English inside the German chapters of the base; D-W-100, D-W-120 and D-W-124 are planning rulings on `main` and are not copied. The brief's `{{DONE_PATH}}` and local merge are not used. | The task names the base, the lane and "bring exactly that change"; precedent D-M8-01-0. |
| D-M8-10-1 | 2026-10-16 | The API client is a separate generated package `api/restclient` from the whole `docs/api/openapi.yaml` (`generate: {client: true, models: true}`, `skip-prune`, no embedded spec, oapi-codegen v2.8.0 of `go.mod`). oapi-codegen writes 6.8 MB; `api/restclient/gen` cuts it at top-level declarations into `restclient.gen.go` and `restclient_02.gen.go` to `restclient_07.gen.go`, each below `repo.max_file_kb`, with the splitting code shared with `api/routes/gen` in `api/internal/gosplit`. oapi-codegen generated every part of the spec. | `api/rest` holds only the enabled operations and cannot serve as a client of all operations (brief note 1); one file would fail `check-added-large-files` (precedent INT-W14/4, INT-W17/4). |
| D-M8-10-2 | 2026-10-16 | Session file: JSON `{version: 1, server_url, device_id, user_id, dpop_private_jwk, access_token, access_expires_at, refresh_token, refresh_expires_at}` at `<os.UserConfigDir()>/bothy/import-session.json` or `--session-file`; mode `0600` in a directory with mode `0700`, created so; a file or directory with any group or other permission bit, or a file that is not regular (a symbolic link), is refused (`ErrInsecureSessionFile`). On Windows the mode check is skipped (the file lives in the user profile). Atomic store through a temporary file, `fsync`, rename and `fsync` of the directory. `access_expires_at` is the client's clock plus `expires_in`. | Brief note 2, requirement 14; the tool knows no server key and trusts only the stored expiry and a `401`. |
| D-M8-10-3 | 2026-10-16 | Lock: every refresh takes the exclusive advisory lock of `<file>.lock` (`flock`, `LockFileEx` through `kernel32` with the standard library on Windows), blocks until it holds it, reads the file again and uses tokens another process already rotated; `logout` holds the lock across `logout` and the deletion and keeps the lock file. A session in the file with another device (a new login) ends the old client with `ErrSessionRevoked`. | 08 7.4: a reuse would revoke the session; a lock file that survives keeps waiters on one inode (brief note 5). |
| D-M8-10-4 | 2026-10-16 | Server URL: an origin (`https`, or `http` only for `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, `localhost`), at most a path `/`, no query, fragment or user info. The egress client has exactly one rule, the server host and port with `allow_private`, and `RedirectNever`; a `3xx` ends the call with `ErrRedirect`. | Requirement 15: the owner names the host, which may be private; `allow_private` would also allow `http`, so the URL check limits it to loopback first. |
| D-M8-10-5 | 2026-10-16 | New tool key `import.login_poll_interval` (default `5s`) in 22 19 and the configuration reference; `import.http_timeout` also bounds each call to Bothy, and `egress.max_response_bytes` bounds its answers. `DefaultLimits` holds the defaults until M8-04 loads the tool configuration; `bothy import login` has no `--config` until then. | Brief scope "Change"; the loader is M8-04's (brief "Out of scope"). |
| D-M8-10-6 | 2026-10-16 | Default device name `bothy import on <host name>`, `bothy import` without a host name or when the name fails the display text rule; it is not cut, because the server has no device name limit (`device.New` checks only the display text rule). | Brief note 5 asks for a cut to the device name limit; none exists on the base. |
| D-M8-10-7 | 2026-10-16 | The DPoP key of the tool is Ed25519 (`alg` `EdDSA`), stored as an RFC 8037 private JWK; proofs carry a random 128-bit `jti`. | 08 7.3 allows `ES256` and `EdDSA`; the standard library signs Ed25519 deterministically without a curve choice. |
| D-M8-10-8 | 2026-10-16 | `tools/archgen` lets the component `cmd` depend on `api` (`.go-arch-lint.yml` regenerated). The transport goes into the generated client as `restclient.DoerFunc(t.RoundTrip)`, a function value of the hand-written type `api/restclient/doer.go`, because the deep scan of go-arch-lint counts a value of a `cmd` type handed into `api` as a dependency of `api` on `cmd` (precedent M1-34/12); it sends over `egress.Client.Do`, so no `http.Client` or transport is built outside `platform/egress` (`bothy-go-egress-only`). `.golangci.yml` excludes `bodyclose` and `noctx` for the generated client files: the client returns every response to its caller and sets the context with `req.WithContext`. `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` outside `/api/v1/auth/` carry one UUIDv7 `Idempotency-Key` per call when the caller set none; the server ignores the header on operations that do not declare it. | The tool talks to the server only through the generated client (brief requirement 17); the rule set had no edge from `cmd` to `api`. |
| D-M8-10-9 | 2026-10-16 | Exit codes of `bothy import login` and `logout`: `2` for an invalid URL, an existing session for the server, no session, a revoked session and an invalid or exposed session file; `1` for an expired, rejected or interrupted pairing and for network and server errors. `logout` without a session is `2` with the login hint. | 22 10.3 and brief note 2 ("missing file means not logged in, exit 2"). |

## M6-02 Console app and serving by the server

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-02-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave33` at `2f845d8`, branch `feat/M6-02-console-app` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it (the brief's landing steps follow the task). Section 5.6 of chapter 01 (screens E1 to E7) and the rule R-PR-14, which main holds since its baseline and this base lacks, enter first as their own commit. Names that differ on this base and are followed: the chapters are `01-produkt.md`, `02-architektur.md`, `08-identitaet.md`, `15-client-architektur.md`, `16-betrieb.md`, `18-release.md`, `19-sicherheit.md`; `console.go` of `httpserver` already holds the CSRF guard of M6-01, so the serving is `console_assets.go`; the configuration key lives in chapter 16 beside the other process keys. | The task names the base and the lane; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M6-02-1 | 2026-10-16 | Routes: `/console/login`, `/console/login/callback`, `/console/step-up`, `/console/step-up/callback`, `/console/logged-out` and one path per navigation entry (`live`, `stats`, `hosts`, `people`, `groups`, `devices`, `agents`, `apps`, `audit`, `import`, `instance`, `status`). The enum `ConsolePage` is the whole router configuration (an own `RouterDelegate` over the state of the session instead of `go_router`, which the workspace does not carry); `routes_test.dart` enumerates it and fails on `channel`, `thread`, `message`, `space`, `/t/`, `/c/`, `/s/`. The enum value of the import page is `dataImport`, because tools/arch-check reads a line that starts with `import` as a directive. URLs use the path strategy below `<base href="/console/">`, so the OIDC callbacks reach the app. | Brief note 1; ADR 0028 "Confirmation"; no new dependency for a table of seventeen paths. |
| D-M6-02-2 | 2026-10-16 | HTTP: `createConsoleApiClient` builds the generated client over the browser adapter of Dio (cookies of the same origin go along), adds `X-Console-Request: 1` to every method except `GET`, `HEAD` and `OPTIONS`, removes `Authorization` and `DPoP` from every request and retries no read. A `401` during a session leads to E7 with the problem code as reason, `step_up_required` back to the step-up; a `401` of the start or of a login form changes no state. Passkey: `navigator.credentials.get` with `userVerification: required` whatever the options say; the JSON conversion is plain Dart (`webauthn_json.dart`), the browser part is `browser_web.dart`. OIDC: the `client_verifier` lives in `sessionStorage` under `bothy.console.oidc.<login|step_up>.<login_attempt_id>`, written right before the tab leaves and deleted on every callback, successful or not. A passkey login without login name sends no `login_name` (discoverable credentials, as the contract allows). The console uses the generated login methods with `client: console` and `PkcePair` of `auth`, no other part of `auth`. | Brief note 2; M6-01 Design note 1 and D-M6-01-11. |
| D-M6-02-3 | 2026-10-16 | Web build: `flutter build web --release --base-href /console/ --no-web-resources-cdn` (JavaScript build, CanvasKit from the build output). The own `web/flutter_bootstrap.js` calls `_flutter.loader.load({})` without `serviceWorkerSettings`, so no service worker is registered; Flutter still writes `flutter_service_worker.js`, which nothing loads. `runConsole` calls `SemanticsBinding.instance.ensureSemantics()` before `runApp`, and the app sets the `lang` attribute of the document to the app language; `index.html` starts with `lang="de"`, the default locale. The E7 sentence about deleted device data is left out: the console keeps no data in the browser. | Brief note 3; a cached console could mismatch the API. |
| D-M6-02-4 | 2026-10-16 | Serving: `console.assets_dir` (empty default: no console, `404` problem under `/console/`; a set directory without a regular `index.html` ends the start). `httpserver.ConsoleAssets` opens it as an `os.Root` and mounts `GET /console/` and `GET /console` (301 to `/console/`) on the public mux; exact files get their type from a fixed table (no MIME database in the image), every other path below `/console/` gets `index.html`; encoded slashes or backslashes, backslashes, NUL and dot segments are refused before any file call, and a name the root refuses (a symbolic link out of the directory) is `404`, never `index.html`. `index.html` has `Cache-Control: no-store`, a name with a hash of at least eight hex digits between dots `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, every other file `no-cache`; Flutter fingerprints no file today, so in practice all assets are revalidated. Revalidation goes by a strong content tag (`ETag`, the first 16 bytes of SHA-256, hashed once per file version) and never by `Last-Modified`: a rollback to an image built earlier serves older files, which a modification time would answer with `304` (finding of the code review). The console routes run steps 1 to 4 of the chain (SEC-HDR-1, trace, client IP, setup gate, 404 and 405 problems) but no per-route stage: they are no operation of the contract, have no action and no rate-limit class, and a page load of a few dozen assets must not draw on `rl.public_auth`. The image copies `build/web` to `/usr/share/bothy/console`, owned by root and read-only, and sets `BOTHY_CONSOLE__ASSETS_DIR` to it. | Brief note 4; a static file needs no authorization, and the core contract has no class for it. |
| D-M6-02-5 | 2026-10-16 | Headers of the console now: SEC-HDR-1 from the chain, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and the `Permissions-Policy` of SEC-HDR-3. The CSP and `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp` come with M6-03; SEC-HDR-3 is complete only after M6-03. | Brief note 4. |
| D-M6-02-6 | 2026-10-16 | Version: `apps/console/tool/version_json.sh` writes `version.json` after the build with `spec_version` (info.version of `docs/api/openapi.yaml`, from which the `api_client` is generated) and `server_version` (`services/server/VERSION`); the Dockerfile runs it, and `TestConsoleVersionJSON` runs the same script and holds the served `spec_version` equal to `routes.SpecVersion`, which `getDiscovery` reports. The console reads `/console/version.json` at start and compares its `spec_version` with `Discovery.spec_version`; a missing or different value shows "The console was updated" with only a reload button, and no further request runs. | Brief note 5. |
| D-M6-02-7 | 2026-10-16 | Deep links: `Discovery` names no URL scheme of the apps, so a thread in the console shows its ID with a copy action and the note to open it in the app (`ThreadLink`); there is no "Open in the app" button until discovery carries a scheme. | Brief note 1 ("record which"). |
| D-M6-02-8 | 2026-10-16 | Builder: no Flutter image exists for the pinned 3.47.7, so the console stage runs on the pinned builder image of the server (`golang:1.27.2-trixie` by digest), installs `unzip` and `xz-utils`, clones Flutter at the tag with `--depth 1` and checks the revision `abaf9c5`, and builds with `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile` at the workspace root. `make image-test` asserts the console in the exported file system (index.html with the base href, `main.dart.js`, `flutter_bootstrap.js`, CanvasKit, `version.json` with the server's API version, root ownership) and the environment variable; starting the server in the image needs a database and is not part of the image test, the answer of `/console/` itself is `TestConsoleServing`. | Brief "Change" (builder pinned by digest, SEC-SC-4); the image test has no database. |
| D-M6-02-9 | 2026-10-16 | Tests of the web-only adapters (`test/web/`, `@TestOn('browser')`) run with `flutter test --platform chrome test/web`; `make test` runs `flutter test` on the VM, which skips them. `test/web/flutter_test_config.dart` replaces the golden setup of `test/`, which reads fonts through `dart:io`. | Brief "Tests"; the gate runs on the VM. |

## M4-15 Sync engine: outbox

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-15-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave27` at `3d4f0f3`, branch `feat/M4-15-sync-outbox` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-115 and D-W-116 are on the base), so nothing is ported. The brief's `15-client-architecture.md` and `04-sync-protocol.md` are `15-client-architektur.md` and `04-sync-protokoll.md` on the base; the lines this ticket adds to 15 7.5 are English. The base has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section of the ticket. Differences to the brief's names: the display view is `OutboxEntryView` (`OutboxEntry` is the stored model of `local_store`), the classification result is `Classification` with `OutcomeClass` `confirmed`, `retry`, `failed`, `conflict`, the backoff function is `fullJitterBackoff` (M4-13 is not on the base). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-15-1 | 2026-10-16 | Outcome classes: a return of the executor is `confirmed` (`2xx`, `200` with `Idempotent-Replay: true`, `204`). Network errors, timeouts, a cancelled request, `5xx` other than `503 setup_required`, `429` and `409 request_in_progress` are `retry` with the same ID; `429` and `503` wait `Retry-After` when present, otherwise the full-jitter backoff. `412` is `conflict`: `failed` with the problem's `current` revision kept in `conflict_current`. Every other status (`400`, `401`, `403`, `404`, other `409`, `410`, `413`, `415`, `422` including `idempotency_key_reuse`, which names a programming error, `428`, `503 setup_required`) is final with the problem `code` and `reason`. A `DioException` of type `unknown` with a `2xx` response (a body the client cannot decode) is `confirmed`, since the server stored the write; one with a transport error inside is `retry`; any other error of an executor is final as `executor_error`, so a bug never loops. | Design note 2 of the brief; 04 5, 04 13.2 items 4 and 5, 04 13.3. |
| D-M4-15-2 | 2026-10-16 | Executor registry: `OutboxExecutors.register(kind, executor, {lane, followUpEdit, followUpRemove})` is the only coupling to `api_client`; executors get the entry and an `ApiCallContext` whose `extra` carries the entry ID as `Idempotency-Key`. A kind without an executor fails the entry with `no_executor`. `MessageOps` and `PersonalOps` register the ten operations of the brief. | Design note 3 of the brief: later tickets add their writes without changing the outbox. |
| D-M4-15-3 | 2026-10-16 | One worker per FIFO lane; the lane is the thread (`thread:<id>`), or what the executor names for writes outside a thread (`setting:<key>` for own settings, `message:<id>` for reactions without a thread, `upload:<id>` for M4-22), else `global`. Lanes run in parallel; the HTTP client's connection limit per host bounds them, and the outbox has no number of its own. Only the oldest entry that is not final in a lane may go; a failed entry is skipped (it waits for the person), a dependency that is not confirmed blocks the lane. | Design note 4 of the brief; 04 13.2 item 2. |
| D-M4-15-4 | 2026-10-16 | Confirmation by echo: `confirmByEcho(clientMsgId, result:)` deletes the matching entry also while its request is in flight; the late response then finds no row and changes nothing (no second display entry, no confirmation hook). An unknown ID returns false. | Design note 5 of the brief; 04 16.2, the echo can come first. |
| D-M4-15-5 | 2026-10-16 | "Not sent yet" is `attempts == 0`. Only such an entry is edited or removed in place. An attempted entry (`sending`, `waiting`, or `pending` again after a restart) keeps its ID and payload; an edit queues a follow-up `updateMessage` and a removal a follow-up `deleteMessage` behind the newest entry of its follow-up chain, and a second edit changes a follow-up that has not been sent yet in place. An edit holds the changed members and is merged into the stored payload, so `message_id` and the slots the outbox fills stay. A failed entry edited or sent again becomes a new entry with a new ID; after a `412` the new entry carries the `version` of `conflict_current` as `If-Match`. All changes by the person and every step of the sender run under one lock, so an edit never changes a payload that is being sent. | Design note 6 of the brief; 04 13.3 and 04 16.2 (a sent entry may be stored on the server). |
| D-M4-15-6 | 2026-10-16 | `depends_on` holds the IDs an entry waits for, comma-separated (a message waits for two uploads; UUIDs contain no comma); the outbox selects the lanes itself and does not use `OutboxDao.nextForThread`, which reads the column as one ID. A confirmed entry leaves the table, so a missing dependency counts as confirmed; discarding an entry first fills the members its dependents hold as null from its own non-null members (a discarded follow-up edit hands the server's `message_id` to the delete behind it) and fails them with `dependency_discarded`, and replacing a failed entry with a new ID (`retry`, `edit`) re-points every entry that waits for it to the replacement through the new `OutboxDao.updateDependsOn`, so nothing is sent without what it needs. Within a lane a dependent never goes before its parent, also when a clock step gave it the older `created_at`. On confirmation the executor's `result` (for a message `message_id` and `version`) fills the payload members a dependent holds as null; a follow-up gets the server's ID of the message it edits this way. | The schema of M4-12 has one `depends_on` column and no update of it; the brief needs several dependencies and follow-ups of entries whose server ID is not known yet. |
| D-M4-15-7 | 2026-10-16 | "Sending delayed": an entry in `waiting` reports `delayed` once `client.outbox_stuck_after` has passed since it was written (`created_at`), not since its last state change; `watchThread` publishes again at that moment from the injected timer, without a database change. | 15 7.5; a backoff resets the state time on every attempt, so the state time would never reach the limit while the person waits. |
| D-M4-15-8 | 2026-10-16 | `packages/local_store` gains one method, `OutboxDao.updateDependsOn(id, dependsOn)`, with its test; no column and no migration change. Closing the outbox waits for the sender's running steps and leaves requests in flight alone, their entries stay `sending` and the next start sends them again with the same ID. A TLS error (`HandshakeException`, any `IOException`) is a transport error and retried. | Findings of the code review: without re-pointing, a message whose upload was sent again with a new ID would go out before the new upload is complete (15 7.5); the brief allows `local_store` changes where the outbox needs a DAO method the base lacks. |
| D-M4-15-9 | 2026-10-16 | The sender picks and claims the head of every idle lane in one section under the lock, on fresh data, so a change by the person or the outcome of another request never slips between the choice and the claim (dependencies and `next_attempt_at` hold). `start()` marks the outbox started before the first reset of `sending` entries and schedules only after it, so a `pause()` at any time holds. An edit of a failed entry drops the never sent follow-up edits behind it (they carry older texts); the entries behind them wait for the new entry. Discarding an entry fails its whole chain, not only the direct dependents, so no entry waits for a failed one and blocks its lane. Every dependency the outbox writes itself (the replacement of `retry` and `edit`, a follow-up of `edit` and `remove`, the bridge over a dropped follow-up) skips an entry that failed with its chain (`dependency_discarded`) and waits for what that entry waited for instead; an entry that failed for its own reason stays a dependency until it is retried or discarded; dependencies named in `enqueue` stay, so a message keeps waiting for its failed upload until the person retries or discards it. Reaction emoji are percent-encoded before they go into the path. | Findings of the red team: a stale snapshot sent a message before its new upload and ignored a `Retry-After`; a pause during the first start was lost; an older follow-up overwrote the newest edit; a discarded create left its delete blocking the thread, and a retried delete waited for its chain-failed edit; `#` and `?` emoji ended the path. |
| D-M4-15-10 | 2026-10-16 | One resolver replaces the case patches of the dependency rule (`lib/src/outbox/dependencies.dart`, owner decision 2026-10-16). It reads every stored entry, final ones included, through the new `OutboxDao.allEntries` (the earlier lookups saw only threads with an open entry). Every row that leaves the table goes through it and re-points every entry that names it: a confirmed entry is dropped from the lists, a retried or edited one becomes its replacement, a dropped follow-up becomes what it waited for, a discarded one is dropped after its chain failed with `dependency_discarded`. So a stored `depends_on` names only stored entries, and a missing ID no longer counts as confirmed (this replaces that sentence of D-M4-15-6): a dependency gone without a recorded confirmation, checked again before every claim, fails the entry and its chain with `dependency_discarded`. `enqueue` resolves the IDs it is given through the replacements and confirmations of the running outbox; an ID gone before the outbox was created is unknown and fails the entry the same way, and sending it again sends it without that dependency. The replacement of `retry` and `edit`, the follow-ups of `edit` and `remove` and the bridge over a dropped follow-up resolve their dependencies with the rule of D-M4-15-9 (a chain-failed parent is replaced by what it waited for, a discarded one is dropped). The lane order stays FIFO except that an entry goes no later than the oldest entry that waits for it, also through other lanes, and a parent before its dependent; a replacement at the end of a lane otherwise waited for good behind an entry that waited for it through another lane. `test/outbox/dependency_model_test.dart`, a seeded randomized model test (enqueue with dependencies, success, own failure, retry class answers, held answers, echo, retry, edit, remove, discard, time and restarts across threads and upload lanes), checks after every step that nothing is sent before what it depends on, that no open entry waits for a gone ID that was not confirmed, and that the outbox empties once every failed entry is retried or discarded; it was red on `2e616d7e` (seed 1). | Owner decision 2026-10-16: five reproduced Majors of one class (an entry sent before its dependency or waiting for good after failures, retries, edits, discards or removes); fix the class at one place and prove it with an invariant test. |

## Integration int/m1-wave35

M4-15 conflicts: the root changelog and this file as unions; `packages/sync_engine/README.md` as a union of the sections of M4-13, M4-14 and M4-15 with one status line; the library file `sync_engine.dart` with both doc paragraphs and the union of the exports in alphabetical order. | Append-only files and registries are unions; no ticket behaviour changes. |

Under a host load above 40 the readiness waits of `internal/wire` fail inside the package run with `-race`, and with them the Go coverage total of `services/server`; the base `53fc4e0` fails the same tests in the same way under the same load, and the failing tests pass when run alone. | Not findings of the integration: M4-15 changes no Go code, and M6-02 adds only the console mount, which mounts nothing with an empty `console.assets_dir`. |

## M4-17 Sync engine: fake server and composition root

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-17-0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave35` at `4773519`, branch `feat/M4-17-fake-server` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100 and D-W-114 are on the base), so nothing is ported; the API documents of the base hold every operation of Design note 3. The brief's `15-client-architecture.md` and `04-sync-protocol.md` are `15-client-architektur.md` and `04-sync-protokoll.md` on the base; the lines this ticket adds there are English. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the local store is `LocalDatabase`; the REST client is `AuthSession.api`, which the session already builds with its interceptors, so `SyncEngine.create` does not build a second one; `create` also needs `client` (`GatewayClientInfo` for `identify`) and takes `ids`, `draftEditing`, `keepConnectedInBackground`, `allowPlainHttpForTests` and `logHook`; `focus` is an `EngineFocus` over the `FocusManager`; the engine exposes `state` and `states`. Fault names: `delay(duration, operationId:)`, `revoke(deviceId:)`, plus `holdDispatches` and `releaseDispatches`; a lost network is `FakeServer.disconnect`. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-17-1 | 2026-10-17 | One composition root: `SyncEngine.create` builds the parts of M4-13 to M4-16 and connects every hook (echo to `Outbox.confirmByEcho` with `message_id` and `version`; the catch-up's live signal to `Outbox.start`; every disconnect to `Outbox.pause`; `run.updated` to `RunStore.onRunUpdated`; `run.state_changed` to an end state to `RunStore.onRunEnded`; focused runs into the gateway focus through `EngineFocus.focusRun`). The run end is read by a listener that wraps the catch-up listener after it applied the frame, so the dispatcher of M4-14 stays unchanged. | Brief Design note 2: the desktop and the mobile app cannot wire the hooks differently. |
| D-M4-17-2 | 2026-10-17 | The fake's REST side routes by the paths of `openapi.yaml` (a literal segment before a parameter) and keeps a handler registry: `FakeServer.handle(operationId, handler)`; this ticket's handlers register from one file per group under `lib/src/testing/rest/`; an operation without a handler answers `501` and is recorded. | Later tickets add operations without editing this file (Design note 3). |
| D-M4-17-3 | 2026-10-17 | Faults are deterministic: `FaultPlan` is a queue of REST rules matched in order and used up per request (applied after the token and proof checks, so a nonce retry does not eat a fault), and gateway faults act at once or after a fixed delay; IDs of the world are counters. | A failing case must repeat on every run (Design note 4, 15 20 rationale). |
| D-M4-17-4 | 2026-10-17 | Contract checks in the fake with an own validator (`ContractSchemas`) for the subset the documents use, plus `allOf` and the bounds `minimum`, `maximum`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, `minItems`, `maxItems` and `pattern`; no JSON Schema package is in the lock file, so no dependency was added. Invalid request bodies answer `400 validation_failed` with the pointers and are recorded; a response the fake would send against the contract becomes a recorded `500`; a frame of the fake against the contract throws; an invalid client frame closes with `4002`. Every scenario fails on a recorded violation. | The fake can never drift from the contract silently (Design note 5). |
| D-M4-17-5 | 2026-10-17 | The scenarios of 04 16 are functions over the interface `ScenarioClient` (send, offline and online, local messages, outbox, channels, notices, cursor, focus, run document, run state) and a running `FakeServer`; they throw `ScenarioFailure` instead of using `package:test`, so the library has no test framework as a runtime dependency. `test/scenarios/` implements the interface over `SyncEngine`; M4-18 implements it over the app. | One set of flows for the headless and the app tests (Design note 7, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-17-6 | 2026-10-17 | `SyncEngine` accepts only an `https` base URL (gateway `wss`); `allowPlainHttpForTests: true` admits `http` (gateway `ws`) for the fake and the app's test entry point. `tools/arch-check` gains rule 10 as a general rule: a library named `testing.dart` of any member and its `lib/src/testing/` folder are imported or exported only from `test/` and `integration_test/` folders, also relatively inside the own member; 15 3.1 lists it. | DPoP and the device binding assume a protected transport (15 8); test support never reaches a production build (Design note 8). |
| D-M4-17-7 | 2026-10-17 | The gateway's `hello` carries the same nonce as the REST `DPoP-Nonce` and the gateway accepts the current and the previous one, as REST does. | 08 7.3 makes nonces stateless for every process; the client keeps one nonce store, so a gateway-only nonce made the next REST call fail with `use_dpop_nonce` (found by the first scenario run). |
| D-M4-17-8 | 2026-10-17 | `crypto` and `pointycastle` move from `dev_dependencies` to `dependencies` of `packages/sync_engine`, and `yaml` (already in the lock file) is added there: `lib/testing.dart` lives under `lib/` and needs them; the production library does not import them. | Brief Scope (`pubspec.yaml`: the dependencies the fake needs); their licenses (BSD-3-Clause, MIT) are on the allowed list. |
| D-M4-17-9 | 2026-10-17 | `FakeWorld.append` takes `resource.version` from the event catalogue of `domain`: a type the catalogue marks without it never carries one (reactions do not raise the message version, `read_state.updated` and `settings.updated` carry the version only in the payload), and a type that needs it fails the write; the contract test checks every logged event against the catalogue. | 04 9.3; found by the code review: a foreign version made the fake answer `412` to an edit after a reaction. |
| D-M4-17-10 | 2026-10-17 | Fixes after the red team: the outbox's `onConfirmed` stores the `Message` a confirmed `createMessage` or `updateMessage` returned through the new `CatchUpListener.storeConfirmedMessage` (version rule, removed targets skipped), so a sent message never disappears between its response and its echo; after `Resynced` the engine ends the documents of focused runs that the local run summaries list as ended or no longer hold; the fake computes `unread_threads` and the read state's `mention_count` (messages of others after the read position, not deleted). | 04 13.2 item 6, 04 7.3 with 15 7.4, 04 14; red team of M4-17. |

## M2-42 Host service: installation, state, doctor and pair

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-42-1 | 2026-10-17 | `pair` and `doctor` run with the identity of the service user: directly when the caller owns the state directory; through the admin endpoint of the running service when an administrator calls them; with the service stopped, on Linux and macOS in a child process with the credentials of the service user, on Windows `doctor` starts the service once with the start argument `--doctor-once` (one full run, its answer on the admin endpoint, exit `0`, no server, no session) and `pair` requires the running service and otherwise exits `2` naming `bothy-host restart`. Every command that needs administrator rights and lacks them exits `2` with `bothy-host <command>: administrator rights required`. | Keys under DPAPI and files owned by the service user can only be written in its context, and places for all must be resolved by the service user (24 2.2 point 6). |
| D-M2-42-2 | 2026-10-17 | Commands `install [--session-user <name>] [--drive-root <drive>] [--drive-root-confirm yes\|no] [--config <path>]`, `session-user add\|remove <name>`, `restart`, `pair --server <url> --code <code> [--ca-file <pem>]`, `doctor`, `run`, `pty-helper --session-user <name>`; exit codes `0`, `1` (a failed check, a runtime failure, a bound name), `2` (usage or environment), `78` (refused start, `EX_CONFIG`). The drive root prompt reads the terminal; the flag answers it, and without both the answer is no. Local output of `doctor`: one line per entry in the order HD1 to HD11 (`HD<n> ok`, `WARN <code>`, `FAIL <code>` with `session_user=`, `user=`, `drive=`, `item=`), then `doctor: <failed> failed, <warnings> warnings` counting checks, then `declaration: <value>`. | 24 2.2 "Output on the host" names the content, not the form; `78` is what no service definition restarts. |
| D-M2-42-3 | 2026-10-17 | One YAML configuration file nested like the key names, decoded strictly with `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3` (unknown and duplicate keys and wrong types name the key); durations in the grammar of the server. Paths: Linux `/etc/bothy-host/config.yaml`, macOS `/Library/Application Support/bothy-host/config.yaml`, Windows `%ProgramFiles%\bothy-host\config.yaml`. New key `host_service.auth_status_timeout` (default `30s`) bounds `claude auth status` and the login shell probe. An invalid file makes `run` log `config_invalid` with the key and exit `78`; the other commands exit `2`. | 24 and 16 named keys but no file; the bound of the probe was missing; a service manager must not loop on a broken file. |
| D-M2-42-4 | 2026-10-17 | The state directory also holds `pairing.json` (server URL, host ID, instance ID, fingerprint) and `declaration.json` (the last `llm_credentials` of `ready`), both without secrets, and on Linux and macOS `admin.sock`; every file is written atomically with mode `0600`, on Windows inheriting the protected DACL. | `run` needs the server and the declaration before a login; neither is a secret or a subscription login. |
| D-M2-42-5 | 2026-10-17 | No cgo on any platform. Every platform behaviour sits behind the interfaces of `internal/hostos`; an ACL entry that cannot be read, parsed or mapped counts as granting every right to every session user. The mode of a symbolic link is ignored; a link is checked by its owner and its folder. macOS ACLs are read from `/bin/ls -led`, because `golang.org/x/sys/unix` has no decoder for `ATTR_CMN_EXTENDED_SECURITY`. | One right too many only blocks, one too few opens a place; Linux and macOS do not use the mode of a link. |
| D-M2-42-6 | 2026-10-17 | The codes of `doctor` are the table "Codes of `doctor`" in 24 2.2. HD3 has no probe on Windows and passes without code. HD9 lists the homes of `root`, the service user, every former session user and every person account (Linux `UID_MIN` to `UID_MAX` of `/etc/login.defs` with the documented defaults, macOS `UniqueID` from 501, Windows every profile under `ProfileList`); all other system accounts are skipped. | Elevation on Windows needs administrator credentials and HD2 covers `Administrators`; system homes such as `/usr/sbin` or `/var/empty` are listable by design and hold no files of a person. |
| D-M2-42-7 | 2026-10-17 | Path resolution walks the path like `realpath`, records every folder of the result, every component as written (`..` kept) and every link with its folder; a missing path, a dangling link, a closed folder or more than 40 links is unresolvable. Identity is `dev:ino`, on Windows the volume serial number and the 128-bit file ID. | 24 2.2 point 6; a component that resolution skips is still a place a person could change. |
| D-M2-42-8 | 2026-10-17 | HD10 adds the code `helper_unreachable` (named with the session user) for a helper whose channel cannot be opened; a setuid or setgid bit on the binary is `helper_untrusted`; a `#!` script checks its interpreter, and for `/usr/bin/env` the first name the search list finds; a missing interpreter makes `host_service.claude_bin` count as empty. | An unreachable helper gives no result, and missing results must not pass (fail closed). |
| D-M2-42-9 | 2026-10-17 | `auth_status_unknown` (a value outside the six methods, no JSON, a timeout or an exit code other than `0` and `1`) counts like a finding; before any `ready` a finding is the warning `declaration_unknown`. | RK-14: an output that cannot be read fails closed; without a declaration no session can start, so a refusal would only hide the names. |
| D-M2-42-10 | 2026-10-17 | The chain files stay `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.mcp.json`, `.claude` and `.git`: the memory page (retrieved 2026-10-17) names CLAUDE.md, CLAUDE.local.md, `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, AGENTS.md and `.claude/AGENTS.md` above the working directory, all inside the list; the CLI reference confirms the JSON and the methods of `claude auth status`. | Brief design notes 9 a and 10. |
| D-M2-42-11 | 2026-10-17 | Peer check: the helper passes one end of a `socketpair` over the activated connection (`SCM_RIGHTS`) and speaks only after the first message of the service; the service compares uid and PID of that end with the session user and the main PID of the helper service; on Windows the pipe is created with `FILE_FLAG_FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE` and `PIPE_REJECT_REMOTE_CLIENTS`. The list of platforms with a proven peer check is empty; HD10 fails with `peer_check_unavailable` until a recorded platform run adds a platform; only the build of `make platform-test` measures it. | With socket activation the peer of a connection is the service manager; RK-23 allows no terminal without proof. |
| D-M2-42-12 | 2026-10-17 | The entry scan runs per session user in its helper after the full run; until its first scan ends the user fails HD11 with `entry_scan_running`, afterwards the previous finding holds until a new full scan ends; a finished scan sends a new report only after the start was not refused. | 24 2.2 "Check of the entries"; a refused start sends exactly one report. |
| D-M2-42-13 | 2026-10-17 | Installation: program folders `/opt/bothy-host/bin/`, `/Library/Application Support/bothy-host/bin/`, `%ProgramFiles%\bothy-host\`; Linux `useradd --system --user-group` for the service user and `useradd --create-home --user-group` per session user; macOS hidden users through `dscl` (service user from UniqueID 400, session users from 501, own primary group, not in `staff`) and `createhomedir`, the binary signed ad hoc with the hardened runtime unless it already carries the runtime flag; Windows the virtual account `NT SERVICE\bothy-host` and per session user a local account with a random password handed only to the service control manager, `SeServiceLogonRight`, `CreateProfile` and an ACL only for the user and the trusted owners. | 24 2.2 asks the build ticket for the way per platform; the platform suite proves it. |
| D-M2-42-14 | 2026-10-17 | Exit policy: systemd `Restart=on-failure` with `RestartPreventExitStatus=78`, no `NoNewPrivileges=` and no option implying it; launchd `KeepAlive` `{Crashed: true}` with `GOTRACEBACK=crash`; Windows failure actions on crashes only and a refused start stopped with the service-specific code `78`; the DACL of a helper service gives authenticated users only query rights. | Under no_new_privs `sudo -n true` fails and hides a sudo rule from HD3; a refused start must not loop. |
| D-M2-42-15 | 2026-10-17 | `run` reads the stored declaration, runs the full check, logs in through `Control` when paired, stores the declaration of `ready` and evaluates HD6 again; a "start refused" finding sends exactly one report, closes and exits `78`; unpaired or without login the stored declaration decides. `HostReady` gains the required member `llm_credentials`, and the server sends `ready` again on every change of the declaration. This ticket wires `NoControl`, which M2-43 replaces. | 24 2.2 "Message on a refused start"; the host service learns the declaration only from `ready`. |
| D-M2-42-16 | 2026-10-17 | `pair` accepts only `https://`, trusts the system roots plus `--ca-file`, sends only public keys and the lists after HD7, stores keys and pairing record only after a `201` whose fingerprint equals its own computation, prints the fingerprint and the host ID, and as an administrator restarts the service afterwards; problem answers print the code, the reason, each field error and for `401` the hint to ask for a new code. | 24 3.2; a stolen or wrong answer must not replace working keys. |
| D-M2-42-17 | 2026-10-17 | The admin endpoint answers the ops `doctor.run` (full run, report, the local output and the report entries) and `pair.run`; on Linux and macOS it is `admin.sock` in the state directory and answers only uid `0`, on Windows the pipe `bothy-host-admin` with a DACL for `SYSTEM` and `Administrators` answers only elevated clients; a client checks that the server process runs as the service user before it sends anything. | Design notes 1 and 15. |
| D-M2-42-18 | 2026-10-17 | The helper keeps its end of the passed descriptor open until the client closed the connection. | On macOS a descriptor in flight whose last reference the sender closes was lost in repeated runs (reproduced in `TestPeerCheckPasses`). |
| D-M2-42-19 | 2026-10-17 | Steering ruling 2026-10-15 (no cost): the ticket is built with unit and integration tests only; the platform suite never runs on the build machine, a workstation or shared infrastructure. It is the manual or scheduled workflow `host-platform.yml` on free GitHub-hosted runners (`ubuntu-latest` with systemd, `macos-latest`, `windows-latest`), which are throwaway machines; Windows 10 has no hosted runner and stays unmeasured. Until measured, the Windows values of 24 2.2 point 6 apply, the default of `host_service.entry_scan_timeout` stays `10m`, the platform suite is a required gate before M2-48, and the terminal stays off on every unproven platform (RK-23). | The owner's ruling; the brief asked to stop without machines. |
