| D-M2-42-21 | 2026-10-17 | The root `Makefile` runs `go vet` (typecheck, lint) and golangci-lint (lint) for `GO_CROSS_MODULES := services/host` with `GOOS` `linux`, `darwin` and `windows`. | Code for the other platforms is checked on every build host. |
| D-M2-42-22 | 2026-10-17 | Windows channels: clients open pipes with `SECURITY_SQOS_PRESENT` and `SECURITY_IDENTIFICATION`; the pipe DACLs add `OWNER RIGHTS` with only `FILE_CREATE_PIPE_INSTANCE`; a pipe server reads the token of its client by impersonating it at identification level, and a client compares the owner of the pipe (the account that created the server end) and `GetNamedPipeServerProcessId` with the session user and the main PID, because the token of a helper process gives the service account no access; the process DACL of a helper gives the service account and `OWNER RIGHTS` only `PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION`; `run` and `pty-helper` run under the service control manager from the first step. | Review of M2-42: without these the second connection, the peer check in both directions and the exit code `78` failed on Windows, and the owner of a helper pipe kept `WRITE_DAC`. |
| D-M2-42-23 | 2026-10-17 | HD4 on Windows also accepts `SYSTEM` or `Administrators` as owner of the state directory when its DACL holds only the three principals; an existing local account whose helper service exists keeps its password at a repeated `install` or `session-user add`. | Handing the folder to the virtual account needs a restore privilege; a password reset would lose the data DPAPI protects for the user. |
| D-M2-42-24 | 2026-10-17 | HD6 also reads the folder the login shell exports in `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (inside the helper only): a `.credentials.json` there is a finding, and `claude auth status` also runs with it. A `host_service.claude_bin` that fails HD10 counts as empty and never runs. An entry whose owner the entry scan cannot read counts as `foreign_entry`. The runner starts each program in its own process group, ends the group on the bound and stops waiting for the output shortly after. | Review of M2-42: a login under an exported folder went unseen, an untrusted program ran in every session user, the scan failed open, and a background job of a login shell stretched the bound. |
| D-M2-42-25 | 2026-10-17 | The host module carries `nosemgrep` markers for the rule `bothy-go-egress-only` at the Unix socket dialers of the local channel and at the HTTP client of `pair`, and for `bothy-go-no-model-credentials` in the fake `claude` of `testdata`. | The egress package belongs to the server module and cannot be imported; the dialers reach only Unix sockets on the host; `pair` calls only the https URL the operator gives, with the system roots plus `--ca-file`, no proxy and no redirect; the fake reads only its fixture folder. |
| D-M2-42-26 | 2026-10-17 | Every helper call has a bound: `check.run` four times `host_service.auth_status_timeout` plus a grace of the channel, `scan.run` `host_service.entry_scan_timeout` plus the grace; a call that runs out counts as `helper_unreachable` or `entry_scan_timeout` for that session user. A chain file that is a link counts as its target: the target, its folders and links and, for a folder, everything in it belong to no other session user; a dangling one fails. HD10 never opens a `host_service.claude_bin` that failed the trust rule, and `Head` reads only regular files. | Red team of M2-42: a session user could stop its helper and hold the start of the whole host, a link of root into a file of another person passed HD11 (TM-T29 (j)), and a FIFO at an untrusted `claude_bin` blocked the run. |

## M1-65 Gateway hub, delivery, catch-up and revocation

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-65/0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave31` at `f63a8aa`, branch `feat/M1-65-gateway-delivery` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. Brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; every core rule the brief cites (R-SY-02, R-SY-03, R-SY-04, R-SY-14 to R-SY-16, R-AZ-04, R-AR-17, R-RE-02) is already on the base, so no contract commit was brought in. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-65/1 | 2026-10-16 | Structure events (`channel.created`, `channel.updated`, `channel.archived`, `space.archived`, `thread.moved`) invalidate every cached delivery snapshot through a process-wide generation counter. | These events are rare, and a global generation is correct without knowing every affected principal (brief, "Permission cache in delivery mode"). |
| M1-65/2 | 2026-10-16 | A delivery snapshot is valid while its rights version is at least the highest rights version any event of the log has carried so far (and its generation is current and `valid_until` lies ahead), not the floor of the principal's last `access.changed` that the brief sketches. | A rights change without a difference of visible targets writes no `access.changed` (04 8.2), for example `token.revoke` of an agent whose grants stay; with a floor per principal the hub kept delivering to the revoked token until the next heartbeat (reproduced in `TestGateway_RevocationCloses4012/revokeToken`), against AZ-P9. Every later event carries the new version, so the next delivery check rebuilds the snapshot, the revoked credential answers `unauthenticated` and the hub closes with `4012` before anything is delivered. A rights change rebuilds every delivery snapshot once, as REST does per request (02 11.3: the cache is keyed by `permission_version`). |
| M1-65/3 | 2026-10-16 | A revocation that writes neither `access.changed` nor `session.revoked` and is followed by no event of the log closes the connection at its next heartbeat (`Revalidate`, M1-64/10) instead of at once; nothing is delivered to it meanwhile (M1-65/2). | The log is the only cross-process signal of the gateway (02 "Log tail per api process"); an extra signal for such revocations would be a change of 04 or 05, which this ticket may not make (P-11). |
| M1-65/4 | 2026-10-16 | AZ-P9 runs as an integration test with a second api process (`wire.Serve`) on the same database in the same test binary; the Owner revokes through the REST API of the first process. | 07 "Required tests"; two real processes with their own log tails and rights caches are the case the rule guards. |
| M1-65/5 | 2026-10-16 | The sequence diagram "Access removal during an open connection" runs without holding the server's writer: frames queued before `access.changed` are purged by `Purge` (unit tests `TestQueue_BoundedAndPurgeByScope`, `TestQueue_ControlFramesSurvivePurge`), and the integration test asserts that no event of the removed channel reaches the connection after `access.changed` while events of the other channel still do. | The in-process server offers no hook to stall its writer; the purge itself is a pure function of the queue. |
| M1-65/6 | 2026-10-16 | A delivery admission check that fails with an error other than `unauthenticated` (database briefly unavailable while a snapshot is rebuilt) closes that connection with `4000`, which stays resumable (M1-64/3); the hub does not skip the event. | Skipping it would let the next frame with a seq (a later dispatch or a live `cursor`) move the client's cursor past the event, which then is never delivered, against at-least-once (04 9 item 1); the client resumes with its own cursor and the catch-up runner retries its reads with backoff. Found by the code review, reproduced in `TestHub_AdmitErrorClosesResumable`. |
| M1-65/7 | 2026-10-16 | The flush of the live buffer after `catchup.complete` waits for queue space like the catch-up pages; only a live buffer above `sync.outbound_queue_max` closes with `4014`. | 04 7.2 step 5 bounds the live buffer, not the moment after a catch-up that filled the queue; without waiting a single buffered event closed a client that caught up more than the queue holds. Found by the code review, reproduced in `TestCatchUp_FlushWaitsForQueueSpace`. |
| M1-65/8 | 2026-10-16 | The catch-up runner reads its pages in filter mode with a request scope per page (the rights version is read once per page), not in delivery mode; only the live path and the flush of the live buffer use delivery mode. | The events up to H can lie ahead of the log tail of this process, whose `Observe` has not yet seen a revocation committed before them, so a delivery snapshot could admit an event of a revoked target (red team round 1, blocker 1; AZ-P9). The flushed events came through the tail and passed `Observe` first. Test `TestCatchUp_PagesReadCurrentRights`. |
| M1-65/9 | 2026-10-16 | `sync.Admit` checks an own personal event against the credential with `gateway.connect` on the instance; an `unauthenticated` answer is its error (the hub closes with `4012`), any other answer leaves the event admitted. | A personal event needs no receive action, so a revoked API token without `access.changed` kept receiving its own events until the heartbeat (red team round 1, blocker 2). REST `listEvents` is unchanged. Test `TestAdmit_SingleRuleForGateway`. |
| M1-65/10 | 2026-10-16 | A target that `access.changed` adds is taken out of the targets a connection filters while it catches up. Replaced by M1-65/15 (removals bound to the events before them); the test still holds. | A channel removed and granted again during one catch-up stayed filtered, and the next frame moved the cursor past its events (red team round 1, major 1). Test `TestCatchUp_RegrantedTargetDelivered`. |
| M1-65/11 | 2026-10-16 | The catch-up runner and the flush queue only while the outbound queue holds fewer than `sync.outbound_queue_max` minus `domain.ControlReserve` (4) frames; control frames of the client's ops (`heartbeat_ack`, `reauth_required`, `error`) use the reserve. The live path keeps the full bound. | Otherwise a heartbeat during a long catch-up on a slow link overflowed the queue the runner kept full and closed with `4014` (red team round 1, major 2). Tests `TestQueue_RoomLeavesControlReserve`, `TestCatchUp_LeavesControlReserve`. |
| M1-65/12 | 2026-10-17 | A context that outlives the request or op it comes from drops its request memo (`authorize.WithoutRequestScope`): the WebSocket handler for the connection and `Hub.Attach` for the catch-up runner. Ops and catch-up pages then get a fresh memo each. | `WithRequestScope` keeps a memo that is already there, so the per-page scope of M1-65/8 inherited the memo of the identify op and the HTTP upgrade request, and a token revoked during catch-up received every page until the heartbeat (delta red team, blocker). Tests `TestGateway_TokenRevokedDuringCatchUpCloses4012` (real database), `TestCatchUp_PagesReadCurrentRights`, `TestWithoutRequestScope`. |
| M1-65/13 | 2026-10-17 | `channel.created` is a candidate of every connection, and an event of a channel admitted for a connection (live, flush or catch-up page) adds its channel and space to the hub indices of that connection. | The default operator agent of a space reads every inheriting channel of the space without a rights change and, in a space without a channel, without a visible target there, so the indices from connection setup never listed the new channel and its events missed the live connection while cursor frames moved past them (red team round 3, major). Test `TestGateway_SpaceDefaultOperatorGetsNewChannelLive`. |
| M1-65/14 | 2026-10-17 | The log tail sink observes the committed rights version, read once per batch after the batch, before it delivers the batch. | An event carries the rights version its transaction read before commit; a rights change without an event (API token revocation) committing in between left the delivery snapshot valid, and the message committed after the revocation reached the revoked token (red team round 3, major). Test `TestGateway_TokenRevokeRacingAMessageCommit`. |
| M1-65/15 | 2026-10-17 | One rule for delivery across the phases of a connection: (a) from `Register` until it goes live a connection is a candidate of every event of a space or channel, so its live buffer holds everything it may be entitled to and the flush decides with the rights of that moment; (b) an event with a seq at most the head of the connection is not sent again (the snapshot or the catch-up covers it), but a `channel.created` among them still adds its channel to the hub indices; (c) a removal of `access.changed` at seq N drops only queued, buffered or paged events before N. The live buffer stays bounded by `sync.outbound_queue_max`. | The rounds before fixed one instance of the same class each: events lost, sent twice or out of order across registering, catching up, live and revoked when space, channel and rights changes ran concurrently. Red team round 4 reproduced a default operator agent that lost the first message of a channel created while it caught up; the model test also found a lagging tail sending events up to the head twice, and a removal processed by the tail after the pages had read a later re-grant dropping entitled events. Class test `TestHub_DeliveryModel` (seeded random runs of the real hub with fakes, red on `d498e61`); regressions `TestHub_OperatorNewChannelWhileCatchingUp`, `TestHub_NewChannelUpToHeadIsLearned`, `TestHub_LiveSkipsTailEventsUpToHead`, `TestHub_StaleRemovalKeepsLaterPageEvents`, `TestHub_StaleRemovalKeepsQueuedLaterEvents`, `TestQueue_PurgeOnlyBeforeTheRemoval`, `TestGateway_OperatorNewChannelWhileCatchingUp` (real database). |

## M4-18 Desktop app shell

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-18-0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave36` at `8493d3f`, branch `feat/M4-18-desktop-shell` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-116 and `auth.device_pending_ttl` in `AppConfig` are on the base), so nothing is ported. The brief's `15-client-architecture.md` is `15-client-architektur.md` on the base; the lines this ticket adds there are English. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the local store is `LocalDatabase` and its wipe `LocalDatabase.wipe`; `openLocalStore` takes `file` and `keys`; the device key client is `DeviceKeyClient` with `ensure`, `sign` and `delete(scope)`, whose `StepUpPrompt` (texts) is a different type from the `StepUpPrompt` port of `auth` (a question); `LoginService` has no discovery call, so E1 calls `getDiscovery` through the generated client; the fake server has no handlers for `getSpace`, `getChannel` and `getThread`, which no flow of this ticket needs against it (the target lookup is tested with a stub client). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-18-1 | 2026-10-17 | `bootstrap.dart` composes per server address (`InstanceServices.open`): the device keys with the `instance_id` of discovery as scope, `InstanceSecrets` with every key prefixed `instance/<instance_id>/`, the database key (32 bytes of `Random.secure()`, stored as `database_key`), the encrypted database `<application support>/instances/<instance_id>/local.db`, the `AuthSession` with browser, loopback receiver and (where present) passkey port, and the `SyncEngine` (`keepConnectedInBackground`). `AppPorts` holds every port and `appPortsProvider` hands it out, so tests replace all of them. On macOS only a release build uses the Data Protection keychain with the keychain access group; a debug or profile build is not signed by a team, has no access group and keeps the items in the login keychain. | One instance never sees the secrets or keys of another, and a wipe can find everything by the instance ID; an access group without a team signature makes the app fail to launch. |
| D-M4-18-2 | 2026-10-17 | One `AccountWiper` per instance for every end of an account: logout, E4 "Cancel", a rejected or expired waiting device, `4012`, `session.revoked`, `401 session_revoked`, and also a refresh token the server no longer accepts (E7 "session expired"). It stops the engine, wipes the database, deletes the instance folder (database files and file cache), both device keys and every instance secret, runs every step even when one fails and keeps only the server address. The controller reacts to the `SignedOut` events of the session and to the engine's `SignedOutNotice` through one guarded path. | 01 5.6 shows "The data of this device has been deleted" on every E7; one path cannot forget a step for one cause. |
| D-M4-18-3 | 2026-10-17 | E1 classifies discovery: a `HandshakeException`, a `TlsException` or Dio's `badCertificate` is "Certificate invalid" with no way to continue and no setting to trust a certificate; `setup_required` (flag or problem) is "Instance not set up yet"; a `min_client_versions` entry of the platform above the build's version (numeric `major.minor.patch`) is "App too old"; everything else is "No instance at this address". Only the integration test entry accepts `http`. The address is remembered after a found instance and asked again at the next start. | R-PR-14: an exception button would let an attacker in the middle defeat DPoP and the device binding. |
| D-M4-18-4 | 2026-10-17 | The router has the paths of 15 11 plus `/onboarding/<step>` and `/settings/...`; `parseLocation` is a pure function that checks IDs with the ID types of `domain`. The redirect follows the onboarding controller: signed out, a content path goes to the current screen and is kept, and after the login it opens. A target that is not in the local database and that REST answers with `404` or `403` shows "Not found or no access"; without an answer it waits. Deep links: macOS registers the URL type in `Info.plist` and the app delegate forwards links; the Windows runner registers the scheme under `HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\Classes` at every start (no administrator rights; MSIX of M7 replaces it), a second start with a link sends it to the running window by `WM_COPYDATA` and exits; both forward over the method channel `bothy/deep_links` (`takePending` for a link that started the app, `open` for later ones). | 15 11; a per-user registration works for a local build without an installer. |
| D-M4-18-5 | 2026-10-17 | Column names `sidebar`, `post_list`, `history`, `session`; the sized columns default to the layout tokens `DsLayout.sidebarWidth`, `listWidth`, `sessionWidth`, never below `DsLayout.columnMin`, and the history keeps at least `columnMin`. One drag is one `putMySetting` of `layout.column_widths` through the outbox (only the widths that differ from the defaults); the dragged widths stay until the stored setting comes back from `user_settings`. "Reset" is `deleteMySetting` (the setting's default is empty). Below `ui.desktop_min_width_four_columns` the session column is not shown, below `ui.desktop_min_width_three_columns` the post list and the history share one place. Until M4-25 the sidebar frame carries the "Log out" button. | 01 5.1 and 11; a column that jumps back while the write travels would look broken. |

| D-M4-19-9 | 2026-10-18 | After the red team: the counter guard of D-M4-19-8(c) judges replayed events by their global `seq` against the snapshot's `head_seq`, not by `thread_seq`, so a replayed deletion or edit no longer cancels a mention the snapshot already holds; the route selection of `/c/<id>` watches the stored channel, so a forum opened by a link before the first snapshot shows its post list once the channel arrives; the generated domain tables are again byte for byte what their generators write. | 04 14 (the snapshot holds the state at its head); 15 11; `make codegen-check`. |
| D-M4-19-10 | 2026-10-18 | Class fix after the delta red team, replacing the guard of D-M4-19-8(c) and D-M4-19-9: the order of server values and own counting lives in one place, `UnreadCounter` (domain). Every server value holds the events up to a global `seq` (the snapshot its `head_seq`, a `read_state.updated` its own `seq`); every counted message keeps the `seq` of the event that counted it. An event or a server value at or below the `seq` a thread's values hold changes nothing (the snapshot seeds a new counter at its head). A newer server value drops the mentions counted from events at or below its `seq`, and the unread messages too where it delivers an unread count; a computed unread count (`read_state.updated` carries none) covers only the messages up to the stored `last_message_seq` and the read position, so messages counted above both stay counted. The sync engine only passes each event's `seq`; `SyncContext.countedUpToSeq` is gone. A seeded model test runs random histories of created, edited, deleted and read events with a random cursor, head and live tail through the engine against the server's exact values. | The three findings (a replayed deletion, a replayed edit, a replayed read state changing a counter the snapshot already set) were instances of one class: a counter change judged without the position of the server value; a live read on another device also kept mentions counted before it. The class-fix red team found that a partial live read dropped unread messages its computed value does not hold (fixed with a regression test; the model reads partially and sends no unread count). 04 14. |

A found instance with a stored session resumes on every connect, not only at the start. | 01 3.6 (offline reading) and 6.6 (the offline banner): a signed-in person must reach the local data without network. |

A certificate error, `setup_required` and a too old app show E1 with their text; a stored session of the remembered instance at that address otherwise opens its local data whatever else the network answered (no answer, a timeout, a `4xx` or `5xx` page of a reverse proxy, a captive portal): the main view, or E4 for a waiting device, where the server's answer about the device wins over the stored state; without a session a found instance shows E2 and everything else "No instance at this address". This refines D-M4-18-13, which opened the local data only without any answer. A table test runs every stored state times every network result times "comes online" or "stays offline" through the controller against the fake server. | Delta red team: a proxy's `502`, `503`, `504` sent a signed-in person to E1, and a waiting device started offline landed in the empty main view; both were instances of one routing rule spread over two places. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |

| D-M4-26-0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave40` at `35d6a9c`, branch `feat/M4-26-admin-people` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The base's `actions.yaml` and the API operations the brief names equal those of `origin/main` in `step_up`, `owner` and shape, so no contract change was brought in. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |

They take no `StepUpService`: as in M4-25 the step-up runs in the `StepUpInterceptor` of the client the repository wraps, and every changing call passes its purpose text (`stepUpPurposeExtra`). The console passes its own repository (M6-04). Failures reuse `SelfActionFailed` of M4-25. Search (`q`) and the state filter (`status`) are request parameters of `listUsers`; the group filter has none and filters the loaded page by the members of the group, with a visible note. | Brief Design note 1; the merged step-up path of M4-09 and M4-25 needs no service in the screens (D-W-100). |
| D-M4-26-2 | 2026-10-18 | Every changing action shows one `ConfirmChange` dialog that says what the change does (form dialogs such as "Add person", "Create group" and "Add member" are that dialog with their fields), then sends the call; the step-up dialog appears only when the server answers `401 step_up_required`. Destructive actions (disable, revoke, unlink, delete, remove, reject, end a session) use the danger banner and button and ask once. A `403` of a list shows the server's reason instead of the section; a refused change hides its control. | 01 5.2 and 6.7, SR5, fixed requirements 9 to 11. |
| D-M4-26-3 | 2026-10-18 | The fake server reads `docs/spec/actions.yaml` (`ActionCatalog`, walking up to the repository root) and the `x-authz-action` of each operation; the administration handlers ask for the step-up exactly for `step_up: true` (and `fresh`). `server.stepUpOf(operationId)` and `server.actionOf(operationId)` expose the table, so the enumeration test (headless in `apps/client/test/admin/admin_step_up_test.dart`, through the screens in `integration_test/admin_step_up_test.dart`) holds the rule without a copy of 05. `createUser` and `createEnrollmentCode` answer a replay of the same `Idempotency-Key` with `409 conflict`, `reason: one_time_secret`; `revokeSession` also accepts an owner for another person's session. | Brief Design note 3; 06 7; 05 `session.revoke` (self or owner). |

The desktop hosts the shell at `/admin`, `/admin/<section>` and `/admin/<section>/<id>`; this ticket adds no entry point in the sidebar, the path is reachable by link. | Brief requirement 15 (the API has no instance-wide list and no hardware field); the sidebar is outside the brief's scope. |

## M1-66 Gateway focus and typing

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-66/0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave38` at `297ab0c`, branch `feat/M1-66-gateway-focus-typing` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. Brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the contract of `focus`, `typing.start`, `typing.stop` and `typing.started` (asyncapi, openapi, actions.yaml) is the same on the base apart from the translation, so no contract commit was brought in. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-66/1 | 2026-10-17 | A `focus` with an empty set succeeds without an authorize check: the handler marks the op as naming no resource, and the dispatcher's proof accepts that marker. Every other successful op still needs a check of its action. | An empty set grants nothing and only clears the focus; there is no thread to authorize, and closing the connection with `4000` would break the documented way to clear the focus. |
| M1-66/2 | 2026-10-17 | `focus` and the typing ops authorize the thread by its id first and read its space and channel from chat afterwards. Unknown and unreadable threads both get `not_found` from authorize; a thread that disappears between the check and the read is treated as unknown. | 02 "404 instead of 403": one code path for both. authz resolves the path itself, so the check needs no data from chat; chat only supplies the scope of `typing.started` and of the focus entry. |
| M1-66/3 | 2026-10-17 | The rate limit of `focus` and the typing ops runs in the handler, after the dispatcher decoded the body; a malformed body closes with `4002` even when the bucket is empty. | The dispatcher of M1-64 decodes before every handler; the order only differs for frames that close the connection anyway. |
| M1-66/4 | 2026-10-17 | The rate-limit classes of the M1-66 ops are constants in `internal/wire` (`GatewayOpClasses`), held against `x-rate-limit-class` of asyncapi by `TestGatewayOpRateClasses`; the route generator stays unchanged. | `heartbeat` and `reauth` carry `rl.gateway_connect` in the contract but must not consume it (05 section 3), so a generic "every op counts its class" would be wrong; only the three new ops count their class. |
| M1-66/5 | 2026-10-17 | The register of ephemeral types holds the two focus-bound types `typing.started` and `run.updated`; the other ephemeral types of 05 enter it with the tickets that build them (M2-20, M3-23, M2, M6-15). | Their candidate rules differ (one connection of the command owner, `push.deliver` with R-AZ-30) and are decided there; an entry now would claim a rule nobody built. |
| M1-66/6 | 2026-10-17 | A failed admission check of an ephemeral frame drops the frame for that connection; it neither closes the connection nor retries. A failed re-check of a focused thread drops the thread. | Ephemeral events are at most once and carry no seq, so no cursor can move past them; the durable path of the next event closes a revoked connection. Dropping a thread fails closed; the client focuses it again. |
| M1-66/7 | 2026-10-17 | A focus entry keeps the space and channel of its thread from the time of the check; `channel.archived` and `space.archived` re-check the entries of that channel or space, `thread.moved` the moved thread, which takes the new channel and space of the event. Pruning changes the hub index only; the session's stored focus is re-checked on `resume` anyway. | The hub has no structure of its own; the event names the affected channel or space, and the moved thread's new place is the scope of `thread.moved`. |
| M1-66/8 | 2026-10-17 | `occurred_at` of an ephemeral event is written in UTC with milliseconds, the form of `expires_at`, so `expires_at - occurred_at` is exactly `sync.typing_ttl` for the client. Durable events keep their form. | The brief asks for milliseconds in `expires_at`; with nanoseconds in `occurred_at` the difference would not be the TTL. |
| M1-66/9 | 2026-10-17 | When the tests of the connection pass their own delivery (no hub), wire gives the gateway a publisher that relays nothing. | The connection tests need no ephemeral delivery; production always builds the hub. |

## M1-72 Performance gate of the server core

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-72/0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave38` at `297ab0c`, branch `feat/M1-72-performance-gate` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (ADR 0063 with the modes of point 3 and the `perf.*` rows are on the base), so nothing is ported. The brief's English chapters are the German files of the base (`02-architektur.md`, `16-betrieb.md`, `07-rechte.md`); the text this ticket adds is English. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; precedent M1-23/9 and M1-25/8 for English text in German chapters. |
| M1-72/1 | 2026-10-18 | `perf/internal/report` reads the thresholds from `02-architecture.md`, or `02-architektur.md` where the English file does not exist, finds the section by either title ("Configuration values of this chapter", "Konfigurationswerte dieses Kapitels"), the place by either name ("Load test", "Lasttest") and the comparison by either meaning ("target (minimum)" or "Ziel (Mindestwert)" is `>=`; "target (maximum)", "Ziel (Höchstwert)", "target" and "Ziel" are `<=`). | The base has the German chapter, `main` the English one; the loader must hold on both without a literal threshold. |
| M1-72/2 | 2026-10-18 | The profile mode also compares the container runtime of the database (`docker info`) with `perf.reference_profile`: when it is below the profile the mode is `below`, whatever the host of the test process has. | The profile names a local PostgreSQL with its memory; a Docker VM with fewer CPUs or less memory cannot give the container the limits of the emulated mode, so its numbers say nothing about the profile. The build machine of this ticket runs its database in a smaller Docker VM: mode `below`; the numbers are in the report. |
| M1-72/3 | 2026-10-18 | `make perf` runs `./perf/...` and `./test/chaos/...` in one `go test -p 1`, then `perfreport` prints the table and decides the exit code; each test also fails itself on a missed target or a broken invariant, and a target without a result fails the report. The shared frame of the load tests is the tagged package `perf/internal/perfkit`; `BenchmarkAuthorizeCached` already reported `p99-ns` and stays unchanged; its test is the package `perf/authorize_cached`. | Two measurements must not share the machine; one results file and one table for all targets (brief scope 1). |
| M1-72/4 | 2026-10-18 | Every simulated client of a load test sends `X-Forwarded-For` with its own documentation address (its own `/64` of `2001:db8::/32`) through the loopback peer that the default `server.trusted_proxies` trusts; the measured server keeps its default configuration. | The credential check reserves a token of `rl.public_auth` per client address before it verifies (02 "Chain in the api node"), so the whole load from `127.0.0.1` measured that limiter (429 answers) instead of the server; behind the reverse proxy of a deployment the server sees distinct clients. |
| M1-72/5 | 2026-10-18 | `TestPerf_FanoutLocalP99` writes into the main threads of ten channels of one space, like `load_messages`, and its receivers read the space, not one channel. | One thread serializes its messages (`thread_seq`); the sustained rate in a single thread exhausted the pool on this machine (503 `no database connection was free within database.acquire_timeout`), which measures the thread lock, not the fan-out. |
| M1-72/6 | 2026-10-18 | `extra` holds the pool occupancy and the lock waiters sampled from `pg_stat_activity` (in use p99 and maximum, share of samples with every connection of `database.max_conns` in use, waiting backends), not the histograms of pool acquire wait and `bothy.db.event_seq.lock_wait`. | The measured server is a child process without an in-process telemetry sink (M1-69 adds the sinks); the database side shows saturation and lock waits without one. |
| M1-72/7 | 2026-10-18 | The production request validator keeps no responses of the operations, so the library compiles request and parameter schemas only; the test build with `contract_validate` keeps them for G8 (`keepResponses`, set by `contractcheck_install.go`). | `make perf` measured the time to ready and the idle RSS above `perf.startup_ready_max` and `perf.idle_rss_max`; the warm-up of the response schemas of every operation took most of the start and memory the process never used. After the fix both are below their thresholds on the build machine (numbers in the report). Tests `TestNewValidatorLeavesOutResponseSchemas`, `TestG8_ChainInstallsTheCheck`. |
| M1-72/8 | 2026-10-18 | The Authorizer reads the thread and channel facts of a resource once per structure scope (`authorize.WithStructureScope`, a memo separate from the request memo and the rights version); the hub opens one scope per live event for all candidate connections, `PageEvents` one per catch-up page. | Every check read thread and channel from the database, so a live event cost two queries per connection: with 100 receivers at the sustained rate the log tail fell behind, receivers had about a tenth of the events after a minute and live delivery looked stalled. After the fix every receiver got every event in order, and the fan-out and the fractional catch-up page dropped by an order of magnitude on the build machine (numbers in the report). Tests `TestStructureScopeReadsThreadAndChannelOnce`, `TestHub_OneStructureScopePerEvent`, `TestCatchUpPageOneStructureScope`, `TestWithStructureScope`. |
| M1-72/9 | 2026-10-18 | `database.max_conns` is neither confirmed nor changed: the run on this machine is in mode `below`, and the saturation it shows comes from a database that commits far below `perf.event_commit_min_per_s` there. The row keeps its default and its note; chapter 16 "Capacity and resources" points to `make perf` and `perf/README.md` for the measurement on the reference host. | Brief design note 9 asks for evidence; a value from a host below the profile would be a guess. The milestone-close run on the reference host is an owner operation (brief "Out of scope"). |
| M1-72/10 | 2026-10-18 | The catch-up page with a large visible set (`largeChannels` channels, each with its own grant) stays above `perf.catchup_page_p99` on this machine: its pages spread over hundreds of channels, so the structure scope saves little and each event still costs two round trips to the database in the Docker VM. It is reported as not proven, not optimized further. | Mode `below`; brief "Out of scope": no optimization beyond what a missed target on the profile requires. Reading the structure of a page in one query per kind is the lever if the reference run misses it. |
| M1-72/11 | 2026-10-18 | The chaos writers repeat an unacknowledged send with the same `client_msg_id` and the same `Idempotency-Key`; the receivers reconnect with `identify` and `cursor` = the last seq they saw (`Client.GatewayFrom`). `harness.Client.Gateway` is built here (M1-71/7 left it out). | A real client repeats a request with its key (06 "Idempotency"); the cursor makes the server catch the receiver up, so a lost frame would show as a gap. |
| M1-72/12 | 2026-10-18 | The chaos receivers keep their gateway connections until every acknowledged message arrived and are never reconnected by the test; the load runs past the delivery bound after the last fault, and every message acknowledged after it must reach every receiver within `sync.log_poll_interval` plus `database.listen_reconnect_backoff_cap` plus a slack (`CheckDeliveredWithin`); after a `LISTEN` loss no receiver may have reconnected. | Red team round 1 (Major, reproduced): the receivers were reconnected with a cursor before the checks, so the catch-up of the reconnect delivered what a dead log tail never sent, and both tests stayed green with a tail that stopped after the first fault. With the same sabotage both tests now fail. |

## M1-70 Leak tests and 404 equality

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-70/0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave38` at `297ab0c`, branch `feat/M1-70-leak-tests` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (AZ-P4, AZ-P5, AZ-P12, AZ-M52 and `authz.not_found_timing_tolerance` are in the base's 07 and configuration reference), so nothing is ported. The brief's `07-permissions.md` is `07-rechte.md` and its `03-data-model.md` is `03-datenmodell.md` on the base; the sentence this ticket adds to 07 is English, and the lint names its data model chapter in `tools/lint/sql_visibility.yaml` (`data_model`), so a port changes one line. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-70/1 | 2026-10-17 | `authz.not_found_timing_tolerance` is planned, not in the server's schema; the timing test reads its standard from the table of `docs/operations/configuration.md` at run time (`TestToleranceFromReference`), never as a literal. AZ-P4 compares medians of `timingSamples` interleaved samples after `timingWarmup` warm-up pairs per route and requester (named constants in `notfound_test.go`). | The server does not read the key; the reference is its only source, and the brief forbids a literal. Alternating the order cancels drift of a loaded host, as ID-T21 does. |
| M1-70/2 | 2026-10-17 | On the tables `spaces` and `channels` the lint accepts their key `id = ANY(<param>)` as the visibility predicate (`ListVisibleSpaces`, `ListVisibleChannels`). | The key of a space is its `space_id`; the brief's `(channel_id\|space_id)` names the column of the tables that reference them. |
| M1-70/3 | 2026-10-17 | `after_authorize` covers a single resource and the rows of exactly one resource that `authorize` allowed (the threads or tags of one channel, the mentions of one message, the own read states of one thread or channel), keyed by its ID in the query. A list across several channels or spaces never takes an exception: `ListSidebarStates` read every sidebar row of the person and filtered only by `authorize` per row; it now carries `(@all_spaces OR st.space_id = ANY(@visible))` from `Scope` (fix in `chat`, regression tests `TestSidebarListCarriesScope` and the repository test). | A key of one allowed resource is a narrower predicate than `= ANY($visible)`; a multi-resource list is exactly what rule 1 asks to filter in SQL. |
| M1-70/4 | 2026-10-17 | Fixtures with `ok=false` exist only for `user_id` and a person as requester (05 `user.read`); every other path parameter has a foreign instance for both requesters. `PrepareExtras` creates what the matrix world lacks through the public API as the owner: a disabled trusted-header login driver, an app with a webhook subscription (`webhook.allowed_hosts` set to the receiver host; egress stays denied) and a host without assignee. `World.Owner` is added to the harness for it. | Routes of apps, hosts, providers and webhook subscriptions are enabled in M1, so they need a real foreign instance, not a reason. |
| M1-70/5 | 2026-10-17 | AZ-P5 property 1 checks every space, channel, thread, agent and message ID a surface or an event names against the per-item truth, except an agent ID in an operator member of a space or channel (`default_operator_agent_id`, `operator.effective_agent_id`, also in the payload of an older event): that is header data of a resource the principal reads (05 `space.read`, `channel.read`), not the agent card `agent.read` protects. Agents as items (`listAgents`, the agents of the snapshot) stay checked. | Catch-up delivers older events of a now readable channel with the payload of their time (07 principle 4); the operator of that time is part of the channel then. Redacting it would change 04, which the brief forbids. |
| M1-70/6 | 2026-10-17 | The AZ-P5 server sets `authz.expiry_sweep_interval` to `24h`; expiring grants and memberships end `expiryLead` (a named constant of `world.go`) after their creation on the real clock, and the build waits until that has passed. The person writes drafts and read states before; the lost channel gets a `write` grant, a draft and read states and is revoked. Agents have no personal data in the snapshot (sync), so their unread list must be empty. `listMyReadStates` needs `channel_id`, so it is queried per channel; an unreadable channel must return no rows. | Expiry is checked on every call (07 8.3); the sweep's `access.changed` would otherwise land between the two collections of property 2. A draft needs `write`. |
| M1-70/7 | 2026-10-17 | `Client.Gateway(ctx)` returns `(*GatewayConn, error)` on `wstest` (hello, identify with the DPoP proof for the HTTPS form of the gateway URL, ready, `Next`, `Send`, `Close`, automatic heartbeat); reauth is not automatic. | A test connection lives far shorter than an access token; `wstest` already holds the frame codec. |
| M1-70/8 | 2026-10-18 | `httpserver.SchemaValidator.Validate` holds a mutex around the libopenapi call; the gateway shares one validator across all connections. | `go test -race` of the leak tests (two gateway connections at once) reported a data race inside libopenapi's schema rendering; `TestSchemaValidatorConcurrent` reproduces it in the owning package. A mutex is the smallest fix; frame bodies are small. |

## M2-07 Agent consumers over the gateway and REST

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-07-0 | 2026-10-17 | Base `int/m1-wave38` at `297ab0c`, branch `feat/M2-07-agent-consumers` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. Brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the rules the brief relies on (table `agent_consumer_cursors` with `holder_session_id`, R-SY-09, R-SY-23, the close code `4015`) are on the base in the German chapters, so no contract change is ported. Text this ticket adds to the German chapters 02 and 09 is English. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00046`. | The task names the base and the lane; the merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M2-07-1 | 2026-10-17 | One delivery engine for both transports: `gateway/app.ConsumerEngine` runs the window of every consumer connection and every REST window, and implements `agents.ConsumerFeed` itself; `internal/wire` binds it into the REST use cases with the late setter `ConsumerService.SetFeed`. There is no separate package `gateway/adapters/consumerfeed`. | The window rules and the delivery hook of M2-08 exist once (brief note 1); a forwarding package around the engine would add a type without behaviour. |
| D-M2-07-2 | 2026-10-17 | Fencing is always on: every takeover writes `holder_session_id`, every save of `acked_seq` is `GREATEST` with `WHERE holder_session_id = <own holder>`, and a save without a row closes the connection with `4015`, in both values of `cluster.mode`. M7-17 adds only the faster signal between nodes. The statements touch one row, append no event and run on the pool outside a unit of work. | 03 states the conditional save without restriction and OQ-07 keeps the code node-neutral; the save ticker has no request, and a single-row statement needs no transaction. |
| D-M2-07-3 | 2026-10-17 | A consumer name matches `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$` (`ConsumerPath`, now also on `GatewayIdentify.consumer`, so a wrong name closes `identify` with `4002`); consumers exist only for agents, the member `consumer` of people and apps is accepted and ignored. | 04 6.2 and 6.4 name consumers for agents only; one pattern for both transports. |
| D-M2-07-4 | 2026-10-17 | Takeover order under a per-process lock per consumer: save the acknowledgement of the older connection of this process, `Take` with the new gateway session as holder (creating the row at `ready.head_seq`), close the older connection with `4015 consumer_replaced`, drop a REST window of the key, log one INFO line with agent, consumer and both session ids. `4015` is not resumable, so the older session leaves the session store and its `resume` gets `4006`. The gateway ignores `cursor` and `sync_epoch` of an agent's `identify` and `resume`; a `resume` first proves with a fenced save that the session still holds the row (otherwise it closes with `4015` and the newer holder stays, also when the takeover happened while the session was detached, through a REST poll or in another process), then continues from the acknowledgement held in the session and closes a still open older connection of the session with `4015` instead of `4017`. | Brief note 4; 04 15.1 items 3 and 4 (the durable cursor decides), agent connections have no device. |
| D-M2-07-5 | 2026-10-17 | The window (`gateway/domain.ConsumerWindow`) marks exactly the ack-timeout redeliveries with `redelivered: true`; the deadline runs from the oldest unacknowledged dispatch. A consumer connection always ends its backlog with `catchup.complete {head_seq}` when the window reaches the head of `ready`; `retention` and `cursor_ahead` send `resync_required`, move the cursor to the head with `Restart` and continue live without `catchup.complete`; `too_far_behind` and `epoch_changed` do not apply. | Brief note 5; `EventEnvelope.redelivered`; the window bounds the backlog, and the client epoch is ignored. |
| D-M2-07-6 | 2026-10-17 | Consumer connections pull from the log up to the hub position (`Hub.Position`: the highest seq the log tail of the process has passed through `Observe` and `Dispatch`, seeded with the head the tail started at); the hub queues no durable dispatch for them and wakes their runners after each event. The pages are read in filter mode with a request scope of their own per page (the rights version is read once per page), not in delivery mode. | Brief note 6 for the bound. A revocation without a log event (a revoked API token) is not seen by the delivery cache until the next event, so delivery mode kept paging a revoked token's backlog; the per-page version refuses the next page with `unauthenticated` and `4012` (`TestGateway_TokenRevokedDuringCatchUpCloses4012`, as M1-65/8 for people). |
| D-M2-07-7 | 2026-10-17 | Acknowledgement rules on both transports: `seq` below 0 or above the head is `validation_failed` on `/seq`; `seq` at or below the acknowledgement changes nothing and is no error; a higher `seq` covers every number up to it, also beyond what was dispatched. The gateway's `ack` checks against the hub position or the window's own position when that lies above it (a fresh consumer starts at `ready.head_seq`). | Brief note 8; 06 2 "No effect"; the cursor a client was told must be acknowledgeable. |
| D-M2-07-8 | 2026-10-17 | The head that consumer operations report and check against is the hub position of the serving process; only a gateway connection creates a missing cursor at the `head_seq` of its own `ready`. One goroutine per process (`ConsumerEngine.Run`, every `agent.ack_persist_interval`) saves every consumer connection whose held acknowledgement is above its saved one and drops the REST windows idle for `sync.resume_window`; a connection that detaches or closes for any reason and step 3 of the shutdown (before `4013`) save at once. | Brief note 9; a consumer is never told about a seq this process cannot deliver yet. |
| D-M2-07-9 | 2026-10-17 | A REST poll is a consumer like an `identify`: per call, under the per-key lock, a gateway connection of the key in this process is saved and closed with `4015`, `Take` writes the holder of the REST window (a UUIDv7 created with the window; the first poll creates the cursor at the hub position and answers an empty page), the window is reconciled with the stored acknowledgement, the ack timeout applied and the resync checked; `retention` and `cursor_ahead` answer `410 resync_required` with the reason after moving the cursor to the head. `next_cursor` is the window's position but never covers an event an earlier poll handed out that is still unacknowledged (it stays below the oldest one), so a lost response cannot acknowledge events away; they come again after `agent.ack_timeout`. `getConsumerState` and `ackAgentEvents` take nothing over; `ackAgentEvents` writes at once without holder condition and tells a window of the key in this process. | Brief note 10; R-SY-09 holds for both transports. |
| D-M2-07-10 | 2026-10-17 | A caller that is not an agent: the descriptor of `agent.events.consume` is the agent itself (`{kind agent, id actor, owner actor}`), and authz decides visibility before the requirement, so a person or app that is no owner gets `404 not_found` and an owner gets `403 forbidden` with `reason: subject_kind_not_allowed`; the gateway's `ack` of a person answers `error` with the same code. The brief's table expected `403` for every caller. | Follows the merged authz (M1-28) and R-AZ-01 (404 before 403, ADR 0017); the code does not deviate from the specification. |
| D-M2-07-11 | 2026-10-17 | The gateway applies `rl.agent_runtime` to `ack` itself with `platform/ratelimit` keyed by the agent (`KeyForAgent`); an exhausted bucket answers `error` with `rate_limited`. An `ack` whose credential was revoked since `identify` closes with `4012`; other errors close with `4000`. | The dispatcher has no per-op rate limit (06 9 item 3); a revoked credential must not keep an open connection. |
| D-M2-07-12 | 2026-10-17 | Tests of M1-64 and M1-65 follow the consumer semantics: every agent principal of the gateway tests gets its `agents` record (the cursor refers to it), the agent catch-up tests of M1-65 acknowledge as they read (with a larger `agent.max_inflight_events`), and the transport tests of `adapters/ws` register every enabled op except `ack`, whose handler needs the engine. | Without an acknowledgement a consumer stops at `agent.max_inflight_events` by design (04 15.2 item 2). |
| D-M2-07-13 | 2026-10-17 | Differences from the names of the brief (D-W-100): the REST use cases are the port `agents.ConsumerAPI` beside `agents.Consumers`; no `sync` port change was needed (`CatchUp.PageEvents` returns events, the hub position is `Hub.Position`); the integration tests create agents and tokens as SQL fixtures like the world of M1-65 and let the Owner post; retention is simulated by deleting the events the log tail has passed; the app part of `TestPeopleAndAppsUnchanged` is not built (people only). The metric `bothy.agent.consumer.lag` and its alert are not registered (out of scope). | The merged code and test harness win; the remaining gaps are listed in the report. |
| D-M2-07-14 | 2026-10-18 | `docs/api/openapi.yaml` gets only the contract changes of the brief (pattern of `GatewayIdentify.consumer`, `minimum: 0` on both `seq`, `410` on `pollAgentEvents`); the descriptions of the three operations that the brief asks for stand in 09 4.3 "REST fetch" instead. | The file was 146 bytes below `repo.max_file_kb` (the large-file hook of `make lint`); the descriptions would exceed it, and raising the limit is not this ticket's to decide. |

## M1-67 Gateway frame conformance (G9)

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-67/0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave39` at `8489df6`, branch `feat/M1-67-gateway-conformance` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. Brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the gateway part of asyncapi.yaml, openapi.yaml and actions.yaml is the same on the base apart from the translation and two later event types (`device.pending`, `gdpr.request_due`) no M1 module emits, so no contract commit was brought in. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-67/1 | 2026-10-18 | G9 validates on the client side of the test harness (`internal/gateway/adapters/wstest` over `api/specschema`), not with a hook in production code; `TestHarness_OnlyEntryPoint` parses every `_test.go` file of the server and fails on a dial of `github.com/coder/websocket` outside the harness, so the harness stays the only entry point. | Production frames carry no test cost and no second code path; the parser test makes a bypass visible. |
| M1-67/2 | 2026-10-18 | Server frames are validated strictly: the bundle adds `unevaluatedProperties: false` to every schema with `properties` or `allOf` that declares neither `additionalProperties` nor `unevaluatedProperties`, except direct inline `allOf` members and definitions an `allOf` references directly (base schemas such as `EventEnvelope`). | In 2020-12 the keyword on the composing schema sees every member's properties, on a member it would reject the siblings' fields. Clients ignore unknown fields, but an undeclared member the server sends is contract drift. |
| M1-67/3 | 2026-10-18 | The harness keeps the existing package and its API; `Dial` takes the `testing.TB` (`Dial(t, ctx, baseURL, opts...)`) and registers the cleanup that drains a paused client, drops the connection and reports. There is no `Start` in `wstest`: the started api process of `internal/wire` and the fake-backed server of `adapters/ws` stay with their tests. `Pause` has the counterpart `Unpause`, because `Resume` is the gateway op. | `wstest` exists since M1-64 (the brief's "if none exists" case does not apply). A `Start` in `wstest` would have to import `internal/wire` or copy the fakes; the validation does not depend on how the server was started. |
| M1-67/4 | 2026-10-18 | The validator is `github.com/santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` v6.0.2 (Apache-2.0), already in the module graph as an indirect dependency, now direct; the component `api` gets the vendor group `jsonschema`. The G8 checker `contractcheck` is not reused. | The brief asks to reuse a 2020-12 validator the module already has; `contractcheck` validates REST responses through libopenapi and has no AsyncAPI bundle. |
| M1-67/5 | 2026-10-18 | The frame limit of the contract defaults to the default of `sync.ws_max_frame_bytes`; the harness takes the value the server under test runs with (`WithMaxFrameBytes`). | Several gateway worlds run with a smaller limit; the default would hide an oversized frame there. |
| M1-67/6 | 2026-10-18 | `instance` emitted `instance.updated` (settings, branding, flags) without registering it; `instance.Register` now declares it and stands first in `moduleRegistrations`, in the order of the module register. | The corpus test requires every M1 event type to be registered (AZ-P2); the producer is fixed, the contract was right. |
| M1-67/7 | 2026-10-18 | `wire.GatewayOpClasses` names the class of every enabled op: `identify`, `resume`, `heartbeat` and `reauth` `rl.gateway_connect` as the contract says, `focus` and the typing ops as before. Only the tests read the map; `heartbeat` and `reauth` still consume nothing (M1-66/4 stays for the counting). | The register comparison needs the class of every registered op. |
| M1-67/8 | 2026-10-18 | The runtime register of the comparison takes the op action from `routes.GatewayOps` (what the dispatcher requires), the class from `GatewayOpClasses`, and kind and receive action of each event type from the server's catalogue (`authz/domain.LookupEvent`). A registered op whose action differs from asyncapi.yaml, actions.yaml or both is one problem. | These are the sources the running server uses; the brief's fixture counts one problem for a wrong action. |
| M1-67/9 | 2026-10-18 | The close code check covers closes the server starts; a close after `Close` or `Abort` of the client is not checked. The order check compares `cursor` and `catchup.complete` with the last durable `seq` delivered on that connection. | The client's own close code is not a server frame; `ready` and `resumed` heads are snapshots, not deliveries. |
| M1-67/10 | 2026-10-18 | The corpus scenario gets `reauth_required` from a token lifetime of 5m with `sync.reauth_lead_time` 298s and `access.expiring` from a worker process on the same database (`authz.expiry_sweep_interval` 1s, `authz.expiry_reminder_lead` 1h); it also lists `thread.moved_out`, which the move of M1-62 emits. | Both are real producers without test hooks; the windows only shorten the waits. |

## M1-68 Server core metrics per catalogue

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-68/0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave39` at `8489df6`, branch `feat/M1-68-core-metrics` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. Brief and spec were read from `origin/main`; the base carries the same catalogue rows and definitions (fan-out lag, `client_error`, `bothy.authz.decisions` without filters, job outcomes, layer rule 5) in its edition of the documents, so no contract commit was brought in. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step. |
| M1-68/1 | 2026-10-18 | `db.client.operation.duration` comes from the query tracer of `platform/pg`, not from otelpgx: otelpgx v0.13.0 records the raw first word of a statement and `pgx.operation.type` and no `db.collection.name` on query metrics, so the view drops every otelpgx instrument. `db.operation.name` is the first keyword (for `WITH` the main statement's) from a closed list, `db.collection.name` the table read or written, from the tables the embedded migrations create (the module register, held by `register-check`); anything else is `other`, without a table the key is absent. Only queries are measured, not batches or `COPY FROM`. | Brief: "otherwise an own pgx query tracer"; the migrations are the closed table list `platform/pg` may read (`migrations` is used by `pg` in the register). |
| M1-68/2 | 2026-10-18 | `kernel/txn` gains `AfterCommit(ctx, fn)`: platform/pg runs the registered functions after a successful COMMIT of the outermost unit, never after a rollback, and a retried attempt starts without them. `bothy.message.created` and `bothy.event.appended` count through it. | Both metrics count "after commit only" and the unit of work had no such step. |
| M1-68/3 | 2026-10-18 | `bothy.gateway.close` counts once per connection: the code of the server's close, else the code of the client's close frame; a connection that ends without any close frame (a lost network) counts no close. | 04 "Close codes" defines codes of close frames; a vanished TCP connection has none. |
| M1-68/4 | 2026-10-18 | `bothy.gateway.connect.duration` before `ready` carries the actor's kind once identify or resume authenticated it (a later setup failure such as `4000` from the snapshot) and `anonymous` before; a refusal of `Open` (sync epoch unreadable, draining) records `server_error` and its close. | signals.md 4.1 and the brief: kind `anonymous` before identification. |
| M1-68/5 | 2026-10-18 | `bothy.gateway.catchup.duration` is recorded at `catchup.complete` and at `resync_required`; a connection attached at the head sends neither and records nothing. | The metric measures the time to these two frames. |
| M1-68/6 | 2026-10-18 | `bothy.gateway.outbound.dropped` with `slow_consumer` counts at a `4014` close the events of the hub's live buffer, the frames of the adapter's outbound queue, and the one frame that did not fit or missed `gateway.write_timeout`. Ephemeral frames dropped on a full queue (no close) are not counted. | The brief: frames discarded at a `4014` close, live buffer included. |
| M1-68/7 | 2026-10-18 | `bothy.authz.decisions` counts no check that failed with an error. | An error is no decision of 05 2.5; it is audited as a decision error and an unknown action counts in `bothy.authz.errors`. |
| M1-68/8 | 2026-10-18 | For `bothy.event.fanout.lag` an event "becomes known" when the sink of the log tail receives the batch read after a NOTIFY or a poll (`Hub.Known`). | The tail reads after every wake-up; the time between a NOTIFY and its read is part of the read, not of the fan-out. |
| M1-68/9 | 2026-10-18 | `bothy.secrets.refresh.failures` is declared in the registry but still created by `platform/secrets` with the same name, unit and description, and `bothy.egress.blocked` is declared without a recorder: no OTel implementation of `egress.Metrics` exists on the base. | `platform/otel` depends on `platform/secrets`, so the register forbids the reverse edge; the view still holds the name and the attribute keys of both. |
| M1-68/10 | 2026-10-18 | The registry also declares `bothy.event.consumer.lag` and `bothy.event.consumer.pending_age` (M2-01, on the base). | Every instrument the server registers comes from the registry; the view drops undeclared ones. |
| M1-68/11 | 2026-10-18 | Catalogue fixes the build proved: section 3 names the series `target_info` (used by a dashboard and an alert), the first row of 4.7 names the five app histograms of 4.6a, and the Source column of `db.client.operation.duration` names the query tracer. | `signal-check` rules 1 and 2 fail without them; the files that use `target_info` are right. |
| M1-68/12 | 2026-10-18 | `bothy.db.pool.connections` observes only the process pool: `internal/wire` calls `Pool.RegisterMetrics` once after `Open`; pools of migrations and tests are not observed. | One pool per process (02 "Deadlines"); a second registration would report two series of the same state. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W42/0 | 2026-10-18 | M1-67 enters as one squash onto `int/m1-wave41` (`14a811d`). The lock of `httpserver.SchemaValidator` came with M1-70 and with M1-67 as the same fix; the base field and test `TestSchemaValidatorConcurrent` stay, the doc comment of M1-67 is kept. The tests of M2-07 and the gateway client of `test/harness` (M1-70, M1-72) dial through the G9 entry point `wstest.Dial(t, ...)`; the harness client reports to the test of its server. | Union; M1-67 changed the signature of `wstest.Dial` after the other tickets branched. |
| INT-W42/1 | 2026-10-18 | The G9 harness accepts a `dispatch` with `redelivered: true` whose `seq` is not above the last delivered one; a first delivery that repeats a seq is still a violation. Test `TestHarness_RedeliveryKeepsOrder`. | M2-07 redelivers from `acked_seq + 1` after `agent.ack_timeout` (04 section 15.2 item 3); M1-67 checked strict order without consumers. |
| INT-W42/2 | 2026-10-18 | `TestConsumerNameRules` sends its identify with an invalid consumer name through `WithRawClientFrames()`. | The frame breaks the identify schema on purpose; G9 validates client frames unless a negative test opts out. |
| INT-W42/3 | 2026-10-18 | `catchup.complete` of an agent consumer names `max(ready head, read)`: a backlog page that read past the ready head ends the catch-up at the position read. Test `TestConsumerWindow_CatchupCompleteNotBelowDelivered`. | The runner pages up to the hub position, so a page could deliver seqs above the ready head and then send `catchup.complete` below them; G9 (and a client that sets its cursor from it) needs it at or above every delivered seq. Found by `TestGateway_OperatorNewChannelWhileCatchingUp` under the G9 harness. |
| INT-W42/4 | 2026-10-18 | `wire.GatewayOpClasses` names `rl.agent_runtime` for `ack`. | M1-67 extended the map to every enabled op before M2-07 enabled `ack`; `TestGatewayOpRateClasses` and the G9 register test failed on the union. The class is the one `agentLimiter` already counts, so behaviour is unchanged. |
| INT-W42/5 | 2026-10-18 | The order check of a durable `dispatch` in `wstest` moves into `(*Client).dispatched`, without a change of behaviour. | INT-W42/1 raised the cognitive complexity of `(*Client).order` above the `gocognit` limit of `make lint`. |

## M4-20 Thread view, input and message actions

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-20-0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave40` at `35d6a9c`, branch `feat/M4-20-thread-view` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The brief relies on no core rule or ruling that `main` has and the base lacks (D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-115, SEC-OUT-1 to SEC-OUT-3, SEC-T13 and the actions of 05 are on the base), so nothing is ported; the base names the chapters in German (`01-produkt.md`, `04-sync-protokoll.md`, `15-client-architektur.md`). Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the outbox view is `OutboxEntryView` with `OutboxStateLabel`; the executors register through the `registerExecutors` hook of `SyncEngine.create`; the app's executors are `AppMessageOps` in `sync/message_ops_app.dart` and the thread from a message is a payload form of `ThreadOps.createThread`; the composer widget is `composer/thread_composer.dart` next to the brief's files, the REST reads are `thread/thread_reads.dart`, the rows are built by `thread/history_model.dart`, the D2 layout by `thread/history_area.dart`. | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-20-1 | 2026-10-18 | Read state: while the newest confirmed message of a thread without a gap after it is in view and the window has the focus (`AppLifecycle` in the foreground and Flutter's state `resumed`, not `inactive`), the history writes `putReadState` with its `thread_seq` through the outbox, at most once per newest message; Esc in the history writes it regardless of the scroll position. After "Mark as unread" the view does not mark read again on its own until a newer message arrives; Esc compares with the stored position only. The app's `putReadState` executor stores the answer, and `markThreadUnread` takes the stored version when it is sent, so an own `putReadState` ahead of it in the lane does not make its `If-Match` stale; a second edit of a message joins the first that is not confirmed (`Outbox.edit`). The focus of the gateway is the set of threads whose history shows (`VisibleThreads`), `idle` while the window is in the background. | Brief Design note 2; 04 14 (the server keeps the maximum, so a repeated write is harmless but not needed). |
| D-M4-20-2 | 2026-10-18 | Drafts: every change of the field updates the one `putThreadDraft` entry that has not been attempted (04 13.3: in place) or enqueues one, so one write is in flight and one waits with the newest text, without a timer; sending drops the waiting write (`clear_draft` deletes the draft on the server). The composer marks its draft as edited (`DraftEditingRegistry`, the `DraftEditing` port of M4-14) while its field has the focus and the person changed the text, and takes a stored draft of another device only otherwise, and only while no own draft write is unconfirmed (its answer after a send must not bring the sent text back); the base version is taken over only with the text. A composer that opens again takes the text of an unconfirmed draft write from the outbox. The app registers its own `putThreadDraft` executor, which hands the `DraftPutResult` to `CatchUpListener.applyDraftPutResult`, so `overwrote_newer` shows the notice. | Brief Design note 3; 04 14. |
| D-M4-20-3 | 2026-10-18 | Edit conflict (`412 version_conflict`): the failed entry shows "Compare", which opens both versions; "Keep mine" edits the failed entry (`Outbox.edit`: a new entry on the version of the kept `current`), "Take theirs" discards it. An edit of a deleted message (`410`) keeps its text with "Copy" and "Discard". No automatic merge. | Brief Design note 4; 04 13.3. |
| D-M4-20-4 | 2026-10-18 | Actions follow the server's answer, never grants: the level and capabilities of the stored `ChannelView` and the channel's `read_only` map to the actions by the table of 05 (`message.create`, `reaction.add`, `thread.create`, `thread.close`, `thread.reopen`: level `write` or `trigger`; `message.update`: write and author; `message.delete`: write and author, or `manage`; `pin.*` and `thread.move`: `manage`; `thread.title.update`: write and creator or `manage`; `message.revisions.read`: read, offered for edited messages). The server checks every write again. | Brief Design note 4; 15 1 principle 1. |
| D-M4-20-5 | 2026-10-18 | The header button "Pin" shows the session column with its section "Pinned"; it writes nothing, because no operation pins a thread (03 has pins of messages). The session column shows the pinned messages of the open thread from the local `pinned` flag of the messages (`MessagesDao.watchPinned`); opening the section reads `listPins` once and stores the pinned messages that are not stored yet (`getMessage`), so pins outside the loaded history show too. | Brief Design note 5; no contract change (P-11). |
| D-M4-20-6 | 2026-10-18 | D2: a thread of a text channel shows to the right of the channel's main history in the history column, and the session column is collapsed until the person shows it ("Column", "Pin"); the area keeps the main history it showed when a removal deletes the rows, so both columns close with "No access anymore"; a channel route of a text channel shows its main history; the main history shows "Thread: title, n replies" under a message that started a thread. | 01 5.1 D2. |
| D-M4-20-7 | 2026-10-18 | Fixes outside the app that the thread view needs: drift shares query streams of equal SQL, and the trigger statements of `watchThread`, `watchForChannel` and `watchContiguous` were all `SELECT 1`, so with a post list open the history did not show new messages or loaded ranges; each now has its own statement text. `CatchUpListener` gets `loadOlderMessages` and `loadMessageRange` (paging up and the jump), `DsDelivery.delayed` shows "Sending delayed", and `DsMarkdown` styles a mention token only for the handles of `mention_ids` (03 content format item 4). | Reproduced in a test of `local_store`; the brief's history needs paging and the delayed state. |
| D-M4-20-8 | 2026-10-18 | The context menu of a message opens on the raw right click (`Listener`), not through a gesture recognizer, because a recognizer of the content won the arena after the first menu and the menu no longer opened. | Reproduced in the integration test. |

"Confirm" is enabled only while the shown preview belongs to the current request, `allowed` is true and the reason is set, so the step-up always follows a preview of exactly what is created. | Brief Design note 1; 07 9.3: the console shows the preview before every confirmation. |
| D-M4-27-2 | 2026-10-18 | Server text only: the panel shows `text` unchanged with `text_locale` as the `Text` locale; around it the client shows labels only (effects as "Gets", "Loses", "becomes" of the server's levels, the invariant code through `problemText` with agent and affected names, the hints as fixed labels with names, the denial reason through `problemText(forbidden, reason)` with three new reason texts of 07 4.1). I1 shows "Switch the agent to organization first."; the guided switch stays M4-28. | Brief fixed requirements 9, 12, 13; 07 9.3. |
| D-M4-27-3 | 2026-10-18 | The run-time example: the fake server (`grantPreviewExample` of `sync_engine/testing`) and the admin_ui fake read the first fenced block between the `language-check` markers after the heading of 07 9.3 and answer it with `text_locale = de` for Sam, `trigger`, channel Marketing/Kampagnen with an expiry; the tests compare against the same read. No German sentence is in a tracked test file. | Brief Design note 3; D-W-94. |
| D-M4-27-4 | 2026-10-18 | `AccessRepository` (`ApiAccessRepository`) holds the grant calls; `AdminRepository` implements it and `ApiAdminRepository` forwards to an `ApiAccessRepository`, so the shell and the app keep one repository (D-M4-26-1). Its types are prefixed `Admin` or `Access` (`AdminTarget`, `AdminSubjectKind`, `GrantHint`) because `api_client` already exports `AccessTarget`, `GrantSubjectKind` and `PreviewHint`. The test "no source of admin_ui reads grants" now exempts `lib/src/grants/`, `lib/src/access/` and the access repository: they show grants and explanations and derive no control from them. | Strictly modular screens without a second data source in the shell; 15 1 principle 1. |
| D-M4-27-5 | 2026-10-18 | Grants live at `/admin/grants/<target key>` (`instance`, `space.<id>`, `channel.<id>`): a target picker over `listSpaces` and `listSpaceChannels`, the grants of the target with "Revoke", "Create access grant" (the wizard in a dialog, target preselected) and "Who has access?" for a space or channel, shown below the list. "View as person" is the picker of A3 inside "Who has access?" and opens the snapshot in a dialog; the step-up of `viewAsUser` comes from the interceptor. Names of IDs in answers come from the directory lists the caller may read; a refused list leaves the IDs as they are. | 01 5.2 A1 to A4; brief Scope. |

## M4-37 Administration: hosts, pairing and grant terminal

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-37-0 | 2026-10-19 | Base `int/m1-wave44` at `8df8fa3`, branch `feat/M4-37-admin-hosts` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The rows stand in a section of the ticket because the base has no section 10 in this file. The operations, schemas and problem codes the brief names are on the base, and the base already holds the later wording of `origin/main` (`attempts_remaining`, `invalid_format`), so no contract change was brought in. `docs/quality/accessibility-checklist.md` is not on the base: the skeleton of `origin/main` comes in with the section of A8 filled and a navigation entry. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-37-1 | 2026-10-19 | Names only from the reported list: the pairing rows and the assign dialog offer the names of `reported_session_users` that have no valid assignment and are not chosen in another row, as pickers, never as a text field. Because `getHost` lists valid assignments only, a name once given and ended is still offered; the server answers `409 conflict` `session_user_taken`, and the screen explains that a name stays bound to its person on the host. `400 validation_failed` with `session_user_not_on_host` says the host must report the name first. Ending an assignment confirms that the name stays taken. | Brief Design notes 1 and 3; 24 2.2, 3.2, ruling T12; no own record of ended names in the client (15 1 principle 1). |
| D-M4-37-2 | 2026-10-19 | Check results as returned: every `doctor` entry in the server's order with its check ID, `ok` or the code, and a named session user with the person of its valid assignment; for a name without one the page reads the audit of the host once (`listAuditEvents`, `action=host.manage`, `resource_id` of the host, details `operation=session_user`, `user_id`, `os_user`, the last entry per name wins) and shows "formerly assigned to"; without an entry the name stands alone with "no assignment known". The audit is read only when such a name exists, because `audit.read` has step-up. A failed audit read leaves the names alone with a note. Entries show for a disconnected host as well. | Brief Design note 2; 24 2.2 rule for names, 10.1; the API has no list of ended assignments. |
| D-M4-37-3 | 2026-10-19 | Host target in the shared wizard: `AdminTargetKind.host`; the target step lists the hosts of `listHosts` below spaces and channels (kept out of the targets that "Who has access?" explains); on a host the level step shows no level and the one fixed capability `terminal`, and `GrantCreate` carries no `level`. The preview shows the server's `text` unchanged and a "Not effective" badge for every hint `terminal_not_effective`; the client tells no cause apart. The host page lists the grants on the host with the server's preview of the same grant (a dry run) and the same badge. `409 i6_violation` is explained everywhere (`adminFailure`, the LLM login section, the confirmation, the preview's invariant) with what I6 means and the server's `hint`. | Brief Design note 4; 07 2.12, 9.3; 24 7.3. |
| D-M4-37-4 | 2026-10-19 | Names that differ from the brief: the page widget is `HostDetail` in `host_page.dart`, because `api_client` exports a model `HostPage`; `CreateHostDialog` is the form of step 1 inside the wizard; `host_labels.dart` holds the labels and the reason dialog of every change ("Confirm" waits for a reason). `HostsRepository` (`ApiHostsRepository`) holds the host calls and `AdminRepository` includes it (as D-M4-27-4). `attempts_remaining` is read from the problem body by the repository (`FingerprintMismatch`), because `ApiProblem` does not carry it. The number in the fingerprint label is the reference default 8 of `terminal.fingerprint_confirm_chars` (the server sends no value); a prefix of another length comes back as `400 validation_failed`. | Brief Boundaries (D-W-100, D-W-114); fixed requirement 9. |
| D-M4-37-5 | 2026-10-19 | The shell lists "Hosts" only when `getMe.is_owner` is true (`HostsRepository.callerIsOwner`); the path `/admin/hosts` stays reachable and the server answers. The state actions follow the life cycle of 24 3.1 from the returned state (continue pairing and re-pair in `pairing` and `confirming`; re-pair, disable, retire when active; enable only after an owner disabled; nothing when retired). Re-pairing asks a reason, issues the code and opens the wizard at step 2; after the last failed fingerprint the wizard returns to step 2, which needs a new code. The fake server stores hosts in `FakeWorld.hosts`, plays `bothy-host pair` with `FakeWorld.pairHost`, writes the assignment audit to `FakeWorld.auditEvents`, and its problems carry extra members (`hint`, `attempts_remaining`). | Brief fixed requirements 11, 13, 17; Scope (fake handlers through the registry of M4-17). |
| D-M4-37-6 | 2026-10-19 | Red team NEIN, fixed: the pairing wizard owns its session user rows (`SessionUserRow`) and sends exactly the complete rows step 3 shows whose name the host still reports; the rows survive "Back" and are dropped when the host falls back to pairing. Before, the rows lived in the row widget and the wizard sent a stale copy after "Back" or the fallback, which binds a name to a person for good on the host. The pickers of a row are keyed by the row, so removing a row keeps the others. | Red team of M4-37 (reproduced Major); brief Design note 1; 24 2.2, ruling T12. |

## M4-29 Administration: audit view
