|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-29-0 | 2026-10-18 | Base `int/m1-wave42` at `c5a86a6`, branch `feat/M4-29-admin-audit` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The audit operations of `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and `audit.read`, `audit.verify` of `actions.yaml` equal those of `origin/main`, so no contract change was brought in. The base keeps the German chapter files, so the A6 line changes in `docs/spec/01-produkt.md` (the brief names `01-product.md` of `origin/main`). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-29-1 | 2026-10-18 | The action filter offers the action IDs of the loaded entries as suggestions of a text field that also takes any typed ID; Enter applies the typed text, a picked suggestion applies at once. Every filter maps one to one to a query parameter of `listAuditEvents`; days are typed as `YYYY-MM-DD` in the person's time zone, "to" is the start of the day after. An action shows its translated label where `l10n` has one (the administration actions), else its ID; a resource shows its kind and ID, because `AuditEvent` carries no name. | Brief Design note 1: the catalogue is not shipped to the client as a list of its own. |
| D-M4-29-2 | 2026-10-18 | "Check chain" sends `verifyAuditChain` without a range while no filter is set (the server checks from the last checkpoint); with a filter it sends the `seq` span of the listed entries as `from_seq` and `to_seq`, because the request takes numbers, not times. | Brief Design note 2; `AuditVerifyRequest` has only `from_seq` and `to_seq`. |
| D-M4-29-3 | 2026-10-18 | "Export" writes the bytes of `exportAuditEvents` unchanged; "Export as CSV" converts the same stream line by line into the columns `occurred_at`, `actor_principal_id`, `action`, `decision`, `resource_kind`, `resource_id`, `deny_reason`, `correlation_id`, `seq`, `hash`, `prev_hash` (header with these field names, RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF), and a cell that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` gets a leading apostrophe. Both need the filter's time range. The save dialog is a port of the host (`AuditSaveTarget`, a `StreamConsumer` of the chosen file), because `admin_ui` also serves the console on the web and the sandboxed desktop app may write only to a file the person picked in the platform's panel; a host without it shows no export buttons. The desktop app wires no panel in this ticket (its files are outside the brief's scope), so the export is reachable once a host passes one. A stream that breaks off says that the file is incomplete. | Brief Design note 3; ADR 0021 (offline verification needs the hash links of the NDJSON). |
| D-M4-29-4 | 2026-10-18 | A streamed response hides its problem body from the `StepUpInterceptor` (Dio passes the raw body), so `exportAudit` first reads one entry of the same range through `listAuditEvents` (same action `audit.read`, same purpose text): the step-up and a `403` arrive on a JSON answer, and the stream follows within `auth.step_up_max_age`. A refused stream itself is read into an `ApiProblem` too. | Found by the fake-server test: the bytes request answered `401 http_401` without the step-up. |
| D-M4-29-5 | 2026-10-18 | `AdminRepository` includes `AuditRepository` (`ApiAdminRepository` delegates to `ApiAuditRepository`), so the shell hands one repository to every screen and the app needs no change; the audit screens use only the audit calls and `allPeople` for the person picker. The audit section of the shell gets the body's height instead of the scroll view, so its list is virtualized. | Brief Scope "Registration of the Audit entry in the admin shell"; fixed rule 2 (the audit code stays in `lib/src/audit/`). |
| D-M4-29-6 | 2026-10-18 | Fixes after the code review: a failed or cancelled load keeps the filter bar (only a `403` or `404` shows the server's reason alone), so a filter the server rejects (`400` for a resource ID that is no UUID) can be changed; a failed "Load more" keeps the loaded entries and retries the same page; a new filter clears the result of the last chain check. | Code review finding: the view lost its filters while the filter still applied to the list, the chain check and the export. |

Option `showCosts` (default on) for topology and backend view. Prose that only contains a colour word passes. The logo `src/img/logo.svg` is an image, not code; a unit test holds its colours to token values. | Brief, requirement 14. |

## M2-43 Control connection between host service and server

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-43-1 | 2026-10-18 | On `hostControl` an unknown op, a server op sent by the host, a second `identify`, a binary frame, invalid JSON and a frame over `sync.ws_max_frame_bytes` (reason `frame_too_large`) close with `4002`. | `hostControl` names no `4001`; every one of these is a frame the server cannot take in this order. |
| D-M2-43-2 | 2026-10-18 | The proof of `identify` is a compact JWS with `alg` exactly `EdDSA`, `typ` exactly `host-auth+jwt` and `kid` the RFC 7638 thumbprint of the login key (`hosts.auth_jkt`), over exactly the claims `challenge`, `host_id` and `iat`; `iat` lies in [`hello.server_time` minus `auth.clock_leeway`, now plus `auth.clock_leeway`]. An unknown host, a host in `pairing` or another `kid` closes with `4110`; every other failure with `4113`. | The `kid` lets the server tell a replaced key ("run `pair` again") from a forged signature; a closed claim set and a short `iat` window keep a proof from being reused outside its exchange. |
| D-M2-43-3 | 2026-10-18 | Audit entries of `host.connect` have empty actor columns and name the host as the resource; a denial for a host without a row has no resource. | Audit actor IDs point to principals with a row; a host is no principal row (07 section 3). |
| D-M2-43-4 | 2026-10-18 | A `report` whose session users changed is the rights change `host.connect` on the host with the people of its valid assignments; its session users go through M2-40's `ApplySessionUserReport` (next `permission_version`, sessions of vanished names end with `access_revoked`, one `host.updated`), the other `reported_*` columns in the same unit. The server-host flag raises no rights version; when it turns on, the sessions of people who are not active owners end with `access_revoked` in the same unit. | Permissions `no` of `host.connect` (05 4.14) does not exempt a report from 03 4.3, which counts the session users as rights state; 03 does not list the flag, which SR10 reads per decision through `authz.HostSource`. |
| D-M2-43-5 | 2026-10-18 | `Host.connected` and `host.presence_changed` mean "usable for sessions": true from the first applied `report` after `ready` until the control connection ends. | A client that retries after `409 host_unavailable` on `connected: true` must not race the first `report`, before which the server sends no `session.open`. |
| D-M2-43-6 | 2026-10-18 | A live duplicate of a host in `confirming` puts it back into `pairing` with its keys dropped (the fallback of M2-41 with a fresh code that is never shown, `host.updated` with `key_changed`); both connections close with `4112`. The life cycle draws `confirming --> pairing` for it (24 3.1, 3.4). | 03 allows `disabled` only after the confirmation; for an unconfirmed host `pairing` without keys is the only state from which only a new pairing helps. |
| D-M2-43-7 | 2026-10-18 | The deny reason `host_duplicate_connection` (decision `forbidden`) joins 07 4.1; a live duplicate writes one deny entry `host.connect` with it and `code: duplicate_connection`, a dead older connection that is replaced one allow entry with `code: stale_connection_replaced`. Until M2-47 the probe runs within the node of the older connection. | The closed list of reasons had no reason for a denial with valid credentials; both outcomes of 24 3.4 must be traceable. |
| D-M2-43-8 | 2026-10-18 | Every session listed in `live_sessions` that is closed for this host, unknown or of another host gets `session.close` right after `ready`: the stored `close_reason`, else `access_revoked`. | A PTY the server no longer allows ends at the next login (24 8.3 "instructs the host service"), fail closed for IDs the server does not know. |
| D-M2-43-9 | 2026-10-18 | Session user names enter the audit details of `host.connect` only as lists of the value kind `auditing.OSUserNames` under the members `hd6_users`, `hd9_users`, `hd10_users`, `hd11_users`, `session_users_added` and `session_users_removed`, each name in the form of `host_session_users.os_user`; those members take nothing else (19 SEC-AUD-5). | M2-39's `OSUserName` is accepted only under `os_user`; a closed set of members with their own form keeps free text out of the details while naming the accounts T-TM-14 needs. |
| D-M2-43-10 | 2026-10-18 | The host service may report only `client_closed`, `process_exited` (with `exit_code`), `idle_timeout`, `detach_expired`, `open_failed` (with any `close_detail`) and `host_policy_violation` (with `subscription_login_detected`, `helper_untrusted` or `shared_location`); `close_detail` exactly with the last two, `exit_code` only with `process_exited`. | The other reasons are decisions of the server (revocation, limits, host state); a host that reports them is wrong or forged and gets `validation_failed` without a change. |
| D-M2-43-11 | 2026-10-18 | The server closes with `4009` when no `heartbeat` arrived for `terminal.host_heartbeat_interval` times (`terminal.host_heartbeat_miss_limit` + 1); `heartbeat_ack` carries `jwks` whenever the set of purpose `terminal` changed since the last set sent on the connection. | The extra interval absorbs the first delay, as M1-64 rules for the gateway; a scheduled rotation publishes the `next` key `agent.jwks_max_age` before use, so a long-lived connection learns it in time (limit after an emergency rotation in 24 5.3). |
| D-M2-43-12 | 2026-10-18 | When a control connection ends without a state change of the host (not `4110`, `4111`, `4112`) and no newer connection of the host is registered on the node, its `active` sessions become `detached`; at shutdown a stop step of `PhaseConnections` detaches and closes every connection with `4013`. | 24 6.2: the PTYs keep running until `terminal.detach_grace`; a close that comes with a state change already ended the sessions. |
| D-M2-43-13 | 2026-10-18 | The host service reacts per close code: `1000`, `1001`, `4000`, `4002`, `4009`, `4014`, `4113` and any unknown code reconnect with exponential backoff and full jitter between `host_service.reconnect_backoff_base` and `host_service.reconnect_backoff_cap`, reset after `ready`; `4013` after a random delay below the base; `4008` after `retry_after`, else the cap; `4111` ends all PTYs and waits the cap; `4110`, `4112` and `4114` end all PTYs and stop with exit code `78`. | The codes of 24 13 name the client action; `78` is the one exit code no service definition restarts (D-M2-42-14). |
| D-M2-43-14 | 2026-10-18 | On a refused start the host service logs in through the same `Control` with empty `live_sessions`, retries for at most `host_service.reconnect_backoff_cap` in total (then `startup.ErrUnreachable`, and the stored declaration decides), sends exactly one `report` and closes with `1000` over the same connection. | D-W-91: the console must name the accounts of a refused start; a separate report connection would be a second login with the same keys. |
| D-M2-43-15 | 2026-10-18 | Recordings in both directions hold the hand-written frames of the host service to the contract: `services/host/testdata/control/host_frames.json` (written by the host test, validated and its proof verified by the server) and `server_frames.json` (written by the server test, decoded strictly by the host); `UPDATE_RECORDINGS=1` (`make -C services/host recordings`) rewrites the host recording and `go test -update` the server recording, because only `cmd/bothy` reads the environment in the server module. The gitleaks configuration allows the rule `jwt` for `host_frames.json` only. | The host module has no generator; M2-38 does the same for Noise. The recorded proof is signed with a test key whose seed is public. |
| D-M2-43-16 | 2026-10-18 | `GET /api/v1/hosts/connect` is the second route marked `AuthorizedInBand` (action `host.connect`, class `rl.host`): the proof and `authorize` run on the connection. The route is mounted only for the roles `api` and `all`, after the gateway, whose hub is the `EphemeralPublisher` of the presence. | Like the gateway (M1-64) the upgrade request carries no credential; counting `rl.host` and failed proofs is M2-46. |
| D-M2-43-17 | 2026-10-18 | The control client takes the proxy from the environment (`http.ProxyFromEnvironment`), unlike `pair`, which uses none (D-M2-42-25). | The brief (Design note 12 a) names it; a long-lived outgoing connection of a host behind a company proxy needs it, and the TLS verification of the server stays end to end. |
| D-M2-43-18 | 2026-10-18 | `startup.Events` is the optional part of a `Control` that keeps its connection: `run` passes every later `ready` to `Service.OnReady` and exits with `78` when the connection stops for good. | M2-42 offers `OnReady` for a second `ready` (`TestDeclarationFromReady`) but no path from the port to it. |
| D-M2-43-19 | 2026-10-18 | The two sections of `docs/api/openapi.yaml` that M2-43 changes stay minimal (no description on `heartbeat_miss_limit` and `HostHeartbeatAck.jwks`), and the description of `HostDoctorCheck.session_user` refers to its schema instead of repeating it; the rules stand in 24 3.5. | The file was 2 bytes below `repo.max_file_kb`; the content is unchanged. |
| D-M2-43-20 | 2026-10-18 | Base without a contract commit: the base `int/m1-wave42` already holds `HostReady.llm_credentials`, `HostIdentify.live_sessions` and the planning rulings that M2-43 relies on (D-W-90, D-W-91, D-W-109 as content of 24 and openapi); the brief, its spec chapters and the core contract are read from `origin/main`. | The task asks to bring a missing contract change in as its own commit only when the base lacks it. |
| D-M2-43-21 | 2026-10-18 | `pair --ca-file` stores the CA in the state directory (`server_ca.pem`, public data; a pairing without `--ca-file` removes it), and `run` trusts it for the control connection in addition to the system roots. | Red team of M2-43: a server with a certificate of a private CA could be paired but never reached by the control connection, so the host never reported and never became usable. |

## M2-44 Terminal sessions, session token and relay

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-44-1 | 2026-10-19 | `ProfileKnown`: the profile must be one the host reports, and either its kind is not `claude` and no `workdir_index` is sent, or its kind is `claude` and `0 <= workdir_index < len(own workdirs)`, where the own workdirs are the list of the caller's own entry in `reported_session_users` (the entry whose `os_user` is the caller's valid assignment; none gives the empty list). A `claude` profile without an index and an index with another profile both give `terminal_profile_unknown`. | 07 4.1 names one reason for "an index of no working directory of the own session user"; one rule in one place (`domain.ProfileKnown`). |
| D-M2-44-2 | 2026-10-19 | The session token is built and signed inside the unit of work of the opening and of the attach, before the commit; a key store failure rolls the session back (`503 unavailable`). | No session exists without a token. The sequence diagram seq-8 shows the order of meaning, not of transactions. |
| D-M2-44-3 | 2026-10-19 | Token encoding: the claims in the order of the table of 24 5.3, IDs as canonical lower-case UUIDs, `iat` truncated to seconds, `exp = iat + terminal.session_token_ttl`, `own` always present (also `false`), `wdi` present exactly for a `claude` profile with `att = open` (also with `0`), no `null`. The protected header has exactly the members `alg`, `kid` and `typ`; `kernel/jose.SignCompact` writes them in its own order (`alg`, `typ`, `kid`). | The host service compares members, not bytes; the shared JOSE profile (ADR 0067) owns the header serialization. |
| D-M2-44-4 | 2026-10-19 | One data connection per grant: every grant (open or attach) replaces the relay slot of its session and sends one `session.open`; the host service opens exactly one data connection per `session.open`. An attach to an `opening` session issues `att = open` with the `wdi` of the opening, which the relay slot of this node keeps (03 does not store it); without that slot the attach answers `409 invalid_state_transition`. 24 6.2 changed accordingly. | A new handshake always runs on a fresh pair of connections, so the host service never has to tell a new message 1 from transport data; 05 allows an attach to every session that is not closed and 24 5.3 allows `wdi` only with `att = open`. |
| D-M2-44-5 | 2026-10-19 | Relay: the first message of each direction since a grant is the handshake and is not counted; every later message adds its length to `bytes_in` or `bytes_out`, a device message sets the time of the last device frame; when a pairing ends for another reason than the end of its session, the counts are written in one update without event or version; the end path takes the live counts (`RelayControl.Counters` takes and clears them, so they are written once). The deadline of a slot is met by `session.started` before the PTY runs and by the pairing afterwards; on a host that reports `require_local_confirm` the pairing meets the deadline of an opening, and a pairing that ends before `session.started` gives the slot a new deadline of `terminal.open_timeout`. The relay logs one record per closed side with session ID, side, frames, bytes, duration and close code. | The server cannot see the handshake; handshake messages are no transport bytes (03); the consent window `host_service.local_confirm_timeout` may be longer than `terminal.open_timeout`; SEC-TERM-1 allows only length, time and session ID. |
| D-M2-44-6 | 2026-10-19 | The host service refuses a handshake by closing its data connection with `4101` (`token_expired`, `token_replayed`) or `4104` (every other detail of 24 5.3) and the `close_detail` as close reason; the relay hands the same code and reason to the device; an opening, and a started session refused with `4104`, end `open_failed` with the detail; a started session refused with `4101` is `detached`. A data connection for a session that is unknown, of another host or closed gets `4100`; without an expectation on the node or while a paired host side exists `1000`; a newcomer replaces an unpaired older host side (`1000` for the older). | 24 5.3 said the host reports the reason but not how; a refusal through `session.ended` reaches the device through `StreamCodeForEnd` with the same code, so the device gets exactly one code either way (R-TM-16). |
| D-M2-44-7 | 2026-10-19 | The effects of an end after its commit come from the event log tail: `internal/wire` hands every event after `Authorizer.Observe` and the hub to `Relay.ObserveLogEvent`, which closes the sides of a session whose `terminal_session.changed` says `closed` (device `StreamCodeForEnd`, host `4100`), drops the slot without writing counters and sends `session.close`. | `txn` has no after-commit hook, and ends commit in transactions of `terminal`, `authz` and `identity`, in api and worker processes and on other nodes; the tail reads every committed row in `seq` order. |
| D-M2-44-8 | 2026-10-19 | After the commit of an opening or an attach the use case calls `authorize` again with the same action and fresh facts under `authorize.Recheck(ctx)` (implies filter mode, passes step 8 only); `HostConnected` keeps the value of the first decision. When it no longer allows, the session ends with `access_revoked`, `i6_violation` (`i6_holder_only`) or the reason of the host state (`host_disabled`, `host_retired`, `host_rekeyed`), no `session.open` is sent and the answer is the recheck's error. | 03 6.3 locks `permission_version` only for rights changes, so a revocation can commit between the decision and the commit of the opening; the recheck can only end what the decision allowed. |
| D-M2-44-9 | 2026-10-19 | Start of the roles `api` and `all`: before the public listener opens, every `opening` session ends `open_failed` `open_timeout` and every `active` session becomes `detached`, one unit of work per session in ascending id. The key `cluster.mode` does not exist on this base (planned with M2-47), so the step always runs; M2-47 adds the key and skips the step with `multi`. Stop: the stop step `terminal_relay_connections` in `PhaseConnections` closes every relay connection with `4013`. | Slots and connections lived only in the memory of the previous process; a single node is the only mode this build runs. |
| D-M2-44-10 | 2026-10-19 | `listTerminalSessions` orders by `id` descending with a keyset cursor on `id`. With `host_id` the collection check is `terminal.read_own` on that host; without it the catalogue has no instance resource for the action, so the check runs in filter mode on a descriptor of an own session that no host holds: the gate and the liveness of the principal answer, `not_found` only means that the rows decide, each through its own check. | 03 has no index for all sessions of a person and the core contract is not edited; M2-40 gives `terminal.read_own` the resources `terminal_session` and `host` only. |
| D-M2-44-11 | 2026-10-19 | `closeTerminalSession`: the reason passes `textcheck.DisplayText` and has at most `audit.reason_max_chars` characters, else `400 validation_failed` at `/reason`; it is the mandatory reason of the action and stands only in the audit details (`reason`). An own session ends `client_closed`, another person's (owner) `closed_by_owner`, with `closed_by`; an already closed session answers `200` unchanged with `no_change: true`. | 03 allows free text in `audit_events.details` only as the mandatory reason of an administrative action; 06 2 "No effect". |
| D-M2-44-12 | 2026-10-19 | Close codes of the device stream before the relay: invalid JSON, an unknown member, a binary frame before `identify` and `protocol_version` other than 1 give `4002`; no `identify` within `terminal.open_timeout`, an invalid token, proof, nonce, `htu` or `device_id`, `identify` without `access_token` and any `forbidden` other than `not_self` give `4004`; `unauthenticated` with `principal_inactive`, `session_revoked` or `device_inactive` `4012`; `not_found` and an own closed session (reason = its `close_reason`) `4100`; `not_self` `4103`. A device without an expectation on this node gets `4102`. Authentication failures are recorded as a denial of `terminal.stream` for the anonymous principal on the session (aggregated, AZ-P13). | Every code is one of `terminalStream`; the denial of a stream that never authenticated has no principal to name. |
| D-M2-44-13 | 2026-10-19 | Base without a contract commit: `int/m1-wave45` holds the claim table of 24 5.3 with `own`, `osu` and `wdi`, the schemas `TerminalSession*`, `TerminalStream*` and `HostIdentify.session_id`, the purpose `terminal` and the five `terminal.*` actions; the rulings D-W-62, D-W-63, D-W-69 and D-W-105 the brief names are planning rulings whose content is already in 24, 07 and signals.md there. The brief, its spec chapters and the core contract are read from `origin/main`. | The task asks to bring a missing contract change in as its own commit only when the base lacks it. |
| D-M2-44-14 | 2026-10-19 | `docs/api/openapi.yaml` stays at most `repo.max_file_kb`: the descriptions of `openTerminalSession`, `workdir_index` and `TerminalStreamIdentify` are shortened while the brief's additions (the `client_ephemeral_key_hash` pattern, the attach to an opening session, the rule of `workdir_index`) are in. | The file was 18 bytes below the gate; the rules stand in 24. |
| D-M2-44-15 | 2026-10-19 | Integration tests run on the full-server harness (`test/harness`) instead of the M2-39 host world: real access tokens, DPoP proofs and device-key step-ups, H1 paired and confirmed through the API. The harness gains `Options.TerminalTimers` (passed as `wire.Deps.TerminalTimers`, also the deadline of `identify`) and `Client.Proof`; `api/specschema.ValidateChannelFrame` gains the channel `terminalStream`. Windows of the step-up and the quick reattach are moved in the database (`step_up_at`, `detached_at`) instead of a clock. | The device stream must authenticate a real access token with a real proof; the fake authenticator of the host world cannot. No test sleeps for a window. |
| D-M2-44-16 | 2026-10-19 | `terminal.Register` lists every op of `terminalStream` and `hostData` (`hello`, `identify`, `error`, the Noise frames), in both directions. | The brief asks for all of them for AZ-P2; M2-43 listed only the ops a host sends on `hostControl`. |
| D-M2-44-17 | 2026-10-19 | The test seam `Deps.BeforeOpenCommit` lets a unit test commit a revocation between the decision and the commit; the race is proven with fakes in `app` (`TestRecheck_EndsSessionWithMappedReason`), not over HTTP. | The full-server harness cannot reach into a use case; the recheck itself is the same code either way. |
| D-M2-44-18 | 2026-10-19 | The ticket is built and integrated as the task of the integration lane says: commits on `feat/M2-44-terminal-sessions-relay`, no push, no pull request, no local merge. | The task overrides the landing steps of the brief (AGENTS.md); the integration lane lands the branch. |

## M1-69 Canary test across all outputs

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |

| M1-69/2 | 2026-10-18 | Two fields of error responses count as echoes to the caller, not as sink 5: `current` of `412 version_conflict` and `409 base_version_conflict` (06 "Errors" item 3: the representation of a resource the caller may read) and the `pointer` of an `unknown_member` error (W7, M1-18/3: it names the member the caller sent). Content there is allowed like in a success response; a secret there stays a finding. `TestSEC_T10_ScanEchoFields` holds the rule. | The contract defines both members; removing them would break M1-18/3 and the 412 contract, and neither tells the caller anything it did not send or may not read. Every other error field stays content-free. |

No new span or metric. |
| M1-69/4 | 2026-10-18 | The test seams are exported fields with a production zero value: `otel.Deps.Exporters`, `wire.Deps.TelemetryExporters`, `wire.Deps.OIDCRootCAs` (ADR 0071). An injected exporter replaces the OTLP exporter of its signal, including the metric exporter. | No collector and no network in tests; one composition root. |
| M1-69/5 | 2026-10-18 | The harness writes the client secret of the fake OIDC provider as a file of the secrets directory (`Options.SecretFiles`, referenced as `file:<dir>/idp/<name>`), not as `env:BOTHY_IDP_<name>`. | The configuration loader refuses a `BOTHY_` variable that no configuration key references, so an `env:` reference stored in a provider row cannot start the server. Reported as a minor in the report, not changed here. |
| M1-69/6 | 2026-10-18 | The scenario server uses `PublicHost: localhost` (listeners stay on 127.0.0.1): with an IP address as host of `server.public_url` passkeys are off (M1-40), so the passkey ways could not run. | WebAuthn needs a domain name as relying party; `localhost` is the one http origin it allows. |
| M1-69/7 | 2026-10-18 | Texts shorter than eight characters are not searched; six-digit TOTP codes are registered but cannot be proven absent. The detector meta test for logs goes through the OTLP log pipeline of the server (the global logger provider of `otel.Setup`), because the server logger is not reachable from outside `internal/wire`. | A six-digit needle matches unrelated digits of timestamps and counters; the OTLP pipeline applies the same catalogue filter and queue as the server's own records. |

Console step-ups (password and OIDC re-authentication) run on a console session of the owner (cookie), passkeys on Alex. | One scenario takes about a minute and a half; three runs would triple `make test` for the same data. ## M1-73 Compose smoke test

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-73/1 | 2026-10-19 | The task names the branch and the base: `feat/M1-73-compose-smoke` from `int/m1-wave46`; the work is not merged locally (the brief's Definition of done says so), the launcher integrates it. No contract commit is brought in: every operation, schema and gateway op the brief names is on the base already. | The task overrides the brief where both name a branch or a merge. |
| M1-73/2 | 2026-10-19 | Design note D1: the probe runs with `network_mode: service:api` and dials `127.0.0.1:8080`; `api` listens on `127.0.0.1:8080` and publishes no port; `server.trusted_proxies` keeps the installation's loopback default. | Through a published port the server sees the bridge gateway, which SEC-TLS-1 rejects for plaintext; widening the trusted proxies would weaken the rule under test (P-10, 16 4.1). |
| M1-73/3 | 2026-10-19 | Design note D2: the overlay drops only `BOTHY_FILE__SCAN__CLAMD_ADDRESS` (`file.scan.clamd_address`, planned for M3) from `x-server-env`. `bothy config check` of the M1 image with the installation values reports only this key as unknown; the other `BOTHY_FILE__*` keys (`file.storage.driver`, `file.fs.root`, `file.s3.*`) are active and stay. No other value is changed. | The brief expected every `BOTHY_FILE__*` key; the server is the authority, and D2 allows removals of rejected keys only. |
| M1-73/4 | 2026-10-19 | Design note D4: the collector overlay `file` lives in `infra/compose/test/otel/` with `tail_sampling.decision_wait` and `batch.timeout` of 1 s and `flush_interval` 1 s; `BOTHY_TRACE_SAMPLE_PERCENT=100`. The collector keeps the image's user; the one-shot `otel-out-init` (the pinned PostgreSQL image, root, no network) makes the export volume writable. `tools/opscheck` validates the overlay with the base alone and reads it in `collector-allowlist` and the provider rule. | Every trace of the run must be exported and the wait after the flush must stay short; running the collector as root could hide a permission defect of its secrets. |
| M1-73/5 | 2026-10-19 | Design note D6: the scan first finds the run canary in a built-in control text (positive control) and fails afterwards unless the export holds a span with `http.route` `/api/v1/threads/{thread_id}/messages`, a log record and a metric data point of `service.name` `bothy-server`; `SMOKE_TELEMETRY_SETTLE_S` defaults to 5 s, at least the 3 s of `decision_wait` + `batch.timeout` + `flush_interval` (`test_settle_covers_collector_waits`). The enrollment code is scanned in its display and its canonical form. | A passing scan must prove something: a broken scanner or an empty export cannot pass. |
| M1-73/6 | 2026-10-19 | Design note D7: the probe image is the target `smoke` of `services/server/Dockerfile` (stage `smoke-build` from the builder stage, stage `smoke` on the same distroless reference as production, user 65532); the production stage is unchanged and last. The build context of `smoke-probe` is the repository root with `dockerfile: services/server/Dockerfile`, not `../../services/server` as the brief writes, and `smoke.sh` passes the build argument `VERSION` from `services/server/VERSION`. `Dockerfile.dockerignore` re-includes only `services/server/test/smoke/` (its `_test.go` files stay out). | The Dockerfile copies `services/server/...` paths from the root context, and the builder stage refuses a build without `VERSION`; reusing it keeps one pin. |
| M1-73/7 | 2026-10-19 | `GatewayHTU` and `RESTHTU` follow the server (M1-33): the gateway htu is `https://<host of server.public_url>/api/v1/gateway`, http on port 80 loses the port, a path of the public URL does not count; the REST htu is scheme and host of the public URL plus the request path. | The server checks the proof against exactly this form; the brief asks to follow the documented rule. |
| M1-73/8 | 2026-10-19 | The probe command reads `SMOKE_PUBLIC_URL` and `SMOKE_CANARY` with `os.Getenv` under a `nolint:forbidigo` with its reason; the lint configuration is unchanged. | The rule keeps server modules on `platform/config`; the probe is a separate test binary whose only inputs from Compose are these two values, as the brief prescribes. |
| M1-73/9 | 2026-10-19 | `smoke.sh` creates its workspace with `mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/bothy-smoke.XXXXXXXX"` and removes only that directory; `TMPDIR` must be shared with the Docker VM (documented in `infra/compose/test/README.md`). | The probe and the scanner use bind mounts below it; colima shares only the home directory by default. |

## D-W-20 Closed problem codes (brought in from main for M3-03)

The planning ruling D-W-20 is on `main` (`docs/DECISIONS-PLANNING.md`) but not on this integration base. M3-03 relies on it for the problem codes of the upload operations, so its delta for those operations (openapi `createUpload`, `uploadPart`, `completeUpload`, chapter 12 sections 3.1 to 3.6) is brought in first, as its own commit. The row is copied with the links adjusted to the chapter names of this base.

| ID | Ruling | Reason | Place |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-W-20 | Problem codes are a closed list: chapters, briefs and plan name only codes from `x-problem-codes`; finer reasons stand in `reason` (for `authorize`, the reason code from 07 4.1) or in `errors[].code` of `400 validation_failed`. The codes so far named only in chapters are mapped, for example `413 payload_too_large` instead of `file_too_large`, `409 conflict` with `reason` `quota_exceeded`, `503 unavailable` with `reason` `storage_full` or `search_timeout`, respectively, `400 validation_failed` with `errors[].code` `patch_path_forbidden`, `403 forbidden` with `reason` `owner_only`. The check `tools/docs/problem_code_refs.py` runs in `make docs`. | Finding L-10: about a dozen codes and statuses (507, 422 outside idempotency) were attached to no operation; the same class of error without a gate. | [06 5 item 7](spec/06-api.md), R-API-28, [openapi.yaml](api/openapi.yaml) `createUpload`, `uploadPart`, `completeUpload`, `bindAgentSession`, [12 3](spec/12-dateien.md), [09](spec/09-agenten.md), [14 7](spec/14-suche.md) |

## M3-03 Upload sessions, parts and completion

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M3-03-1 | 2026-10-19 | `UploadCreate.sha256` is required and lowercase hex (`^[0-9a-f]{64}$`); the verify pass checks it. | The base64 form of 12 3.1 and the optional hex of openapi disagreed; a required hex value is what the client already computes and what `File.sha256` shows (design note 19). |
| D-M3-03-2 | 2026-10-19 | Parts are stored per driver through one `PartStore` (`internal/files/app/partstore.go`), chosen in `internal/wire` from `file.storage.driver`: `fs` writes part `n` as the object `uploads/<file_id>/<n>`, discards by deleting every key from 1 to `part_count`; `s3` creates the multipart upload lazily at the first part outside any transaction, stores its id with a conditional update (the loser aborts its own upload), uploads part `n` and keeps the ETag, discards with `AbortMultipart`. `CompleteMultipart` and the joining of file system parts run in the verify pass of M3-04, not in `completeUpload`. | `createUpload` and `completeUpload` accept `Idempotency-Key`, the idempotency stage runs their handlers inside the unit of work (02 8.4), and 02 8.5 forbids object store calls inside a transaction; 12 3.2 and 3.4 are updated. |
| D-M3-03-3 | 2026-10-19 | The ETag of a file system part is the quoted lowercase hex SHA-256 of the part, derived from `file_upload_parts.sha256`; `file_upload_parts.etag` stays NULL for it. | 03 names `etag` the ETag of an S3 part; the derived value equals the ETag M3-01's driver computes (M3-01/6). |
| D-M3-03-4 | 2026-10-19 | Check order of `createUpload`: authorize, size, name cleaning with the early type check, then in one unit of work quota, open sessions, free space, insert. | 12 3.1 numbers authorize, size, quota, sessions and space; the type check needs no database, so it comes right after the size. |
| D-M3-03-5 | 2026-10-19 | Early type check: with an empty `file.denied_mime_types` nothing is checked (an unparsable declared type is then stored as NULL); otherwise the declared type without parameters in lower case (`mime.ParseMediaType`; unparsable: `400 validation_failed`, pointer `/declared_mime_type`, code `invalid`) or the extension of the cleaned name, ignoring case, against the extension of each denied type gives `415 unsupported_media_type` with reason `file_type_denied`. Extensions come from `github.com/gabriel-vasile/mimetype` (`mimetype.Lookup`, one extension per type) through the port `TypeExtensions`. | The standard library's `mime.ExtensionsByType` reads tables of the operating system and differs between hosts; 12 6.1 names the library; the second check against the detected type is M3-04. |
| D-M3-03-6 | 2026-10-19 | The quota sums `size_bytes` of the principal's files with `deleted_at IS NULL AND upload_state <> 'failed'`. | Failed files count as deleted because their objects go at once (12 12); an aborted upload must not block the quota until `file.failed_row_ttl`. |
| D-M3-03-7 | 2026-10-19 | Before the quota and session counts `createUpload` takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('bothy.files.uploads:' \|\| principal, 0))`, a domain lock in step 2 of 03 6.3. | Without it two parallel creates of one principal both pass a limit (`TestCreateUpload_ParallelAtLimit`). |
| D-M3-03-8 | 2026-10-19 | At `file.upload_max_open_sessions` open sessions (state open, `expires_at` in the future) `createUpload` answers `429 rate_limited` with `Retry-After` until the earliest expiry, rounded up to whole seconds and at least one; `detail` names the key. | 12 3.6 names no code, and `rate_limited` is the only code with status 429. |
| D-M3-03-9 | 2026-10-19 | Name cleaning: NFC (port `NameNormalizer`, adapter with `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm`), the last segment after a slash or backslash, invalid UTF-8, Cc controls, the bidi controls of `kernel/textcheck` (now exported as `IsBidiControl`) and the zero-width characters U+200B to U+200D, U+2060, U+FEFF removed, trimmed; empty, `.` and `..` become `file`; above `file.name_max_bytes` the extension stays when it alone is shorter than the limit and the stem is cut at a rune boundary. Names are never rejected, and the schema minimum of `file.name_max_bytes` is 4 so `file` fits. | 12 6.2; a `400` for an already cleaned name gives the client nothing to correct; one source for the bidi set. |
| D-M3-03-10 | 2026-10-19 | `Content-Digest` is parsed as an RFC 8941 dictionary; the member `sha-256` must be a byte sequence of 32 bytes, other members are ignored. Malformed: `400 validation_failed`, pointer `#header/Content-Digest`, code `invalid`; missing: the request validator's code `required` at the same pointer (the header is required in openapi). A wrong digest answers `part_digest_mismatch` at `#header/Content-Digest`, a wrong length (a shorter or longer body, an early end) `part_digest_mismatch` with an empty pointer. | RFC 9530; the validator of M1-18 answers a missing required header before the handler, with its own code. |
| D-M3-03-11 | 2026-10-19 | Answers per session state: `uploadPart` and `getUpload` give `410 gone` for aborted, expired or an open session past `expires_at`; a completed session answers `getUpload` with `complete` and a repeated part with the confirmed one; `completeUpload` answers a completed session `200` with the file and an ended one `410`; `abortUpload` answers aborted or expired `204` without change and completed `409 invalid_state_transition`. | 06 7 (a DELETE of an ended resource succeeds); 12 3.6; a completed session has no expiry of its own (12 2). |
| D-M3-03-12 | 2026-10-19 | `UploadComplete.parts` is cross-checked: a duplicate number `400` (pointer `/parts`, code `duplicate`), a number outside 1 to `part_count` `400` (code `out_of_range`), a listed etag that differs from the confirmed one `409 conflict` `part_conflict`; the list may name fewer parts than exist; missing confirmed parts give `409 conflict` `upload_incomplete` with the missing numbers as ranges in `detail`. | In mode direct the confirmed rows are the truth (12 3.4 point 1). |
| D-M3-03-13 | 2026-10-19 | `UploadState.upload_state` maps the session: open without a confirmed part `pending`, open with at least one `uploading`, completed `complete`. | The schema has its own enum, not the one of `File.upload_state`. |
| D-M3-03-14 | 2026-10-19 | The same new part number sent twice in parallel: both stream, the first committed row wins, the other answers `200` with the same digest or `409 part_conflict`; the later write may replace the bytes of the winner, which the verify pass of M3-04 catches with `checksum_mismatch` (and on S3 the stored ETag no longer matches at `CompleteMultipart`). A confirmed part is never written again. | 12 4.4 fixes the part key; no wrong byte is ever delivered. |
| D-M3-03-15 | 2026-10-19 | A lost read right (authorize answers `not_found` for an existing own session) aborts the open session in its own unit of work in `uploadPart`, `getUpload` and `abortUpload`, then answers `404`; `completeUpload` only answers `404`; a `403` aborts nothing. | 12 3.6; the idempotency stage rolls back every final error of a keyed request, so an abort there cannot persist; the session ends at the next request without key or by expiry (M3-08). |
| D-M3-03-16 | 2026-10-19 | `file.updated` carries `audience_principal_id` = uploader before attachment; `createUpload` and `completeUpload` write no event. The builder `app.FileUpdated` is kept for M3-04 to M3-10. | `ResourceFromEvent` carries neither uploader nor attachment, so without an audience the hub and catch-up would deliver the file name to every reader of the channel (05 2.2); 03 `event_log` allows an audience with a receive action other than `self`; neither state of the file changes on create or complete. |
| D-M3-03-17 | 2026-10-19 | Visibility of kind `file`: purpose `attachment` and not attached only for the uploader with read on the path of the scope channel (dm: participant); attached for readers of the message's path; `avatar`, `branding`, `gdpr_export` not visible until M3-10 and M7-06. `authorize.Resource.File` (`FileFacts{Purpose, Attached}`) is required in request mode, a missing value is an internal error; only filter and delivery mode, which decide stored events by their scope, may leave it out. `file.read` requires read; `file.delete` keeps self or manage with the same visibility. | 05 2.2 and 07 4.3; fail closed, so no caller widens access by omitting the facts. |
| D-M3-03-18 | 2026-10-19 | `File.sha256` is shown only when `upload_state = 'complete'`. | The schema says "of the stored bytes"; before the verify pass only the declared value exists. |
| D-M3-03-19 | 2026-10-19 | Start checks: `upload_part_plan` (a file of `file.max_upload_bytes` in parts of `file.upload_part_bytes` has at most the S3 part-count limit of parts), `upload_part_size_s3` (with the driver s3 the part size is not below the S3 minimum part size), `presigned_mode_unavailable` (`file.s3.presigned_upload = true` fails). The S3 limits are protocol constants (12 3.1, Q1). | Otherwise every upload of more parts would fail at `CompleteMultipart`, and the presigned mode stays off until the proofs of 12 3.3 exist (R-FI-02, RK-42). |
| D-M3-03-20 | 2026-10-19 | `abortUpload` commits the abort and `file.updated` first and calls `PartStore.Discard` after the commit; a storage error there is logged with `bothy.file.id` only and the answer stays `204`. | 02 8.5; leftovers belong to the cleanup of failed files (12 12, M3-08). |

The two summaries carry no information beyond the key, no gate or generator reads them, and the gate must not be loosened; the split stays the owner's decision (report). |

## M2-02 Channel behaviour and agent configuration

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-02-1 | 2026-10-19 | The task names the branch and the base: `feat/M2-02-channel-behavior-config` from `int/m1-wave46` (`9c05469`); the work is neither merged locally nor pushed, the launcher integrates it. The base holds the core contract in its earlier language; its entries `channel.behavior.read`, `channel.behavior.update`, `agent.config.read`, `channel.settings_changed` and `agent.config_changed` equal those of the authoritative contract in content, so no contract change is brought in. The full `make check` of the start check ran at the end, because three full suites were running on the machine when the work began. | The launcher's task overrides the brief's branch and landing steps; at most three full suites may run at once. |
| D-M2-02-2 | 2026-10-19 | `putChannelBehavior` runs in one unit of work without a rights change: `authorize` first (unreadable `404`, without `manage` `403 capability_missing` before any step-up, stale step-up `401`), then the space row `FOR SHARE` and the channel row `FOR UPDATE`, then If-Match against the locked `settings_version` (a weak, list or non-decimal value is a mismatch, `412` with `current` the behaviour at that version), then the field rules, then `409 conflict` for an archived channel or a channel of an archived space, then the no-op check. | Parent before child as `setSpaceOperator` locks (03 6.3); comparing after the lock makes two parallel PUTs with one If-Match end in one `200` and one `412`, and the effective operator read after the locks is the one at commit. |
| D-M2-02-3 | 2026-10-19 | Field rules, all failures in one `400 validation_failed`: `history_backfill_limit` below zero is the schema code, above `channel.history_backfill_limit_max` `too_large`; a prompt above `channel.prompt_max_bytes` bytes of UTF-8 `too_long` (not `413`), stored unchanged without trimming or character check except U+0000 (`invalid_characters`), which PostgreSQL `text` cannot store; a skill reference empty `required`, with a C0, C1 or bidi control or invalid UTF-8 `invalid_characters` (`kernel/textcheck`), repeated `duplicate`, each on `/skills/<i>`. No detail repeats the input. The limits are read at request time: a stored value above a later lowered maximum reads back unchanged, and a PUT that keeps it fails until it is lowered. No count or length limit of skill references beyond `http.max_json_body_bytes` (spec gap); a reference too large for the unique index answers `too_long` on `/skills` instead of `500` (red team of M2-02). | 03 leaves the codes open; the prompt is a multi-line instruction, not a display text of SEC-IN-4; the unique index `(channel_id, skill_ref)` makes a duplicate a field error. |
| D-M2-02-4 | 2026-10-19 | Events: every effective change appends `channel.settings_changed` (receive action `channel.read`), and only a change of prompt or skills also `agent.config_changed` to the effective operator read after the locks (receive action `agent.config.read`), in this order, both with resource `channel` without version and the payload `{channel_id, settings_version}`. A channel without effective operator gets no `agent.config_changed`. One function, `Service.settingsChanged`, raises `settings_version` and appends them; M2-03 calls it for binding changes with the configuration flag set. | 05 6 and 09 4.4 name prompt, skills and project binding; a switch-only change reaches the operator through `channel.settings_changed`, which it receives by the operator rule. |
| D-M2-02-5 | 2026-10-19 | Audit of `channel.behavior.update`: one allow entry per successful request with `space_id`, `previous_settings_version`, `settings_version`, `changed` (codes `require_mention`, `thread_require_mention`, `free_response`, `auto_thread`, `allow_mentioned_agents`, `history_backfill_limit`, `prompt`, `skills` in this order), the new switches and `history_backfill_limit`, and `skills_count`; a no-op entry has `no_change: true` and equal versions. Never the prompt or a skill reference. | SEC-AUD-5: free text never enters audit details. |
| D-M2-02-6 | 2026-10-19 | `Scope` serves `agent.config.read`: for an agent the channels that are not archived (nor in an archived space) whose effective operator it is and that it reads after the acting-for intersection, and their spaces; nothing fully visible, not instance-wide. Any other kind gets `403 forbidden` with `subject_kind_not_allowed`. `listServedChannels` filters with both lists, orders by channel id, drops archived channels and checks every item in filter mode (a refusal is logged without ids and counted as `filter_mismatch`). The skills are read in the same statement as the channels. | Lists count through `Scope` (02 9.3), and M1-28 allowed `Scope` only for read-level actions; one statement keeps a list item consistent with a concurrent change. |

## M4-31 Client metrics and crash reports

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-31-0 | 2026-10-19 | Base `int/m1-wave46` at `9c05469`, branch `feat/M4-31-client-metrics` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The contract the brief needs (`submitClientMetrics`, `client_metrics.submit`, `client.metrics.flush_interval`, R-CL-10, D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-116) is on the base, so nothing is ported. The base names the chapters in German (`15-client-architektur.md`, `adr/0057-client-telemetrie-ueber-einen-server-endpunkt.md`); the rulings stand in a section of the ticket because the base has no section 10. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the server use case lives in `internal/stats/app` and `internal/stats/domain` with the adapters `httpapi` and `otelmetrics` (02 4 layers), the build define of the crash target is `CRASH_DSN` (with `CRASH_ENVIRONMENT`, next to the upper case `BRANDING_*` defines), and the app wiring is `lib/telemetry/telemetry_providers.dart`. | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-31-1 | 2026-10-19 | Histogram path: the app histograms reach the Collector as own cumulative OTLP histogram data points. `metrics.AggregatedSet` (a `sdkmetric.Producer` on the OTLP metric reader) keeps one histogram per name and attribute set with the bounds of signals.md 4.7 and adds the client's bucket counts unchanged; count and bucket counts are exact, the sum is an upper bound (each value at its bucket's upper bound), minimum and maximum are absent. An entry with a bound outside the 4.7 row, a repeated bound or a negative count is discarded; discarded entries count in `bothy.client_metrics.discarded` by reason (added to signals.md). The app clamps a value above the top bound into the top bucket (`le` 10). | ADR 0057 left the path open; one measurement per bucket count would let the SDK re-aggregate and could not keep the counts exact. Brief Design note 3. |
| D-M4-31-2 | 2026-10-19 | The sender takes the collected entries every `client.metrics.flush_interval` and when the app quits or signs out, sends them with `submitClientMetrics` outside the outbox, split at `maxItems` (200), only while `privacy.client_metrics` is on (off: the entries are dropped, nothing waits). A network error keeps the newest batch for the next interval and drops older ones; any other error drops the batch; `403 feature_disabled` stops the sender until the setting changes. | Telemetry is not user data and the operation is idempotency-exempt; never more than one batch waits (brief Design note 2, 01 11). |
| D-M4-31-3 | 2026-10-19 | The crash target comes from the build configuration only (define `CRASH_DSN`); `BuildBranding.hasCrashTarget` is true when it is set, replacing the stand-in define `BRANDING_CRASH_REPORTS` of D-M4-25-4. Sentry starts only with a target and `privacy.crash_reports` on (default on with a target) and closes when the switch is turned off. Options: no default PII, no screenshots, no view hierarchy, no replay, no user interaction breadcrumbs, no traces; `beforeSend` and `beforeBreadcrumb` clear messages, exception values, titles, request data with every header and body, user, extra, non-technical contexts (an allow-list), file paths outside `package:` and `dart:` frames and the URL parts after the path, and replace path segments that look like IDs, tokens or file names by `:id`; the hashed instance ID is the tag `instance`. | 15 16 allows the build configuration or the instance settings, but no instance setting key exists (a core contract change, out of scope). The canary test found that a context set by the app survived a deny-list, so contexts use an allow-list. |
| D-M4-31-4 | 2026-10-19 | `tools/ci/dart_sdk_denylist.py` runs in `make licenses` over the root `pubspec.lock` and fails on a package matching an OpenTelemetry, analytics or monitoring pattern; `sentry_flutter` and its Dart core `sentry` are exempt by name, because the lock file has no dependency graph. | ADR 0057 "Confirmation"; brief Design note 6. |
| D-M4-31-5 | 2026-10-19 | Crash reports start and metrics are sent only after the personal settings of the signed-in person are read from the local database (`personalSettingsLoadedProvider`); before the login and while the settings load, both are off, and a sign-out closes Sentry. | The code review reproduced that the defaults applied while the settings loaded started Sentry at every app start, also for a person who had turned crash reports off (01 11, 15 16: the person can turn them off). The stored choice exists only per instance after the login. |
| D-M4-31-6 | 2026-10-19 | Crash reports cover Dart errors only: `enableNativeCrashHandling` and `enableAutoSessionTracking` are off. | The native handlers of `sentry_flutter` (a breakpad minidump with stack memory on Windows, the native `beforeSend` on macOS) and the session pings never pass the Dart scrubber, so they could carry content past `beforeSend` (15 16, 17 rule 3). Native crash reports need a native scrubber first, which is outside this ticket. |

## M3-04 Scan pass with clamd

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M3-04-1 | 2026-10-20 | A transient failure of the pass (PING failed, connection error, timeout, a reply the parser refuses, an object store error) returns no error to River: in a short unit of work that checks again that the file awaits the pass, the handler inserts the next `files.scan` with `Round + 1` at `now + NextScanDelay(Round, file.scan.health_interval, file.scan.retry_backoff_cap)` and returns `nil`. The delay doubles from `file.scan.health_interval` per round, never exceeds the cap and saturates for any round (no overflow; the round stops growing at the largest `int64`). Only a failed database unit returns its error to River. | 12 5.3 allows no upper limit of attempts while the file is not deleted; River's attempt limit would end the retries (brief design note 7). |
| D-M3-04-2 | 2026-10-20 | `files.scan` is registered without River job timeout: `platform/jobs.HandlerOptions` gains `Timeout` (zero: River's default, negative: none, mapped to River's per-worker timeout). The pass bounds itself with `file.scan.timeout`; PING uses `file.scan.health_interval` as deadline. | River's default job timeout (1 minute) is shorter than `file.scan.timeout` and would cut the unit of work that follows the scan. |
| D-M3-04-3 | 2026-10-20 | Outcome precedence (first row wins): size different, SHA-256 different, detected type denied (`failed` with `size_mismatch`, `checksum_mismatch`, `file_type_denied`, objects deleted), then `file.scan.mode = off` (`skipped`), then the clamd verdict: `clean`; `error` with `scan_limits_exceeded`; `infected` with `malware_detected`; `error` with `scan_stream_limit_exceeded`; `error` with `scan_engine_error`. The three failed rows decide also when the scan failed transiently, and a `FOUND` for such a file writes the malware audit entry as well. | Size, digest and type are known from the complete stream; `file_type_denied` is the reason of the early `415`; the audit on failed files closes the gap that a wrong checksum could hide a detection. |
| D-M3-04-4 | 2026-10-20 | A clamd reply the parser refuses (`ErrClamdProtocol`) leaves the file `pending` and retries; a connection that clamd closes without any byte is a transport error, not a protocol error. The reply is bounded by `file.scan.chunk_bytes`. | A broken reply says nothing about the file; failing it would block clean files. A closed connection is how a vanished clamd looks behind a socket. |
| D-M3-04-5 | 2026-10-20 | `scan_engine = 'clamd'` and `scanned_at` are set exactly when clamd's verdict decided the file; both stay NULL for `skipped` and `failed`. `files.sha256` and `files.size_bytes` take the computed values when the file becomes `complete`. | The columns record who checked the bytes; a skipped or failed file was not decided by clamd. |
| D-M3-04-6 | 2026-10-20 | The detected type is stored without parameters, trimmed and in lower case (`domain.MediaType`); it is denied when it equals an entry of `file.denied_mime_types` treated the same way (`domain.TypeDenied`). The early check of M3-03 keeps its own matcher in `app/create.go`, because it also matches file extensions; both compare media types by the same rule. An empty head is `application/octet-stream`. The detector reads only the buffered head of `file.sniff_bytes` and never unpacks an archive; `mimetype.NewDetector` sets the process-wide read limit of the library to `file.sniff_bytes`, because its default of 4 KiB would miss Office markers further in (code review finding). | 12 6.1 and SEC-UP-2, SEC-UP-7; the library calls an empty input `text/plain`. |
| D-M3-04-7 | 2026-10-20 | Source of the pass: `Head(object_key)` first; an existing object is read once with `Get` (an earlier attempt joined it; objects are never overwritten). A missing object with a `storage_upload_id` (S3) is first completed by the pass with `CompleteMultipart` over the confirmed parts, because merged M3-03 leaves the multipart upload open at completion (D-M3-03-2); otherwise the parts `uploads/<file_id>/<n>` are read in order and joined into `object_key` through a pipe from the same stream. The source is limited to the declared size plus one byte; the scanner gets the tee, and whatever it leaves is drained. | Brief design note 4 with the merged M3-03 layout: the file system pass reads every byte once (D-FI-04 holds); S3 joins server side. |
| D-M3-04-8 | 2026-10-20 | The result unit of work locks the session row and then the files row `FOR UPDATE`, the order of the completion and the abort of M3-03, checks again that the file awaits the pass and otherwise changes nothing; a run that lost the race or found the file deleted removes only the object it joined itself, and only when the current state is not deliverable. Objects of failed and blocked files and the parts are deleted after the commit; a failed delete is logged and left to M3-08. | 03 6.3 asks to keep the order M3-03 uses; the losing run must not remove the object of a clean file, and must not leave the object of a blocked or deleted one. |
| D-M3-04-9 | 2026-10-20 | Compose: clamd listens only on `/run/clamav/clamd.sock` in the named volume `clamav-socket` (mounted in `clamav`, read-only in `worker`, in no other service), `LocalSocketMode 666`, no `TCPSocket`, no ports and no expose; `infra/compose/clamav/clamd.conf` (from the image's own configuration of the pinned version) replaces the image's file read-only. The volume is created owned by root, so the service's entrypoint runs `install -d -o clamav -g clamav -m 0755 /run/clamav` before the image's `/init`. The health check pings over the socket (`clamdscan --ping 1`, which reads `LocalSocket` from the configuration), not over TCP. | 19 SEC-TLS-5; tried against the pinned image: without the entrypoint step clamd cannot bind the socket, and a process of another user connects over the read-only mount. |
| D-M3-04-10 | 2026-10-20 | The health monitor keeps the scanner state (`unknown`, `ok`, `unreachable`, `off`) per worker process in memory and records `bothy.file.scan.queue` on every round; it runs as the start step `scan_health` after the event consumers and ends in the jobs phase of the shutdown. It never changes readiness. With mode `off` it logs one Warn at start and sends no PING. | 12 5.3 and brief design note 11; M7-09 reads `files.ScannerHealth` for the status component. |
| D-M3-04-11 | 2026-10-20 | `bothy.file.scan.duration` measures the whole pass (read, join, scan, result) per outcome with the long-duration buckets; `bothy.file.scan` counts final outcomes only (failed files and retries are not counted). | The pass and the scan are one stream; `BothyMalwareScanFailing` keeps its meaning. |
| D-M3-04-12 | 2026-10-20 | Test clamd (`adapters/clamd/clamdtest`): the pinned image runs `clamd --foreground` with a test configuration (AlertExceedsMax, small explicit limits, a Unix socket and a TCP listener inside the container published on 127.0.0.1 only) and a database of one hash signature for the EICAR string computed at run time (`Local.Test.EICAR`, reported with `.UNOFFICIAL`). Instead of an unexported dialer option, `clamdtest` offers the loopback TCP port on a Unix socket of the host through a small relay that resolves the mapped port at every connection, so the production client (unix only) and the whole server through `internal/wire` talk to the real clamd unchanged. Containers that a test stops and starts run without AutoRemove and are removed by `Terminate`. | VM-based Docker runtimes do not pass Unix sockets of bind mounts to the host; the full signature database needs minutes and gigabytes and must not be downloaded in tests (RK-52); the relay keeps every TCP code path out of production. |
| D-M3-04-13 | 2026-10-20 | `AuditSignature` lower-cases the ClamAV name, replaces every character outside `[a-z0-9_.:-]` by `_`, prefixes `x` when the result does not start with a letter or digit, gives `unknown` for an empty name and cuts to 64 bytes, the code bound of `audit_details.v1`; the bound already holds a hex SHA-256, so the audit module is unchanged. | SEC-AUD-5 allows only these characters in codes; ClamAV names contain upper case. |
| D-M3-04-14 | 2026-10-20 | Merged names win over the brief (D-W-100): the type detector is `Detector` in the existing `adapters/mimetype` (the only package with the vendor `mimetype`) instead of a new `adapters/sniff`; the scan repository is `app.ScanRepository`, implemented by the M3-03 repository; `bothy config check` reports a configuration problem with exit code 1 (the merged command), `bothy serve` with 2. The rulings stand in a section per ticket like M3-03, because this base has no section 10 form. | One adapter per vendor; the command's exit codes are fixed by M1-07. |
| D-M3-04-15 | 2026-10-20 | Start checks `scan_stream_limit` (with mode `required`, `file.max_upload_bytes` at most `file.scan.clamd_stream_max_bytes`) and `clamd_address_unix` (`unix://` plus an absolute, clean path, the rule of `clamd.SocketPath`); both messages name keys only. `file.scan.pending_alert_after` stays out of the schema until M7-09. | 12 5.4, 19 SEC-TLS-5, 02 13 item 5 (a key enters with its first user). |
| D-M3-04-16 | 2026-10-20 | Tests: the race, deletion and health cases run as unit tests over fakes in `internal/files/app` (a fake unit of work that serializes units like row locks, and a test subscriber of `files.FileFinalized` in its own registry, because the registry of a harness server is sealed); the real-clamd cases run in `adapters/clamd` and in `test/scan` through the harness in role `all` (River picks `files.scan` from the queue `files`). Verification ran as targeted local tests: `build-verify.yml` is not on this base and the task forbids the push. | The brief lists these cases as integration tests; the harness offers no hook into the transaction registry. |

## M2-04 Trigger decision

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-04-1 | 2026-10-20 | The trigger decision is split into `Decide` and `DecideCommand` (steps 1 to 6 of 09 3.4, the voice rule of 09 13 and 10.4) and `Settle` (step 7); the package `internal/runs/domain` never calls `authorize`, every permission fact (`AuthorHasTrigger`, `WriteOnChannel`) is an input gathered by the caller from `authz`, and `Decision.Check` names the check that runs next (`CheckInvoke`, `CheckFollowUp`). The operator and guest rules appear twice by design: here they choose the candidate and the reason the person sees, the `agent.invoke` guard of M1-28 re-checks them under the permission snapshot; when the two disagree, `authz` wins through `Settle`. Every outcome that can lead to a run or a delivery carries a check, so no path invokes an agent without `agent.invoke` (T-AI-08). |

Place: `trigger_settle.go`, 09 3.4 and 3.5. | The closed deny list of 07 4.1 has no `agent_disabled`, and the M1-28 guard answers `agent_not_reachable` for an inactive agent, while 09 3.5 shows the person "agent disabled". The M1-28 guard is not changed. The re-check before each delivery (04 15.3 point 2) belongs to M2-08. |

The error names the field, never a value. `Outcome` is an integer type whose zero value is `OutcomeNone`.
