## M7-02 Backup service: schedules, manifest and metrics

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-02-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave46` at `9c05469` with `feat/M3-03-upload-sessions` at `0b60fd7` merged first (stacked build), branch `feat/M7-02-backup-service` in its own worktree; not merged locally, no pull request; the full verification runs on the build branch `build/M7-02`. The base names the chapters in German (`16-betrieb.md`, `03-datenmodell.md`, `02-architektur.md`) and has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section of the ticket and added text is English. The brief relies on no core rule or D-W ruling the base lacks (R-OP-06 to R-OP-13 and 03 4.12 are on the base; D-W-100 and D-W-125 are rulings about writing briefs), so nothing is ported first. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the migration is `00049_status_backup_runs.sql`, the image lives in `services/server/images/bothy-postgres/`. | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-02-1 | 2026-10-20 | Cron: an own five-field parser in `internal/status/domain` (`ParseSchedule`, `Matches`, `Next`), no library: `*`, numbers, ranges, lists, `*/n` and `a-b/n`; day of week 0 to 7 (0 and 7 Sunday); when day of month and day of week are both restricted, either matches. Schedules are evaluated in UTC. | Five fields with this grammar cover every `backup.*` default; a library would add a vendor and the license gate for a hundred lines; UTC because every instant of the server is UTC (03 Grundsatz 2). |
| D-M7-02-2 | 2026-10-20 | Scheduler: once a minute the kinds due in (previous tick, now] start; the first window starts at the start of the process, so missed times while it was down are not caught up and the next due time runs. `pg_full` and `pg_diff` share one slot ("physical"); a full and a differential due in the same window run only the full; a kind whose slot runs is skipped, not queued. `bothy-backupd backup <kind>` runs one kind now and exits `0` or `1`. | Brief design note 1; pgBackRest holds one backup lock per stanza, so two physical runs at once would fail anyway. |
| D-M7-02-3 | 2026-10-20 | Role `bothy_backup` (03 4.12, design note 3): created by `migrate up --bootstrap` like the four roles of 16 7.1 with `LOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOREPLICATION NOBYPASSRLS`, `CONNECT` and `USAGE` on `public`; the migration grants it `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `backup_runs` and nothing else; `bothy_app` gets `SELECT` (console and status page read the table, 03). Secret `db_backup_password` (generated, to `migrate` and `backup`), key `migrate.backup_password_ref`; the Compose service `backup` connects with it as `database.user`. The manifest queries run as `migrate.bootstrap_user` in one `READ ONLY` transaction with `REPEATABLE READ` and an exact `count(*)` per ordinary or partitioned table of `public` (partitions through their parent), in a pool without statement deadline. | Least privilege for the writer of the row; one snapshot for every count; exact counts because the restore test compares them. |
| D-M7-02-4 | 2026-10-20 | Off-site targets: only S3-compatible buckets with versioning and Object Lock, `backup.offsite.target = s3:https://<host>[:<port>]/<bucket>[/<prefix>]`, new keys `backup.offsite.region` (`us-east-1`) and `backup.offsite.use_path_style` (`true`); in the bucket repo2 at `<prefix>/pgbackrest`, the restic repository at `<prefix>/restic`, the object mirror at `<prefix>/objects/`. The key of `backup` is `offsite/access_key_id` and `offsite/secret_access_key` (plus `offsite/ca.crt` for a private CA); `bothy-backupd` passes repo2 to its pgBackRest children as `PGBACKREST_REPO2_*` variables with `PGBACKREST_EXPIRE_AUTO=n`, and `expire` runs only on repo1. The owner's expiry is `bothy-backupd expire --offsite --credentials DIR` with a separate key that never enters the volume `secrets` (runbook 9). restic's REST server and SFTP are not supported. | pgBackRest's repo2 cannot use restic's REST server, and restic over SFTP needs an SSH client the image does not have (the brief allows only restic as a new binary); one target kind for all three tools keeps one key and one proof. Append-only follows from Object Lock, not from the tools: the probe and `TestBackupKinds` show that SeaweedFS refuses `DeleteObject` with a version id to the key of `backup`. |
| D-M7-02-5 | 2026-10-20 | Object backup of the driver `s3`: always a mirror (`adapters/s3mirror`) of the file bucket into `<prefix>/objects/` of the off-site bucket: list, `HeadObject`, then `GetObject` and `PutObject` with `If-None-Match: *` and a SHA-256 checksum for every missing key; it never deletes or overwrites, a deleted source object stays. A provider replication mode that only checks the replica is not built; the driver `s3` without an off-site target fails its object run with `s3_mirror_not_configured`. | Brief design note 5; checking a provider's replication needs a provider API this build cannot test for free (D-W-125), and a mirror works with every S3 service. |
| D-M7-02-6 | 2026-10-20 | Manifest: built after the backup step from the snapshot of D-M7-02-3 and the references (`pgbackrest.label` of repo1 for `pg_full` and `pg_diff`, `restic.snapshot_id` for `logical` and `objects` with the driver `fs`); stored in `backup_runs.manifest` and as a restic snapshot of stdin (`manifest-<run id>.json`, tags `manifest`, `<kind>`, `<run id>`) in the restic repository (off-site, else local), which restic encrypts. A failed run stores no manifest and `error` is a code (`<tool>_exit_<n>`, `manifest_store_failed` and others). `size_bytes` is what the run added: repo1's `repository delta`, restic's `data_added_packed`, or the bytes the mirror copied. Without an off-site target the local restic repository (`/var/lib/pgbackrest/restic`) is pruned after each run with `backup.logical.retention` and `backup.objects.retention` (manifests with the larger of both). | A row of the same table and an encrypted copy next to the backup, as backup-restore.md 3 asks; restic is the one tool here that encrypts arbitrary files. The counts describe the database at the end of the run, after the backup; M7-03 compares them knowing that writes between the end of the backup and the snapshot may differ. |
| D-M7-02-7 | 2026-10-20 | `bothy.backup.wal.last_archived.timestamp` comes from `pg_stat_archiver.last_archived_time` (superuser connection), not from `pgbackrest info`. The three `bothy.backup.restore_test.*` instruments stay to M7-03; `bothy.backup.duration` and `bothy.backup.size` already accept `restore_test` in their closed attribute set. Metrics go through `otel.SetupMetrics` (new) with `service.name = bothy-backupd` and without a pseudonym key. | `pgbackrest info` reports WAL ranges but no archive time; `pg_stat_archiver` is the server's own record of the last archived segment. `bothy-backupd` holds no pseudonym key and records no person. |
| D-M7-02-8 | 2026-10-20 | `bothy migrate up` (16 10 item 1, R-OP-13): before River and goose run, with `migrate.require_recent_backup`, an existing schema (version above 0) with pending goose migrations needs a succeeded `pg_full`, `pg_diff` or `logical` run whose `started_at` is at most `migrate.recent_backup_max_age` old; otherwise exit `1` with the hint `bothy-backupd backup pg_full`. Exempt: an empty database, no pending migration, and a schema without the table `backup_runs` (it predates M7-02, so no run can exist; one line on stdout says so). | Brief rule 17; without the exemption the first upgrade to this version could never pass. |
| D-M7-02-9 | 2026-10-20 | `bothy-backupd` loads the full server schema (`wire.ConfigSchema`), so the image builds with `api/` and `migrations/` in its context; it leaves the image variables `BOTHY_PGBACKREST_REPO_HOST`, `BOTHY_PGBACKREST_PG_HOST`, `BOTHY_PGBACKREST_REPO2` and `BOTHY_PG_ARCHIVE_TIMEOUT` out of the configuration sources. New image variable `BOTHY_PGBACKREST_REPO2=on` (Compose: set when `BOTHY_BACKUP_OFFSITE_TARGET` is set) makes the database host name repo2 on the repository host, so `archive-push` writes every segment to both repositories. The image creates `/var/lib/bothy-backup` for the user `999`. | One schema means `bothy config check` and the service read the same keys; the image variables share the prefix `BOTHY_` but are read by the entry point, and R-OP-04 would end the start for them. |
| D-M7-02-10 | 2026-10-20 | The restic binary of `bothy-postgres` carries the file capability `cap_dac_read_search=+ep` (set in the build stage, kept by the copy), and the Compose service `backup` gets `cap_add: [DAC_READ_SEARCH]`: the volume `files` belongs to the server's user `65532` with mode `0700` (files `0600`), which the file system driver requires, and `backup` runs as `999`. Only restic gets the capability; `bothy-backupd`, pgBackRest and `pg_dump` run without it. Without `cap_add` the kernel refuses to start restic, so a stack that forgets it fails its logical and object runs loudly. | restic's documented way to back up files of another user without running as root; the server keeps its ownership rule, and the entry point keeps refusing root. |
| D-M7-02-11 | 2026-10-20 | The executor orders the restic calls of one process: backups (and the manifest store) share a lock, `forget --prune` and the first `init` hold it alone. | restic refuses a prune while another restic holds a lock; without the order a local prune after a logical run fails a concurrent object backup or manifest store, or the other way round. A second process (a `bothy-backupd backup` by hand during a scheduled run) is not ordered; its failure is recorded as a code and retried by the next run. |
| D-M7-02-12 | 2026-10-20 | The owner's off-site expiry (`bothy-backupd expire --offsite`) does not delete object versions itself. The runbook requires a lifecycle rule on the bucket prefix that expires noncurrent versions after `NoncurrentDays` of at least the Object Lock period and removes expired delete markers; `restic forget --prune` and `pgbackrest expire` on repo2 only add delete markers, and the rule removes the old versions later. Runbook 2 and 9 and 16 8.2 say so; off-site data lives up to its retention plus `NoncurrentDays`. Found by the red team: without the rule nothing ever left the versioned bucket while the command exited `0`. | A command that deleted every noncurrent version with the owner's key would also make permanent whatever delete markers a taken-over `backup` key had set (its key may add delete markers for restic's lock files); the delay of the rule leaves the owner time to notice. A bucket rule is the provider's own mechanism and works without the owner running anything. |

## M7-03 Automatic restore test

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-03-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave49` at `eb15f40`, branch `feat/M7-03-restore-test` in its own worktree; not merged locally, no pull request; the full verification runs on the build branch `build/M7-03`. The base names the chapters in German (`16-betrieb.md`, `03-datenmodell.md`, `02-architektur.md`, `19-sicherheit.md`) and has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section of the ticket and added text is English. The brief relies on no core rule or D-W ruling the base lacks (R-OP-10 and `backup_runs.checks` of 03 4.12 are on the base; D-W-100 and D-W-125 are rulings about writing briefs), so nothing is ported first. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the dependency table of 02 is section 4.4 "Erlaubte Abhängigkeiten" (4.5 holds the kernel ports), the subcommand is `bothy-backupd restore-test`, the scratch directory is `/var/lib/bothy-backup/restore`, the image test lives in `services/server/test/postgresimage/restore_test.go` (`TestRestoreTest`). | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-03-1 | 2026-10-20 | Order and skipping (design note 1): check `pgbackrest` reads the newest succeeded `pg_full` or `pg_diff` from `backup_runs`, restores its label, runs `pgbackrest verify` on repo1 and starts the copy. No backup (`no_backup`), a manifest that does not parse (`manifest_invalid`), a failed restore (the exit code of pgBackRest) or a failed start (`scratch_start_failed`) fail check 1 and skip checks 2 to 6 with `restore_failed`; a failed verify fails check 1 with its code, but the copy is still checked. A check that panics fails with `check_panicked` and the others run. `duration` runs always: from the start of the row to the end of the clearing. A skipped check fails nothing; the row is `failed` with `checks_failed` when a check failed and with `scratch_cleanup_failed` when the directory could not be emptied. The test has its own slot `restore_test` and runs next to the backups. | Only a missing copy makes checks 2 to 6 meaningless; a damaged repository and a sound copy are two findings. Its own slot keeps a long test from skipping a scheduled backup. |
| D-M7-03-2 | 2026-10-20 | Audit port (design note 2): the root package `audit` gets `RowsVerifier` with `RowsInput` (instance id, public keys of `server_signing_keys`, iterators over `audit_events` and `audit_checkpoints` in seq order); `app.RowsVerifier` and `app.VerifyRows` implement it with the walk of `audit.verify` (`domain.Walk` from row 1 with ZeroHash, no anchors yet) and the same verdict function as `Log.Verify` (missing checkpoint row, first invalid checkpoint, row below 1, smallest seq wins). The checkpoints come from `audit_checkpoints` of the copy and are trusted like those of the sink: JWS typ `audit-checkpoint+json`, signature of the key its kid names among the supplied keys of purpose `audit_checkpoint` with the same algorithm, payload of the instance naming the stored seq and hash, links (`domain.CheckCheckpoints`). `status` reads the rows of the copy over its own pool (`adapters/postgres.ScratchDB`) and never runs SQL on audit tables of production; `modules.yaml`, 02 4.4 and the component diagram name the edge `status` to `audit`. | Owner rule 2: one implementation of the chain verification. The copy has no sink of its own; its `audit_checkpoints` are the signed record it carries. |
| D-M7-03-3 | 2026-10-20 | Manifest check: the manifest of a physical run is read after the backup step (D-M7-02-6), the copy ends at the consistency point of the backup (recovery target `immediate`), so writes in between may differ. The check fails when schema version, `instance_id` or `sync_epoch` differ, when the copy is newer than its manifest (event head or audit head above it, `copy_newer_than_manifest`), when the audit heads are equal but the hashes differ, or when a table of the manifest is missing or the copy has another one. When the event head and the audit head of the copy equal those of the manifest, the row counts of `event_log` and `audit_events` must be equal (`row_counts_differ`); otherwise no count is compared. The counts of the other tables are never compared: sessions, tokens, cleanup jobs and `backup_runs` itself change rows without an event or audit row while a backup runs (found by the code review). | Exact counts on a busy instance would fail tests of undamaged backups and page the owner; every row of the two logs carries the number of its log, so at equal heads an undamaged copy holds exactly the rows of its manifest. |
| D-M7-03-4 | 2026-10-20 | Sampling (rule 15): per test `ORDER BY random() LIMIT n` over `threads` and over `files` with `deleted_at IS NULL AND upload_state = 'complete' AND scan_status IN ('clean', 'skipped')`; with fewer rows every row is checked. A pending upload has no object at its key yet, and the scan pass deletes the object of an infected or unscannable file while its row stays (found by the red team), so both are left out. Thread rule: `count(*) = max(thread_seq) - min(thread_seq) + 1`, `min >= 1` and `max = threads.last_message_seq`, or no messages and `last_message_seq = 0`. Objects: `restic restore latest --tag objects --path <file.fs.root>` with one `--include` per key into `<scratch>/objects` (the path picks the object backup, because the manifest snapshot of an objects run carries the tag `objects` too), read through an `os.Root`; a key the backup lacks is `object_missing`, other bytes `object_hash_mismatch`. With the driver `s3` the objects lie in the mirror of the off-site bucket, which this build does not fetch: check 6 is `skipped` with `objects_driver_s3`. | The brief names the restic fetch; a fetch from the mirror needs an S3 client in the restore path, left for the ticket that needs it (follow-up in the report). |
| D-M7-03-5 | 2026-10-20 | Test hook (design note 3): only the build tag `restoretesthook` compiles `adapters/scratch/hook_restoretesthook.go`; release builds compile the empty hooks of `hook.go`. The image test builds its own `bothy-backupd` with the tag and runs `restore-test` through it inside the container `backup`; `RESTORE_TEST_HOOK` (no `BOTHY_` prefix, so R-OP-04 never sees it) selects `event_log_gap`, `audit_hash` or `object_bytes`; every run of the tagged build writes `/proc/net/tcp` and `/proc/net/tcp6` while the copy runs to `/tmp/restore-test-tcp`. | A test-only build keeps the manipulation out of every shipped binary and still tests the real image. |
| D-M7-03-6 | 2026-10-20 | A deleted `event_log` row fails check `event_log` (`event_log_gap`) and check `manifest` (`row_counts_differ`), because the copy is quiet and its `event_log` count is one below the manifest; the image test expects both, the other five pass. The audit hash and the object bytes fail exactly their check. | A gap inside a partition always changes the count or the bounds of `event_log`, so "exactly its check" (design note 3) cannot hold for this case while check 2 compares row counts as 16 8.3 item 2 asks. |
| D-M7-03-7 | 2026-10-20 | Scratch PostgreSQL: restored with `--repo=1 --reset-pg1-host --pg1-path=<scratch>/pgdata --type=immediate --target-action=promote --archive-mode=off`, `restore_command` overridden with the same options; started by its absolute path with `listen_addresses` empty, `port=5432` (the socket file name), the socket in `<scratch>/socket` with mode `0700`, its own `pg_hba.conf` (`local all all trust`), `archive_mode=off`, `ssl=off`, `max_parallel_workers_per_gather=0`; other settings, such as `max_connections`, stay those of the copy (recovery refuses lower values). Stopped with `SIGINT`, after a minute with `SIGKILL`, then the directory is emptied. | The repository host has no database role of its own in pgBackRest; promotion makes the copy writable for the test hook only, and archiving off keeps its new timeline out of the repository. The socket mode limits access to the user of `bothy-backupd`. Parallel query would need the shared memory of a database container. |
| D-M7-03-8 | 2026-10-20 | Metrics: `bothy.backup.restore_test.duration` and `bothy.backup.duration{bothy.backup.kind=restore_test}` after every test, no `bothy.backup.size`; `bothy.backup.restore_test.last_success.timestamp` after a test without a failed check and without a cleanup failure, primed from `backup_runs` at start; `bothy.backup.restore_test.failures` plus one per failed check with `bothy.reason` the check name (closed set of the seven names). | alerting.md: one failure `ticket`, two in a row or an audit chain error `page`, `BothyRestoreTestStale` on the last success. |
| D-M7-03-9 | 2026-10-20 | Configuration: `backup.restore_test.schedule` (string, also in the cross-check `backup_schedules`), `backup.restore_test.sample_files` and `backup.restore_test.sample_threads` (int, at least 1) and `dr.rto_target` (duration, at least 1s) become active keys; `backup.restore_test.max_age` and `dr.rpo_target` stay planned, because only the alert rules read them. | R-OP-04: a key enters the schema with its first consumer. |

## M2-03 Project binding and readiness

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-03-1 | 2026-10-20 | Schema `project_runner_config.v1` is defined in 09 section 18.1 and as `ProjectRunnerConfig` in `docs/api/openapi.yaml`: a closed object with the optional members `model` (string, `minLength: 1`) and `allowed_tools` (array of strings with `minLength: 1`, `uniqueItems: true`). The server checks only this shape and the rules of D-M2-03-3 and never interprets a tool name; the runner derives its flags from `agents.tool_class` and only narrows them by the list, so it enforces "within `agents.tool_class`" of 03. The stored `jsonb` keeps the members as sent (an empty tool list stays an empty list). | 03 and the contract named the schema, no file defined it; W7 of M1-18 requires closed request objects. |
| D-M2-03-2 | 2026-10-20 | Configuration key `project.binding_text_max_chars` (Server, default `512`, minimum `1`, maximum `600`, chapter 09): the longest `workspace_ref`, `repository_url`, `default_branch`, `runner_config.model` and tool name in Unicode characters, read at request time; longer gives `too_long`. 512 characters of at most 4 bytes in UTF-8, also after `lower()`, stay below the PostgreSQL B-tree entry limit of the unique index on `lower(workspace_ref)`; the maximum keeps that true for every configured value, and an index error that still occurs answers `400 too_long` on `/workspace_ref` instead of `500`. The reason uses `audit.reason_max_chars`. | Without a limit an over-long reference reaches the index and ends as an internal error. |
| D-M2-03-3 | 2026-10-20 | Field rules, all failures in one `400 validation_failed`, per text first `invalid_characters` (`kernel/textcheck`), then `too_long`, then `invalid_format`: `workspace_ref` blank after trimming is `required`; leading or trailing white space, a leading `/`, `\`, `~` or `-`, a drive letter with colon or a segment `..` between `/` and `\` is `invalid_format`; `repository_url` must be an absolute `http` or `https` URL with a host and without user information; `default_branch` must not be empty, contain white space or `..`, or start with `-`; `model` and each tool must not be empty or start with `-`; the reason blank is `required`. | 03 "never an absolute path of the server", 03 principle 9 (no credentials), SEC-OUT-1, the runner passes model and tools as process arguments (09 18.2); defense in depth beside the confinement under `runner.projects_root`. |
| D-M2-03-4 | 2026-10-20 | A PUT replaces the whole binding: an absent optional member clears its column and an absent `runner_config` is `{}`; `created_by` and `created_at` stay. Equal values (the runner configuration compared as `jsonb`, so the order of `allowed_tools` and the presence of an empty list count) are a no-op: `200` with the stored binding and version, no write, no event, `ready` untouched, and an audit allow entry with `op = unchanged`. | HTTP PUT semantics; a client that retries a lost PUT must not reset readiness. |
| D-M2-03-5 | 2026-10-20 | Every effective PUT change (`op = replaced`) writes the new values with `version + 1` and `ready = false`; every change of the effective operator of a bound forum (`setChannelOperator`, `setSpaceOperator` for its inheriting forums that are not archived) sets `ready = false` with `version + 1` in the same transaction, without an extra event. The audit detail `ready_reset` is true when a ready binding was reset by the PUT. | The runner reported readiness for the old configuration or as the old operator; 05 section 5 point 7 must fail closed until the serving runner reports again. |
| D-M2-03-6 | 2026-10-20 | `ApplyProjectReadiness` applies a report only for a bound forum that is not archived (nor in an archived space) whose effective operator, read after the locks, is the reporting agent; every other entry is ignored without error. A channel reported twice is `validation_failed` with code `duplicate` on `/project_readiness` and changes nothing. A readiness change raises only `project_bindings.version`; it writes no event, no audit entry and no `settings_version`. Locks: the spaces of inheriting reported channels `FOR SHARE`, the channels `FOR SHARE`, the bindings `FOR UPDATE`, each in ascending id. | 09 4.8 "runner only"; the result tells an agent nothing about forums it does not serve; readiness is runtime state, a `settings_version` change on each flip would fail pending `putChannelBehavior` calls with `412`. |
| D-M2-03-7 | 2026-10-20 | Status codes: a channel that is not a forum is `400 validation_failed` with `not_a_forum` on `#path/channel_id` (PUT and DELETE, after authorize); an archived forum or a forum of an archived space `409 conflict`; DELETE without a binding `404 not_found` without an audit entry; `If-Match` without a binding `412 version_conflict` without `current`, a stale or malformed `If-Match` `412` with the stored binding as `current`; a `workspace_ref` taken case-insensitively by another forum `409 conflict` without `reason`. | 06 5 point 3 (pointer form), 07 4.5, 06 7 (no tombstone), RFC 9110 (no current representation), 03 unique `lower(workspace_ref)`. |
| D-M2-03-8 | 2026-10-20 | A binding change appends `channel.settings_changed` and, when the forum has an effective operator after the locks, `agent.config_changed` to it, through `Service.settingsChanged` of M2-02 with the configuration flag set; without an effective operator only `channel.settings_changed`. | No receiver exists; M2-02 handles this case the same way (D-M2-02-4). |
| D-M2-03-9 | 2026-10-20 | `docs/api/openapi.yaml` stays below `repo.max_file_kb`: the schemas `ProjectBinding` and `ProjectBindingPut` and the responses and headers of both binding operations are written in YAML flow style, which frees the bytes for `ProjectRunnerConfig`, `minLength: 1` on the reason and `409` on `deleteProjectBinding`; the descriptions of the rules of design notes 3, 5 and 6 of the brief are not added, this table and the module README carry them. | The base file was 18 bytes below the limit (D-M2-02-10); the closed schema and the `409` are needed by the request and response validators, the descriptions are not. |
| D-M2-03-10 | 2026-10-20 | The port follows the brief where it differs from M2-02: `chat.ProjectBindings` takes the caller from `kernel/actor` in the context. `PutProjectBindingCmd.IfMatch` is the existing `chat.Precondition` instead of `ExpectedVersion *int64`, so a malformed header is a `412` like a stale one. The handlers live on `chathttp.Behavior` next to the behaviour operations. | The brief fixes the context form; `Precondition` already carries the parsed `If-Match` of every other chat operation. |
| D-M2-03-11 | 2026-10-20 | `agent.invoke` reads `ProjectBound` and `ProjectReady` from the `LEFT JOIN` on `project_bindings` of every channel query of the structure source; inside a structure scope (one per dispatched event of the hub) the guard reads the project facts of the channel again instead of the memo. Nothing raises `permission_version` or writes `access.changed`. | Readiness changes write no event that would end a cached state (07 SR2); the REST mode reads facts per call anyway. |
| D-M2-03-12 | 2026-10-20 | The task names the branch and the base: `feat/M2-03-project-binding` from `int/m1-wave46` (`9c05469`) with `feat/M2-02-channel-behavior-config` (`eb37ba7`) merged first; the work is neither merged locally nor opened as a pull request, the launcher integrates it. The base holds the core contract in its earlier language; the rules the brief cites (R-AZ-01, R-AZ-03, R-AZ-04, R-AZ-09, R-AZ-10, R-AZ-14, 05 section 5 point 7) say the same there, so no contract change is brought in. The integration tests live in `test/integration` on its in-process world (the port `ApplyProjectReadiness` has no route until M2-20); the world keeps its chat service and unit of work and serves the behaviour handlers. | The launcher's task overrides the brief's branch and landing steps. |

## M6-03 Content Security Policy with a headless test

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-03-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave46` at `9c05469`, branch `feat/M6-03-console-csp` in its own worktree, with `feat/M1-73-compose-smoke` at `70589fb` merged first (stacked build); not merged locally and not pushed except the build branch, the PR lane integrates it. The contract rule R-AZ-33 (action `console.csp_report`), which main holds and this base lacks, enters first as its own commit. Names that differ from the brief and are followed: the chapters are `19-sicherheit.md`, `05-aktionen.md`, `02-architektur.md`, `06-api.md`; the brief index of this base has no table "Make targets created by tickets" (main already lists `console-e2e` there); there is no `ci_ok.py`, the rule mechanism of M4-01 is the `if` of the job plus the `success` or `skipped` case in `ci-ok`. `docs/api/openapi.yaml` had 18 bytes left below `repo.max_file_kb`: the 403 generic example summaries `summary: Request` and `summary: Response` go in their own commit, the same content-neutral change as D-M3-03-21 of the next wave. | The task names base, stack and lane; merged names win (D-W-100); the gate must not be loosened. |
| D-M6-03-1 | 2026-10-20 | Policy builder (`httpserver.ContentSecurityPolicy`, `csp.go`): the directives of 19 5 in one function, the nonce of the response in `style-src`, the Trusted Types names from the one list `TrustedTypesPolicies`, `report-to csp`; no `frame-src`, no `'unsafe-inline'`, no `'unsafe-eval'`. It applies to every response of the console mount below `/console/` (index.html, assets, the SPA fallback, the redirect of `/console` and the 404 of a path that leaves the build), never to `/api/`, together with COOP `same-origin`, COEP `require-corp`, CORP `same-origin`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, the `Permissions-Policy` and `Reporting-Endpoints: csp="/api/v1/csp-reports"`. | Brief note 1; SEC-HDR-3. |
| D-M6-03-2 | 2026-10-20 | Nonce: the server reads `index.html` once at the start and refuses a build without the placeholder `__BOTHY_CSP_NONCE__` (the start ends); each response gets 128 bits from `crypto/rand` in base64 in the placeholder and in `style-src`, `no-store` and no ETag. The placeholder sits only in the nonce attribute of the bootstrap script tag; `flutter_bootstrap.js` hands `document.currentScript.nonce` to the loader option `nonce`, so one substitution serves both (an inline loader option would need a script nonce, which 19 5 does not allow). `index.html` has no `<base>` element, because `base-uri 'none'` blocks it (observed in Chromium); the build writes `/console/` into the absolute URLs of the script and the icon (`$FLUTTER_BASE_HREF`), the loader derives `entrypointBaseUrl`, `assetBase`, `canvasKitBaseUrl` and `fontFallbackBaseUrl` from the URL of its script, and the app's path URL strategy takes `/console/` from `ConsolePlatformLocation`. The fallback fonts stay the ones the build bundles (`assets/fonts/fallback/`); the console's own texts need no other font (no fallback request in any browser), a glyph outside them asks the own origin and gets 404, never a foreign origin. | Brief note 2; 19 5 rules 1 and 2. |
| D-M6-03-3 | 2026-10-20 | Trusted Types: the observed policy names are exactly `flutter-js` (the loader's policy for the script URLs it injects) in Chromium 156, Firefox 157 and WebKit 27.2 with the CanvasKit build; the engine creates no policy, there was no `trusted-types` violation. `TrustedTypesPolicies` and 19 5 rule 4 say so. | Brief note 3; the list follows the test. |
| D-M6-03-4 | 2026-10-20 | WebSocket: `connect-src 'self'` covers `wss://` to the own origin in all three browsers; the test opens `/api/v1/gateway` from the page, sees no `connect-src` violation and the handshake reaching the server. No `wss://<host>` is added. | Brief note 3; 19 5 rule 5. |
| D-M6-03-5 | 2026-10-20 | Reports: `submitCspReport` (`POST /api/v1/csp-reports`, `console.csp_report`, `rl.public_auth`, `x-idempotency-exempt: telemetry`) takes `application/reports+json` and `application/csp-report` as the new body kind `report` of the route table and the chain (the only operation with two request media types): JSON guard and `http.max_json_body_bytes`, no schema check beyond that, the handler reads the raw bytes. It reads only the effective directive (legacy: `effective-directive`, else the first word of `violated-directive`), the disposition and the blocked URL, reduces them to fixed lists (directives of CSP Level 3, `enforce`, `report`, else `other`) and to the origin (http, https, ws, wss) or a keyword (`inline`, `eval`, `wasm-eval`, `trusted-types-policy`, `trusted-types-sink`, `data`, `blob`, else `other`), counts every violation in `bothy.console.csp_violations`, logs one Info record `csp violation reported` per distinct violation (identical ones share it) with `bothy.csp.directive`, `bothy.csp.disposition` and `bothy.csp.blocked_origin`, at most `console.csp_report_max_records` (default 20) per request, and stores nothing; without that bound one body of `http.max_json_body_bytes` produced about 30000 records (review finding). Reports of other types in the Reporting API array are skipped. The console guard of M6-01 does not check this action: a browser sends a report with the cookie of the same origin but never with `X-Console-Request`. | Brief note 4; R-AZ-33. |
| D-M6-03-6 | 2026-10-20 | Headless test (`tools/console-e2e`, Python with Playwright 1.64.0 from `tools/uv.lock`): the browsers run in the Playwright image of that version pinned by digest, plus the locked packages, in the network namespace of `api`, locally as in CI, because WebKit does not start headless on the macOS build host. Chromium runs as the full browser in its new headless mode (the headless shell never delivers Reporting API reports) with `--short-reporting-delay` and trusts the leaf by its SPKI hash; Firefox and WebKit accept the test certificate by the context option. The console has no passkey page yet (M6-04), so Chromium registers the passkey through `beginPasskeyRegistration` and `completePasskeyRegistration` from the page of the console session with the virtual authenticator (user verification on), logs out, logs in again and does the step-up with that passkey through the console. The negative test answers the navigation to `/console/csp-negative` with the headers and body the server just sent for that path plus one inline script (Playwright's route instead of a rewriting sidecar; the server image and its headers stay untouched) and requires the block, the violation event and the report in the server log. Text is typed key by key after the editing element has the focus, because Flutter loses a key typed before. Violations and Trusted Types names go from the init script through bindings of the context into Python, so the check covers every document of the session (the login and the navigation as well as the second login); a control after the negative test proves that a violation survives a navigation (review finding). | Brief note 5; a supported browser of 15 2 must work. |
| D-M6-03-7 | 2026-10-20 | Topology and CI: the overlay `infra/compose/test/compose.console-e2e.yaml` adds the TLS sidecar `tls` (Caddy 2.11.7 by digest, `network_mode: service:api`, port 8443, a test CA and leaf for `localhost`, `api` and 127.0.0.1 generated per run), the seed `console-seed` (`bothy-smoke seed`: the owner's device redeems the enrollment code, sets a password and confirms TOTP) and `console-browser`; `server.public_url` is `https://localhost:8443`, `auth.webauthn_rp_id` `localhost`, `auth.webauthn_origins` that origin, `server.trusted_proxies` stays loopback. No port is published on the host, because the browsers share the namespace. `make console-e2e` (not in `gates`) runs `tools/console-e2e/console_e2e.py`; the CI job `console-e2e` runs `make image` for the host platform and `make console-e2e` when `apps` or `server` changed, uploads the result files only on a failure and feeds `ci-ok` (`success` or `skipped`). The image builds the console and the server with the Flutter and Go pins of its Dockerfile, so the job installs neither. | Brief notes 6 and 7; M1-73 D1. |

## M4-35 Package terminal_ui and terminal view

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-35-1 | 2026-10-20 | The controller is the only owner of the ephemeral X25519 key: it creates the pair right before `openTerminalSession` or `attachTerminalSession` (only its hash leaves), hands it to `NkInitiator` and calls `destroy()` of `cryptography` (the private bytes are zeroed) right after message 2 and on every abort (cancelled step-up, refused request, key mismatch, closed stream). `ephemeral_key_test.dart` checks that the pair is destroyed and no longer extractable. | Brief Design note 1, R-CL-15; a weak reference cannot be checked reliably in a VM test, zeroing can. |
| D-M4-35-2 | 2026-10-20 | The escape filter (`render/escape_filter.dart`) sits in `terminal_ui` in front of `xterm` on the byte stream before UTF-8 decoding. Allow list: text and C0 controls pass; ESC sequences pass except DCS, SOS, PM and APC (dropped with their content); CSI passes except window operations and reports (final `t`), status reports other than CPR (`n` unless `6`), DA3 (`c` other than DA1 and DA2), every `$` intermediate (DECRQM and the report family), DECREQTPARM (`x`) and XTVERSION (`> q`); every OSC is dropped except OSC 8, which becomes inert text plus a link record. The only answers left are CPR, DA1 and DA2. On top, sequences that make `xterm` 4.0.0 throw or hang are dropped (a parameter above 9999, SGR 38 or 48 without sub-parameters or with a palette index above 255, REP above 512, `CSI 0 X`), and `TerminalScreen` catches any renderer exception so hostile output cannot end the session as tampering. | Brief Design note 2, 24 11.2, SEC-TERM-6, TM-T14; the blocks must not depend on the renderer's version. |
| D-M4-35-3 | 2026-10-20 | Pins compare the key bytes only (base64url decoded): no pin opens and pins `noise_public_key`, `key_generation`, fingerprint and first seen after the first successful handshake; the same key writes nothing; a different key shows the warning whatever `key_generation` says, and a confirmed change sends `pin_changed = true` (open and attach) and writes the new pin only after message 2. | Brief Design note 3, 15 23.3, TM-T18. |
| D-M4-35-4 | 2026-10-20 | `lib/testing.dart` holds `FakeHost` (static X25519 key, `NkResponder` of `bothy_noise/testing.dart`, checks the token of message 1, records the device frames, sends `data`, `exit`, the replay and any close code) and `FakeTerminalTransport` (in memory, `hello` and `identify`). The fakes of the server API, the pins, the step-up, the clock, the links and the prompts are test support of the package (`test/support/`). | Brief Design note 4; M4-36 and M4-38 test against the real host service. |
| D-M4-35-5 | 2026-10-20 | `xterm` 4.0.0 (2024-02-27, MIT) is adopted: no advisory in OSV or the GitHub advisory database on 2026-10-20, no security issue in its tracker. Its own handlers are the second layer: the view leaves `onTitleChange`, `onIconChange` and `onPrivateOSC` unset (OSC 52 and OSC 8 reach only `onPrivateOSC`; there is no clipboard handler). Bugs found and handled in the view: `TerminalView.onTapUp` is never called (a `Listener` detects link clicks), CPR answers zero-based (the screen adds one to row and column), cursor and selection are painted over the text (the view paints its own, D-M4-35-18); palette index 15 shows as white (not handled; a colour, no security effect). | Brief Design note 5; details and sources in 24 12. |
| D-M4-35-6 | 2026-10-20 | Names differ from the brief: the server port is `TerminalServerApi` (the generated `TerminalApi` of `api_client` has the brief's name); the step-up port is the typedef `TerminalStepUp` (`session.stepUp.confirm` as a tear-off, because `StepUpService` is a final class built over the internal `SessionCore`); the snapshot is `TerminalTabState` (the generated enum `TerminalSessionState` has the other name); the dialogs answer the new port `TerminalPrompts` (`DialogTerminalPrompts` in the package); the controller also takes `selfUserId` (24 8.4) and `AppConfig` (`client.terminal.scrollback_lines`). | Brief "Boundaries": follow the merged code and list each difference. |
| D-M4-35-7 | 2026-10-20 | Close codes: no reaction retries by itself. `4100` ends (blocked with a danger banner when `close_reason` is a revocation: `access_revoked`, `i6_violation`, `user_disabled`, `device_revoked`, `session_revoked`, `host_disabled`, `host_retired`, `host_rekeyed`), `1000` ends, `4104`, `4002` and `4012` block, `4101` detaches and the next "Reattach" asks for a step-up before the request, `4102`, `4103`, `1001`, `4013`, `4014`, `4004`, `4008` and a broken stream without a code detach with their own text and offer "Reattach" as a manual action. | 15 23.2 item 8, asyncapi `x-close-codes`; an automatic reattach on mobile foreground or network return is M5's wiring. |
| D-M4-35-8 | 2026-10-20 | Attach runs its own step-up: on `401 step_up_required` it runs one step-up and repeats the request once. An app must therefore not let the `StepUpInterceptor` of `auth` answer `attachTerminalSession` itself (M4-36 wires the adapter accordingly). | 15 23.2 item 6, 24 6.2; the warning of 24 8.4 must come before any step-up. |

## M3-06 Images: removing metadata and thumbnails

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M3-06-1 | 2026-10-20 | `files.scan` keeps its read pass; for `clean` and `skipped` its final transaction leaves a JPEG, PNG, GIF or WebP `upload_state = 'pending'` and inserts `files.thumbnail` with `InsertTx`; MP4 and QuickTime files become `complete` and get the job; other types and blocked or failed files get none. The image becomes `complete` only in T1 of the job, which stores its cleaned original, and only T1 or the failure transaction publishes `files.FileFinalized` for it. `image/apng` (the detected name of an animated PNG) takes the PNG filter; the container types are `video/mp4`, `video/quicktime`, `video/x-m4v`, `audio/mp4` and `audio/x-m4a` as the pinned detection names them. Place: `domain.OriginalPlanFor`, `domain.HasContainerMetadata`, `app.ScanPass.write`, 12 2 and 3.4. | 12 3.4 set `complete` before the processing of 7, a window in which the original with location data was deliverable and final for a held agent invocation; 12 7.1 and SEC-UP-10 say images are free of metadata as soon as they are stored. Without the APNG name its text chunks would reach the store unfiltered. |
| D-M3-06-2 | 2026-10-20 | The JPEG filter keeps every marker T.81 needs to decode (`SOI`, `DQT`, `DHT`, `DAC`, `DRI`, `DNL`, `DHP`, `EXP`, `SOF0` to `SOF15` except `JPG`, `SOS` with its data and `RST0` to `RST7`, `EOI`), the first `APP0` `JFIF` rewritten with the same version, units and densities but without its embedded thumbnail, every `APP2` `ICC_PROFILE` part in input order, `APP14` `Adobe`, and one new `APP1` with only "Orientation"; `JFXX`, other `APP0`, every other `APPn`, `COM`, `JPGn`, reserved markers and every byte after the first `EOI` fall away. Output order: `SOI`, `APP0`, `APP1`, then the other kept segments in input order. Stray bytes between segments are dropped like fill bytes; a second `SOI` is a broken structure. Place: `adapters/imaging/jpeg.go`, 12 7.1. | An embedded thumbnail can show what a crop removed, the reason EXIF thumbnails fall away in ADR 0047; the decoder needs exactly the T.81 markers. |
| D-M3-06-3 | 2026-10-20 | The orientation comes from the first `APP1` `Exif\0\0`; an unreadable block or a value outside 1 to 8 yields 1 and does not reject the image. The new `APP1` is big-endian with IFD0 holding only "Orientation" and no sub-IFD, written only for the values 2 to 8. Place: `adapters/imaging/exif.go`. | The block falls away anyway; value 1 needs no tag. |
| D-M3-06-4 | 2026-10-20 | A PNG with a CRC mismatch, a chunk beyond the input, `IHDR` not first or no `IEND` is a broken structure; bytes after `IEND` fall away; `eXIf` falls away like every unlisted chunk and its orientation is not applied to the thumbnails. Place: `adapters/imaging/png.go`, 12 7.1. | Broken structure in the sense of 12 7.1; original and thumbnails show the same pixels. |
| D-M3-06-5 | 2026-10-20 | A still WebP within `file.image.max_pixels` is decoded, its EXIF orientation applied to the pixels, and stored as PNG (not opaque) or JPEG with `file.image.jpeg_quality`, with its ICC profile unchanged when the profile fits the output (an `RGB ` profile; otherwise none) and no orientation tag; `mime_type` becomes `image/png` or `image/jpeg`, `original_name` keeps its extension. A GIF or still WebP above the limit or failing to decode, a malformed GIF or WebP, and every animated WebP stay unchanged as revision 1: T1 sets `complete` without a new object and without thumbnails. Place: `adapters/imaging/codec.go`, `app.ImageProcessor.keepOriginal`, 12 7.1 and 7.2. | 12 6.1: the extension decides nothing. 12 7.2 does not say what happens to the original of such a file (spec gap); keeping it is the only choice without libvips. |
| D-M3-06-6 | 2026-10-20 | Thumbnails: the first frame of an animated GIF and the default image of an APNG; all three variants for every decodable image, also when they end up the same size; PNG when the decoded image is not opaque, otherwise JPEG with `file.thumbnail.jpeg_quality`; resampler `x/image/draw.CatmullRom` (scaling before the turn of the orientation); the colour profile of the original only when its data colour space fits the thumbnail (`RGB ` for colour, `GRAY` for gray), so CMYK input carries none; a PNG profile larger than the `APP2` capacity of a JPEG means no thumbnails. Place: `adapters/imaging/render.go`, 12 7.3. | 12 7.1: the app shows `medium` in the chat and `large` in full screen, so both always exist; RK-41 names CMYK as a fidelity risk; a profile of another colour space is wrong for the output. |
| D-M3-06-7 | 2026-10-20 | A JPEG or PNG the filter refuses ends `upload_state = 'failed'` with `file_upload_sessions.failure_reason = 'image_malformed'`, `file.updated` and `files.FileFinalized`, without an audit entry; `orig-1` and any `orig-2` are deleted after the commit. Place: `app.ImageProcessor.failMalformed`. | A code without content next to `checksum_mismatch` and `size_mismatch` of 12 3.4; 05 audits `file.upload` only on a malware finding. |
| D-M3-06-8 | 2026-10-20 | Width, height and duration of an MP4 or QuickTime file are written in T3 only where a value is new, with one `file.updated`. Place: `app.ImageProcessor.containerPhase`. | The event covers every change of the `File` payload (12 2). |
| D-M3-06-9 | 2026-10-20 | `files.width` and `files.height` of images and videos are display sizes after the EXIF orientation or the `tkhd` matrix of the first video track (swapped for 90 and 270 degrees); the duration is `mvhd` duration over timescale in milliseconds, else `mehd`; any box error leaves the fields absent and never blocks the file. Place: `adapters/isobmff`, 12 7.3. | Clients lay out the original from them, and thumbnails have the same aspect; the values are display hints, not checks. |
| D-M3-06-10 | 2026-10-20 | `File.thumbnails` lists the rows of `file_thumbnails` in the order `small`, `medium`, `large`, `poster` with `variant`, `mime_type`, `width` and `height`; an empty list while there are none. `getFile` and the payload of `file.updated` use the same mapping. Place: `app.FileView`. | One order for every reader. |
| D-M3-06-11 | 2026-10-20 | Cross-check `thumbnail_sizes_ordered`: `file.thumbnail.small_px` below `file.thumbnail.medium_px` below `file.thumbnail.large_px`; the error names the keys only. `file.image.jpeg_quality` and `file.thumbnail.jpeg_quality` have the bounds 1 to 100 of Go's `jpeg.Options`. `file.image.libvips_enabled` and `file.poster_max_bytes` are not registered. Place: `internal/wire`. | Three equal variants would be pointless; the unbuilt keys would promise behaviour that does not exist. |
| D-M3-06-12 | 2026-10-20 | Every processed original is revision 2 (`orig-2`), also when the filter changed no byte; `files.object_key` points to it in T1, revision 1 is deleted after the commit and no object is overwritten. `domain.ParseOriginalKey` (M3-01) already returns the revision, so no `OriginalRevision` function was added. Place: `app.ImageProcessor`, 12 4.4. | One code path; the revision tells a re-encoded GIF from an unchanged one, the marker M3-05 uses for inline delivery. |
| D-M3-06-13 | 2026-10-20 | Build: base `int/m1-wave48` at `bba7d5a`, branch `feat/M3-06-image-metadata-thumbnails` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed (the task forbids the push and `build-verify.yml` is not on this base), so verification ran as targeted local tests and gates. No contract or spec commit from `origin/main` was needed: 03 `files` and `file_thumbnails` read the same on both. Names that differ from the brief: the ports live in `app/imageports.go` and the use case in `app/images.go` (`ImageProcessor.ProcessImage`); the per-process limiter is in the use case, not the job adapter; `domain.Variant` already existed (M3-01); `HandlerOptions.Timeout` already existed (M3-04). | The task names the base and forbids the push; the brief allows the names of the merged code. |
| D-M3-06-14 | 2026-10-20 | Retries: a JPEG is read three times (structure scan, filter into SHA-256 and counter, filter into `Put(orig-2)`), PNG twice; the second filter pass fails before its last byte when size or SHA-256 differ, so the store keeps nothing and River retries. A run that finds a `complete` file deletes the original revisions below its own and a run that finds a failed JPEG or PNG deletes its originals again, so a failed `Delete` after a commit is repaired by the retry. | 12 3.2 and 4.2 keep the server free of spool files; 12 12 deletes failed objects at once. |

Re-review fixes: the GIF block scan reads every extension exactly like `image/gif` (plain text with 13 fixed bytes, graphic control with 6, application identifier of any length including 0, unknown labels malformed), and a losing concurrent scan keeps the original of an image the winner left pending. Place: `adapters/imaging/webp.go`, `gif.go`, `app/scan.go`. | `x/image/webp` decodes the first bitstream at its own size and never reads a later VP8X, and `image/gif` read frames the old scan did not count; either let an image above `file.image.max_pixels` be decoded (SEC-UP-4). |

## M3-05 File download with download tickets

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M3-05-1 | 2026-10-20 | Ticket layout: the payload is a version byte `1`, the file id (16 raw UUID bytes), a variant code byte (`original` 0, `small` 1, `medium` 2, `large` 3, `poster` 4), the principal id and the auth session id (16 raw UUID bytes each) and the expiry as `uint64` big-endian Unix seconds; the ticket is base64url without padding (RFC 4648 section 5) of the payload followed by the full HMAC-SHA-256 tag over it, and any other length, alphabet, version or variant code is invalid. Key ring: `files/app` keeps the current key of `file.ticket_key_ref` and the key it saw before the last version change, verifies against both and mints with the current one; a rotation takes effect per process after `secrets.cache_ttl`, and a ticket minted by a process that already switched may be refused by one that has not for at most `secrets.cache_ttl`, after which the player asks for a new ticket. The previous key is kept only in the memory of a process that saw it, so a process started after the change, and every process after a second change within `file.ticket_ttl`, refuses tickets of the older key until they expire. | A fixed binary layout has one encoding per content and no parser to attack, and two keys at the same time let a key change happen without an outage (12 9.2 point 2, ADR 0048 point 2). |
| D-M3-05-2 | 2026-10-20 | `createFileTicket` for a principal without a login session (API token, agent, app) is `403 forbidden` with `reason` `step_up_not_possible`; the rule sits in the `file.read` entry of `authz` (descriptor fact `IssueTicket`) and runs after visibility, so a file the principal cannot see stays `404`. | 12 9.2 binds every ticket to `auth_session_id`, and 07 4.1 already defines this reason as "token without a session"; a token holder sends its token with every request and needs no ticket. |
| D-M3-05-3 | 2026-10-20 | On `downloadFile` and `downloadThumbnail` an `Authorization` header wins over the query parameter `ticket`: an authenticated request ignores the ticket and gets the authenticated `Cache-Control` (`private, max-age=<client.cache_max_age>, immutable`). | The header is the stronger, proof-bound credential, and one request must have exactly one actor. |
| D-M3-05-4 | 2026-10-20 | Every failure of the ticket path answers the unknown-id `404`: bad encoding or MAC, expiry, another file or variant than the route, an error of `Auth.SessionActor`, and every decision of `authorize(file.read)` that is not an allow. | 12 9.2 point 3: a ticket holder learns nothing about why a ticket stopped working or whether the file exists. |
| D-M3-05-5 | 2026-10-20 | A deleted file and a thumbnail variant the file does not have answer like an unknown id (`404 not_found`). | 12 2 ends the state "deleted" without delivery; clients request only the variants `File.thumbnails` lists, so a missing variant of a readable file reveals nothing worth a code of its own. |
| D-M3-05-6 | 2026-10-20 | A missing object of a deliverable file is `500 internal_error` with the file id only in the error log, never in the body; every other failure of the object storage is `503 unavailable`. | A deliverable file without its object is a server fault, not a client error, while a storage outage is transient (02 14.4). |
| D-M3-05-7 | 2026-10-20 | `createFileTicket` checks readiness like a download: no ticket for a file that is not deliverable (`409 file_not_ready` with `Retry-After` or `409 file_blocked`). | A ticket for a file that cannot be delivered would only lead the player into a second `409`. |
| D-M3-05-8 | 2026-10-20 | `getTranscript` answers every readable deliverable file, also one with `status` `none`; `text`, `language` and `source` are present only with `done` (no JSON `null`). | The status itself is the information a client needs, and ADR 0053 forbids `null` members. |
| D-M3-05-9 | 2026-10-20 | Delivery headers as in 12 9.3; the ASCII fallback of `Content-Disposition` replaces every character outside printable ASCII and every `"`, `\`, `%` and `;` by `_`, and an empty name becomes `file`; `filename*` carries the UTF-8 bytes percent-encoded outside the RFC 8187 `attr-char` set. A thumbnail's name is the original name without its last extension, then `-<variant>`, then `.jpg` or `.png` after its type. | The fallback can never break the header or be read as a parameter separator, and a thumbnail gets a name with the extension of its own type. |
| D-M3-05-10 | 2026-10-20 | The `ETag` and `Repr-Digest` of a thumbnail come from one extra full read of its object through `ObjectStore.Get` before the second, possibly ranged read that serves it. | 03 gives `file_thumbnails` no checksum column; hashing a stream keeps memory constant and thumbnails are small. |
| D-M3-05-11 | 2026-10-20 | Range rules: only the unit `bytes`; one range `first-last`, `first-` or `-suffix` gives `206`; several ranges or an invalid value give `200`; a first byte at or after the end or a zero suffix gives `416` with `Content-Range: bytes */<size>`. `If-Range` applies the range only with the current strong `ETag`; there is no `304` and `If-None-Match` is not evaluated. | RFC 9110 section 14 within 12 9.3, which supports exactly one range; the server sends no `Last-Modified`, and content never changes once deliverable (12 4.4). |
| D-M3-05-12 | 2026-10-20 | New key `file.not_ready_retry_after` (default the same value as `http.unavailable_retry_after`) gives the `Retry-After` of `409 file_not_ready` in whole seconds. | 12 9.3 demands `Retry-After` but named no key, and limits come only from named keys. |
| D-M3-05-13 | 2026-10-20 | A ticket request is anonymous in the chain, so `rl.read` is taken from the bucket of the client IP; an invalid ticket is a `404` of the handler, not a failed credential, so it does not count against `rl.public_auth` and is not audited. A valid ticket whose `authorize` fails is audited with the ticket's principal. | Before the ticket verifies no principal exists to audit, and the MAC is not guessable; once it verifies the denial belongs to a real principal (07 4.2). |
| D-M3-05-14 | 2026-10-20 | Transfer deadline: the route flag `Download` of M1-17 already marks binary `2xx` responses and `StageRoute` applies `http.transfer_timeout` to them, so no new flag. The route generator accepts the requirement `fileTicket` beside a subset of the root security and records such an operation as root security: the chain authenticates the header as on every route, an anonymous request reaches the handler, which checks the ticket, and a console cookie authenticates a download like any route. | The existing flag already has the meaning the brief asked for, and the ticket is checked by the use case, not by the chain. |
| D-M3-05-15 | 2026-10-20 | Start check: the roles `api` and `all` do not start when `file.ticket_key_ref` is missing, unreadable or holds less than 32 bytes as hex, the length of `file_ticket_key` in [configuration.md](operations/configuration.md) section 5. | A server that cannot mint or verify tickets would fail every player at run time; failing at start makes the fault visible at once. |
| D-M3-05-16 | 2026-10-20 | The migration `00049_files_thumbnails.sql` creates `file_thumbnails` exactly as 03 4.7 describes; M3-06 uses it and creates nothing. | `downloadThumbnail` reads the table, and M3-06 is not in the dependency closure of this ticket. |
| D-M3-05-17 | 2026-10-20 | The ticket is declared as the optional query parameter `FileTicketQuery` (name `ticket`) on `downloadFile` and `downloadThumbnail`, besides the security scheme `fileTicket`. | The strict server hands a handler only declared parameters, so without the parameter the handler would never see the ticket. |
| D-M3-05-18 | 2026-10-20 | `file.read` per purpose: the visibility of `avatar` (every authenticated principal), `branding` (everybody) and `gdpr_export` (nobody) applies only to `file.read`; `file.delete` keeps its rule. `downloadFile` is not a public operation, so an anonymous caller gets `401` before the purpose rule and reaches a branding file only through `getBrandingLogo` (M3-10). The requirement is substituted inside `Decide` (`avatar` `Auth`, `branding` `Public`), and the requirement table row stays `read`. | Scope requires exactly `read` for list actions, and filter mode decides `file.updated` events without facts by their channel path; substituting per purpose keeps both intact (05 2.2). |
| D-M3-05-19 | 2026-10-20 | `Auth.SessionActor` also treats a device without contact for `auth.device_inactive_revoke_after` as an inactive key, like the Authenticator, and uses two reads (`AuthState` and `ActiveFamily`) instead of one joined query. | A ticket must not outlive what a request of the same device would be refused for, and the two existing store reads avoid a new query with the same result. |
| D-M3-05-20 | 2026-10-20 | The ticket is built on the integration base int/m1-wave48 on branch feat/M3-05-file-download and is not merged or opened as a pull request by the build; the brief's landing steps (pull request, squash merge, worktree removal) belong to the integration lane. Full verification runs as build-verify on the build branch. | The build task of the steering lane overrides the landing steps of the brief, so several tickets integrate in one place. |

## M2-45 Host service: sessions, PTY and profiles

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-45-1 | 2026-10-20 | OQ-03 (brief Design note 0d): path A (a token of `claude setup-token` in `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`) and path B (`/login` with the login in a file of the home, on macOS in the keychain with the file as fallback) are documented, so the profile `claude` with `personal` is built (`profiles.PersonalLoginDocumented`); the quotes stand in 24 7.4. The question itself stays with the owner. `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` and `docs/RISKS.md` do not exist on this integration base, so OQ-03, RK-30 and RK-23 are updated by the port to `main`, not here. | The brief builds `personal` only when one of the two paths is documented; the three pages were read on 2026-10-20 and contain no restriction to non-interactive use. The missing files are a known gap of this base (D-M2-42-20). |
| D-M2-45-2 | 2026-10-20 | `HostChannelHello` carries `clock_leeway_ms`, `idle_timeout_ms` and `detach_grace_ms` (`auth.clock_leeway`, `terminal.idle_timeout`, `terminal.detach_grace`); the host service applies those of the data connection that carries the handshake (24 2.2 point 5, 5.3, 6.3), and every attach refreshes them. | These are server values the host service must apply; `hello` already carries server values and comes on every data connection, and the server reads its configuration only at start, so no extra push is needed. |
| D-M2-45-3 | 2026-10-20 | Brief Design note 2b was found merged: `ready` is sent again after a committed change of the declaration by `ControlLinks.SendReady` (`SetLlmCredentials`, `afterConfirm`), on the node that served the request. The log tail observer of the brief was not built. ## M2-47 Terminal relay across several nodes

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-47-1 | 2026-10-20 | Scope: besides `session.open` over `NOTIFY ephemeral` and the hash rule of the edge, M2-47 builds the probe of an older control connection across nodes (24 3.4), the closing of a host's connections on every node at a transition or re-pairing (24 3.1, 3.3) and the check "host connected" across nodes behind `409 host_unavailable` (24 6.1 point 2, 13). The end of a session needs no signal: it reaches every api node through the event log tail (M2-44 Design note 11), which M2-47 proves with two nodes. The code stays node-neutral; with `cluster.mode = single` none of it runs. | Without these paths `cluster.mode = multi` would keep the control connection of a disabled or re-paired host open on another node, hide a copied key and answer `409 host_unavailable` by chance of the node; no other ticket builds them (M7-17 covers gateway, `runs` and `agents`). Place: 24 section 9, `internal/terminal`. |
| D-M2-47-2 | 2026-10-20 | Mode and roles: `cluster.mode` is the enum `single` or `multi` of 02 22 (default `single`, place Server), registered in `internal/wire/configkeys_cluster.go` with `chapter02`. `single`: `LocalSignals`, no listener, no `NOTIFY`, no presence view, no probation. `multi` in the roles `api` and `all`: `NotifySignals` and one `EphemeralListener` per process. `multi` in the role `worker`: `NotifySignals` without listener. | Keys enter with their first consumer (M1-07); the worker holds no connection, and the ends it commits need no signal. Place: `internal/wire/terminal_cluster.go`, 02 22. |
| D-M2-47-3 | 2026-10-20 | Kinds and payload: `terminal.session_open` (`host_id`, `session_id`), `terminal.host_close` (`host_id`), `terminal.host_present` and `terminal.host_gone` (`host_id`, `conn_id`), `terminal.session_paired` (`host_id`, `session_id`); compact JSON with exactly these members, each a UUID string, never `null`. Decoding is strict: unknown or missing members, a non-UUID or an unknown kind with the prefix `terminal.` drop the payload with one log record; a kind outside the prefix is ignored without a log. `terminal.session_open` registers the expectation on every api node (`RelayControl.Expect` with the device of the row and the receipt time as grant time); only the node of an eligible control connection sends `session.open`. On a host with `require_local_confirm = true` a side-less expectation that received `terminal.session_paired` only removes itself at its deadline. The receiver always reads the state from the database. | The node the edge hashes the session to must hold the expectation whichever node served the REST call; late, repeated, reordered or forged signals then change nothing wrong; M7-17 adds its own kinds to the same channel; only the fact of a pairing may spare an opening waiting for local consent, because the server does not know `host_service.local_confirm_timeout`. Place: `domain/cluster.go`, 24 section 9, 02 11.6. |
| D-M2-47-4 | 2026-10-20 | Port `app.Signals` (`Stage` in the unit of work, `Committed` after it, `Send` outside any unit) with one `SignalHandler` for both modes: `LocalSignals` (`single`) calls the local handler in `Committed`; `NotifySignals` (`multi`) executes `NotifyEphemeral` on the unit's transaction in `Stage` and does nothing in `Committed`, because every node, the sender included, receives the notification through its listener. No use case calls the relay directly after a commit any more. | PostgreSQL delivers a staged notification only after the commit and never after a rollback; a local shortcut on the sending node would act twice or need a second code path, so the single-node behaviour is the multi-node behaviour with a local transport. Place: `app/signals.go`, `adapters/postgres/signals.go`. |
| D-M2-47-5 | 2026-10-20 | Listener: `EphemeralListener` opens its own connection (`bothy-terminal-ephemeral`), executes `LISTEN ephemeral`, hands each signal in order to the handler, logs a loss once, retries at once and then every `database.listen_reconnect_backoff_cap`, and after `LISTEN` succeeded again runs the reconciliation (host states of all its control and data connections by `HostStatesByIDs`, close what is no longer `active`, announce every eligible control connection at once). Soft dependency like the tail's `LISTEN`: no readiness check; stop step `terminal_ephemeral` in `lifecycle.PhaseEventTail`; an unexpected end of its goroutine goes to `lifecycle.Runner.Fail`. One listener per process. | Reconciling only after `LISTEN` succeeded misses nothing committed in between; ends of sessions reach the relay through the log tail, which has its own `LISTEN` and poll; no new key, because 02 22 already bounds a `LISTEN` reconnect. Place: `adapters/postgres/ephemeral_listener.go`, 02 14.4, 24 section 9. |

"Host connected" is an eligible local control connection or a counting remote entry. | 03 has no column for the connection of a host; the window is the silence after which the holder closes the connection itself, so a dead node's entry counts at most that long and an opening it lets pass ends `open_failed` with `open_timeout`. ## M7-04 Restore finalize and sync epoch

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-04-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave49` at `eb15f40`, branch `feat/M7-04-restore-finalize-epoch` in its own worktree; not merged locally, not pushed, no pull request (the launcher's task forbids the push, so the build branch `build/M7-04` and the GitHub run of the full suite do not exist for this ticket; locally only the tests of the touched packages ran). The core rule R-DM-50 of `main` (the two `instance` columns, item 4 of 03 6.7) is on `main` but not on the base and comes as its own commit first, in English inside the German chapter `03-datenmodell.md`. The base names the chapters in German (`02-architektur.md`, `16-betrieb.md`, `0059-sync-epoche-nach-wiederherstellung.md`) and the brief's 02 section 11 (readiness) is 15.1 and 15.2 there; added text is English. The rows stand in a section of the ticket, because the base has no section 10 in this file. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-04-1 | 2026-10-20 | Identity source and grants: `system_identifier` of `pg_control_system()` as decimal text and `timeline_id` of `pg_control_checkpoint()`, read in one statement. PostgreSQL 18 grants `EXECUTE` on both to `PUBLIC`: `pg_proc.proacl` is NULL (the default ACL) in the pinned image `postgres:18.6-trixie`, and `has_function_privilege` is true for a role without grants (checked 2026-10-20); the documentation lists them under "Control Data Functions" (https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/functions-info.html). So `migrate up --bootstrap` grants nothing and the grant test of 16 7.1 rule 3 stays as it is. | Brief design note 1: a grant is only needed when `bothy_app` may not execute them, which the catalog of the image disproves. |
| D-M7-04-2 | 2026-10-20 | Audit actions: `instance.restore.finalize` after a restore and `instance.restore.finalize_relocation` after a relocation; decision `allow`, no actor (system), resource `instance` with `instance_id`, details `head_seq`, `previous_sync_epoch`, `sync_epoch`, `pg_system_identifier` (code) and `pg_timeline_id`, no content. The entry is written by the audit hook of the unit of work that moves epoch, cursors and identity. The actions are not entries of the action catalogue (05, `actions.yaml`), which this ticket may not edit; they are host commands without authorization, like the other audit codes of system steps. | Brief design notes 2 and 5; the catalogue is core contract (fixed requirement 10). |
| D-M7-04-3 | 2026-10-20 | Start lock: every role compares the stored identity with the current one once at start, after the instance row loaded (02 15.1 step 5); a mismatch logs one ERROR line with the runbook step and registers the readiness check `restore_not_finalized`, which fails for good like `schema_requires_newer_server` (the process keeps running, `/healthz` stays 200); unlike the version lock it also skips the steps that serve or process (log tail, River jobs, worker consumers, public listener), because the Compose stack publishes `api` without a proxy that follows `/readyz`, so readiness alone would not keep writes and deliveries of a mixed timeline out (red team finding). The documented command in the stack is `docker compose run --rm migrate restore finalize` (the entrypoint of the image is `bothy`), with the manifest of `--relocation` mounted into the read-only container. A read error of the identity ends the start. An empty stored identity never blocks. Because the comparison runs at start, a failover while the server runs is detected at the next start. `bothy restore finalize` refuses while a server runs (D-M7-04-5), so the order is: stop `api` and `worker`, finalize, start. | Brief design note 3; the same pattern as the version lock of M1-15. |
| D-M7-04-4 | 2026-10-20 | `database.restore_guard` (`on`, `off`; default `on`, chapter 16): `off` skips the comparison at start; `bothy restore finalize` behaves the same with either value. `--relocation <file>` reads `backup_manifest.v1` (checked by `status/domain.ParseManifest` as bothy-backupd writes it) before the database is touched and compares, in this order, `event_log.head_seq` with `event_seq` (equal to `max(seq)` because the partition of the head is never dropped), `audit.last_seq` and `audit.last_hash` (lowercase hex) with the last row of `audit_events` and `sync_epoch` with the instance row; the first difference is exit `1` naming the field, and nothing changes. A major upgrade into a new cluster and a lossless managed failover are finalized the same way with the manifest of a backup taken right before (runbooks 16 8.4, disaster recovery case G). | Brief design note 3 and requirement 13; reading the manifest first keeps a wrong path from touching the database. |
| D-M7-04-5 | 2026-10-20 | Refusal while servers run: inside the unit of work, before the instance row is locked, `bothy restore finalize` counts the sessions of `pg_stat_activity` with `usename = bothy_app` on the current database (`datname = current_database()`) other than its own and refuses with exit `1` when there is one. The command runs as `database.user`, the schema owner, as in the Compose service `migrate`. Restricting the count to the current database keeps other databases of a shared cluster out (the integration tests share one cluster). | Brief requirement 16; `usename` and `datname` of every row are visible to every role. |
| D-M7-04-6 | 2026-10-20 | Module boundaries: instance declares the outgoing ports `CursorMover` and `AuditHeadSource` itself, and agents, apps and sync each get a new one-method port `RestoreCursors` (`MoveCursorsTo`) with `app.NewRestoreCursors` over their repository; `internal/wire` passes them in (`RestoreCursors`, in the order agents, apps, sync) and adapts the audit repository's bounds and hash to `AuditHeadSource`. No `depends_on` changes in `architecture/modules.yaml` and 02 4.5, because instance imports none of these modules. The webhook epoch needed no change: every attempt already reads `sync_epoch` from the instance row when it signs (M2-26), and `TestWebhookCarriesEpoch` proves the new epoch after finalize. Differences to the brief's names (D-W-100): the use case is `instance.Restore.Finalize` (the brief says `FinalizeRestore`, which is its input type here), the identity check is `instance.State.CheckIdentity`, and the readiness test of the brief's `TestFinalizeRestore_RefusesWithConnectedApp` exists in `internal/wire` and, for the exit code, in `cmd/bothy`. | agents, apps and sync depend on instance (02 4.5), so a direct call would be an import cycle; one small port per module keeps the change minimal. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|

A failure stores nothing; a staged file whose unit of work fails stays an open session until `file.upload_session_ttl`. | D-M3-10-3 | 2026-10-20 | `original_name` of an avatar file is `avatar` and of a logo file `logo` (`FixedOriginalName`); the file name of the multipart part is ignored. Place: `files/domain/purpose.go`, 12 3.7. | Every signed-in principal reads these files, and a client's file name could carry personal data (12 1 point 5). |
| D-M3-10-4 | 2026-10-20 | Staging an avatar or logo checks the open sessions (`file.upload_max_open_sessions`, `429`) and the free space of the file system driver (`file.fs.min_free_bytes`, `503 storage_full`) as 12 3.1, but not the quota of 12 3.1 point 3. Place: `files/app/profileimages.go`, 12 3.7. | An avatar or logo replaces the previous one, so it adds at most one live file per person or instance, and `openapi.yaml` documents no `409` for these operations. |
| D-M3-10-5 | 2026-10-20 | The new file is linked in the unit of work of the upload, before it is deliverable: lock the anchor row (the principal row, or the setting row `branding.logo_file_id` under the settings lock of M1-46, inserted when absent), complete the session and insert `files.scan` (`CompleteStagedImage`), write the new id, set `deleted_at` on the previous file of the same purpose and uploader (`ReleaseImage`), append `user.updated` or `instance.updated`. Readers get `409 file_not_ready` with `Retry-After` until the file is deliverable, `getBrandingLogo` `404`. The objects of a released file and of an abandoned session stay until `files.purge_objects` and `files.cleanup_pending` of M3-08 remove them. Place: `identity/app`, `instance/app`, `files/app/profileimages.go`, 12 3.7 and 12. | One unit of work keeps link and release consistent and the lock order of 03 6.3 (anchor row, then `files`); a client sees the change at once and retries the bytes, the login screen shows the product mark meanwhile (01 12). |
| D-M3-10-6 | 2026-10-20 | Purpose rule: a file with purpose `avatar` or `branding` is kept only when its original is stored as filtered JPEG, filtered PNG or converted still WebP (`StoredOriginalAllowed` over `OriginalKind`). The scan pass fails a detected type whose original would be stored unchanged or as a re-encoded GIF, and `files.thumbnail` fails a WebP that stays unchanged (animated, or above `file.image.max_pixels`); both set `upload_state = 'failed'` with `failure_reason` `file_type_denied` and `file.updated` before the file can become deliverable, and the objects go like those of every failed upload. A JPEG or PNG above `file.image.max_pixels` is filtered without decoding, stays deliverable and gets no thumbnails. Attachments are unchanged. Place: `files/domain/purpose.go`, `files/app/scan.go`, `files/app/images.go`, 12 3.7. | `ImageUpload` admits JPEG, PNG and WebP, and the promise "without metadata" holds only for originals the server filtered or converted (12 7.1 'Grenze der Zusage'). |
| D-M3-10-7 | 2026-10-20 | `file.read` chooses its requirement by purpose: `attachment` keeps the read right on the path (M3-03); `avatar` and `branding` need only `auth`, so every active, signed-in principal that passes step 1 of 07 4.2 reads them (apps still need the scope `files:read`), and anonymous callers are answered `unauthenticated` in step 1 because `file.read` is not public; `gdpr_export` is never visible through `file.read` (`not_found`, its path is `gdpr.export.download`). A deleted file is `not_found` like an unknown id. A `file` descriptor without path and purpose (built from an event row) is visible only to its `OwnerID`. Place: `authz/domain/requirements.go`, `visibility.go`, 07 4.3. | 05 2.2 gives each purpose its own read path; the only anonymous path to logo bytes is `getBrandingLogo` with `instance.discovery.read`; a descriptor that cannot be checked fails closed. |
| D-M3-10-8 | 2026-10-20 | `file.updated` of an avatar or logo file is a personal event of the uploader: `audience_principal_id = uploaded_by`, empty scope, resource `{kind: file, id}`, receive action `file.read`, payload `FilePayload`. `authorize.ResourceFromEvent` takes the audience and sets `OwnerID` from it for kind `file` only, so the receive check admits exactly the uploader. Place: `files/app` (`FileUpdated`), `kernel/authorize`, 12 3.7. | Only the uploader waits for the result; other readers learn of the new file through `user.updated` or `instance.updated` and retry after `Retry-After`; `event_log` has no column for the purpose (03 4.10), so a broadcast could not be checked per recipient. |
| D-M3-10-9 | 2026-10-20 | `getBrandingLogo` serves only the variant `medium` of a deliverable file with purpose `branding` (`OpenImageVariant`), read once to compute `ETag` (`"<sha256 hex>"`) and `Repr-Digest` before headers and body are sent, with `Content-Disposition: inline` and the file name `logo.<ext>`, the SEC-HDR-4 headers, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` without credentials, `Cache-Control: no-store`, and no `Range` support (`Range` is ignored, `200` with the whole variant). No logo, a pending, blocked, failed or deleted file and a file without that variant give the same `404`. Every allow of `instance.branding.update` is audited in the unit of work: upload `{changed_keys: ["branding.logo_file_id"], file_id}`, removal `{changed_keys: ["branding.logo_file_id"]}`, a removal without logo `{changed_keys: []}` without a write or an event. Avatar operations write no audit entry.
