## M2-05 Runs, state machine and trigger state

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-05-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave49` at `eb15f40`, branch `feat/M2-05-runs-state-machine` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed as a pull request, the PR lane ports it. Brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the columns `renewed_from_jti` and `invocation_held_until` of 03 `runs` with R-DM-44 and R-DM-47, and the reasons `channel_read_only`, `tool_class_owner_only` and `tool_class_mismatch` of 09 5.2, which the base lacked, were brought in from main as their own commit. Text this ticket adds to the German chapters is English. The migrations take the next free numbers of the base. | The task names the base and the lane; the merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M2-05-1 | 2026-10-20 | The acceptance of 09 3.4 point 7 is built here; the personal event `agent.invocation` is written by M2-08 together with its delivery check. | A written but unchecked invocation could reach an agent connection before I2 is checked. |
| D-M2-05-2 | 2026-10-20 | With the auto thread, `agent.invoke` is checked on the thread of M and the new thread is created only on `allow`. Place: `runs/app/accept.go`, 09 3.4. | No empty thread after a denial, and the mention rule of 09 3.4 point 3 and the guard judge the same thread. |
| D-M2-05-3 | 2026-10-20 | `createMessage` appends the `message.created` events of the unit (system line, then the message) before it publishes `MessagePosted` (same order); the acceptance sets `received` through `SetTriggerState` (version plus one, `message.updated`), and the `201` and replay bodies are the stored row after the unit of work. | A subscriber that appends a message to the same thread (the anchor) must not overtake the message in the log (04 10); 04 16.5 shows `trigger_state received` in the `201`. |
| D-M2-05-4 | 2026-10-20 | `runs.invocation_jti` of a new run is a fresh UUIDv7 that no token carries until the first delivery replaces it. | 03 requires the column and its uniqueness; no token exists at acceptance. |
| D-M2-05-5 | 2026-10-20 | `billing_mode_expected` of a new run is the agent's billing mode at acceptance (`agents.Facts`); each delivery sets it again (03). | The column is NOT NULL and the mode is known at acceptance. |
| D-M2-05-6 | 2026-10-20 | The anchor is `silent`, written in the target thread after the run row; its `client_msg_id` is a server UUIDv7 and its request hash covers `run-anchor:<run>`, kind `run` and the empty content. | 09 5.4: the anchor notifies nobody; the deferred FK `messages.run_id` allows either order and `Open` writes the run first. |
| D-M2-05-7 | 2026-10-20 | The reason table is 09 5.2 plus `user_disabled` (`cancelled`, server), which 02 4.3 names for runs cancelled by `user.disable`; 09 5.2 has the row. Place: `runs/domain/reason.go`. | One table for the server, the adapter rule and the metrics. |
| D-M2-05-8 | 2026-10-20 | An adapter `failed` keeps its reason only when 09 5.2 lists the adapter as setter; every other code, server-only codes included, is stored as `adapter_error`, and the original code is the detail `original_reason` of one server step `kind = status`, `name = adapter_reason`, `status = failed`, cut to `run.step_detail_max_bytes` after `kernel/textcheck` (a rejected code gives `{}`). 09 5.2 names this place. | 09 5.2 last sentence; a server-only code from an adapter would let it claim a server finding. |
| D-M2-05-9 | 2026-10-20 | `runs` and `run_steps` have no `channel_id` (03, M1-62). A read of them either reads the rows of exactly one thread or run whose visibility was checked first (`after_authorize`, `GetRun` as `authorize_resolution`), or, for the active-run lists, reads the active runs, resolves their threads through `chat.Threads.Locate`, keeps only threads whose channel is in `Scope.ChannelIDs` and runs `authorize` per row (the new category `thread_scope` of the SQL visibility gate); the gauge query has no principal (`system_job`). 07 7.4 rule 1 says so. | The predicate of 07 7.4 rule 1 needs a channel column; the copy would go stale when a thread moves. |
| D-M2-05-10 | 2026-10-20 | `listThreadRuns` proves its route action through `Scope(run.read)`; an invisible thread gives the same `404` as an unknown one. | 02 9.3 and 02 9.2. |
| D-M2-05-11 | 2026-10-20 | `listActiveRuns` pages by `(queued_at DESC, id DESC)` and fills a page across batches; `next_cursor` is the keyset of the last examined row when the page is full and another row exists. | Rows dropped by the filter must not shorten a page, and the next page must not repeat or skip a row. |
| D-M2-05-12 | 2026-10-20 | Until M2-11 the follow-up outcome writes nothing; `multiple_agents_mentioned` writes `failed` without `authorize` and without audit. | There is no single agent to check, and M2-11 owns follow-ups. |
| D-M2-05-13 | 2026-10-20 | A candidate that M does not mention is first checked in filter mode; a denial with `level_insufficient` there writes nothing and no audit entry. | R-AG-05: someone who may only write sees no error for every message. |
| D-M2-05-14 | 2026-10-20 | When the decision of M2-04 rejected but `authorize` allows, the acceptance fails closed with the reason of M2-04 and logs `trigger_decision_mismatch` without IDs. | Two rules that disagree are a bug; the safe outcome is no run. |
| D-M2-05-15 | 2026-10-20 | Run metrics are recorded after the commit of the outermost unit through `txn.AfterCommit`; `bothy.run.active` is an observable gauge registered in the roles `worker` and `all`. `bothy.agent.handle` has the closed source `agent_handles` of the metric registry: values are checked against the handle pattern of 03 `principals.handle` at recording, so a new agent needs no restart. | A retried unit would count twice; signals.md 2 item 1 counts agent handles among the sets the operator bounds. |
| D-M2-05-16 | 2026-10-20 | The FKs of `runs` to `agent_sessions`, `commands`, `interactions` and `dev_environments` come with M2-12, M2-16, M2-15 and V2-01; `messages.run_id` gets its deferred FK here. | The referenced tables do not exist yet. |
| D-M2-05-17 | 2026-10-20 | `SetTriggerState` with the stored state and reason writes nothing and appends no event. | A repeated delivery must not raise the version. |
| D-M2-05-18 | 2026-10-20 | The ports gain two small read interfaces that the brief's note 6 step 2 needs and the merged ports lack: `chat.TriggerFacts.TriggerChannel` (effective operator and the five switches of 09 3.3, no authorize) and `agents.Facts.RunFacts` (handle, status, billing mode, holder and tool class of a set of agents, no authorize). | The acceptance must read these facts through ports (02 4.4); `AgentConfig` authorizes `manage` and `agents.Agents` authorizes `agent.read`, neither fits a subscriber. |
| D-M2-05-19 | 2026-10-20 | The acceptance is the in-transaction subscriber `runs/app.Acceptor`, created with the module objects of the txn registry and bound to the runs use cases in the chat step of the start; before `Bind` it writes nothing, so a test world built without the runs module keeps the behaviour of M1. | The registry is built before the use cases exist (`wire.NewModules`), like the subscribers of sync and notify. |
| D-M2-05-20 | 2026-10-20 | The acceptance passes `AuthorHasTrigger = true` and `WriteOnChannel = true` to `Decide`: the trigger right and the agent's own write level are decided by `agent.invoke` (filter mode first for a candidate without mention, then request mode, which audits the denial), whose result maps through `Settle`. A rejection that names a candidate (`agent_not_allowed_here`, `run_active_other_agent`) is checked with `authorize` too. | Facts about rights come from `authorize`, never from a grant query (brief note 6 step 2); the audit of a denied mention needs the request-mode call. |
| D-M2-05-21 | 2026-10-20 | 03 `runs` allows ten transitions, not the nine the brief counts: `queued` has three, `running` four and `waiting_input` three targets. `CanTransition` and its test follow 03. | The data model is the core contract. |
| D-M2-05-22 | 2026-10-20 | In the integration tests the mentions of Texter come from the owner, not from Alex as the brief's test list says. | `agent.read` (05, "read on a channel the agent operates") lets Alex mention only agents that operate a channel Alex reads; Texter operates Marketing, so "@texter" from Alex stays text and the rejection under test would never be reached. |

## M3-11 Channel export

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M3-11-1 | 2026-10-20 | Range rule of `exportChannel`: `from` must be strictly before `to`; an empty or reversed range is `400 validation_failed` with one field error, pointer `#query/to`, code `invalid`, checked before any lookup. There is no upper bound on the length of the range. | openapi states no rule, and a check without a lookup cannot reveal whether the channel exists; no configuration key names an upper bound, `http.transfer_timeout` bounds one response, and clients split long ranges. |
| D-M3-11-2 | 2026-10-20 | Line content: every line is the shared `Message` representation of M1-57 with the full `content_md`, never cut at `message.list_content_max_bytes`, so `content_truncated` is absent; `my_reactions` is absent; tombstones are included in the representation of M1-57 at their `thread_seq`. `attachments`, `reactions`, `pinned` and `components` are whatever the shared representation emits. | An export is the complete record of the channel; `my_reactions` is the caller's state, not channel content, and optional in `Message`; a tombstone keeps `thread_seq` and shows no content (04 "Thread sequence"); one representation lets the export follow M3-07, M1-59 and M2 without a change. |
| D-M3-11-3 | 2026-10-20 | Order: threads ascending by their UUIDv7 id, the messages of a thread ascending by `thread_seq`. The pair `(thread_id, thread_seq)` is the keyset of the walk; thread pages (`ListExportThreadIDs`) and message pages (`ListExportThreadMessages`) both use the page size `api.page_size_max`, and a batch is one message page. | openapi fixes only "`thread_seq` order per thread"; UUIDv7 ids are ordered by creation and order never comes from timestamps (03 "Principles" items 1 and 2), so the pair is a total, resumable order in which every message of the selection is emitted exactly once. |
| D-M3-11-4 | 2026-10-20 | Audit: exactly one allow entry `export.channel` with resource kind `channel`, the channel id and the details `from` and `to` only, recorded with `auditing.Recorder.Record` in its own unit of work and committed before the status line is written. If that unit of work fails, the request ends with the problem of the error kind and no line is sent. | No export without its audit entry (T-AZ-04); the entry names the range and not the result size, because the size is known only at the end and the chain only appends. |
| D-M3-11-5 | 2026-10-20 | Batches: there is no snapshot across batches. Before every page the stream starts a fresh request memo (`authorize.WithoutRequestScope`, then `authorize.WithRequestScope`; `kernel/authorize` is unchanged), runs the credential re-check (D-M3-11-9), checks `channel.read` in filter mode and takes `Scope(message.read)` at that moment; any refusal ends the stream with `ErrExportAborted`. Every row passes `message.read` in filter mode on the same memo; a refused row is dropped, logged and counted in `bothy.authz.errors` as `filter_mismatch`. The capability `export` and the step-up are checked once, at the start. | Each line shows the rights at the time of delivery (07 8.2 item 2, 04 8.1), and no transaction stays open while lines go out (02 8.5); one memo per batch keeps a revocation from looking like a filter mismatch. The action is the start of the export (05 2.4), so an ageing step-up does not cut a running export, but a lost read right does. |
| D-M3-11-6 | 2026-10-20 | Abort: after the first byte, an aborted stream, a read error, the deadline or a shutdown logs only a cause code and the route pattern and panics with `http.ErrAbortHandler`, so HTTP/1.1 ends without the final chunk and HTTP/2 resets the stream. A write error (client gone) ends the stream quietly. A range without messages gives `200` with an empty body. | After the status line no problem document can follow; a cut transfer is the only signal a client cannot take for a complete export, and an empty selection is a valid, complete export. |
| D-M3-11-7 | 2026-10-20 | Transfer deadline: the route flag `Download` of M1-17 already marks every operation whose `2xx` media type is not JSON, `application/x-ndjson` included, and `StageRoute` applies `http.transfer_timeout` to it. Enabling `exportChannel` sets the flag, so the route generator and `StageRoute` need no change and nothing is renamed; `exportAuditEvents` has had the flag since M1-50. | The existing flag already has the meaning the brief asked for (02 14.2 "upload and download"); without it a long export would end after `http.request_timeout`. |
| D-M3-11-8 | 2026-10-20 | The export streams NDJSON and stores nothing in `files`; export packages in `files` belong only to the privacy export (M7-06). The bullet of 02 section 4.6 is corrected accordingly. | openapi `exportChannel` streams the export (06 "Basic rules": NDJSON lets large exports stream), and 03 `files.purpose` has no value for a channel export. |
| D-M3-11-9 | 2026-10-20 | Credential re-check: before every page the stream calls `kernel/actor.Authenticator.Revalidate` through the messaging port `CredentialCheck`; `internal/wire` binds it to the Authenticator of identity lazily, because the session start step runs after the chat step. A refusal ends the stream like a lost read right (`ErrExportAborted`). | A revoked session or device raises no rights version, so the cached liveness facts of `authz` would keep allowing a stream that outlives its request (T-AZ-03). |
| D-M3-11-10 | 2026-10-20 | The ticket is built on the integration base on branch feat/M3-11-channel-export on top of the stacked M3-05 build and is not merged or opened as a pull request by the build; the brief's landing steps belong to the integration lane. Full verification runs as build-verify on the build branch. | As D-M3-05-20: the build task of the steering lane overrides the landing steps of the brief, so several tickets integrate in one place. |

## M4-22 Files, images and voice messages on the desktop

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-22-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave48` at `bba7d5a` with `feat/M3-05-file-download` at `9db6919` merged first (stacked build); branch `feat/M4-22-files-desktop` in its own worktree, not merged locally and not pushed by the build, the PR lane lands it. Nothing is ported from `main`: D-W-100, D-W-114, D-W-115, SEC-OUT-2 and T-AI-06 are on the base, and the file operations of `docs/api/openapi.yaml` on the base are the newer ones of M3-03 and M3-05 (`UploadCreate.sha256` is required lowercase hex, not Base64 as 12 3.1 says; the client follows the API). The base names the chapters in German, so 15 7.5 and 15 14 are changed in `15-client-architektur.md`; on `main` the same change belongs into `15-client-architecture.md`: "the message waits until the upload session of each of its files is completed (`completeUpload` with `200`, 12 3.4) and then attaches them; the check of the server goes on, and the server holds the agent invocation until it is final (12 10)". Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code: the instance folder is `<application support>/instances/<instance id>` (M4-18), so the cache lies in `instances/<instance id>/files`; the composer is `composer/thread_composer.dart`; the session column frame is `session_column/session_column.dart`; the voice controller lives in `voice/voice_button.dart`; providers are in `files/providers.dart`. | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-22-1 | 2026-10-20 | Attach after completion: an upload entry is confirmed when `completeUpload` answered `200` (12 3.4), which releases the message; the message holds one slot `file:<key>` per attachment that the outbox fills from the upload's result (D-M4-15-6), and the wrapped `createMessage` executor turns the slots into `attachment_file_ids`. An upload confirmed before the message was written gives its file ID through `UploadProgress` of the run. Parts: at most two in flight per upload (`UploadOps.parallelParts`), each read alone from disk with `Content-Digest`; after any failure the entry is retried by the outbox with the same `Idempotency-Key`, so `createUpload` returns the same session and only the parts `getUpload` did not confirm are sent. `completeUpload` uses a second key derived from the entry ID. Uploads send in their own lane `upload:<entry id>`, so a long upload never holds the writes of the thread written before its message; the message itself waits in the thread's lane until its uploads are confirmed, and the writes written after it wait behind it (04 13.2 item 2). `part_conflict`, `410 gone` and other final errors fail the entry with the reason; "Upload again" sends it with a new ID and so a new session. | Brief Design notes 1 and 2; 12 3.2 item 4 leaves the parallelism to the client, and two parts keep one slow part from stalling an upload without flooding the server. |
| D-M4-22-2 | 2026-10-20 | Hashing: the SHA-256 of the whole file is computed in a background isolate over a stream of the file (`sha256OfFile`) when the entry is created; parts are read one at a time with `RandomAccessFile`, never the whole file in memory. Downloads stream into the cache while their SHA-256 is computed and are kept only when it equals `Repr-Digest`. | Brief Design note 1; 12 9.3. |
| D-M4-22-3 | 2026-10-20 | Tickets: audio and video take a new `createFileTicket` per playback; the URL is resolved against the instance address, handed to the player and kept in no log, database row or file (test `download_test.dart`). Downloads of the app itself never use a ticket: they go through the REST client with DPoP. | Brief Design note 3; 12 9.2 item 4. |
| D-M4-22-4 | 2026-10-20 | Packages, each under the license gate: `record` 7.1.2 (BSD-3-Clause) for AAC-LC mono recording, `audioplayers` 6.8.1 (MIT) for voice and audio playback on macOS and Windows with the rates 1, 1.5 and 2, `video_player` 2.14.1 (BSD-3-Clause) for video through a ticket (macOS; on Windows `video_player` has no implementation, so a video there plays only after "Open"), `file_selector` 1.1.0 (BSD-3-Clause) for the open and save dialogs, `desktop_drop` 0.8.4 (Apache-2.0; 0.9 needs `cross_file` 0.4, which `file_selector` does not accept) for drag and drop and `pasteboard` 0.5.0 (Apache-2.0) for pasted files and images. `just_audio` was not taken because it has no Windows implementation of its own. | Brief Design note 4: choose by the license gate and the targets. |
| D-M4-22-5 | 2026-10-20 | Cache layout: thumbnails at `<instance folder>/files/<file id>/<variant>`, the original at `<instance folder>/files/<file id>/original/<file name>` so the system opens it with the app of its extension; downloads in progress in `files/.partial`, files the app writes before an upload (recordings, pasted images) in `<instance folder>/outgoing` and deleted after their upload. The rows stay in `file_cache` of the encrypted database. `FileCache` is the `FileCacheEraser` of the engine (access removal deletes the bytes), runs the `CacheCleaner` of M4-12 once per start and deletes the paths it returns, and the account wipe deletes the instance folder with everything in it. | Brief Design note 5; 15 6.3. |
| D-M4-22-6 | 2026-10-20 | States: a stored file maps to the views of 12 2 by `upload_state` and `scan_status` ("being checked", "Upload failed", "blocked", ready, and a tombstone for a deleted file, recognised by the redacted fields of 04 2.2 rule 5); an upload entry maps to waiting, uploading with the confirmed share, "Upload failed" with the reason, and canceled for `410 gone`; a confirmed upload whose message waits shows "being checked". A thumbnail that answers `409 file_not_ready` shows "being checked" and loads again on the next `file.updated` of the file; `409 file_blocked` shows "blocked". A failed upload is not listed as a refused write of its own; it shows on its message and in the composer. | 12 2; brief Goal and Design note 3. |
| D-M4-22-7 | 2026-10-20 | The internal reference form of an attachment in the Markdown content is `![alt](attachment:<file_id>)`; the resolver of `DsMarkdown` turns it into the thumbnail of that file only when the file is one of the message's own servable images, and every other image stays the text of M4-07. A message may consist of attachments only: `DsComposer` gained `hasAttachments`, which enables sending without text. | M4-07 Design note 2 leaves the form to this ticket; SEC-OUT-2. |
| D-M4-22-8 | 2026-10-20 | Voice: the recorder asks for the microphone at the first start; a denied permission shows how to allow it in the system settings. The recording stops by itself at `voice_max_duration_ms` and then waits to be sent or discarded. A voice message is a message with `kind = voice`, no text, no `clear_draft` (the typed draft stays) and exactly one `audio/mp4` attachment. "Transcribe" shows for a transcript that is `none` or `failed`; the server's answer decides, and `409 conflict` with `transcription_unavailable` shows a short text. The voice switch `voice.enabled` of 01 3.4 is not read: the server refuses when it is off. | 15 13; 12 8; 15 1 principle 1 (no own derivation in the client). |
| D-M4-22-9 | 2026-10-20 | The fake server serves the file operations from `rest/file_handlers.dart` over `world_files.dart`; `RestFake` reads `application/octet-stream` bodies as bytes, accepts a file ticket instead of DPoP on `downloadFile` and `downloadThumbnail`, and `FakeResponse.bytes` takes a status and headers; `FaultPlan.onRequest` takes a `reason`. Presigned parts go to `PUT /objectstore/<file_id>/<n>`, which refuses a request with `Authorization`. | Brief Scope (handler registry of M4-17) and Tests. |
| D-M4-22-11 | 2026-10-20 | Findings of the review and the red team: attached files are copied into `<instance folder>/outgoing/<key>/` and the copy is uploaded and deleted after the upload, so an upload resumes after a restart (a sandboxed macOS app loses access to a picked file when it quits); the local file is read only while parts are missing, so a session the server completed before the app quit is confirmed with `getFile` instead of a second completion; a file that cannot be read fails its upload with `file_unreadable` instead of retrying; `api_client` decodes the JSON problem of a binary response before the auth interceptors (`BinaryProblemInterceptor`), so downloads get the DPoP nonce retry, the refresh and the revocation like every other request; the logger of `audioplayers` is off; a second start or stop of a recording waits for the first; a failed Save as or Open shows its reason; and `message.deleted` or a redacted message deletes the file cache rows of the message's files and their bytes after the commit (`MessagesDao.tombstoneWithFiles`, 20 4.7). | Review and red team of M4-22 (Majors reproduced). |
| D-M4-22-10 | 2026-10-20 | The ticket is built on the integration base and not merged or opened as a pull request by the build; full verification runs as build-verify on the build branch, the integration tests run headless here with `-d flutter-tester` because this host has no Xcode, and on macOS and Windows in CI (`make apps-platform`). | Instructions of the launcher override the landing steps of the brief. |
| D-M4-22-12 | 2026-10-20 | The goldens of `files_golden_test.dart` pass with up to one differing pixel in 100,000: the close icon of a tile is anti-aliased one colour step apart on macOS and on the Linux runner, so no exact CI golden matches both hosts. The tolerance is set for this file only; the other goldens keep the exact comparison. | build-verify on the build branch showed the one-pixel difference in `ci/attachment_tiles_dark.png`; a golden regenerated on the runner fails on macOS by the same pixel. |

## M7-15 OpenTofu module generic-vm

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-15-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave49` at `eb15f40`, branch `feat/M7-15-tofu-generic-vm` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the full verification runs on the build branch `build/M7-15` and the PR lane lands it (the brief's landing step is replaced by the launcher's instructions). The brief relies on no core rule and no D-W ruling the base lacks in a way the code depends on (16 3.1, 18 2.1 and 6 are on the base from M7-29; D-W-100, D-W-125 and D-W-127 restate cost and trial rules the brief repeats), so nothing is ported first. The base has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section of the ticket, and the German `infra/tofu/README.md` and module README are rewritten in English from their `main` form. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the chapters are `16-betrieb.md` and `18-release.md`; the VM runner is `tools/ci/tofu_vm_test.py` (next to the trial it reuses, so ruff, mypy, bandit and coverage cover it) and `infra/tofu/test/generic-vm/` holds the root and its README; the root that calls all four modules is `infra/tofu/test/validate/`. | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-15-1 | 2026-10-20 | Tool choice (Design note 1, "Open" of the module README): one POSIX `sh` script `files/bothy-setup.sh` plus four rendered files (`setup.env`, `install-options`, `firewall.nft`, `release.pub`). New hosts get them as `#cloud-config` user data (`write_files`, `runcmd`, and `users` for `ssh_user` with `admin_ssh_public_keys`), rendered with `yamlencode` (output `user_data`); an existing host gets the same files from `terraform_data.ssh_setup` (`provision_over_ssh = true`), whose `remote-exec` writes a payload to a `mktemp` file of `ssh_user` and runs it with `sudo`; the key comes from the SSH agent, never from an input. New inputs: `ssh_port`, `sshd_port`, `provision_over_ssh`, `release_repository`, `release_base_url`, `release_public_key`, `owner_handle`, `owner_name`, `virus_scan`, `stack_dir`. `bothy_version` is the product release (its bundle pins the server version, 18 2.1). Inputs `install.sh` cannot honour stop the plan with a precondition: `database_mode = managed`, `object_storage_mode = s3`, a vendor exporter in `telemetry_exporters`, `backup_offsite.type = sftp`; `environment` reaches only the header of `setup.env`, because `install.sh` has no option for it. | One tested path for both deliveries; the module never writes `.env` or `secrets/` (requirement 15), and no input can carry a secret into the state (requirement 13). |
| D-M7-15-2 | 2026-10-20 | Verification on the host (requirement 14): cosign 3.1.3 from its GitHub release, checked against the SHA-256 of `tools/bin/tools.lock.json` (a test holds the module's copy equal); then `cosign verify-blob` of `SHA256SUMS`, the SHA-256 of bundle and `install.sh` from it (each listed exactly once), their own `.sigstore.json` signatures, and `BOTHY_REGISTRY` of the bundle against `image_registry`, all before `install.sh --bundle` unpacks anything. Signing mode: `release_public_key` set means the key of the private phase (`--key`, `--insecure-ignore-tlog`, ADR 0062); `null` means keyless against `^https://github\.com/<release_repository>/\.github/workflows/release\.yml@refs/tags/v` and the GitHub OIDC issuer, as the job `product-signing` verifies. Docker Engine and the Compose plugin come from `download.docker.com`, the key checked by its fingerprint; the package versions are not pinned (the repository signature and the minimum versions of 16 3 rule 7 are checked), because one version string per distribution and release would need a Renovate manager of its own. | Integrity from signatures, not from the transport, so the download address may be plain HTTP in the VM test. |
| D-M7-15-3 | 2026-10-20 | Offline `tofu-check` (Design note 3): the module uses only built-in features of OpenTofu (`templatefile`, `yamlencode`, `terraform_data`), so it has no provider to pin or mirror. `make tofu-check` runs `tofu fmt -check`, then `init -backend=false` with an empty local provider mirror as the only provider source (as `ops-check`) and `validate` of `infra/tofu/test/validate` and `infra/tofu/test/generic-vm`, in `make check` and therefore in CI. A target module with a provider (M7-16) fails here until it brings a pinned offline path. | No network in the gate; the same rule `ops-check` already enforces per module. |
| D-M7-15-4 | 2026-10-20 | The enrollment code of the first owner: `install.sh` prints it once on standard output, and cloud-init and the SSH delivery keep that output in a log (`/var/log/cloud-init-output.log`, the console of `tofu apply`). The setup runs `install.sh` with `setsid -w` and standard input closed, so it has no controlling terminal even under the pseudo terminal of `remote-exec` (with a terminal it would print the code to `/dev/tty`, past any filter), and replaces the line by the command that issues a new code (`bothy setup issue-enrollment-code --user <owner_handle>` in the API service of the edge, `api` or `api-tailscale` as in `compose.yaml`); the VM test checks that the code is not in the cloud-init log and that `detached` removes the terminal of an SSH PTY. With `edge_mode = tailscale` the one-time auth key is a root file `/etc/bothy/tailscale-auth-key` placed by the operator; the setup passes it to `install.sh` in the environment and removes the file after the installation. | A secret must not land in a log or the user data (requirement 13). |
| D-M7-15-5 | 2026-10-20 | Edge and firewall (requirement 16): `reverse_proxy` maps to `--edge direct` with port 443 public; `tailscale` to `--edge tailscale`, `private` to `--edge direct`, both without a public port. The nftables table `inet bothy_edge` (input policy drop) admits established traffic, loopback, the Docker bridges, ICMP, DHCP replies, the sshd port (`sshd_port`, default `ssh_port`, so a host with sshd on another port keeps its access) and the edge port, loaded at boot by `bothy-firewall.service` before the network (`DefaultDependencies=no`, `Before=network-pre.target shutdown.target`, as `nftables.service` does; with the default dependencies the unit forms an ordering cycle through `cloud-init.service` on Ubuntu, found by the red team); the Compose stack publishes on `127.0.0.1` only. The module installs no reverse proxy: the proxy belongs to the host or the target (a load balancer of M7-16). | The brief maps `edge_mode` to the options of 16 3.1; SSH stays for the maintenance access of the contract. |
| D-M7-15-6 | 2026-10-20 | VM runner and image pin (Design note 2): `make tofu-vm-test` runs `tools/ci/tofu_vm_test.py`: the trial of `release_trial.py` (registry, images, keys), the product build, SBOMs and private-phase signatures of bundle, `install.sh` and `SHA256SUMS` with the trial key; Ubuntu 24.04 `ubuntu-24.04-server-cloudimg-<arch>.img` of `release-20261017`, pinned by SHA-256 from its `SHA256SUMS`, cached in `.cache/tofu-vm/`; QEMU with user networking, SSH forwarded on `127.0.0.1` with a random port, the NoCloud seed by SMBIOS from the static server, KVM on Linux, HVF on macOS, TCG otherwise. A test-only cloud-config part forwards `127.0.0.1:<port>` of the registry and the static server to the host (`192.0.2.2`; the user network of QEMU is moved to the documentation range `192.0.2.0/24`, so no private address appears in the repository) with nftables DNAT, so bundle and Docker use the trial addresses unchanged; Docker accepts a loopback registry over HTTP, so no `insecure-registries` entry is needed. The smoke test is the probe of M1-73 (`bothy-smoke run`) through an SSH forward with a code from `issue-enrollment-code`; `precheck` and `scan` do not apply to an installed stack. Idempotence: a snapshot before and after the SSH delivery (file hashes, the listing of `secrets/` without contents, packages, firewall, container IDs and start times) must be equal, and the next plan empty. Two broken copies (a changed bundle; the changed bundle with a matching `SHA256SUMS` under the old signature) must stop the setup in the step `verify` before the stack directory exists. A reboot at the end must load the firewall without an ordering cycle in the journal and bring the stack back through `bothy.service` (discovery `200`). QEMU is installed with Homebrew on the build host; no CI job (D-W-127). | The brief's Design note 2 with the trial of M7-29 as the only release path; no cloud account (D-W-125). |

## M2-06 Invocation token with EdDSA and JWKS

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-06-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave50` at `d200587`, branch `feat/M2-06-invocation-token` in its own worktree with `feat/M2-05-runs-state-machine` at `20d1a89` merged in (stacked build); not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. Brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`. Section 4.5 with 4.5.1 of the agents chapter came in from main as its own commit, because the base lacked the claims `tool_class` and `dev_environment_id`, the adapter check items 1 to 10 and the reason table; the claim table of 05 section 5 names the same set on both, so the core contract is unchanged. The base names the chapters and ADR 0023 in German (`05-aktionen.md`, `09-agenten.md`, `0023-signierte-aufruf-marke-und-pruefung-im-adapter.md`); the schema test matches the chapters by number. The merge resolves the migration numbers of M2-05 after `00051_file_thumbnails.sql` of the base (`00052` to `00054`). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; the base carries the German chapters. |
| D-M2-06-1 | 2026-10-20 | `InvocationTokens` stays in `runs/app`; the root package `internal/runs` gets no type for it. M2-14, the first consumer in another module, adds the port with its own DTOs. | Every consumer of this milestone up to M2-17 is inside `runs` (02 section 5 rule 1). |
| D-M2-06-2 | 2026-10-20 | The triggering object of `followup` is the follow-up message (`message_id`), of `autocomplete` the command of the request (`command_id`, no `run_id`). A grant that breaks the table, a `command` without `run_id` included until M2-16 adds the control command grant, is an internal error and nothing is signed. | 05 section 5 names one object per token; `AgentFollowupPayload.message_id` and `CommandAutocompleteRequestedPayload.command_id` are those objects; fail closed. |
| D-M2-06-3 | 2026-10-20 | The header has exactly `alg`, `kid` and `typ`; `iat` is the issuing time truncated to seconds and `exp` is `iat` plus `agent.invocation_token_ttl` truncated to seconds; `aud` is one string; absent claims are omitted, never `null`. The issuer is set by the app layer (`WithIssuer`), not by the grant, and `Payload` refuses claims without one. | RFC 7519 NumericDate and section 4.1.3, ADR 0053; the issuer is the instance, not a decision about the delivery. |
| D-M2-06-4 | 2026-10-20 | `Verify` looks the key up by purpose `invocation` and accepts `next`, `active` and `retiring`; it also compares the purpose, the algorithm and the state of the returned key. An unreadable key store is returned as it is and is not `invalid`. | A process may lag a promotion by `signing_key.cache_ttl`; no `next` key ever signs. An outage is no verdict about a token and must not make an adapter finish the run as `invalid_invocation_token`. |
| D-M2-06-5 | 2026-10-20 | `typ` is compared byte for byte after the case-insensitive profile check of `kernel/jose`; a header `jwk` is never used. | 09 4.5 item 2 says "exactly". |
| D-M2-06-6 | 2026-10-20 | Every cause of `invalid` is checked before the expiry: `expired` only when the token is otherwise valid; `IgnoreExpiry` skips only the expiry. | 09 4.5 reason table: "`exp` passed, otherwise valid and current"; 09 4.5.1 step 1. |
| D-M2-06-7 | 2026-10-20 | Unknown payload members are ignored; member names are matched exactly (no case folding) and the issuer writes exactly the set of 05 section 5. | RFC 7519 section 4: a token of a newer server version stays verifiable during an upgrade. |
| D-M2-06-8 | 2026-10-20 | The server applies `auth.clock_leeway` to `iat` and `exp` of its own invocation tokens: valid while now is before `exp` plus the leeway. | 08 7.2 defines the leeway for `exp`, `nbf` and `iat`; the retiring window of 09 4.5 "Keys and rotation" point 2 already contains this margin, so an accepted token always has a published key. |
| D-M2-06-9 | 2026-10-20 | The reference verifier is test support in `runs/adapters/runstest`, written with the standard library only and independent of `kernel/jose`; it reports in this precedence: points 1 to 4 and 6 (`ErrRefInvalid`, `ErrRefUnknownKey` for a `kid` missing after one forced reload), then 8 and 10 (the mismatches), then 5 (`ErrRefExpired`, `ErrRefInvalid` for an `iat` in the future), then 7 (`ErrRefReplay`). A duplicate header or payload member is `ErrRefInvalid`. | A defect shared by issuer and server verifier cannot hide behind one parser (as G10 for webhooks); the precedence follows Design note 6 of the brief. |
| D-M2-06-10 | 2026-10-20 | This ticket proves AG-22 at token level: scheduled, no token fails at three adapters or a second process inside its validity through two rotations and no adapter needs a forced reload; emergency, the old key fails at once in the same process and within `signing_key.cache_ttl` in another, and a new token verifies after at most one reload. The run level of both halves is M2-31's. The late check of an adapter that saw the token before expects `ErrRefReplay`, which proves every other point held. | PLAN row M2-06 and M2-31; the replay check is the last point of the adapter check. |
| D-M2-06-11 | 2026-10-20 | `InvocationToken` wraps the compact JWS and renders as `[redacted]` through `String`, `GoString`, `Format`, `LogValue`, `MarshalText` and `MarshalJSON`; only `Reveal` returns it. | SEC-LOG-2, SEC-SEC-3; the same type as the terminal session token of M2-44. |
| D-M2-06-12 | 2026-10-20 | `internal/wire` builds the invocation tokens in the signing keys step (`Runs.BindInvocationTokens`), not where the runs module is built in the chat step. | The chat step runs before the signing keys exist; the bind is once per process and adds no route, subscription or job. |

## M2-20 Heartbeat, presence and desired versus actual mode

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-20-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave49` at `eb15f40`, branch `feat/M2-20-heartbeat-presence` in its own worktree; not merged locally and no pull request from this build; the full verification runs on the build branch. The contract the brief needs (the `agents` columns of 03, `agent.presence_timeout` of 04, `agent.heartbeat` and `agent.presence_changed` of 05, the openapi schemas) is on the base, so nothing is ported; the base names the chapters in German, and text this ticket adds there is English. Differences to the brief's names, following the merged code (D-W-100): `domain.ReportedChange(stored, next Reported)` reads the evidence from `next.Evidence` (`Reported` gained `Evidence`); the brief's `GetTargets` is `GetPresenceRows`, one query for the configured values and the stored presence; `kernel/clock` has no timers, so the deadline timers and the flush loop run on the port `app.Timers` (`time.AfterFunc`, a fake in tests, the pattern of `terminal/app`); the metrics port is `Tracker.Samples()`, read by the observable gauges of `internal/agents/adapters/otelmetrics` over the registry specs (no separate no-op port); the snapshot takes `AgentPresence` from the agent listing, whose presence comes from the tracker; the field type `agents.PresenceStatus` replaces the earlier struct `agents.Presence`, whose name is now the port; the change path stores the heartbeat time of the clock in `last_heartbeat_at`. A person who is no owner and an app get `404 not_found` from `sendHeartbeat` (and `error` with `not_found` over the gateway), because the resource is the agent itself and 404 comes before 403 (R-AZ-01, D-M2-07-12); the brief's `403 forbidden` with `subject_kind_not_allowed` is what the owner gets. The row D-G-280 the brief names does not exist on the base or on `origin/main`; the placeholder `unknown` it means is ruling M1-63/3, which names the end of its presence part. | The task names the base and the lane; the merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M2-20-1 | 2026-10-20 | One decoder (`domain.DecodeHeartbeat`) and one use case (`Tracker.Heartbeat` behind the port `agents.Presence`) serve both transports: the REST handler `SendHeartbeat` encodes the checked body again, the gateway passes the raw member `agent`. The presence lives in one process-wide tracker in `agents/app`, started only in the roles `api` and `all`; it publishes through `kernel/eventlog.EphemeralPublisher` and reaches project readiness only through the `chat` port. Place: `internal/agents/presence.go`, `internal/agents/app/presence.go`, `internal/agents/app/heartbeat.go`. | `sendHeartbeat`, `agent.heartbeat`, the table `agents` and `agent.presence_changed` belong to `agents`, the op `heartbeat` to `gateway`, which may use `agents` (02 4.4, 4.6, 4.7); only api and all receive heartbeats (02 3.2), and one mapping keeps both transports equal. |
| D-M2-20-2 | 2026-10-20 | `agent_heartbeat.v1` is closed: the top-level members of the openapi schema `Heartbeat` and the `details` keys `capabilities` (`followups` `steer` or `queue`; `components`, `voice_input`, `title`, `files_out` booleans), `active_runs` (distinct UUIDs), `degraded_reasons` (distinct codes), `adapter` (`name`, optional `version`), `runtime` (`name`), `protocol_version` (integer, at least 1); any other key, also inside `capabilities`, `adapter` and `runtime`, is an unknown member. Forms: names `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]*$`, versions `^[0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.+_-]*$`, codes `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.:-]*$`; no duplicates in the lists and among the `channel_id` values of `project_readiness` (pointer `/project_readiness`); `rate_limit` is `not_enabled` until M6-14. `runtime_version` is the runtime's version (`reported_runtime_version`), `details.adapter.version` the adapter's (18 9 point 5). The domain checks every rule itself on both transports. Place: `internal/agents/domain/heartbeat.go`, 09 4.8. | 09 4.8 forbids secrets, contents and paths; these forms make path separators, whitespace, control and bidi characters and most key formats unrepresentable, and the code form of SEC-AUD-5 lets `method` enter the audit unchanged (SEC-IN-1). |
| D-M2-20-3 | 2026-10-20 | Full state: every heartbeat replaces the whole reported state; an absent optional member means "not reported" (`reported_billing_mode` and `reported_tool_class` NULL, the key absent in `reported_details`), except `project_readiness`. A change of the reported mode or class against the stored value, also the first report ever and a change from or to "not reported", runs one unit of work: lock the row, compare again, write both columns with the whole reported state and record `agent.heartbeat` `allow` with only `billing_mode_old`, `billing_mode_new`, `tool_class_old`, `tool_class_new` (each only when it exists) and `key_type` and `method`; one entry per heartbeat, also when both columns change; equal after the lock writes nothing. Only this path writes the two audited columns. Place: `internal/agents/app/heartbeat.go`, `queries/presence.sql` (`LockPresenceRow`, `WriteReportedChange`). | 09 4.7 "last one wins"; the reported mode is the evidence of I3, so its history must be checkable (10 8, 14), and the row lock lets exactly one process audit a change. |

Place: `domain.StateAt`, `Tracker.publish`. | One boundary rule for timer and tests; the pair covers the payload `AgentPresencePayload`, and 03 names the "last reported mode", so clients keep showing it while the agent is away. |
| D-M2-20-5 | 2026-10-20 | The flush loop writes every dirty entry at most once per `agent.heartbeat_persist_interval`, ascending agent id, in one unit of work, with the monotone `PersistHeartbeat` (`last_heartbeat_at IS NULL OR last_heartbeat_at <= $hb`); an entry is clean afterwards either way, and the offline write uses the entry's last heartbeat time. The stop step `agent_presence` in the phase of the connections, after the gateway's step, stops the timers without publishing and flushes within the rest of `shutdown.total_timeout`. Place: `internal/agents/app/presence_flush.go`, 02 15.3. | A fresher heartbeat stored by another process is never overwritten (OQ-07: node-neutral code); a restart of the server is not an agent going offline. |
| D-M2-20-6 | 2026-10-20 | Start rule: the tracker loads the agents stored `online` or `degraded`; a missing `last_heartbeat_at` or one older than `agent.presence_timeout` plus `agent.heartbeat_persist_interval` is `offline` at once, the others keep their state until the start plus `agent.presence_timeout`. Agents not loaded get an entry with their first heartbeat. Place: `domain.StartRule`, `Tracker.Start`. | The stored value lags the real last heartbeat by at most the persist interval, so an older value proves no heartbeat within `agent.presence_timeout`; the grace gives adapters time to reconnect after `4013`. |

Warning codes of `AgentRuntimeStatus.warnings`: `billing_mode_mismatch` only after (a) has held for longer than `billing.mismatch_alert_after`, `billing_mode_unsupported` (b) and `billing_evidence_unknown` (c) at once, `tool_class_mismatch` for a reported tool class unequal to `tool_class`; no warning and no difference while `offline` or `unknown`. Place: `domain.Assess`, `Assessment.Warnings`. | 10 8 names the duration only for row 1; an undeterminable mode counts as unequal to every configured mode (10 7); an agent that is not present reports nothing current. |

The rule `BothyBillingModeMismatch` keeps its counter expression and adds `or max by (bothy_agent_handle) (bothy_agent_billing_mode_mismatch_age_seconds{job="bothy/bothy-server"}) >` the default of `billing.mismatch_alert_after` in seconds as the last comparison; severity `page`. | No | Date | Ruling | Reason |

| D-M7-16-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave50` at `d200587`, branch `feat/M7-16-tofu-cloud-modules` in its own worktree, with the built but not yet integrated `feat/M7-15-tofu-generic-vm` at `add1b93` merged in first (the conflict in this file resolved as a union); not merged locally and not pushed as a pull request by this build, the full verification runs on the build branch and the PR lane lands it, which replaces the brief's landing steps. The brief relies on no core rule or D-W ruling the base lacks (D-W-100 and D-W-125 are here; `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` with OQ-09 is missing on the base, a known gap), so nothing is ported first. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the chapter is `docs/spec/16-betrieb.md`; the root of the `tofu test` files is `infra/tofu/test/cloud/`, the static test `tools/ci/tests/test_tofu_cloud_modules.py`, the provider mirror `tools/bin/tofu_providers.py`. | Instructions of the launcher; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-16-1 | 2026-10-20 | Static proof (Design note 1): the pinned OpenTofu 1.13.1 supports `tofu test` with `mock_provider`, so `infra/tofu/test/cloud/{hetzner,aws,azure}.tftest.hcl` apply each module against mocked providers with representative inputs and assert the security rules (only 443 from the internet and only with `reverse_proxy`, SSH only from `ssh_allowed_cidrs`, the Azure deny rule), the user data of `generic-vm` within the limit of the target, the outputs, the off-site bucket of `aws` and every refusal (`expect_failures`); `make tofu-check` runs them. A pytest test reads the configuration for what a plan cannot show: the variable and output names against the contract (requirement 13), one resource per module that admits traffic so the assertions see every rule (requirement 14), no secret name, `sensitive` flag or secret-holding resource, the enforced encryption of the example root (requirement 15) and the pins against the lock files. No HCL parser was added: `tofu fmt` fixes the layout the regular expressions read. | Mocked plans test the real module code with the real provider schemas; no credential, no cost (D-W-125). |
| D-M7-16-2 | 2026-10-20 | Offline providers (D-M7-15-3): each module pins its provider to one version (`= 1.70.0`, `= 6.69.1`, `= 5.10.0`); the lock files of `test/validate` and `test/cloud` (equal, written by `tofu providers lock` for linux and darwin, amd64 and arm64) hold the `zh:` hash of every archive. `tools/bin/tofu_providers.py mirror` asks the OpenTofu registry for the archive of the current platform, accepts it only when the registry's SHA-256 is one of the locked `zh:` hashes, checks the downloaded bytes again and stores it in the packed mirror layout in `.cache/tofu-providers/` (a cached archive is checked on every call). `tofu-check` and the check `tofu` of `ops-check` use that mirror as the only provider source with a temporary plugin cache, and `tofu-check` inits with `-lockfile=readonly`. The registry refuses the default User-Agent of urllib (HTTP 403), so the tool sends its own. Renovate moves pins and lock files in one group ("OpenTofu providers"). | The gate stays without registry access during `init`; the only download is the pinned archive, as for `tools/bin/tools.lock.json`. |
| D-M7-16-3 | 2026-10-20 | PostgreSQL 18 per provider (requirement 17), retrieved 2026-10-20: Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL offers 18 with the minor versions 18.1 to 18.6 (https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/PostgreSQLReleaseNotes/postgresql-versions.html); Azure Database for PostgreSQL flexible server supports 18, current minor 18.6, with some extensions not yet available (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/postgresql/flexible-server/concepts-supported-versions); Hetzner Cloud has no managed PostgreSQL service (the provider `hetznercloud/hcloud` 1.70.0 has no resource for one). Written into 16 3 rule 6 and the module READMEs. The unused inputs `rds_instance_class` (aws) and `postgres_sku` (azure) of the planned contract are removed; the managed path returns with the setup option, and the PostgreSQL 18 facts of D-M7-16-3 are ready for it. | Build the minimum that works; no resource that cannot be used. The setup option is a change of 16 3.1 and of `install.sh`, outside this brief. |
| D-M7-16-5 | 2026-10-20 | Off-site append-only per provider (requirement 16, 16 8.2 item 3, R-OP-09): `aws` with `offsite_region` creates a bucket in that region with versioning, Object Lock in mode Compliance for `offsite_retention_days` (default 90, the default of `backup.objects.retention`), the lifecycle rule of backup-restore.md 2 (noncurrent versions expire after the same days, expired delete markers go), public access blocked, `BucketOwnerEnforced`, SSE-S3, TLS only, and a writer user whose policy has only `ListBucket`, `ListBucketVersions`, `GetBucketLocation`, `GetObject`, `PutObject` and `DeleteObject`; the location goes to `install.sh --offsite`. It creates no access key, because its secret would be in the state: the owner creates the writer's key, the separate deleting key and sets `backup.offsite.region` by hand. `offsite_region` and `backup_offsite` exclude each other. `hetzner`: the provider has no resource for Object Storage, so the owner creates the bucket with Object Lock in the Console and passes it in `backup_offsite`. `azure`: `bothy-backupd` writes to S3 only and Blob Storage has no S3 API, so the target is an S3 bucket elsewhere in `backup_offsite`. | Compliance mode is enforced by the provider for every account; what a module cannot create is written down per README. |

No load balancer and no Application Gateway: the stack publishes on `127.0.0.1` only, so the TLS proxy of the edge runs on the host as with `generic-vm` (D-M7-15-5), and 16 3 notes this as built. The setup inputs of `generic-vm` are inputs of every cloud module and are passed on unchanged. Found by the mocked apply: the user data of `generic-vm` exceeds the 16 KiB of EC2, so `aws` passes it gzip-compressed (`user_data_base64`), and the tests hold the limits of Hetzner (32 KiB) and Azure (64 KiB). `hetzner` and `azure` require one SSH key (Hetzner otherwise mails a root password, Azure needs one for the administrator). Place: `authz/domain/requirements_table.go`, `apps/app/incoming.go` (`asNotFound`). | 07 4.2 allows anonymous only for the right `public`, and this right is "public with token; the app needs write"; 11 8.4 sends the branch "authorize app: message.create on the channel" with "no" to `404`, so a denial reveals nothing. |

A replay (same author, `client_msg_id` and hash) answers `200` with `Idempotent-Replay: true` and publishes nothing. | The hash covers the request as sent (04 5.1 point 3), and a `client_msg_id` repeated through another webhook of the same app gives `422 idempotency_key_reuse` instead of a replay of another webhook's message. |

Payload values have control characters replaced by a space and every ASCII punctuation character escaped; a URL is written only as an autolink of an absolute `http` or `https` URL with a host made only of printable ASCII without `<` and `>` (a character such as U+FEFF would end the autolink in a client and let the rest render as Markdown), else its line is left out; above `message.max_content_bytes` items are dropped from the end, then the footer, then the text is cut at a UTF-8 boundary with `...`. | 11 8.2 lists the contents, not the layout, and `docs/observability/alerting.md` 2 adds severity and runbook link; the escaping keeps a label from adding formatting, links or images (SEC-OUT-1), and the server never fetches these URLs (SEC-OUT-2). |

## M2-21 Switching and guided flow (I4)

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |

| D-M2-21-0 | 2026-10-20 | Base `int/m1-wave50` at `d200587`, branch `feat/M2-21-billing-switch-i4` in its own worktree with `feat/M2-20-heartbeat-presence` at `cb2a50f` merged first (stacked build); not merged locally and no pull request from this build, the full verification runs on the build branch. The base holds the contract the brief needs (05 rule 1.6, R-AZ-17, the German chapters of the specification), so no contract commit is ported; the spec paragraphs of this ticket are English additions to the German chapter files of the base, as M2-20 did. Place: this branch. | The task of the steering names the base, the stacked dependency and the verification lane; the brief's own merge and pull request steps follow the integration. |
| D-M2-21-1 | 2026-10-20 | The guided flow crosses module borders through two ports: `authz.I1Resolver` (validation, `authorize` of every listed agent, `AddAffected`, the call of the switch) that identity and chat get through their constructors, and the source port `authz.AgentPolicySwitch` that agents implements (`authzsource.Switch`). `EmptyAgentPolicySwitch` fails closed; `internal/wire` binds the real switch in the agents step and `bindGuidedFlow` fails the start when the empty one remains. Place: `internal/authz/resolvei1.go`, `internal/authz/app/resolvei1.go`, `internal/wire/serve.go`. | authz owns the rights logic of every action, agents owns its table, event and audit entry; identity and chat may not import agents (02 4.4), and the agents module is built after authz. |
| D-M2-21-2 | 2026-10-20 | Order for all five actions: the whole body is validated first where the merged use case validates its body (authz before `authorize`; identity and chat with their reason after `authorize`, as M1-31 and M1-54 do), then the outer `authorize` and the outer checks that write nothing, and the switch runs inside the unit of the outer action after those checks (for the second unit of identity and chat under its locks) and before its first write. Place: `CreateGrant`, `addMember`, `addOwnerWrite`, `setSpaceOperatorWrite`, `setChannelOperatorWrite`. | A caller without read right on the outer target learns nothing about the listed agents, and the switch never commits without the change (BR-07). |
| D-M2-21-3 | 2026-10-20 | An operator change without effect switches no agent; it answers as M1-54 D2 with the no-change entry of the operator action. Place: `chat/app/operators.go`. | The guided flow exists to make a change admissible; a request without change cannot violate I1, and a switch alone goes through `setAgentBillingMode`. |
| D-M2-21-4 | 2026-10-20 | A listed agent already on `organization` keeps its row and gets an allow entry `agent.billing_mode.set` with `no_change: true`; a disabled personal agent is switched like an active one; no `If-Match` is read for the listed agents. Place: `agents/app/switch.go`. | Another owner may have switched the agent since the preview and the aim still holds; a disabled personal agent counts for I1 again at `agent.enable`; `set_all` reads no `If-Match` either (06 8). |
| D-M2-21-5 | 2026-10-20 | One apply step `applySwitchToOrganization` writes the row and `agent.updated` of every switch to `organization` for `SetBillingMode`, `SetBillingModeAll` and `SwitchToOrganization`; each caller keeps its own audit entry (one per agent for the single switch and the guided flow, one with the list for `set_all`). Place: `agents/app/switch.go`, `agents/app/policy.go`. | All three paths must write the same rows and events (`TestSwitchPathsShareApplyStep`). |
| D-M2-21-6 | 2026-10-20 | A successful guided flow writes one allow entry `agent.billing_mode.set` per listed agent and then the entry of the outer action, all with the request's `correlation_id`; with one agent these are the two entries of BR-07. Place: `agents/app/switch.go`. | 05 rule 1.6 asks for one audit entry per action. |
| D-M2-21-7 | 2026-10-20 | The presence tracker learns a desired mode changed by the guided flow with the agent's next heartbeat; no after-commit step is added. `presence.billing_mode_matches` in `getAgent`, `listAgents` and `agent.updated` compares the reported mode with the desired mode of that read instead of the tracker memory. Place: `agents/app/service.go` `agentView`. | `kernel/txn` has no after-commit phase and the HTTP chain owns the outermost unit under `Idempotency-Key` (02 8.1, 8.4); without the read-time comparison the card would show a match until the next heartbeat. |
| D-M2-21-8 | 2026-10-20 | `previewGrant` with `resolve_i1` authorizes `access.preview` with the target action `agent.billing_mode.set` for every listed agent (owner, no step-up, no audit), switches them in the dry run before the grant and starts `text` with one switch line per agent switched from `personal` and the not-ready line when `organization` is missing from its `billing_modes_supported`, by agent name. Place: `authz/app/preview.go`, `authz/domain/previewtext`. | R-RE-10: the preview is a dry run of the whole request; the console shows the lines of 10 section 11 point 2. |
| D-M2-21-9 | 2026-10-20 | No new key in `features`. Place: none. | `resolve_i1` belongs to the spec version that declares it (06 13 point 1). |
| D-M2-21-10 | 2026-10-20 | The brief's test of the agent's own consumer reads the events through catch-up (`syncapp.CatchUpService.PageEvents` as the agent), the read the consumer engine of `pollAgentEvents` uses, because the integration world of M1-54 builds no gateway engine; a personal token of an owner gets `403 forbidden` with `owner_only` on `addOwner` (the order of M1-31 and SR5) where the brief expected `step_up_not_possible`. Place: `test/integration`. | The merged code of M2-07 and M1-31 decides these answers; the tests follow the code (D-W-100). |
| D-M2-21-11 | 2026-10-20 | The entry lock of the presence tracker (`e.mu`) is never held across a database call. A heartbeat runs under a second per-entry lock (`e.beat`, taken only by `Heartbeat`, never inside a unit of work because the route is exempt from `Idempotency-Key`) and takes `e.mu` only for its memory steps; it keeps configured values that a `Changed` set during its database calls. Every other holder of `e.mu` (`memory` for views and `agent.updated`, `Changed`, `Samples`, `Flush`, `expire`, `Stop`) therefore never waits for a heartbeat that waits for a row; the fallback of the first fix (`TryLock` with the stored row) is removed. Place: `agents/app/heartbeat.go` `Heartbeat`, `agents/app/presence.go` `entry`. | Class fix of the lock cycle of M2-21 (ruling of the steering under the owner rule "same error class a second time: fix the class at one place"): the cycle was any holder of an agents or channel row that takes `e.mu` while a heartbeat holds `e.mu` and waits for that row (switch and guided flow through `memory`, red team round 1; `disableAgent` and `enableAgent` under `Idempotency-Key` through `Changed`, delta red team). Fixing the heartbeat, the only holder of `e.mu` across the database, closes every entry at once; `TestPresenceNoTrackerCallWaitsForAHeartbeatInTheDatabase` parks a heartbeat in each of its database calls against every entry, the `TestLockCycle_*` integration tests keep the repros. |

The base names the chapters in German (`09-agenten.md`), so the key's chapter is `09-agenten.md`, and the run-driven tests stand in for `pollAgentEvents` with `runs.Delivery` like the M2-09 tests (the world has no gateway). | The steering task wins over the brief; the merged names win (D-W-100). |

A new language is a new file plus the new default in 09 section 21. | REUSE 5.1 "Adaptation", D-L4; reading an embedded file touches only memory, so the domain stays free of input and output. |
| D-M2-18-2 | 2026-10-21 | The default holds no empty entry; the empty title is a placeholder through 09 section 12 point 2 itself, and a configured entry that is blank after normalisation is ignored. | D-M2-18-6 | 2026-10-21 | A person's title change always gives `manual`, also with a placeholder text and on a `manual` thread. | Placeholder detection runs only at creation (R-AG-15); a person who sets a title chose it. |
| D-M2-18-7 | 2026-10-21 | An agent or app other than the thread's operator agent that passes `thread.title.update` (as creator) sets `manual`; 09 section 12 point 1 and the description of `setThreadTitle` say so now. | 05 `thread.title.update` exempts only the operator agent, and the core contract wins (SPEC 8). |
